# MONTH OF FEBRUARY

*Having 28 days; but if it be a leap year, then it has 29.*

*February 1: Forefeast of the Meeting of our Lord, God, and Savior Jesus Christ; and the Holy Martyr Tryphon:*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Forefeast, 3, Tone 1. Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ*):*[^5]

*The radiant Church prepares herself to receive within herself the Lord, Who comes as an infant and spiritually enlightens by grace His most faithful and God-loving assembly; therefore she cries out: "Thou art the glory and praise, and the adornment of my fullness, O Word, Who hast become an infant in the flesh for my sake!"*

*The bridal chamber of many lights, the most honorable tabernacle, the holy and spacious temple, which brings into the inner chambers the Church of the Lord, betrothes herself to His most honorable Church, and prays unceasingly that they who truly glorify her as the Birthgiver of God may be delivered from corruption and perils.*

*Let us now offer a forefeast praise unto Christ, glorifying His descent: for He Who is borne as an infant by virginal hands comes to recline in the arms of Symeon, working the restoration of all mankind, and deifying human nature, as Benefactor and Lord.*

*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 4. Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (*Всехва́льнїи*):*[^6]

*O all-lauded Tryphon, thou hast become a most radiant partaker of incorruptible sweetness in the heavens; by valiant struggles thou hast suffered gloriously, and having become a witness of the Truth, pray thou unto Christ to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.*

*(Fol. 1v)*

*great mercy.*

*O most glorious Tryphon, with the beams of the unoriginate Light thou didst become wholly light-resembling, having overthrown all dark delusion and cast down the ruler of the darkness through divine grace; therefore pray thou that there be granted unto our souls peace and great mercy.*

*O all-blessed Tryphon, thou hast been accounted worthy of angelic joy and gladness; having rejected the beauties of life, and through soul-strength having counted perils as nothing, in utmost endurance thou didst complete thy martyrdom, and thou dost pray for our souls.*

*Glory... Tone 2. By Cyprian:*[^7]

*Being sought by those on earth, O all-blessed and venerable Tryphon, thou didst manly hasten to the tribunal; and having skillfully overthrown the proud one through the wrestling of blood, O martyr, thou didst receive the crown of victory: praying unto Christ our God for us, fail thou not, O passion-bearer, that our souls may be saved.*

*Both now... Tone 2. By Germanus:*[^8]

*Today Symeon receives into his arms the Lord of glory, Whom Moses beforehand saw under the gloom, when He gave him the tablets on Mount Sinai; this is He Who spoke in the prophets and is the Maker of the law. This is He Whom David proclaims, Who is fearful unto all, having great and rich mercy.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (*До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ*):*

*Receive, O Symeon, the Lord of glory, even as it was revealed unto thee by the Holy Spirit; for behold, He is come!*

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.*[^9]

*Bearing the Creator and Master as an infant in her arms, the pure and most immaculate Virgin enters into the temple.*

*Verse: A light for revelation to the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*[^10]

*A great and fearful mystery, and a marvelous dispensation: He Who embraces all things and created infants, is borne as an infant in arms!*

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2. Idiomelon: Of Holy Symeon:*[^11]

*For the holy sanctuary bore the Fire; and Symeon, stretching forth his hands, received Him rejoicing, and cried out: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word, O Lord!"*

*Troparion of the Martyr, Tone 4:*

*Thy martyr Tryphon, O Lord, in his contest received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the tormentors, (Fol. 2r)*

*and shattered the feeble audacities of the demons. By his intercessions save Thou our souls.*

*Glory... Both now... Troparion of the Forefeast, Tone 4. Podoben: "The choir of angels" (*Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй*):*

*The heavenly choir of the heavenly angels, stooping down unto earth, beholds the Firstborn of all creation carried as an infant to the temple by a Mother who knew not wedlock; therefore rejoicing with us, they sing a forefeast hymn.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Forefeast, twice; Glory... of the Saint; Both now... of the Forefeast.*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (*Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства*):*

*Beholding Thee incarnate of the Virgin, O Christ, holy Symeon rejoicing took Thee up, crying: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master!" And Anna the blameless and glorious prophetess now brings confession and singing unto Thee. And we, O Giver of life, cry unto Thee: Glory to Him Who has thus been well-pleased!*

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "The pastoral pipes" (*Свирѣ́лей па́стырскихъ*):*[^12]

*Thou wast born on earth, Who art co-unoriginate with the Father; Thou wast brought into the temple, Who art incomprehensible. And the elder, rejoicing, received Thee into his arms, crying: "Now lettest Thou depart him whom Thou hast visited according to Thy word, O Thou Who hast been well-pleased to save the human race as God!"*

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Forefeast with the Heirmos to 8; and of the Saint to 4.*

*Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*Having traversed the watery deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, Israel of old conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness through the hands of Moses stretched out in the likeness of the Cross.*[^13]

*Troparion:*

*Having all prepared our hearts even as hands, let us purely receive Christ Who comes today, and let us delight through His Spirit in divine gifts.*

*Troparion:*

*Having opened wide its doors today, the temple of the Law prepares radiant entrances for the Lawgiver and Creator, and prepares honorable gifts, a divine praise unto Him.*

*Troparion:*

*Singing unto Thee, O Lord my God, Who hast become an infant in the flesh, and submitted to the Law, and saved man, for whose sake Thou didst become man.*

*Theotokion:*

*Having perceived thee, O most pure Lady, to be a pure habitation for the Master of all and God, the ancient company of the temple proclaimed, magnifying thy glory.*

*Another Canon of the Martyr.*

*(Fol. 2v)*

*Whose acrostic is: "Account me worthy to partake of sweetness, O Tryphon."*[^14]

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*Traversing the water as on dry land, and having escaped from the wickedness of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God!*[^15]

*Troparion:*

*Truly delighting in the divine sweetness in the heavens as an invincible sufferer, by thy prayers deliver my soul from the turmoil of the passions.*

*Troparion:*

*Having been instructed by the divine words, O most glorious one, thou didst accomplish these things actively, having loved holiness and embraced temperance.*

*Troparion:*

*Thou wast truly a shepherd, shepherding the thoughts of thy soul in wisdom, turning wandering souls and bringing them unto God, O glorious one.*

*Theotokion:*

*O most immaculate Maiden, who didst bear the Offshooting Radiance of the Father's glory, enlighten me with noetic illumination, and dispel the dark gloom of sin.*

*If it be Sunday, Katavasia: The sun once shone upon dry land:*[^16]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

*Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and firm foundation!*

*Troparion:*

*The processions within the house of God leaped spiritually with joy, being about to receive the King Christ in the flesh.*

*Troparion:*

*Having today prepared acceptable gifts of praise unto Christ as honorable offerings, O ye faithful, let us meet Him with joy.*

*Troparion:*

*Now the shadow has passed away, and the truth has come forth, since grace has arrived: now receive Christ, O Symeon!*

*Theotokion:*

*The proclamation contained in shadows and symbolic depictions has ceased, for pure Truth has been made manifest unto the ends of the earth through thee.*

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

*Thou art the firm foundation of them that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the Light of the darkened, and my spirit sings praises unto Thee.*

*Troparion:*

*Thou didst blunt the power of the proud one, O glorious martyr of Christ, having been clothed with power from on high.*

*Troparion:*

*Though encompassed by flesh, thou didst quench the insolence of the bodiless enemy, and didst proclaim the incarnate God the Word.*

*Troparion:*

*At thy mere approach alone the spirits of wickedness are driven away, being expelled by the grace of the Holy Spirit.*

*Theotokion:*

*Having thee as my help, I shall not be put to shame, O most pure Mother of God; having thee as the mediatress of my life, I shall be saved.*

*(Fol. 3r)*

*Kontakion of the Martyr, Tone 8. Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки*):*

*With the firmness of the Trinity, thou didst utterly destroy polytheism from the ends of the earth, O all-glorious one, having become honorable in Christ; and having conquered the tormentors in Christ the Savior, thou didst receive the crown of thy martyrdom, and the gifts of divine healings, as invincible.*

*And Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (*Ско́рѡ предварѝ*):*

*Rejoicing in bodily afflictions, O blessed one, thou didst worthily receive divine and painless nourishment, having received the crown of immortality from God. Therefore thou dost pour forth rivers of healings unto them that flee with love unto thy shelter, O martyr.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast, Tone 1. Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (*Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ*):*

*O eternal Treasure of the life of all things, Who for my sake didst become an infant under the Law, and Who of old didst inscribe the Law upon the tablets on Mount Sinai, that Thou mightest change all things from the ancient bondage of the Law: Glory to Thy loving-kindness, O Savior; glory to Thy descent; glory to Thy dispensation, O only Lover of mankind!*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

*Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, crying worthily: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*[^17]

*Troparion:*

*Borne upon a light-bearing cloud bodily as the Sun of righteousness, He presents today His divine beams, and enlightens beforehand His temple.*

*Troparion:*

*Today the Church brings forefeast praise unto the Master, and brightly prepares to receive Him as the Bridegroom, and rejoices with Symeon; the angelic ranks were struck with awe, beholding Him Who is praised with the Father and the Spirit held in the arms of the elder, as the Son and our God.*

*Theotokion:*

*Being about to receive thee as the tabernacle, O all-glorious Birthgiver of God, bearing in thy hands the Lord of the Law, the shadow of the Law was illumined by thy grace.*

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I considered Thy works, and I glorified Thy Divinity.*[^18]

*Troparion:*

*Thou didst astonish the onlookers by the firmness of thy endurance, O blessed one; for thou didst endure torments as if suffering in another's body, O wise Tryphon.*

*Troparion:*

*Thou wast nourished, O blessed one, with the wounds of natural suffering, but now thou dost purely delight in the eternal (Fol. 3v)*

*glory.*

*Troparion:*

*While thy body was being scraped with the sharpness of iron claws, O marvelous one, the strength of thy soul was being revived by divine grace.*

*Theotokion:*

*All my hope I place upon thee, O Virgin Mother: preserve my soul, O thou who didst bear God, my Savior.*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

*Thou, my Lord, art come into the world as a Light, a holy Light, turning back from dark ignorance them that sing Thy praises in faith.*[^19]

*Troparion:*

*Being about to receive Thee in the flesh, O Lord, the Church was gloriously illuminated by Thy divine and ineffable radiance of Divinity.*

*Troparion:*

*Account them worthy to receive Thee with virginal arms, O Lord, who praise Thy dispensation; and receive us who sing Thy praises in faith, as Lover of mankind.*

*Troparion:*

*Account them that receive Thee with a joyful soul, Who art willing to come in the flesh for our sake, O Lord, to give thanks unto Thee together with Symeon.*

*Theotokion:*

*Delivered through thee from the shadowy service, O pure one, with true understanding and firm faith we worthily glorify thee.*

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

*Rising early, we cry unto Thee, O Lord: Save us! For Thou art our God; besides Thee we know no other.*[^20]

*Troparion:*

*Thou didst abolish the seductive idols, O passion-bearer; for thou didst love only to bow down to the image of Christ.*

*Troparion:*

*Having perceived the most radiant grace of our God, O glorious passion-bearer, for its sake thou didst suffer.*

*Troparion:*

*Illumined by divine grace, O sufferer, thou didst zealously suffer for Christ's sake.*

*Theotokion:*

*Ineffable was thy conception, and thy childbearing beyond expression; for thou didst remain a pure Ever-Virgin.*

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood which flowed in mercy from Thy side.*[^21]

*Troparion:*

*Having woven hymns like flowers today, let us prepare in faith a divine crown of thanksgiving unto the Master, Who is about to come spiritually.*

*Troparion:*

*Let us prepare for Him the hands of our soul, even as the elder Symeon, and let us receive Him with gladness as He enters into the temple of His divine glory.*

*(Fol. 4r)*

*Troparion:*

*Receiving the Creator of the ages as an infant, the elder understood Him to be the pre-eternal God, and the Light of the Gentiles unto the glory of Israel, even Christ.*

*Theotokion:*

*Writing with the spiritual reed, the God-beholding prophets, seeing thee as a temple and tabernacle and divine house for the Master of all, adorned themselves concerning thee.*

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!*[^22]

*Troparion:*

*Having overthrown the abominable sacrifices and foul offerings and demonic frenzies with thy blood, O glorious one, thou didst water all the noble plants of the Church, O Tryphon.*

*Troparion:*

*The martyr's wounds fragrance like myrrh, the blood of the sufferer pours forth healings, and the relics of his body sanctify the souls of them that touch them in faith.*

*Theotokion:*

*As a living ark thou didst receive the unoriginate Word; as a holy sanctuary thou didst contain the Creator; as a fiery throne thou bearest the Master of all creation, O Mother of God.*

*Kontakion of the Forefeast, Tone 6:*

*He Who is invisibly the Word with the Father is now seen in the flesh, having ineffably been born of the Virgin, and is given into the hands of the elder priest. Let us bow down before Him, our true God!*

*Ikos:*

*A sacred banquet is set forth; the assembly of feast-lovers meets today, bearing the forefeast signs: the forty-day-old Lord, the fearful childbearing from the Virgin, the embracing of the honorable elder, and the honorable memory of the sufferer, while singing forefeast praises unto our true God.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

*In the Persian furnace the Abrahamic youths, scorched rather with the love of piety than by the flame, cried out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*[^23]

*Troparion:*

*The Church, having beforehand adorned herself with spiritual beams, prepares in love to receive Him, the Master and Creator of all, and weaves joyful ascents unto Him:*

*Troparion:*

*The Word Who is uncircumscribedly in the bosom of the Father, Her only-begotten Son, maternal hands bearing Him in the flesh into the temple, bring Him in according to the Law as an infant.*

*Troparion:*

*God, Who was made manifest in the womb of the Virgin, sits today upon the arms of Symeon as upon a throne of fire.*

*Theotokion:*

*The nations saw thy glory, (Fol. 4v)*

*all ye nations, O only pure one; therefore all creation piously blesses thee as Queen, and ceaselessly glorifies thee who art the Mother of God beyond nature.*

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

*The youths having arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled down the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*[^24]

*Troparion:*

*Crown-bearer, thou rejoicest with the choirs of martyrs, rejoicing: for having conquered delusion by the word of truth, thou dost sing rejoicing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*

*The Master of all crowns the martyr through his sufferings, and settles him in heavenly habitations, crying and saying: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Theotokion:*

*Thou who, being young, didst bear as an infant the pre-eternal God incarnate of thee, O all-immaculate one: cease not to pray for the salvation of them that sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

*Stretching forth his hands, Daniel stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, having girded themselves with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*[^25]

*Troparion:*

*Stretching forth his hands, Symeon, having cleansed beforehand his soul with divine deeds, received the Lord Who had become an infant solely out of goodness, crying unto Him with gladness: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparion:*

*The Light of Christ's grace has shone forth upon us, being about to enter into His temple, transforming the obsolete figures as Renewed, unto them that truly cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparion:*

*Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, praising the pre-eternal God Who was well-pleased to become an infant for our sake, and to be received into the arms of the elder Symeon!*

*Theotokion:*

*Christ, Who through His abundant goodness became man from thee for our sake, O most pure one, offers today acceptable purifications unto the Father, He Who alone is most pure and immaculate, and Who preserves thee alone as the most pure and all-holy one.*

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

*The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly ones; but having seen them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Redeemer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!*[^26]

*Troparion:*

*Even unto beheading and burning, even unto the shedding of blood (Fol. 5r)*

*...thou didst suffer even unto a mortal end, resisting against sin, O martyr of Christ; but now, having received the life hidden in Christ, thou criest out: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*

*Thou didst contend upon earth, wrestling against the ungodly, against deceitful and God-opposing deceit, who bitterly compelled thee; but in the heavens, O Tryphon, thou dost delight, bearing a crown, and with the angels thou keepest festival, crying: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supreme exalt the Lord unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*

*Thou didst refute tyrannical godlessness, O all-wise one, and didst quench demonic God-opposing rebellion with the streams of thy blood, worthily and purely proclaiming the one God in the Trinity; and honorably thou didst cry out: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Theotokion:*

*Following thy words, O most pure one, we bless thee as the blessed one who truly didst bear in the flesh the Blessed One, the unsetting Light Who lives, the Guide of Light and Master the Giver of Light, Whom the children bless, the priests praise, and supreme exalt unto all ages!*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

*A cornerstone cut out without hands from an uncut mountain, which art thou, O Virgin: Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God.*[^27]

*Troparion:*

*Account me worthy, O Benefactor, to receive Thee with the hands of my soul, even as Symeon did of old, and to delight in Thy goodness; for Thou art the only Desire and the most longed-for Sweetness.*

*Troparion:*

*Having all spiritually kindled the lamps of the virtues, in love with Symeon and Anna, as candle-bearers let us anticipate Christ, Who is about to come to sanctify them that sing His praises.*

*Troparion:*

*Today in the temple the elder Symeon received in his hands Christ our God, and cried out, saying: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master!*[^28]

*Theotokion:*

*Surpassing all beings, the Word of the Most High received flesh from thee; being pure, He offers purification unto the unoriginate Father, and purifies them that in love magnify thee as the Mother of God.*

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

*Thee, the unwedded Mother of God the Most High, thee who beyond understanding didst bear by the Word the true God, who art higher than the most pure hosts, with unceasing doxologies do we magnify.*

*(Fol. 5v)*

*Troparion:*

*Surpassing visible things is the wonder of the martyrs, full of immortal delight; their hope is seen full of sweet fragrance, and of joy, and of ineffable radiant brightness; therefore worthily are they blessed.*

*Troparion:*

*Thou becamest a second light, drawing near unto the first Light, enlightened by His beam and conformed to His image, O wise Tryphon, and shining with His bountiful splendors; therefore we all bless thee.*

*Troparion:*

*Thou didst show thyself undaunted in thy struggles, O passion-bearer; thou didst show thyself truly namesake of divine and holy nourishment, of which now partaking and clearly delighting, ask salvation for us who honor thee.*

*Theotokion:*

*Put to death the carnal mind of my flesh, O Mother of God, and make alive the mortification of my soul by the operation of true Life, Who was born of thee in the flesh according to His ineffable loving-kindness, and unto the salvation of them that praise thee.*

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "In spirit in the sanctuary" (*Дх҃омъ во ст҃и́лище*):*

*Delighting in glorious and divine nourishment, thou becamest a fellow-dweller with the angels, O blessed one; while pasturing geese in the meadows, thou didst receive from God the grace of healings, O great-martyr Tryphon, to heal the infirmities and diseases of men.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast:*

*The all-immaculate Maiden, having brought the Child into the sanctuary to accomplish the things according to the Law, and as righteous Symeon received Him into his arms, he spoke directly unto the Virgin: Behold, this Child is set for the fall and rising again of many, and for a sign in the world.*[^29]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Forefeast, Tone 2. Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (*До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ*):*

*Without defilement, Christ is born of a Virgin even as incorruptibly from the Father, the Son before the morning star, delivering Adam.*

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.*[^30]

*Be opened, ye heavenly gates! For Christ is brought as an infant into the church by the Virgin Mother, unto God and the Father.*

*Verse: A light to lighten the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*

*Receive, O Symeon, the Lord of glory, even as it was made known unto thee by the Holy Spirit: for behold, He is come!*

*Glory... Both now... in the same Tone. Idiomelon:*

*The Creator of heaven and earth was borne in the arms of the holy (Fol. 6r)*

*...elder Symeon today: for he spoke by the Holy Spirit: Now am I set free, for I have seen my Savior.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the Canons: of the Forefeast, Ode 3; and of the Saint, Ode 6.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*

*The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.*[^31]

*Verse: Hear my voice, O God, when I pray unto Thee.*

*Epistle to the Romans, Pericope 94 [Rom. 8:28–39].*

*Alleluia, Tone 4:*

*Verse: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, and shall be multiplied like a cedar in Lebanon.*[^32]

*Gospel of Luke, Pericope 51 from the half [Luke 10:19–21].*

*Communion Verse: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings.*[^33]

*If the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord fall on a Saturday: Note well.*[^34]

*On Friday evening, after the stichera: Glory of the Saint; Both now, the first Theotokion of the current Tone.*

*If the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord occur on a Sunday before the Publican and the Pharisee:*

*On Saturday at Small Vespers: Stichera of the Resurrection, and to the Theotokos according to custom; Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... Both now... its Theotokion.*

*At Great Vespers: on "Lord, I have cried", Stichera of the Resurrection 3, and of Anatolius 1; and of the Forefeast 3; and of the Martyr 3. Glory of the Saint; Both now, Theotokion of the current Tone. At the Litiya: Sticheron of the temple, then the evening Aposticha stichera of the Forefeast. Glory of the Forefeast, which is written at "Lord, I have cried"; Both now, which is written at Matins at the Aposticha.*

*At the Aposticha: Stichera of the Resurrection; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast.*

*At the Blessing of Loaves: Troparion, "O Theotokos Virgin" twice, and of the Forefeast once; and a reading.*

*At Matins: on "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Resurrection twice; Glory of the Saint; Both now of the Forefeast. After the Kathismata: Sessional hymns of the Resurrection, and their Theotokia; and the reading in the Gospel commentary. Thereafter we sing the Blameless [Psalm 118] and the Hypakoe, and a reading. The Gradual Hymns of the Tone. Then the Prokeimenon, and "Let every breath"; and the Gospel of the Resurrection. Then "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ", and Psalm 50, and the Sticheron of the Resurrection. Canon of the Resurrection with its heirmos on 4, and to the Theotokos on 2; and of the Forefeast on 4; and of the Martyr on 4. Katavasia: "The sun once shone upon dry land".*[^35]

*After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the Forefeast, and Sessional hymn of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Resurrection. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable". Exapostilarion of the Resurrection; Glory of the Martyr; Both now of the Forefeast. At the Praises: Stichera of the Resurrection 4, and of Anatolius 1, and of the Forefeast 3.*

*(Fol. 6v)*

*...of the Aposticha, with their refrains. Glory, the Gospel Sticheron; Both now, "Most blessed art thou". Great Doxology, Troparion of the Resurrection, and the litanies, and the dismissal, and the catechesis; and the customary procession into the narthex. And the First Hour:*

*At the First Hour: Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory of the Forefeast; Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father", Kontakion of the Forefeast, and the final dismissal. At the Third Hour: Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory of the Martyr; Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father", Kontakion of the Resurrection. Likewise also at the other Hours, we say first the Troparion of the Resurrection; and alternating between the Forefeast and the Martyr we say the troparia. But the kontakia we say of the Resurrection and the Forefeast, alternating.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Octoechos on 4; and of the Forefeast, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Saint, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Resurrection and of the Forefeast; then of the temple of the Saint, and of the Martyr. Kontakion of the Resurrection, and of the temple of the Saint; Glory of the Martyr; Both now of the Forefeast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel: first of the Sunday, then of the Saint.*

*It is meet to know: If the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord fall on the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee, or of the Prodigal Son, or of Meatfare:*[^36]

*On Saturday at Small Vespers: Stichera of the Resurrection and to the Theotokos according to custom.*

*At Great Vespers: Stichera of the Resurrection 3, and of Anatolius 1, and of the Triodion 3, and of the Forefeast 3. But if it be Meatfare Sunday: Stichera of the Resurrection 3, and of the Triodion 4, and of the Forefeast 3; Glory of the Triodion; Both now, Theotokion of the First Tone.*

*At the Litiya: Sticheron of the temple; of the Forefeast, the evening Aposticha; Glory of the Triodion; Both now of the Forefeast, the morning Aposticha.*

*At the Aposticha: Stichera of the Resurrection; Glory of the Triodion; Both now of the Forefeast.*

*At the Blessing of Loaves: Troparion, "O Theotokos Virgin" twice, and of the Forefeast once; and "Blessed be the name of the Lord"; and a reading. Note well.*[^37]

*If there be no All-Night Vigil: after "Now lettest Thou depart", Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. At Compline: after "It is truly meet" and after "Our Father", Kontakion of the Forefeast; Glory... Both now... Kontakion of the Triodion.*

*At Matins: at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Resurrection twice; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast; and the rest up to the canon is of the Resurrection. Canon of the Resurrection with its heirmos on 4, and to the Theotokos on 2; and of the Forefeast on 4; and of the Triodion on 4. Katavasia of the Feast. Note well.*[^38]

*If it be Meatfare Sunday: Canon of the Resurrection on 4, and of the Forefeast on 4, and of the Triodion on 6. Katavasia (Fol. 7r)*

*...of the Triodion. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the Forefeast, and Sessional hymn of the Triodion twice; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Triodion. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable". Exapostilarion of the Resurrection; Glory of the Triodion; Both now of the Forefeast. At the Praises: Stichera of the Resurrection 5, and of the Triodion 3, with their refrains.*

*If it be Meatfare Sunday:*

*And we sing Stichera of the Resurrection 4, and of the Triodion with their refrains 4; Glory of the Triodion; Both now, "Most blessed art thou". Great Doxology, Troparion of the Resurrection, and litanies, and dismissal. Then Glory... Both now..., the Gospel Sticheron; and the First Hour in the narthex. At the Hours: Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory of the Forefeast; Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Forefeast and of the Triodion, alternating. At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone on 4; and of the Triodion, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Forefeast, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance: Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the Forefeast; Glory, Kontakion of the Triodion; Both now of the Forefeast. Prokeimenon of the Tone; Epistle, Gospel, and Communion verse of the day.*

*The service of Saint Tryphon is sung whenever the superior directs. Note well.*[^39]

*If the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord fall on Meatfare Saturday:*

*Then we sing the service of the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord beforehand on the Friday of that week, together with Saints Cyrus and John, and Tryphon together. At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera of the Forefeast 3, and of the Unmercenaries 3; Glory of the Unmercenaries; Both now of the Forefeast. At the Aposticha: Stichera of the Forefeast; Glory of the Unmercenaries; Both now of the Forefeast. At Matins: after the Kathismata, Sessional hymns of the Forefeast. Canon of the Forefeast with the heirmos on 6, and the two canons of the Saints on 8. The Stichera of Tryphon at the Praises we sing on 4; Glory unto him, written at "Lord, I have cried"; Both now of the Forefeast. At the Aposticha, of the Forefeast; and the rest according to custom. But the service of the reposed we sing in its order according to custom on Meatfare Saturday, even as always. Note well.*[^40]

*If the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord fall on Monday, or on Tuesday, or on Thursday of Cheesefare Week:*

*At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera of the Forefeast 3, and of the Saint 3; Glory of the Saint; Both now of the Forefeast. At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the Triodion twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. After the Trisagion: Troparion of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast (Fol. 7v)*

*...of the Forefeast. At Compline: after "It is truly meet", Kontakion of the Forefeast. At Matins: at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Forefeast twice; Glory of the Saint; Both now of the Forefeast. After the First Kathisma: litany and Sessional hymn of the Forefeast. After the Second Kathisma: Sessional hymn of the Triodion without a litany. Canon of the Forefeast with the heirmos on 8, and of the Saint on 4. But where the Triodion triode arrives, we sing the canon of the Forefeast with the heirmos on 4, and of the Saint from the verse on 2, and of the Triodion on 8. Katavasia of the Triodion. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos and Sessional hymn of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Forefeast. After Ode 9: Exapostilarion of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the Triodion twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. "It is a good thing to give thanks unto the Lord", and after the Trisagion, Troparion of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. At the Hours: Troparion of the Forefeast; Glory of the Saint; Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion we say the Kontakion of the Forefeast, without changing. At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the canon of the Forefeast, Ode 3, and of the Saint, Ode 6. After the Entrance: Troparion of the Forefeast, and of the temple of the Saint, and of the Saint; then Kontakion of the temple of the Saint; Glory of the Saint; Both now of the Forefeast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion verse of the day, and of the Saint.*

*It is meet to know: If the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord fall on Wednesday or on Friday of Cheesefare Week:*[^41]

*On Tuesday evening at "Lord, I have cried": Stichera of the Forefeast 3, and of the Saint 3; Glory of the Saint; Both now of the Forefeast. At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the Triodion twice, and the Martyrikon, with the customary refrains; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. After the Trisagion: Troparion of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. Litany, and 3 great prostrations, and the dismissal. And we sing Great Compline, "Hear me when I call": upon which we sing the first morning triode of the Triodion. After "It is truly meet" and after the Trisagion: Kontakion of the Forefeast, and 3 great prostrations; and the prayer, and the customary dismissal. At Matins: at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Forefeast twice; Glory of the Saint; Both now of the Forefeast. After the First Kathisma: Sessional hymn of the Forefeast. After the Second Kathisma: Sessional hymn of the Triodion twice; Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion, without a litany. Canon of the Forefeast with the heirmos on 6, and of the Saint on 4, and of the Triodion on 4. But when the triode arrives, we sing the canon of the Forefeast with the heirmos (Fol. 8r)*

*...with the heirmos on 4, and of the Saint, 3 verses on 2, and of the Triodion on 8: of the current canon the ode in order on 4, and of the second triode on 4, the first triode having been sung beforehand at Compline. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the Saint, and his Sessional hymn; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. After Ode 6: Kontakion of the Forefeast and Ikos. After Ode 9: Exapostilarion of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the Triodion twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast; litany, and 3 great prostrations, without dismissal, and the First Hour. At the First Hour: Troparion of the Forefeast; Glory of the Saint; Both now, Theotokion of the Hours; and the rest all according to custom; and at each Hour we make 3 great prostrations. At the Sixth Hour: customary troparion, and the reading of the day. But Vespers of the Feast takes place in its own due time.*

*Thus do we sing the service of the Forefeast if it also fall on a Friday, evening and morning without change. At Compline: the first morning triode, of Tone 6.*

*If the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord fall on Cheesefare Saturday: Note well.*[^42]

*In the evening: the Kathisma in order. At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera on 6: of the Forefeast 3, and of the Holy Fathers from the Triodion 3; Glory of the Fathers; Both now, the first Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and reading of the Triodion; and the Prokeimenon: "Let Israel hope in the Lord."*[^43] *At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the Triodion twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory of the Fathers; Both now of the Forefeast. After "Now lettest Thou depart" and after the Trisagion: Troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. Litany and dismissal. At Compline: after "It is truly meet" and after the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast.*

*At Matins: at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Forefeast twice; Glory of the Fathers; Both now of the Forefeast. After the Kathismata: both Sessional hymns of the Fathers; Both now of the Forefeast. Canon of the Forefeast with the heirmos on 6, the heirmos twice; and of the Fathers on 8. Katavasia: the heirmos of the Triodion; of the second ode we sing the heirmos of the Triodion once, and afterwards we cover with the same heirmos. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the Forefeast, and Sessional hymn of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Fathers. Exapostilarion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. And at the Praises: Stichera of the Fathers on 4; Glory of the Fathers; Both now of the Forefeast. Great Doxology, and the rest of Matins. At the Hours: Troparion of the Forefeast; Glory of the Fathers; Both now, Theotokion (Fol. 8v. Woodcut headpiece: Scrolling foliate and floral cartouche)*

*...Theotokion of the Hours. But the kontakia of the Forefeast and of the Fathers we say alternating. At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Forefeast, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Fathers, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance: Troparion of the Forefeast, and of the Fathers; Glory, Kontakion of the Fathers; Both now of the Forefeast. And all the rest of the service of the day and of the Fathers. But the service of the Martyr Tryphon is sung at Compline, or whenever the superior directs. Note well.*[^44]

*On the Second Day of the Same Month: The Meeting of our Lord, God, and Savior Jesus Christ:*

*At Small Vespers, Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "Of the heavenly hosts" (*Нб҃ныхъ чинѡ́въ*):*

*The uncircumscribed and super-essential Word, Who is borne with glory upon heavenly thrones, Symeon receives into his arms, crying out: Now lettest Thou me depart according to Thy word, O Savior, the salvation and delight of the faithful! (Twice)*

*Seeing Thee as an infant, marvelous Symeon cried out unto the pre-eternal Word born of the Father: With fear and dread do I embrace Thee with my hands, O Master; but send forth Thy servant in peace, now lettest Thou depart, as Thou art merciful!*

*Now let the heavenly door be opened: for He Who was born of the Father without seed, God the Word, was born of the Virgin, having taken human flesh of His own will, to renew nature as Good, and to seat it at the right hand of the Father.*

*Glory... Both now... Tone 1. Idiomelon: By Andrew of Crete:*[^45]

*Today the holy Mother, who became the sanctuary, came into the sanctuary, revealing unto the world the Lawgiver and Creator of the Law. Receiving Him into his arms, the elder Symeon rejoicing cried aloud: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, for I have seen Thee, the Savior of our souls!*[^46]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (*До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ*):*

*Today the Savior is brought as an infant into the church of the Lord; and with aged hands the elder takes Him up.*

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.*[^47]

*(Fol. 9r)*

*...peace, according to Thy word.*

*Troparion:*

*The Coal revealed unto divine Isaiah—even Christ—as with tongs, by the hands of the Mother of God, is now given unto the elder.*[^48]

*Verse: A light to lighten the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*[^49]

*Troparion:*

*Holding the Master in his arms with fear and joy, Symeon asked release from life, praising the Mother of God.*

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6. Podoben the same (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*

*Receive into thine embrace as an infant, O Symeon, cried out the all-immaculate one, the Lord of glory, and the salvation of the world!*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*

*Rejoice, O Virgin Mother of God, full of grace, for from thee hath shone forth the Sun of righteousness, Christ our God, enlightening them that are in darkness; be glad also, thou righteous elder, who receivedst in thine embrace the Liberator of our souls, Who grants unto us the Resurrection!*

*Then the Little Litany, and Dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers, "Blessed is the man", the 1st Antiphon.*

*At "Lord, I have cried", we sing 8 Stichera, Tone 1, Idiomela. By Germanus the Patriarch:*[^50]

*Speak, O Symeon, carrying Whom in thine arms into the church dost thou rejoice? To Whom dost thou cry and say: "Now am I set free, for I have seen my Savior"? This is He Who was born of the Virgin! This is God the Word of God, Who became incarnate for our sake, and saved man: let us bow down before Him! (Thrice)*

*Receive, O Symeon, Him Whom Moses in the darkness foresaw legislating on Sinai, Who has become an infant, submitting to the Law! This is He Who spoke through the Law! This is He Who was spoken of among the Prophets, Who became incarnate for our sake, and saved man: let us bow down before Him! (Thrice)*

*Let us also come, and with divine songs let us meet Christ, and let us receive Him Whose salvation Symeon saw! This is He Whom David proclaimed beforehand; this is He Who spoke among the Prophets, Who became incarnate for our sake, and spoke through the Law: let us bow down before Him!*

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6. By John the Monk:*[^51]

*Let the heavenly door be opened today: for the unoriginate Word of the Father, having taken a beginning in time, not having departed (Fol. 9v)*

*...from His Divinity: as an infant of forty days, by His mother He is willingly brought according to the Law into the church; and the elder takes Him into his arms, crying: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master, for mine eyes have seen Thy salvation, O Thou Who didst come into the world to save the human race; O Lord, glory to Thee!*[^52]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and 3 Readings.*

*Reading from Exodus [Chapter 12, composite Paroemia]:*[^53]

*The Lord spoke unto Moses on that day wherein He brought the sons of Israel out of the land of Egypt, saying: Sanctify unto Me every firstborn that opens the womb among the sons of Israel.*

*And Moses went and gathered all the people, and said: Remember this day, wherein ye came out from the land of Egypt, out of the house of bondage: for with a strong hand the Lord brought us out thence; and keep ye His law. And it shall come to pass, when the Lord God shall bring you into the land of the Canaanites, in the manner as He swore unto thy fathers, that thou shalt set apart every firstborn that opens the womb, males, unto the Lord.*

*And it shall come to pass, when thy son shall ask thee hereafter, saying: What is this? that thou shalt say unto him: With a strong hand the Lord brought us out from the land of Egypt, out of the house of bondage. For when Pharaoh hardened his heart not to let us go, the Lord slew all the firstborn in the land of Egypt, from the firstborn of men even unto the firstborn of beasts. Therefore I sacrifice unto the Lord every male that opens the womb, and every firstborn of my sons do I redeem. And it shall be for an immovable sign before thine eyes.*

*For thus said the Lord God Almighty: The firstborn of thy sons shalt thou give unto Me. And every woman that bears a man-child, on the eighth day ye shall circumcise the flesh of his foreskin. And thirty-three days she shall not enter into the sanctuary of God unto the priest, until the days of her purification are fulfilled. And after these she shall bring unto the Lord a lamb of one year, blameless, for a whole burnt offering, and a young pigeon or a turtledove, at the doors of the tabernacle of witness, unto the priest. Or instead of these she shall bring before the Lord two young pigeons or two turtledoves, and the priest shall make atonement for her.*

*For as a recompense these are given unto Me from all the sons of Israel, and I have taken them; and I have sanctified them unto Myself instead of the firstborn of the Egyptians, in the day wherein I smote every (Fol. 10r)*

*...every firstborn in the land of Egypt, from man unto beast: said the Most High God, the Holy One of Israel.*[^54]

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah [Chapter 6]:*[^55]

*It came to pass in the year wherein King Uzziah died, that I saw the Lord sitting upon a throne, high and lifted up; and the house was full of His glory. And Seraphim stood round about Him: six wings had the one, and six wings had the other; and with twain they covered their faces, and with twain they covered their feet, and with twain they did fly. And one cried unto another, and said: Holy, holy, holy is the Lord of Sabaoth; the whole earth is full of His glory! And the lintel was lifted up at the voice whereof they cried, and the house was filled with smoke. And I said: Woe is me, for I am pierced to the heart! for I am a man, and have unclean lips, and I dwell in the midst of a people having unclean lips; and mine eyes have seen the King, the Lord of Sabaoth! And there was sent unto me one of the Seraphim, and he had in his hand a live coal, which he had taken with tongs from the altar. And he touched my lips, and said: Lo, this has touched thy lips, and shall take away thine iniquities, and shall purge thy sins. And I heard the voice of the Lord, saying: Whom shall I send, and who will go unto this people? And I said: Lo, here am I; send me. And He said: Go, and say unto this people: Hearing ye shall hear, and shall not understand; and seeing ye shall see, and shall not perceive. For the heart of this people is become gross, and with their ears they have heard heavily, and their eyes have they closed; lest at any time they should see with their eyes, and hear with their ears, and understand with their heart, and should turn, and I should heal them. And I said: Until when, O Lord? And He said: Until cities be left desolate, so that none dwell in them, and houses, so that there be no men, and the land be left desolate. And after these things God shall remove men far off, and they that are left upon the earth shall multiply.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah [Chapter 19]:*[^56]

*Behold, the Lord sits upon a swift cloud, and shall come into Egypt, and the idols of Egypt shall be shaken at His presence, and their heart shall melt within them. And their spirit shall be troubled within them, and I will scatter their counsel.* [Archival paper patch restoration:] *And I will deliver Egypt into your hand and under masters; for thus says the Lord, the Ruler, the Holy One of Sabaoth. And the Egyptians shall drink water that is by the sea; and the river shall fail and be dried up. Thus says the Lord: Where are now thy wise men (Fol. 10v)*

*...that they may announce unto thee, and may tell thee what the Lord of Sabaoth has purposed against Egypt? In that day the Egyptians shall be in fear and in trembling, from before the hand of the Lord of Sabaoth, which He shall lay upon them. And there shall be an altar unto the Lord in the land of Egypt, and a pillar at its border unto the Lord. And it shall be for a sign forever unto the Lord of Sabaoth in the land of Egypt; for they shall cry unto the Lord, and the Lord shall send them a man who shall save them. And the Lord shall be known unto the Egyptians, and the Egyptians shall know the Lord in that day, and shall offer sacrifices and gifts, and make vows, and pay their vows unto the Lord.*

*At the Litiya, Stichera Idiomela, Tone 1. By Anatolius:*[^57]

*He Who of old gave the Law unto Moses upon Sinai, today is seen as an infant, and according to the Law, as the Maker of the Law fulfilling the Law, is brought into the temple, and given unto the elder. And having received Him, righteous Symeon, beholding the fulfillment of the promises accomplished, rejoicing cried aloud: Mine eyes have seen the mystery hidden from the ages, revealed in these last days: the Light that dispels the darkness of the unbelieving Gentiles, and gives glory unto newly-chosen Israel! Therefore release Thy servant from the bonds of this flesh unto the ageless and marvelous life that has no end, granting great mercy unto the world!*

*By John the Monk:*[^58]

*He Who of old gave the Law unto Moses upon Sinai submits to the ordinances of the Law, having become an infant like us for our sake. Now the pure God, as a holy child, having opened a pure womb, is offered unto Himself as God, freeing us from the curse of the Law, and enlightening our souls.*

*Tone 2. By Andrew Pyrros:*[^59]

*Him Whom the supreme ministers worship with trembling, below now Symeon receives with material hands, proclaiming God united with men; and seeing the heavenly God as a man, departing from things on earth, he rejoicing cried aloud: O Thou Who revealest the never-setting Light unto them that sit in darkness, Lord, glory to Thee!*

*By Germanus:*[^60]

*Today Symeon receives into his arms the Lord of glory, Whom Moses of old saw under the cloud upon Mount Sinai giving him the tablets. This is He Who spoke among the Prophets, and the Maker of the Law. This is He Whom (Fol. 11r)*

*...David proclaims: Who is terrible unto all, having great and rich mercy.*

*Two sacred gifts of the consecrated one did she bring unto the sanctuary for the priest; and stretching forth his hands, Symeon received Him rejoicing, and cried out: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word, O Lord!*

*The Creator of heaven and earth was borne today in the arms of the holy elder Symeon; for he spoke by the Holy Spirit: Now am I set free, for I have seen my Savior!*

*By Anatolius:*

*Today the elder Symeon enters into the temple rejoicing in spirit, to receive into his arms the Lawgiver of Moses and the Fulfiller of the Law. He indeed in darkness and with an obscure voice was deemed worthy to be a beholder of God, and with covered face rebuked the unbelieving hearts of the Hebrews; but this one bore the pre-eternal Word of the Father incarnate, and revealed the Light, the Cross, and the Resurrection unto the nations; and Anna the prophetess was revealed, proclaiming the Savior and Deliverer unto Israel. Unto Him let us cry aloud: O Christ our God, for our sake have mercy on us!*

*Glory... Tone 5. By Andrew of Crete:*[^61]

*Search the Scriptures, even as Christ our God said in the Gospels: for in them we find Him born, and wrapped in swaddling clothes, laid in a manger, and nourished with milk, receiving circumcision, and borne by Symeon, not in semblance nor in apparition, but in truth appeared unto the world! Unto Him let us cry aloud: O pre-eternal God, glory to Thee!*

*Both now... The same tone. By Germanus:*

*Having become an infant in the flesh, He Who is the Ancient of Days is brought into the church by the Virgin Mother, fulfilling the promise of His own Law. Receiving Whom, Symeon said: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace according to Thy word; for mine eyes have seen Thy salvation, O Holy One!*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 7. By Cosmas the Monk:*[^62]

*Adorn thy bridal chamber, O Sion, and receive Christ the King! Kiss Mary the heavenly gate: for she has appeared as the Cherubic throne, she bears the King of glory; the Virgin is a cloud of light, bearing in her arms the Son before the morning star; Whom receiving into his arms, Symeon proclaimed unto the peoples that He is the Master of life and death, and the Savior of the world.*

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word (Fol. 11v)*

*...Thy servant, O Master, according to Thy word, in peace.*[^63]

*Carrying Him into the church, the unwedded Mother brought Him Who shone forth from the Father before the ages, and in the last times from a virginal womb; Who gave the Law on Mount Sinai, submitting to the ordinance of the Law, unto the elder priest and righteous one, who saw the promised Christ God; Whom receiving into his arms, Symeon rejoiced, crying aloud: This is God, co-eternal, and the Deliverer of our souls!*

*Verse: A light to lighten the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*[^64]

*Borne upon the Cherubic chariot, and praised in the hymns of the Seraphim, Mary the Mother of God, holding in her arms Him Who was incarnate of her without wedlock, the Lawgiver fulfilling the order of the Law, gave Him into the hands of the elder priest; and bearing Life, he asked for release of life, saying: Master, now release me to proclaim unto Adam that I have seen the unchangeable Child, the pre-eternal God, and the Savior of the world!*

*Glory... Both now... Tone 8. By John the Monk:*[^65]

*He Who is borne upon the Cherubim, and praised by the Seraphim, today being brought into the divine sanctuary according to the Law, sits upon aged arms as upon a throne; and from Joseph He receives gifts in God-befitting manner: as a pair of turtledoves, the spotless Church and the newly-chosen people from the Gentiles; and two young pigeons, as the Author of both the Old and the New. Receiving the fulfillment of the promise made unto him, Symeon, blessing Mary the Virgin Mother of God, foretold the emblems of the passions of Him that came from her; and asking release from Him, he cried aloud: Now lettest Thou me depart, O Master, as Thou didst announce unto me beforehand; for I have seen Thee, the pre-eternal Light, and the Savior Lord of the people named after Christ!*

*Then, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." Trisagion, and after "Our Father...", at the Blessing of the Loaves, the Troparion of the feast, thrice: written at Small Vespers. And reading. But if there be no Vigil: after "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart...", the Troparion of the feast, and Dismissal.*[^66]

*At Compline, after "It is truly meet", the Kontakion of the feast. Midnight Office with censer. After the 1st Trisagion, instead of "Behold the Bridegroom", the Troparion of the feast. After the 2nd Trisagion, instead of "Remember, O Lord, for Thou art good", we say the Kontakion of the feast. Then "Lord, have mercy" 12 times, and Dismissal. But the prayer "Remember, O Lord, our Benefactor" we do not say.*[^67]

*At Matins (Fol. 12r)*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", the Troparion thrice.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Automelon:*

*Let the angelic choir marvel at the wonder, and let us mortals raise our voices in song, beholding the ineffable condescension of God: for He before Whom the heavenly hosts tremble is now embraced by the aged hands of the only Lover of mankind!*

*Glory... Both now... The same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben: "The angelic choir" (*Ли́къ а҆́гглскїй*):*

*Thou Who sittest with the Father upon the holy throne, having come to earth wast born of the Virgin, and as an infant didst become uncircumscribed in years; Whom Symeon receiving into his arms, rejoicing said: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Merciful One, having filled him with joy!*

*Glory... Both now... The same.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (*Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ*):*

*Thou dost become an infant for my sake, O Ancient of Days; Thou dost partake of purification, O most pure God, that Thou mayest confirm unto me the flesh taken from the Virgin! And hereby Symeon was instructed, knowing Thee to be God manifested in the flesh; and as Life he embraced Thee, and rejoicing cried aloud in an aged voice: Release me, for I have seen Thee, the Life of all!*

*Glory... Both now... The same.*

*Then the Gradual Antiphons, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: I will remember Thy name in every generation and generation. Verse: My heart has poured forth a good word.*[^68]

*Let every breath:*

*Gospel according to Luke, Section 50 [Luke 2:25–32].*[^69]

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*

*Let the heavenly door be opened today: for the unoriginate Word of the Father, having taken a beginning in time, not having departed from His Divinity, as an infant of forty days, by His mother is willingly brought into the church according to the Law; and the elder takes Him into his arms, crying: Release Thy servant, O Master, for mine eyes have seen Thy salvation, O Thou Who didst come into the world to save the human race; O Lord, glory to Thee!*[^70]

*The Canon, whose acrostic is: "Joyfully the elder embraces Christ". The composition of the Lord Cosmas, Tone 3. The Heirmos twice, troparia to 12:*[^71]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*The sun once shone upon dry land produced from the deep: for the water became solid as a wall on both sides for the people passing on foot through the sea, and singing in a God-pleasing manner: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!*[^72]

*(Fol. 12v)*

*Let the clouds drop down water: for Christ, the Sun borne upon a swift cloud, has come upon an undefiled arm as an infant into the church; therefore let us faithful cry aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!*

*Be strengthened, O hands of Symeon, weakened by old age; and the weary aged legs, move swiftly to meet Christ, forming a choir with the bodiless hosts: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!*

*O heavens stretched out in wisdom, be glad, and rejoice, O earth: for Christ the Craftsman, coming forth from the super-divine bosom, is offered by the Virgin Mother unto God the Father, an infant Who was before the ages: for He is gloriously glorified!*

*Katavasia: The same Heirmos.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

*O Lord, the Confirmation of them that put their trust in Thee, confirm the Church which Thou hast purchased with Thy precious blood!*[^73]

*Firstborn from the Father before the ages, firstborn an infant from the incorrupt Virgin, He has appeared, stretching forth His hand unto Adam.*

*Setting right again the first-formed man who was overcome by deceit, God the Word has appeared, having become an infant.*

*Earth's offspring, having again slipped into corruption, the Creator, as unchangeable, has restored, having become an infant conforming our nature to His Divinity.*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (*Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ*):*

*On Mount Sinai of old Moses saw the back parts of God, and was deemed worthy to hear a still, divine voice in the darkness and tempest; but now Symeon has received in his arms God unchangeably incarnate for our sake, and rejoicing hastened to depart from things here unto life eternal; therefore he cried aloud: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master!*[^74]

*Glory... Both now... The same.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

*Thy virtue has covered the heavens, O Christ: for coming forth from the ark of Thy sanctification, Thine incorrupt Mother, Thou hast appeared in the temple of Thy glory as an infant borne in arms; and all things are filled with Thy praise!*[^75]

*Rejoicing, O Symeon, initiate of ineffable mysteries, the Mother of God cried aloud: Receive into thine arms Christ the Word, Who has become an infant, of Whom the Holy Spirit assured thee of old, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!*

*Upon Whom thou hast set thy hope, O Symeon, cried she who bore the Child, rejoicing take up Christ (Fol. 13r)*

*...the divine Consolation of Israel, the Maker and Master of the Law, fulfilling the order of the Law, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!*

*Seeing the unoriginate Word borne in the flesh by the Virgin as upon a Cherubic throne, the Cause of the being of all things, Symeon, marveling that He is an infant, cried unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

*When Isaiah typically saw God upon a throne lifted high, escorted by angels of glory, Woe is me! he cried aloud: for I have seen beforehand God incarnate, the never-setting Light, Who rules over peace!*[^76]

*Understanding the glory revealed of old unto the prophet, the divine elder, seeing the Word held by motherly hands, rejoicing cried aloud, O pure one: For as a throne thou holdest God, the never-setting Light, Who rules over peace!*

*The elder bowed down, and reverently touching the steps of the unwedded Mother of God, said: O pure one, thou bearest fire; I fear to embrace the infant God, the never-setting Light, Who rules over peace!*

*Isaiah was cleansed when he received the coal from the Seraph; the elder cried aloud unto the Mother of God: Thou enlightenest me with thy hands as with tongs, giving me Him Whom thou bearest, the never-setting Light, Who rules over peace!*[^77]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

*Beholding with his eyes the salvation that comes from God unto the people, the elder cried aloud unto Thee: O Christ, Thou art my God!*[^78]

*Thou hast been laid as a stone for Sion, a stumbling-block and a rock of offense unto the disobedient, but an indestructible salvation unto the faithful.*[^79]

*Plainly bearing the impress of Him Who brought Thee forth before the ages, through tender mercy Thou hast now clothed Thyself with the weakness of mortals.*[^80]

*Unto the Son of the Most High, the Son of the Virgin, God become a child, he bowed down: Now lettest Thou me depart in peace!*

*Kontakion, Tone 1:*

*Thou Who by Thy birth didst sanctify the virginal womb, and didst bless the hands of Symeon as was meet, having anticipated, hast now saved us, O Christ God; but grant peace to our commonwealth in warfare, and strengthen the people whom Thou hast loved, O only Lover of mankind!*[^81]

*Ikos:*

*Let us run to the Mother of God, wishing to see her Son borne unto Symeon; Whom the bodiless hosts, beholding from heaven, marveled at, saying: We see most marvelous things now (Fol. 13v)*

*...and most glorious, incomprehensible, and ineffable: for He Who fashioned Adam is borne as an infant, the Incomprehensible is contained in the arms of the elder, and He Who is in the uncircumscribed bosom of His Father is voluntarily circumscribed in the flesh, and not in His Divinity, the only Lover of mankind!*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

*Thee, God the Word, Who didst dew the pious youths in the fire and didst take up Thine abode in the incorrupt Virgin, we piously hymn and sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!*[^82]

*Wishing to announce unto Adam living in Hades, and to bring glad tidings unto Eve, Symeon cried aloud, rejoicing with the prophets: Blessed is the God of our fathers!*

*God, Who delivers the earthly race, shall come even unto Hades; and He shall grant remission unto all captives, and sight unto the blind, so that even the mute cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!*

*A sword shall pierce thine own heart, O incorrupt one, Symeon foretold unto the Mother of God, beholding thy Son upon the Cross, unto Whom we cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!*[^83]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

*Standing together for piety in the unbearable fire, yet unharmed by the flame, the youths sang a divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all forever!*[^84]

*O people of Israel, beholding your Glory, Emmanuel, a Child from the Virgin: dance now before the face of the divine ark: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all forever!*

*Lo, Symeon cried aloud, this Child, being God, shall be a sign that is spoken against: unto Him let us faithful cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all forever!*[^85]

*Being Life, God the Word, having become an infant, shall be the fall of the disobedient, but the rising of all that sing with faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all forever!*

*Refrains at the Ninth Ode:*[^86]

*O Virgin Mother of God, hope of Christians, protect, preserve, and save them that put their trust in thee.*

*O Virgin Mother of God, gracious helper of the world, protect and preserve us from every necessity and sorrow.*

*O God-bearing Symeon, come and take up Christ, Whom the pure Virgin Mary brought forth.*

*(Fol. 14r)*

*The elder Symeon embraces in his arms the Maker of the Law and the Master of all.*

*It is not the elder that holds Me, but I hold him: for he asks of Me release.*

*O mystical tongs, how bearest thou the Coal? How dost thou nourish Him Who nourishes all?*

*O daughter of Phanuel, come stand with us, and give thanks unto Christ the Savior, the Son of God!*

*Chaste Anna proclaims awesome things, confessing Christ, the Creator of heaven and earth.*

*Incomprehensible unto angels and men is that which has been wrought concerning thee, O pure Virgin Mother!*

*The pure dove, the undefiled ewe-lamb, brings the Lamb and Shepherd into the church.*

*O Christ, King of all, grant victories over enemies unto Thy faithful servant, our King!*[^87]

*O Christ, King of all, give me warm tears, that I may weep for my soul which I have grievously ruined.*

*Instead of "Glory...":*

*Let us piously praise the thrice-radiant and tri-hypostatic Godhead!*

*Instead of "Both now...":*

*O Virgin Mary, enlighten my soul, which is sorely darkened by the pleasures of this life.*

*Be it known that we sing these refrains at the beginning to the Heirmos. Then to each troparion we sing 4 refrains. And after the completion of the 14 refrains with the troparia, at the Katavasia we sing the sessional hymns together, with the 1st Refrain: "O Virgin Mother of God, hope of Christians...".*[^88]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

*In the shadow and letter of the Law, we faithful see a type: every male child that opens the womb is holy unto God; therefore the firstborn Word of the unoriginate Father, the firstborn Son of a Mother unwedded, we magnify!*[^89]

*In place of the pair of newborn turtledoves and two young pigeons prescribed of old, the divine elder and chaste Anna the prophetess minister, magnifying Him Who is born of the Virgin, the only-begotten Son of the Father, as He is brought into the church.*

*Thou hast rendered unto me the joy of Thy salvation, O Christ, cried Symeon; receive Thy minister, weary with the shadow, the sacred and mystical herald of the new grace, magnifying Thee in praise!*[^90]

*In a holy manner chaste and venerable Anna, an aged prophetess, made confession unto the Mother of God, openly preaching Christ in the church (Fol. 14v)*

*...and openly preaching Christ in the church unto all that were present, she magnified Him.*

*Exapostilarion. Automelon:*

*Standing in spirit in the sanctuary, the elder received into his arms the Master of the Law, crying aloud: Now release me from the bonds of the flesh in peace, even as Thou hast said; for mine eyes have seen the revelation of the Gentiles, and the salvation of Israel! (Thrice)*[^91]

*At the Praises, 4 Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (*Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе*):*

*Fulfilling the Law written in the Scriptures, the Lover of mankind is now brought into the church; and the elder Symeon receives Him with aged hands, crying aloud: Now lettest Thou me depart unto yonder blessedness; for I have seen Thee today clothed in mortal flesh, yet ruling over life and having dominion over death! (Twice)*

*Thou hast appeared, O Lord, as a light for the revelation of the Gentiles, sitting upon a swift cloud, O Sun of righteousness, fulfilling the shadow of the Law and declaring the beginning of the new grace; therefore, beholding Thee, Symeon cried aloud: Release me from corruption, for I have seen Thee today!*

*Not departing from the bosom of the Father in Thy Divinity, having become incarnate as Thou didst desire, held in the embrace of the Ever-Virgin, Thou wast delivered into the hands of Symeon the God-Receiver, Thou Who holdest all things in Thy hand; therefore, Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, he rejoicing cried aloud, for I have seen Thee, O Master!*

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6. By Germanus:*[^92]

*O Christ God, Who today wast pleased to sit upon aged arms as upon a Cherubic chariot: being invoked, deliver us who sing of Thee from the tyranny of the passions, and save us as the Lover of mankind!*

*Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the feast, litanies, and Dismissal. And the First Hour.*[^93]

*At the First Hour, Troparion of the feast. After "Our Father...", Kontakion of the feast, and the rest; and complete Dismissal.*

*At the second hour of the day, we go forth in procession with Litiya outside the monastery, singing the stichera of the feast and the canon according to custom. And having returned, we chant the Hours and the Liturgy. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the canon of the feast: Ode 3 and Ode 6.*[^94]

*Introit: The Lord has made known His salvation; before the nations He has revealed His righteousness. Then the Troparion of the feast; Glory... Both now... Kontakion.*[^95]

*Prokeimenon, the Hymn of the Mother of God, Tone 3: My soul magnifies the Lord, and my spirit has rejoiced in God my Savior. Verse: For He has looked upon the low estate of His handmaiden*[^96] shall call me blessed, all generations.
*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 316.*[^97]
*Alleluia, Tone 8:* Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word. *Verse:* A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 7.*[^98]
*Communion Verse:* I will take the cup of salvation, and call upon the name of the Lord.[^99]

*Be it known that if the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on a Sunday before the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee, when the Sunday service is sung apart from the Triodion:*[^100]

*On Saturday at Small Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried," the Resurrection stichera of the Tone, 4; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: one Resurrection sticheron, and the Aposticha of the feast from Great Vespers with the refrains of the feast; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Resurrection Troparion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Little Litany, and the Dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried," Resurrection stichera, 3; and one of Anatolius; and of the feast, 6; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Entry; Prokeimenon of the day; and the readings of the feast, 3. At the Litiya: the stichera, and Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: Resurrection stichera; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves: Troparion of the feast, thrice; and "Blessed be the name of the Lord"; and the reading of the feast. (And where there is no Vigil: after "Now lettest Thou depart," and after the Trisagion, the Resurrection Troparion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. And at Compline, after "It is truly meet," and after the Trisagion, the Resurrection Kontakion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. And Midnight Office we sing according to custom with the Triadic Canon.)*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," the Resurrection Troparion twice; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After the usual Kathismata: the Resurrection Sessional Hymns with their Theotokia, and the reading from the Commentary Gospel. Then the Polyeleos; and the Troparia "The Angelic Assembly"; and the Sessional Hymns of the feast, both once; Glory... Both now... the Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos; and the reading of the feast. The Anavathmoi of the Tone. Prokeimenon and Gospel of the feast. Then "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"; and Psalm 50; and the veneration of the Gospel according to custom; and the sticheron of the feast. Canon: of the Resurrection with the Heirmos, 4; and of the Theotokos, 2; and of the feast, 8. Katavasia of the feast. After Ode 3: the Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos, and the Sessional Hymn of the feast, 4; Glory... Both now... the same. After Ode 6: the Kontakion and Ikos of the feast; and we read the Prologue. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable." Exapostilarion:*

*Resurrection Exapostilarion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Praises: Resurrection stichera, 4; and of the feast, 4, with the festal verses; Glory... of the feast; Both now... "Most blessed art thou." Then the Great Doxology; the Resurrection Troparion only; the Litanies; and the Dismissal. Then the Gospel Sticheron, and the Catechesis, and the usual procession, and the First Hour in the narthex. At the Hours: the Resurrection Troparion; Glory... Troparion of the feast; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. At all the Hours we recite the Kontakion of the Resurrection and of the feast, alternating. At the Divine Liturgy: Beatitudes of the Resurrection Tone, 6; and of the feast, Ode 3, 4. After the Little Entrance: the Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the feast; Glory... Kontakion of the Resurrection; Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel we read first of the Sunday Tone, then of the feast. Communion verse: "Praise the Lord," and of the feast:*

*It is fitting to know if the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee, or of the Prodigal Son, or Meatfare, or Cheesefare:*[^101]

*On Saturday at Small Vespers: Resurrection stichera of the Tone, 4; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: one Resurrection sticheron, and the Aposticha of the feast from Great Vespers with their refrains; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Resurrection Troparion; Glory... Both now... of the feast; and the Dismissal. At Great Vespers we sing: "Blessed is the man," the entire Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Resurrection stichera of the Tone, 3; and of the Triodion, 3; and of the feast, 4.*

*If it be Meatfare Sunday:*

*Resurrection, 3; and of the Triodion, 4; and of the feast, 3; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast. Entry, and readings of the feast, 3. At the Litiya: stichera of the feast, Tone 1: "Ancient of Days," and the other two; then two stichera of the Triodion from the Praises:*[^102]

*If it be the Sunday of the Pharisee: the 1st sticheron, Tone 3: "The boastful Pharisee"; the second, Tone 8: "Boasting of deeds."*

*If it be the Sunday of the Prodigal Son:*

*After the stichera of the feast, we sing at the Litiya 3 stichera of the Triodion from the Praises: the 1st sticheron, Tone 2: "The voice of the Prodigal"; the 2nd, Tone 8: "And I squandered in prodigality"; the 3rd, Tone 6: "O Good Father."*

*If it be Meatfare Sunday:*

*And after the stichera of the feast, we sing 2 stichera from the Praises: the 1st, Tone 6: "I ponder that day"; the 2nd: "O what an hour then!"*

*If it be Cheesefare Sunday: And we sing at the Litiya of the Triodion likewise 2 stichera from the Praises: the 1st, Tone 5: "Woe is me!"; the 2nd,* "The arena of virtues." And Glory we sing on all these Sundays—of the Pharisee, and of the Prodigal, and Meatfare, and Cheesefare—of the Triodion, which is written at the Litiya; Both now... of the feast.

*(For this reason it is appointed to sing the Triodion stichera from the Praises at the Litiya at Vespers, so that not a single sticheron of the Triodion remains unsung.)*

*At the Aposticha: Resurrection stichera; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast.*

*At the Blessing of the Loaves: Troparion of the feast, thrice; and the reading from the Apostolic Epistles. And where there is no Vigil: after "Now lettest Thou depart," Resurrection Troparion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At Compline, after the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Triodion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. And Midnight Office we sing according to custom, with the Triadic Canon.*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," Resurrection Troparion, twice; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After the Kathismata: Resurrection Sessional Hymns, with their Theotokia; and the reading from the Commentary Gospel; and the Polyeleos of the feast; and the Troparia "The Angelic Assembly":*

*If it be the Sunday of the Prodigal Son, or Meatfare, or Cheesefare:*

*We chant in addition also the 3rd Psalm, "By the rivers of Babylon."*[^103] *Then the Megalynarion of the feast, and the Sessional Hymns of the feast, each once; and the reading of the feast. The Anavathmoi of the Tone. Prokeimenon of the feast. "Let every breath"; Gospel of the feast. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"; and Psalm 50. Sticheron of the feast. Canon: of the Resurrection with the Heirmos, 4; and of the Triodion, 4; and of the feast, 6. Katavasia of the feast, both choirs together.*

*If it be Meatfare Sunday: Canon of the Resurrection, 4; and of the Triodion, 6; and of the feast, 4. After Ode 3: the Kontakion and Ikos, and the Sessional Hymn of the Triodion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After Ode 6: the Kontakion of the feast, and the Ikos. And we read the Synaxarion. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable." Resurrection Exapostilarion; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast. At the Praises: Resurrection stichera, 4; and of the feast, 4, with the festal refrains; Glory... of the feast; Both now... "Most blessed art thou":*

*If it be Meatfare Sunday: At the Praises: Resurrection stichera, 4; and of the feast, 4, with the Doxasticon, with the refrains of the feast. And after the conclusion of the stichera of the feast, we recite the verse: "Arise, O Lord my God"; and we sing the idiomelon sticheron of the Triodion, Tone 8: "Daniel the Prophet"; Glory... Tone 1:*[^104]

..."...cleanse ourselves, brethren"; and Both now... "Most blessed art thou."

*If it be Cheesefare Sunday: At the Praises we sing as on Meatfare Sunday; we recite the verse likewise: "Arise, O Lord my God"; and we sing the idiomelon sticheron of the Triodion, Tone 6: "Adam was cast out of Paradise"; Glory... the same Tone: "The season has arrived"; and Both now... "Most blessed art thou." Great Doxology; Resurrection Troparion; and the usual Dismissal. And the procession into the narthex; Glory... Both now... the Gospel Sticheron; and the First Hour.*

*At the First Hour, and at the others: the Resurrection Troparion; Glory... of the feast; Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we recite the Kontakion of the feast and of the Triodion, alternating. At the Divine Liturgy: Beatitudes of the Tone, 4; and of the Triodion, Ode 3, 4; and of the feast, Ode 6, 4. Introit of the feast. After this, the Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the feast; Glory... Kontakion of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon of the Tone, and of the feast.*

*If it be Meatfare Sunday: Prokeimenon of the Triodion and of the feast. Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion verse: first of the day, and of the feast.*
