*If the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Meatfare Saturday:*[^105] *The service of the reposed is sung in the evening and in the morning on the preceding Saturday, or on Meatfare Thursday. On Saturday itself, the entire service of the feast alone is sung without variation.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Monday, or on Tuesday, or on Thursday of Cheesefare Week:*[^106]

*In the evening at Small Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the feast, 4; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: the idiomelon of the day from the Triodion twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... Both now... of the feast; Troparion of the feast; Litany, and the Dismissal. At Great Vespers and at Matins the entire service of the feast is performed without variation, as indicated previously in order. For the Triodion is not sung with the feast on whatever day it may fall during Cheesefare week, except on Saturday and Sunday, apart from the idiomelon stichera of the day which are recited at Small Vespers, as indicated.*

*And where there is no All-Night Vigil: we sing at Great Vespers according to what is prescribed above. At the Aposticha: Glory... the idiomelon of the day from the Triodion; Both now... of the feast.*

*At the Praises: after the stichera of the feast, Glory... the idiomelon of the day from the Triodion; Both now... of the feast. Great Doxology. At the Hours and at the Divine Liturgy: the entire service of the feast;*

...and the Epistle and Gospel of the day we read beforehand.

*If the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Cheesefare Wednesday, or on Friday:*[^107]

*At Small and Great Vespers, and at Matins, the entire service is celebrated without variation, as was indicated for Cheesefare Monday. But at the conclusion of Vespers, Matins, and each Hour, there are 3 great prostrations only. At the Hours we recite the Troparion and Kontakion of the feast. At the Sixth Hour, the Troparion of the Prophecy is recited, and the Paroemia is read. The Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated in its proper time. And Vespers we sing according to custom in its proper time. After the usual Psalm, the Kathisma in order.*

*And where there has been an All-Night Vigil: we do not recite the Kathismata, but after the usual Psalm we recite "Lord, I have cried." Then the stichera of the feast, 3; and of Saint Symeon, 3; Glory... of the Saint; Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon of the day, and the Paroemia. Then the Prokeimenon: "Let Israel hope in the Lord."*[^108] *At the Aposticha: the idiomelon of the day from the Triodion twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... Both now... of the feast; Troparion of the feast; and the Dismissal.*

*It is fitting to know if the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Cheesefare Monday:*

*We sing the Matins Triodion-canon at Compline on Sunday, but the Matins idiomelon of the day from the Triodion we sing on the feast as the Doxasticon at the Praises.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Cheesefare Tuesday:*

*We sing the Tuesday Triodion-canon at Compline on Cheesefare Wednesday; but the Matins idiomelon of the day we sing on the feast as the Doxasticon at the Praises.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Cheesefare Wednesday:*

*We sing the Wednesday Canon and Triodion-canon at Compline on Cheesefare Thursday; but the Matins idiomelon of the day we sing on the feast as the Doxasticon at the Praises.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Cheesefare Thursday:*

*We sing the Thursday Triodion-canon on Cheesefare Tuesday at Compline.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Cheesefare Friday:*

*We sing the Friday Canon with the Triodion-canon on Cheesefare Wednesday at Compline.*

*If the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Cheesefare Saturday:*

*On Friday evening at Small Vespers: stichera of the feast, 4; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon, and the Paroemia of the day; and after it the Prokeimenon: "Let Israel hope in the Lord." At the Aposticha of the Triodion:*

*...the idiomelon of the day twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Troparion of the feast, and the Dismissal. At Great Vespers we sing "Blessed is the man," the 1st Antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," we sing stichera of the feast, 6; and of the Fathers, 4;*[^109] *Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. Entry; Prokeimenon of the day; and the reading of the feast. At the Litiya: stichera, and Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: stichera of the feast; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves: Troparion of the feast, twice; and of the Fathers, 1; and the reading of the feast.*

*And where there is no All-Night Vigil: We sing at Great Vespers all according to what is prescribed above. Prokeimenon and reading of the day, and the Prokeimenon: "Let Israel hope in the Lord"; then the readings of the feast. At the Aposticha of the feast, then of the Fathers; refrain: "Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints";*[^110] *and we sing their idiomelon: "Multitudes of monastics"; Glory... the idiomelon of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast. At Matins: At "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the feast, twice; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast.*

*After the Kathismata: Sessional Hymns of the feast, and the reading of the feast. After the Polyeleos: Sessional Hymn of the feast. Anavathmoi: the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon. "Let every breath"; Gospel; and after Psalm 50, the sticheron of the feast. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos, 8; and of the Fathers, 6; Katavasia of the feast. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the Fathers and Ikos; and the Sessional Hymn of the Fathers, twice; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After Ode 6: the Kontakion of the feast and the Ikos. And we read the Synaxarion. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable." Exapostilarion of the feast; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. At the Praises: stichera of the feast, 3; and of the Fathers, 3; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. Great Doxology; Troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the feast; the Litanies; and the Dismissal; and the usual procession into the narthex; and the First Hour.*

*At the Hours: Troparion of the feast; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the feast. At the Divine Liturgy: Beatitudes of the feast, Ode 3, 4; and of the Fathers, Ode 6, 4. Introit of the feast; Troparion of the feast, and of the Fathers; Glory... Kontakion of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion verse: first of the feast, and of the Fathers.*

*It is fitting to know that if the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Monday of the First Week of Great Lent:*[^111]

*The service of the feast is to be sung on Cheesefare Sunday according to what has been prescribed above.*

*It is fitting to know: If the Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on a Sunday before the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee:*[^112]

*On Saturday at Small Vespers: Resurrection stichera, and of the Theotokos according to custom; Resurrection Troparion; Glory... Both now... its Theotokion. At Great Vespers: after the Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrection stichera, 3; and one of Anatolius; of the feast of that day, 3; and of the Saint from the Menaion, 3. (If the Saint is sung at 6, or has a Polyeleos: we sing Resurrection stichera, 3; and of the feast, 3; and of the Saint, 4; Glory... of the Saint.) If not: Glory... of the feast; Both now... the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. At the Litiya: stichera of the feast, the daily Aposticha of that day; and of the Saint, if there is a Polyeleos; Glory... of the Saint; Both now... of the feast; but if not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: Resurrection stichera; Glory... of the Saint, if there be one; Both now... of the feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves: Troparion "O Theotokos and Virgin," twice; and of the feast, 1; and the reading from the Apostolic Epistles. At Matins: At "God is the Lord," the Resurrection Troparion twice; Glory... of the Saint, if there be one; if not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. After the Kathismata: Resurrection Sessional Hymns, and their Theotokia, and the reading from the Commentary Gospel. After this: the Blameless (Amomos), and the Hypakoe, and the reading of the day. (If the Saint has a Polyeleos, after the Kathisma we sing the Polyeleos of the Saint; and after the Polyeleos, the Troparia "The Angelic Assembly"; and after the Litany, the Sessional Hymn of the Saint once; Glory... the Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos; Both now... its Theotokion, and the reading.) The Anavathmoi and the Prokeimenon of the Tone. "Let every breath"; Sunday Gospel; and "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ." Canon: of the Resurrection with the Heirmos, 4; and of the Theotokos, 2; and of the feast, 4; and of the Saint, 4. (If there is a Polyeleos or Doxology: we sing the Canon of the Resurrection with the Heirmos, 4; and of the feast, 4; and of the Saint, 6.) Katavasia of the feast. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the feast; and of the Saint, if he has one, and his Sessional Hymn; Glory... Both now... of the feast; and the reading. After Ode 6: the Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos; and we read the Prologue. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable." Resurrection Exapostilarion; Glory... of the Saint, if he has one; Both now... of the feast. At the Praises: Resurrection stichera, 4; and of the Saint, if he has prosomoia, 3; and the idiomelon which is for the Doxasticon we sing with its verses. But if the Saint has no stichera at the Praises: we sing Resurrection stichera, 4; and of the feast, 4, the Matins Aposticha of that day with their verses:*

*...Glory... the Gospel Sticheron; and Both now... "Most blessed art thou." Great Doxology; the Resurrection Troparion only; the Litanies; and the Dismissal; and the Catechesis; and the usual procession into the narthex; and the First Hour.*

*At the First Hour: the Resurrection Troparion; Glory... of the feast. And if there is a Saint, we recite his Troparion and Kontakion, alternating; and Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the feast; and the final Dismissal. Likewise at the other Hours we recite the Resurrection Troparion first, and alternate the feast and the Saint. Kontakion of the Resurrection and of the feast, we recite likewise alternating; and if there is a Polyeleos, also of the Saint. At the Divine Liturgy: Beatitudes of the Octoechos, 6, with the Trinity Hymn; and of the feast, Ode on 4. [If the Saint has an Ode: Beatitudes of the Resurrection, 4; and of the feast, Ode 3, 4; and of the Saint, Ode 6, 4.] After the Little Entrance: Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the feast; then of the Temple, and of the Saint if he has one. Kontakion of the Resurrection, and of the Temple; Glory... of the Saint if he has one; Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, and Gospel: first of the Sunday in order; then of the Saint, if he has them. Communion verse: "Praise the Lord"; and of the Saint, if he has one; if not: of the feast.*

*If the Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on the Sunday of the Pharisee, or of the Prodigal Son, or Meatfare:*[^113]

*At Small Vespers: at "Lord, I have cried," and at the Aposticha, stichera of the Octoechos according to custom. At Great Vespers: at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrection stichera, 4; and of the Triodion, 3; and of the feast, 3.*

*If it be Meatfare Sunday:*

*Resurrection stichera, 3; and of the Triodion, 4; and of the feast, 3; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. At the Litiya: stichera of the feast, the daily Aposticha of that day; and from the Praises of the Triodion; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: Resurrection stichera; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves: Troparion "O Theotokos and Virgin," twice; and of the feast, 1; and the reading. At "God is the Lord," the Resurrection Troparion twice; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After the Kathismata: Resurrection Sessional Hymns with their Theotokia; and the reading from the Commentary Gospel. After the Blameless (Amomos), the Troparia "The Angelic Assembly."*

*If it be the Sunday of the Prodigal Son, or Meatfare:*

*We sing the Polyeleos, adding also the 3rd Psalm, "By the rivers of Babylon." The Anavathmoi, and the Prokeimenon of the Tone. "Let every breath"; the Sunday Gospel; "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"; and the rest. Canon of the Resurrection with the Heirmos with the Heirmos, 4; and of the Theotokos, 2; and of the Triodion, 4; and of the feast, 4. Katavasia of the feast.*

*If it be on Meatfare Sunday:*

*Canon of the Resurrection, 4; and of the Triodion, 6; and of the feast, 4. Katavasia of the Triodion. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the feast; Glory... Sessional Hymn of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion of the Triodion, and the Ikos. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable"; then the Resurrection Exapostilarion; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast. At the Praises: Resurrection stichera, 4; and of the feast, idiomela, 4, with its verses.*

*If it be Meatfare Sunday, we recite the verse:*

*"Arise, O Lord my God"; and we sing the idiomelon sticheron of the Triodion: "Daniel the Prophet"; Glory... of the Triodion likewise: "Let us cleanse ourselves beforehand"; and Both now... "Most blessed art thou."*

*If it be the Sunday of the Pharisee, or of the Prodigal Son:*

*We do not recite the verse of the Triodion; but after the stichera of the feast we recite Glory... of the Triodion; and Both now... "Most blessed art thou." Great Doxology. After the Trisagion: Resurrection Troparion. The Litanies, and the Dismissal. And the usual procession into the narthex; Glory... Both now... the Gospel Sticheron; and the First Hour. At the Hours: the Resurrection Troparion; Glory... of the feast; and Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we recite the Kontakion of the Triodion and of the feast, alternating.*

*At the Divine Liturgy: Beatitudes of the Tone, 4; and of the Triodion, Ode 6, 4; and of the feast, the Ode in order, 4. After the Little Entrance: Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the feast; Glory... Kontakion of the Triodion; and Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion verse of the day.*

*If the Afterfeast of the Meeting occurs on Meatfare Saturday, the service of the Afterfeast is omitted, and we sing the regular service of the Memorial Saturday of the Reposed.*[^114]

*It is fitting to know: If the Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Monday, or on Tuesday, or on Thursday of Cheesefare Week:*[^115]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the feast, 3; and of the Saint, 3; Glory... of the Saint, if he has one; and Both now... of the feast. If not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: the idiomelon of the day from the Triodion twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... of the Saint, if there be one; and Both now... of the feast. If not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. Troparion of the Saint, if there be one; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Litany, and the Dismissal.*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the feast, twice; Glory of the Saint, if there be one; and Both now... of the feast. After the Kathismata: Sessional Hymns of the feast. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos, 8; and from the Menaion, 4. Where there is a Triodion-canon, we sing the Canon of the feast with the Heirmos, 4; and 3 troparia from the Menaion on 2; and of the Triodion, 8. If the Saint has a Kontakion and Ikos, we recite them after Ode 3; then the Sessional Hymn of the Triodion, written after the 2nd Kathisma; Glory... of the Saint; and Both now... of the feast. After Ode 6: the Kontakion and Ikos of the feast. After Ode 9: Exapostilarion of the feast; Glory... of the Saint; and Both now... of the feast; but if not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Praises: stichera of the feast, 4, from the Aposticha of that day; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: the idiomelon of the Triodion, 2; and the Martyrikon; Glory... of the Saint, if there be one; and Both now... of the feast; but if not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. After "Our Father": Troparion of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the feast; but if not: of the feast only; and after the Litany, the First Hour. At the Hours: Troparion of the feast; Glory... of the Saint, if there be one; and Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion we recite the Kontakion of the feast, without variation.*

*It is fitting to know:*[^116]

*If the Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs during Cheesefare Week on Wednesday or Friday, there are no prostrations during the psalmody, but only at the conclusion of each service 3 great prostrations. In the evening at "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the feast, 3; and from the Menaion of the Saint, 3; Glory... of the Saint, if there be one; and Both now... of the feast; if not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha: the idiomelon of the Triodion twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... of the Saint, if there be one; and Both now... of the feast; if not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. Troparion of the Saint, if he has one; Glory... Both now... of the feast; if not: of the feast. Litany, and 3 great prostrations; and the Dismissal. Compline we sing Great Compline, starting from "When I called upon Thee"; at which we sing the Matins Triodion-canon of the Triodion on 8. After "It is truly meet," and after the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the feast. After "More honorable," 3 great prostrations; and at the Dismissal, 3 prostrations. Likewise at the Midnight Office, at "More honorable," 3 great prostrations. At Matins: At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the feast, twice; Glory... of the Saint, if there be one; and Both now... of the feast; if not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. After the Kathismata: Sessional Hymns of the feast. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos, 6; and from the Menaion, 4; and of the Triodion, 4. But when the Triodion-canon occurs, we sing the Canon of the feast with the Heirmos, 4; and from the Menaion of the Menaion for 2, taking two verses as one; and of the Triodion for 8: the appointed ode of the present canon for 4, and of the second triodion for 4; the first triodion is chanted beforehand at Compline. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the Saint; then the Sessional Hymn of the Triodion; Glory..., of the Saint; Both now..., of the feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the feast, and the Prologue. After Ode 9: Exapostilarion of the Saint, if there be one; Glory... Both now..., of the feast. If not, of the feast, twice. At the Praises: Stichera of the feast, Podoben, for 4; the Aposticha of that day. Glory... Both now..., of the feast. At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory..., of the Saint, if there be one; Both now..., of the feast. If not: Glory... Both now..., of the feast. After the Trisagion: Troparion of the Saint, if there be one; Glory... Both now..., of the feast. If not, of the feast only. Litany, and 3 great prostrations; and the First Hour. At the First Hour: Troparion of the feast; Glory..., of the Saint; Both now..., Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion: Kontakion of the feast. At 'More honorable': 3 great prostrations, and the rest, and the complete dismissal; and 3 prostrations. Likewise at the other Hours we say the Troparion of the feast, and after the Trisagion the Kontakion of the feast; after 'More honorable', 3 great prostrations each. At the Sixth Hour: Troparion of the Paremia, and the reading; then the Prokeimenon. After the Ninth Hour, we chant the Beatitudes quickly without singing, and the rest. On Wednesday evening: Stichera of the feast, 3; and of the Menaion for the Saint, Podoben, 3; Glory... Both now..., of the feast; then the Prokeimenon, and the Paremia of the day. At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... Both now..., of the feast. After this, the Litany: 'Have mercy on us, O God', and 3 great prostrations, and the prayer: 'All-Holy Trinity', and 'Blessed be the name of the Lord', thrice; and Psalm 33; and the Dismissal; and 3 prostrations.*

*If the Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord occurs on Cheesefare Saturday:*[^117]
*On Friday evening, after the dismissal of the Hours, we immediately begin Vespers. And after the customary Kathisma, Stichera of the feast, 3; and of the Fathers, 3; Glory..., of the Fathers; Both now..., of the feast. Prokeimenon, and the Paremia of the day. Then: 'Vouchsafe, O Lord'. At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the Triodion, twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory..., of the Fathers; Both now..., of the feast. Troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now..., of the feast; litany; and the Dismissal. At Compline: Kontakion of the feast. At Matins, at 'God is the Lord', the Troparion of the feast, twice; Glory..., of the Fathers; Both now..., of the feast. After both Kathismata: Sessional Hymns of the Fathers, two each; Glory* Both now..., of the feast; and the reading of the feast, or of the Fathers. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos for 6, and of the Fathers for 8. Katavasia of the feast. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the feast, and the Sessional Hymn of the Fathers, twice; Glory... Both now..., of the feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Fathers. Exapostilarion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now..., of the feast. At the Praises: Stichera of the feast, 3; the Aposticha of that day; and of the Fathers, 3; Glory..., of the Fathers; Both now..., of the feast. Great Doxology. Troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now..., of the feast. Litanies, and the Dismissal. And the customary procession into the narthex; and the First Hour. At the Hours: Troparion of the feast, and of the Fathers. Kontakion of the feast and of the Fathers, saying them alternately. At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the feast, the appointed ode for 4; and of the Fathers, Ode 6, for 4. After the Entrance: Troparion of the feast, and of the Fathers, and of the temple of the Saint. Then the Kontakion of the temple of the Saint; Glory..., of the Fathers; Both now..., of the feast. Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Hymn of the feast and of the Fathers. Epistle and Gospel of the day, and of the Fathers.*

*On the Same Month, on the 3rd Day: Holy and Righteous Symeon the God-Receiver, and Anna the Prophetess.*[^118]

*At 'Lord, I have cried', Stichera of the feast, 3; and of the Saint, 3.*

*Stichera of the feast, Tone 1.*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небесныхъ чинѡвъ):*

*Sticheron 1:*
The Creator of all things and our Redeemer is brought into the temple by the Virgin Mother; therefore the elder, receiving Him, cried out with joy: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Blessed One, as Thou hast willed.

*Sticheron 2:*
Symeon, receiving into his hands from the Virgin Him Who was begotten before all ages, cried out: I have seen the Savior, the enlightenment of Thy glory unto the ends of the earth; now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Blessed One, for I have seen Thee today.

*Sticheron 3:*
Born in the end of ages for the salvation of mankind, Symeon, bearing the Savior in his hands, rejoiced, crying: I have seen the Light of the Gentiles and the Glory of Israel; now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, as Thou hast spoken, as God, from those from those that are here by Thy command.

*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 4.*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Далъ еси знаменїе):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Righteous and perfect, and glorious in all things being, O divinely-inspired one, thou didst take up into thy hands the incarnate God, the only Converse, Who came to justify the world; and that thou mightest be loosed from the body, thou didst cry out to Him: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Lover of mankind, for I have seen Thee today.

*Sticheron 2:*
Youthful in spirit, yet aged in body, O Symeon, thou didst receive the promise not to see death until thou shouldest see a young child, the Creator Who is before the ages, and the God of all, Who became poor in the flesh; and having seen Him, thou didst leap for joy, and didst ask release from the flesh, passing with rejoicing unto the divine habitations.

*Sticheron 3:*
Anna the divinely-inspired, and Symeon the most-rich, shining with prophecy and shown blameless in the law, having seen the Giver of the Law manifest as a child like unto us, now bowed down before Him; whose memory we celebrate today with joy, worthily glorifying Jesus the Lover of mankind.

*Glory... Both now..., Tone 5:*
*By Germanus:*[^119]
The Ancient of Days, having become an infant in the flesh, is brought into the temple by the Virgin Mother, fulfilling the promise of His own Law; Whom Symeon, having received, said: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, according to Thy word, for mine eyes have seen Thy holy salvation.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1.*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небесныхъ чинѡвъ):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Today we celebrate the saving grace of the ineffable Theophany, for Christ our God, having become an infant without change from the Virgin maiden, is now brought into the temple unto God the Father by His Mother; Whom Symeon, having received, embraced in his hands.

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master, according to Thy word, in peace.*

*Sticheron 2:*
In the prophets Thou wast seen, O Jesus, in such manner as it was possible of old to see Thee; but now, O Word of God, Thou hast appeared in the flesh to all the world of Thine own will, of the Virgin Mary, O Christ; Thou hast revealed Thy salvation to all that are of Adam, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Verse: A light for revelation to the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*

*Sticheron 3:*
On Mount Sinai of old Thou didst establish the Law, and now in the city of Zion Thou dost fulfill the requirements of the Law; by the hands of the Birthgiver of God, an infant, O Christ O Christ, in Thy good pleasure, Thou didst desire to be carried in the flesh into the temple in the arms of Symeon.

*Glory... Both now..., Tone 4:*
*By John the Monk:*[^120]
Today He Who of old gave the Law to Moses on Sinai submits to the ordinances of the Law, having become for our sakes an infant like unto us; now the pure God, as a holy child, having opened the pure womb, is offered unto Himself as God, delivering from the curse of the Law, and enlightening our souls.

*Troparion of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, full of grace, for from thee hath shone forth the Sun of Righteousness, Christ our God, enlightening those in darkness. Rejoice thou also, O righteous elder, having received in thine arms the Redeemer of our souls, Who grants unto us the resurrection.

*At Matins, at 'God is the Lord', the Troparion, thrice.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1.*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*
Thou wast brought into the temple, O Giver of life, having become an infant for my sake; Thou wast under the Law, Who of old didst inscribe the Law upon the tablets on Mount Sinai, that Thou mightest deliver all from the ancient servitude of the Law. Glory to Thy compassion, O Savior; glory to Thy kingdom; glory to Thy dispensation, O only Lover of mankind.

*Glory... Both now..., the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5.*

*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*
Thou wast brought into the temple, fulfilling the customary things, O Creator, Master, and Lawgiver, O Savior; and in the arms of Symeon, by Thine own good pleasure, to be carried in the flesh, Thou Who art terrible unto all and upholdest all creation, didst desire, O only Merciful One, to reveal Thy light unto us all.

*Glory... Both now..., the same.*

*Canon of the feast with the Heirmos for 6, and of the Saint for 6.*

*Canon of the feast, Tone 3.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The sun once traversed the dry land of the deep, for the water was congealed on both sides like a wall, as the people walked on dry land across the sea and sang in a manner pleasing unto God: Let us sing unto the Lord, for gloriously is He glorified.

*Troparia:*
Let the clouds drop down water, for the Sun, borne upon a light cloud, hath arrived upon an undefiled arm, Christ in the church as an infant; therefore let us, the faithful, cry aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for gloriously is He glorified.

The hands of Symeon, weakened with age, are strengthened; and the weary feet of the elder move swiftly and directly, to meet Christ to meet Christ, assembling a choir with the bodiless hosts: Let us sing unto the Lord, for gloriously is He glorified.

Be glad, O heavens stretched out in wisdom, and rejoice, O earth! For coming forth from the most divine bosom, Christ the Craftsman is brought unto God the Father by the Virgin Mother, an infant Who was before all things: for gloriously is He glorified.

*Canon of the Saint, whose acrostic is: "Receive the song, O Symeon the God-Receiver. By Joseph." Tone 4.*[^121]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter a word unto the Queen Mother; and I shall be seen radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.

*Troparia:*
Joyfully taking up thy dwelling in the righteous habitations of the righteous Lord, O righteous Symeon: pray that those who celebrate thy divine passing may be justified, O most blessed one.

Having been a sacred minister in the Law, O most sacred one, Thou didst behold as an infant Him Whom the Law proclaimed; and having died according to the law of nature, O God-receiver, Thou didst pass rejoicing unto immortal life.

Thou didst see Him Who holds the ends of the universe in His hands carried in the hands of the Virgin; and having received Him into thy venerable embrace, thou wast shown to be greater than Moses, O most rich one.

*Theotokion:*
O well-spring of water, the Pre-eternal One begins, the Word grows thick, the Creator is formed, the Uncontainable is contained, being incarnate in thy womb, O woman full of the grace of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, the confirmation of those that hope in Thee, confirm the Church which Thou hast purchased with Thy precious blood.

*Troparia of the Feast:*
The First-begotten of the Father before the ages, the First-begotten infant of the undefiled Virgin, appeared stretching forth His hand to Adam.

God the Word, setting right again the first-created man who through deceit had become childish in mind, appeared having become an infant.

The Creator, having shown that His nature is conformable to the Godhead, unchangeably appeared as an infant, that the offspring of the earth might run back again unto it.

*Another Heirmos:*
As the barren church bore from the nations, and the assembly great in children grew weak, let us cry aloud unto our marvelous God: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Having been exalted by sacred deeds, O speaker of divine things, as a high and luminous pillar, thou wast manifestly confirmed in the Holy Spirit; therefore we praise Thee.

Thou didst behold the Lawgiver of the Lord, O Symeon, Who opened the virginal womb and became an infant bearing flesh, Who bestows redemption upon all.

He Who created man with His hands, being incarnate, is held by thy hands, manifestly sanctifying thee; and when thou didst ask for the life to come, O blessed one, He dismisses thee.

*Theotokion:*
When the all-blameless Symeon saw thee, the noetic tongs bearing the divine Coal, Who beyond understanding was incarnate of thy blood, rejoicing he called thee blessed.

*Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Я҆ви́лся е҆сѝ дне́сь):*
The elder today, praying to be loosed from the bonds of this corruptible life, received in his arms Christ, the Creator and Lord.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства твоегѡ̀):*
The holy Symeon, rejoicing, took up into his hands Thee Who wast incarnate of the Virgin, O Christ, crying: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master; and Anna, the blameless and glorious prophetess, now brings thanksgiving and praise unto Thee. And we, O Giver of life, cry aloud unto Thee: Glory to Him Who was thus well-pleased!

*Glory... Both now..., the same.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thy virtue, O Christ, hath covered the heavens; for having come forth from the ark of Thy sanctification, of Thine undefiled Mother, Thou didst appear in the temple of Thy glory as an infant borne in arms, and all things were filled with Thy praise.

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Rejoice, O Symeon, initiate of ineffable mysteries, cried the Birthgiver of God; receive in thine arms Christ, the Word Who hath become an infant, concerning Whom the Holy Spirit revealed to thee of old, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise.

In Whom thou hadst hoped, O Symeon, a youthful child, take up Christ with joy, the divine consolation of Israel, the Creator and Master of the Law, Who fulfills the order of the Law, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise.

Seeing the co-unoriginate Word borne in the flesh by the Virgin as upon a cherubic throne, the Cause of all existence, Symeon marveled at Him as an infant, and cried aloud unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise.

*Another Heirmos:*
Seated in glory upon the throne of the Godhead upon a light cloud, Jesus the most divine came by an incorrupt hand, and saved those who cry faithful: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparia:*
Thou wast grown very old in years, yet renewed by faith, O Symeon, desiring to see the new all-perfect infant, Who renews the world that had grown old by the assault of the ancient adversary.

Let us in harmony call the blessed Symeon blessed, for he was counted worthy to see the blessed God clothed in the flesh, that He might make blessed those who formerly were wretched.

I know Thee as Lawgiver, incarnate outside the natural laws, and introducing a new law, Symeon cried aloud: O only Lord, now dismiss me unto incorruptible life.

*Theotokion:*
He Who upholds all things by His hand, being carried in the hands of the Ever-Virgin, made her higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim as His Bearer; whom let us sing and piously call blessed.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
When Isaiah saw God figuratively exalted upon a throne, escorted by angels of glory, he cried out: Woe is me, wretched man! For I have foreseen God incarnate, the Unwaning Light, Who rules in peace.

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Understanding the glory shown of old to the prophet, when the divine elder saw the Word held by motherly hands, rejoicing with her he cried aloud: Pure one, as a throne thou holdest God, the Unwaning Light, Who rules in peace.

The elder bowed down, and having divinely touched the footsteps of the unwedded Mother of God, he said: Pure one, thou bearest Fire; I fear to embrace an infant Who is God, the Unwaning Light, Who rules in peace.

Isaiah was cleansed, receiving a coal from the seraphim, the elder cried to the Mother of God: Thou, as with tongs, dost enlighten me with thy hands, giving Him Whom thou bearest, the Unwaning Light, Who rules in peace.

*Another Heirmos:*
All creation was amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O unwedded Virgin, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst give birth to the timeless Son, granting peace unto all that hymn thee.

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Having served God the Almighty with purity of mind like an angel, O blessed one, with sacrifices of blood thou didst cleanse of old the people of Israel, clearly foreshadowing the blood of the Savior thou didst make thyself a temple unto the God of all by divine deeds, O speaker of divine things; therefore in the holy temple thou didst behold God in the flesh as an infant, translating thee unto the divine habitations.

Today let us all sing, spiritually keeping festival, for Symeon the God-Receiver, and with him Anna the chaste, being prophets of God, who beheld Him Who for our sake became an infant.

*Theotokion:*
Without burning thy womb, being unchangeable in nature, O thou who alone art brighter than the Cherubim, He Who was born of thee appeared as man, and united God to those entangled in corruption, O Mother of God, by thy childbearing.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Seeing with his eyes the salvation that hath come from God unto the peoples, the elder cried aloud unto Thee: O Christ, Thou art my God!

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Thou hast been set as a stone for Zion, a stone of stumbling and an offense to the disobedient, but the unshakable salvation of the faithful.

Manifestly bearing the express image of Him Who begat Thee before the ages, out of compassion Thou hast now clothed Thyself with the weakness of earthly men.

Unto the Son of the Most High, the Son of the Virgin, unto God Who became a child, having bowed down before Thee, [the elder cried:] Now dismiss [me] in peace.

*Another Heirmos:*
Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah prayed in the whale, crying: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!

*Troparia of the Saint:*
When thou didst see the Lord, O Symeon, thou didst cry aloud: As Thou hast promised, now dismiss Thy servant, O Savior, proclaiming Thy divine incarnation unto all that are in hades.

Thou wast adorned, and becamest more luminous than Moses, O elder Symeon, receiving in thine arms Him Who is beautiful in fairness, Who for our sake became an infant like unto us.

The generation of the prisoners that were there, seeing thee, O Symeon, full of divine gifts and descended into the dark places of hades, was filled with divine dew.

*Theotokion:*
The all-crafty one ever seeks to ensnare me; but snatch me from his nets, O good one, and by the divine shelter of thy wings preserve me whole.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Thou Who didst sanctify the virginal womb by Thy birth, and didst bless the hands of Symeon as was meet, having anticipated now also hast Thou saved us, O Christ God; but make peaceful our commonwealth in wars, and strengthen the king whom Thou hast loved,[^122] loved, O only Lover of mankind.

*Ikos:*
Let us run to the Birthgiver of God, wishing to see her Son carried to Symeon; Whom seeing from heaven, the bodiless hosts were amazed, saying: Marvelous things we see now and most glorious, incomprehensible and ineffable! For He Who created Adam is carried as an infant; the Uncontainable is contained in the arms of the elder; He Who is in the uncircumscribed bosom of His Father is willingly circumscribed in the flesh, and not in the Godhead, the only Lover of mankind.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thee, Who didst bedew the Children theologizing in the fire, and didst dwell in the incorrupt Virgin, God the Word, we piously hymn, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Desiring to make known unto Adam dwelling in hades, and to bring the good tidings unto Eve, Symeon cried aloud, keeping festival with the prophets: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

God, delivering the earthly race, shall come even unto hades; and unto all the captives He shall grant remission, and sight unto the blind, so that even the mute shall cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

A sword shall pierce through thine own heart also, O incorrupt one, Symeon proclaimed unto the Birthgiver of God, when thou seest thy Son upon the Cross, unto Whom we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Another Heirmos:*
The divinely-wise ones worshipped not the creature rather than the Creator; but having manfully trampled underfoot the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, the all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Lawfully didst thou serve the Lord Almighty, being truly righteous, and blameless in the Law, O blessed one; therefore thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, the all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

The Joy of the sorrowful hath come, the Redemption of Israel hath truly appeared as an infant in His temple: Dismiss me unto the life to come! Symeon cried aloud, rejoicing.

According to the multitude of Thy compassions, dismiss Thy servant, O Savior, who am overburdened by the letter of the Law and weakened by old age: for I have seen Thee, Symeon cried aloud, bearing flesh upon the earth.

*Theotokion:*
Without emptying the fullness of the most pure bosom of the Father Who begat Him, in thy bosom, O pure one, He sits as an infant, preparing for those whom He willed to become like, O most pure one, a sacred seat for Thee.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Standing together for piety in unendurable fire, yet unhurt by the flame, the youths sang a divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparia of the Feast:*
O people of Israel, beholding Thine Emmanuel, the Child born of the Virgin, dance now before the face of the divine ark: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

Behold, Symeon cried aloud, this Child, being God, shall be a sign spoken against! Unto Him let us, the faithful, cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

Being Life, this God the Word, having become an infant, shall be the fall of the disobedient, and the resurrection of all who sing in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos:*
The childbearing of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious Children in the furnace: then prefigured, but now in truth accomplished, it rouses the whole world to chant unto thee: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparia of the Saint:*
The praiseworthy Anna, being a widow and radiant with the grace of prophecy, glorified the most divine One Who came to betroth the world that had been widowed of divine glory, and showed forth unto all the divine redemption that was to come before their eyes.

Thou didst offer legal sacrifices, O blessed priest, proclaiming from afar the Lamb of ineffable mercy and the saving Blood; and having received Him incarnate, O Symeon, thou wast glorified above Moses and all the prophets.

Having seen the Desired One, thou didst receive release from corruption, O divinely-inspired one; and as ripe wheat thou didst pass unto the fathers, O glorious one, nurtured in good old age; therefore we celebrate thy most-festal memory with gladness of soul.

*Theotokion:*
As a lily, as a sweet-smelling rose, as a divine fragrance did the all-divine Word find thee, O all-pure Bride of God, and dwelt in thy womb, filling with sweet fragrance our nature that was filled with the foul stench of sin, O Mother Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*In the Law* In the Law, in the shadow and the scriptures, we the faithful see an image: every male that opens the womb is holy unto God.[^123] Therefore the firstborn Word of the unoriginate Father, the Son Who is the firstborn of an unwedded Mother, do we magnify.

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Instead of the pair of turtledoves and two young pigeons of the ancients, the divine elder and the chaste Anna the prophetess minister, magnifying Him Who was born of the Virgin, the Only-begotten Son of the Father, Who is brought into the temple.

Thou hast rendered unto me, Symeon cried aloud, the joy of Thy salvation, O Christ; receive Thy servant, who is wearied by the shadow, the secret sacred-herald of the new grace, magnifying Thee in praise.

Anna, the chaste and venerable prophetess and elder, divinely and openly confessed the Sovereign Lady in the temple; and preaching the Mother of God unto all present, she magnified her.

*Another Heirmos:*
Eve, through the sickness of disobedience, brought in a curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, through the burgeoning of thy childbearing, hast blossomed forth blessing unto the world. Therefore do we all magnify thee.

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Truly blessed wast thou, O God-Receiver, whose venerable hands touched the divine members of Him Who touches the mountains and they smoke, as David sings;[^124] therefore do we worthily praise thee.

Thy reliquary pours forth healings unto the faithful, and thy blessed memory, shining brighter than the sun, enlightens the souls of all and snatches them from spiritual darkness, O all-honored initiate of sacred mysteries.

Anna, the chaste and prophetess, and the glorious elder Symeon illuminate all under the sun with spiritual radiance as the sun and the moon; for whose sake, O Lord Who lovest mankind, deliver us from the darkness of sin.

*Theotokion:*
When the elder saw thee who wast revealed as the Mother of God, O Birthgiver of God, he spoke as a prophet: Behold, this thy Son is set for the fall and rising again of many, O Sovereign Lady, and for a sign spoken against.[^125]

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
Inalterably according to hypostasis, the Word was seedlessly united with flesh in thee, O Mother of God, and became an infant; Whom, carrying in thine arms cherubically as a throne, thou hast now offered unto God the Father: and the elder Symeon rejoicing received Him.

*Glory... Both now..., of the Feast:*
Standing in the sanctuary by the Spirit, the elder took into his arms the Master of the Law, crying: Now loose me from the bonds of flesh in peace, as Thou hast said; for with mine eyes have I seen the revelation of the Gentiles, and the salvation of Israel.[^126]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Receive into thy hands, he said, O Symeon, the Creator of all things; O elder, take up Christ, Whom the Virgin Maiden bare without seed unto the joy of our race.

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master, according to Thy word, in peace.*

Come together, O ye peoples, let us hymn the Creator and God of the Law, Whom the angelic hosts tremble before, the only Benefactor and Lawgiver of the Law, unto the salvation of our souls.

*Verse: A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*

Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, as Thou hast said; for I have seen Thee, O Christ, the Pre-eternal Light; loose me from the bonds of flesh, unto the revelation of the Gentiles and of Israel.

*Glory... Both now..., Tone 2:*
The holy Virgin brought the Holy One into the sanctuary unto the priest; and stretching forth his hands, Symeon received Him with joy, and cried aloud: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master, according to Thy word, in peace, O Lord.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon of the Feast, Ode 4; and of the Saint, Ode 6.*
*Prokeimenon of the Feast, and of the Saint, Tone 7: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.*[^127]
*Verse: Hear my voice, O God, when I pray unto Thee.*
*Epistle to the Hebrews, section 321, from the half.*[^128]
*Alleluia, Tone 6: Light hath shined forth for the righteous, and gladness for the upright in heart.*[^129]
*Gospel of Luke, section 8.*[^130]
*Communion Hymn of the Feast, and of the Saint: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be.*[^131]

*On the Same Month, on the 4th Day: Our Venerable Father Isidore of Pelusium.*[^132]

*If there is no fast, the service of the feast is celebrated together with that of Saint Isidore. But if it is a fast, only that of the Saint:*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Saint, 3.*
*Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Fulfilling the Law written in Scripture, the Lover of mankind is now brought into the temple, and the elder Symeon receives Him with aged hands: Now lettest Thou me depart, he cried aloud, unto that blessedness; for I have seen Thee today clothed with mortal flesh, Who rulest over life and holdest dominion over death.

A Light unto the revelation of the Gentiles hast Thou appeared, O Lord, seated upon a light cloud, O Sun of righteousness, fulfilling the shadow of the Law and manifesting the beginning of the new grace; therefore, seeing Thee, Symeon cried aloud: Loose me from corruption, for I have seen Thee today.

Without departing from the Father's bosom in Thy Godhead, incarnate as Thou wast well-pleased, held in the embrace of the Ever-Virgin, Thou wast surrendered into the hands of Symeon the God-Receiver, Who holdest all things in Thy hand; therefore, Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, he joyfully cried aloud, for I have seen Thee, O Master.

*Other Stichera of the Saint, the same tone [Tone 4], Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Having ascended unto God by contemplation and deeds, O all-wise one, thou didst accomplish thy life, having dared to make action the stepping-stone to contemplation; and thou didst love the ultimate Wisdom of things desired, and having entered into that desire, thou hast now been counted worthy of a blessed end and of the thrice-radiant brightness.

With the flood of grace and the downpouring of thy words, O divinely-wise one, thou dost water all, having placed thy mouth to the cup of higher wisdom, as from a fountain thou didst richly draw; and thou didst distribute everywhere the rays of teachings, sending forth and teaching and instructing, O wondrously praised one.

By temperance thou didst put to death the carnal mind, having clothed thyself in life-bearing mortification, and having enlarged the disposition of thy soul, O venerable one thou didst make it a vessel receptive of the gifts of the Spirit, and becamest a nourisher of divinely-inspired teachings, and an abode of wisdom surpassing understanding.

*Glory... Both now..., of the Feast, Tone 6. By Germanus:*[^133]
Today Symeon takes up into his hands the Lord of glory, Whom Moses of old saw under darkness, when He gave him the tablets upon Mount Sinai.[^134] This is He Who spake in the Prophets, and the Maker of the Law. This is He Whom David proclaims: Who is terrible unto all, having great and rich mercy.[^135]

*If it is a fast, Glory... Both now..., Theotokion. But if it is Wednesday or Friday, Stavrotheotokion: written on the 2nd day of this month.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Receive, O Symeon, the Lord of glory, as it was revealed unto Thee by the Holy Spirit: for behold, He is come.

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master, according to Thy word, in peace.*

Bearing the Maker and Master as an infant in her arms, the pure and all-undefiled Virgin enters into the church.

*Verse: A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*

A great and terrible mystery, and a strange dispensation: He Who embraces all things and fashions infants is carried as an infant in arms.

*Glory... Both now..., the same tone [Tone 2], Idiomelon:*
The holy Virgin brought the Holy One into the sanctuary unto the priest; and stretching forth his hands, Symeon received Him with joy, and cried aloud: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master, according to Thy word, in peace, O Lord.

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 8:*
In thee, O father, was preserved with certainty that which is according to the image; for taking up the Cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thy deeds didst teach us to overlook the flesh, for it passes away, but to attend unto the soul, a thing immortal; therefore, O venerable Isidore, thy spirit rejoices together with the angels.[^136]

*Glory... Both now..., of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, full of grace; for from thee hath shone forth the Sun of righteousness, Christ our God, Who enlightens those in darkness. Rejoice thou also, O righteous elder, who received in thine arms the Deliverer of our souls, Who grants us resurrection.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory..., of the Saint; Both now..., of the Feast.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*

*Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гглскїй):*
Being with the Father on the holy throne, having come upon earth He was born of the Virgin, and became an infant, Who is uncircumscribed in years; Whom Symeon, having received into his hands, rejoicing said: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Compassionate One, having gladdened him.

*Glory... Both now..., the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Him Who in the flesh became an infant of an unwedded Mother, thou didst embrace with thine aged hands, O Symeon, Christ our God; thou didst receive gloriously the answer of thy departure, and didst obtain the unceasing grace of wonders; therefore do we all worthily glorify thee.

*Glory... Both now..., the same.*

*Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos to 8; and of the Saint to 4.*
*Canon of the Feast, Tone 3.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The sun once traversed the dry land of the deep-bearing earth; for the water on both sides hardened like a wall for the people walking dryshod through the sea, and singing pleasingly unto God: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified![^137]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Let the clouds drop down water, for the Sun, borne upon a light cloud, hath arrived upon the incorruptible arm, Christ in the church as an infant. Therefore let us, the faithful, cry aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

Be ye strengthened, ye hands of Symeon weakened by old age; and the wearied legs of the elder, move ye swiftly straightway unto the Meeting of Christ, forming a choir with the bodiless hosts: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

Ye heavens outspread with understanding, be glad, and rejoice, O earth! For coming forth from the most divine bosom, Christ the Craftsman is offered by the Virgin Mother unto God the Father, an infant Who is before all things: for He is gloriously glorified!

*Canon of the Saint.*
*Whose acrostic is: I hymn thee, the glory of monastics, O father. Tone 6.*[^138]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having walked on foot dryshod through the deep, Israel, seeing the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory![^139]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Having heard the groaning of a contrite heart, O Master, heal the grievous contrition of my soul by the prayers of Thy venerable one, O Christ, for Thou art almighty.

Having departed into active virtue, O God-bearing Isidore, through pure contemplation and beautiful revelation conversing always with God thereby wast thou enriched.

Having brought thy mouth to the cup of wisdom, O all-wise one, thou didst draw from thence the fountain of gifts, singing a song of victory unto God.

*Theotokion:*
As upon a light cloud, the Creator came upon thee, O pure Sovereign Lady, that as the Mighty One He might cast down the handmade idols of Egypt, Christ being all-powerful.[^140]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Confirmation of those that hope in Thee, confirm, O Lord, the Church which Thou hast purchased with Thy precious Blood.[^141]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Firstborn of the Father before the ages, the firstborn infant from the incorrupt Virgin hath appeared, stretching forth His hand unto Adam.

God the Word, correcting the first-formed man who through deception had become childish of mind, hath appeared, having become an infant.

The offspring of earth that had run back into it, the Creator showed forth as a nature conformed to God, having become an infant without change.

*Another Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.[^142]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
By the sharpness of thy mind, O wise one, having gathered the nature of existing things, O most glorious one, through which as a pure theologian thou hast guilelessly attained unto the primal Cause.

Having made the remembrance of death the constant study of thy life, O thrice-blessed God-bearer, thou didst put to death the material scourges of the passions, being robed in dispassion through desire for God.

Rejoicing, thou didst follow after Christ, bearing thy cross, O father, in steadfast fasting, and wast conformed unto Him as much as is possible by the purity of thy life.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth to the Master outside the laws of nature, O Virgin Mother of God, thou didst destroy the ancient curse of disobedience, pouring forth the Fountain of blessing.

*Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Finding thee as another morning star, O most glorious one, the Church, illumined by the lightnings of thy words, cries aloud unto thee: Rejoice, O all-blessed and divinely-wise Isidore!

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*
A book of understanding composed with the riches of the faith of teachings, thou wast shown forth as a throne unto the Spirit, revealing divine things unto those in need, and treasuring up life for those who desire it: O venerable father, entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now..., of the Feast.*
*Sessional Hymn of the Feast, Tone 8, Podoben: "Having risen" (Воскре́съ):*
Thou Who art co-unoriginate with the Father wast born upon earth, the Incomprehensible One wast offered in the church; and rejoicing, the elder took Thee into his hands, crying: Now lettest Thou depart him whom Thou hast visited according to Thy word, O Thou Who wast well-pleased to save the human race as God.

*If it is a fast, Glory... Both now..., Theotokion. But if it is Wednesday or Friday, Stavrotheotokion: written on the 3rd day of this month.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thy virtue, O Christ, hath covered the heavens; for having come forth from the ark of Thy sanctification, of Thine incorrupt Mother, Thou didst appear in the temple of Thy glory as an infant borne in arms, and all things were filled with Thy praise.[^143]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Rejoice, O Symeon, initiate of ineffable mysteries, cried the Birthgiver of God; receive in thine arms Christ, the Word Who hath become an infant, concerning Whom the Holy Spirit revealed to thee of old, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise.

In Whom thou hadst hoped, O Symeon, a youthful child, take up Christ with joy, the divine consolation of Israel, the Creator and Master of the Law, Who fulfills the order of the Law, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise.

Seeing the unoriginate Word borne in the flesh by the Virgin as upon a cherubic throne, the Cause of all existence, Symeon marveled at Him as an infant, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise.

*Another Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church divinely sings, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.[^144]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Without grudging, O venerable one, submitting to Christ, as a well-obedient servant thou didst distribute the wealth given unto thee, and the Master counted thee worthy of ineffable joy.

Having sailed across the abyss of wisdom, as a good merchant thou didst acquire the good pearl, and this alone didst thou treasure up as inexhaustible riches.[^145]

Shined upon by the Divine Spirit, thou didst send forth the rays of thy words like the sun unto all, O divinely-manifest one, desiring salvation for the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
Truly ineffable and incomprehensible, O Birthgiver of God, are the mysteries of thy divinely-fitting childbearing unto those on earth and in heaven, O Ever-Virgin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
When Isaiah saw God figuratively exalted upon a throne, escorted by angels of glory, he cried out: Woe is me, wretched man! For I have foreseen God incarnate, the Unwaning Light, Who rules in peace.[^146]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Understanding the glory shown of old to the prophet, when the divine elder saw the Word held by motherly hands, rejoicing with her he cried aloud: Pure one, as a throne thou holdest God, the Unwaning Light, Who rules in peace.

The elder bowed down, and having divinely touched the footsteps of the unwedded Mother of God, he said: Pure one, thou bearest Fire; I fear to embrace an infant Who is God, the Unwaning Light, Who rules in peace.

Isaiah was cleansed, receiving a coal from the seraphim, the elder cried to the Mother of God: Thou, as with tongs, dost enlighten me with thy hands, giving Him Whom thou bearest, the Unwaning Light, Who rules in peace.

*Another Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, enlighten with love the souls of those who awake early unto Thee, I pray Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, calling them from the darkness of sin.[^147]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
By the mortification of the passions, O all-wise one, having come to be outside the flesh, by love thou didst draw near unto God, the super-radiant Light and throne, Who alone possesses purity.

Enlightened in mind by Thy divine radiance, O Good One, the God-bearer became an all-radiant pillar, sending forth unto all the divine beams of divine light, and driving away the darkness of ignorance.

He Who desires that all men should be saved, O all-wise one, because He is good, showed thee forth as a guide to the wandering, to turn many unto the path of salvation, O thrice-blessed Isidore, all-rich father.[^148]

*Theotokion:*
Truly with mouth and soul, O good Sovereign Lady of the world, I confess thee, O all-immaculate and pure one, who bare the incarnate God; and in faith I set thee forth as the mediatress of my life.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Seeing with his eyes the salvation that hath come from God unto the peoples, the elder cried aloud unto Thee: O Christ, Thou art my God![^149]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Thou hast been set as a stone for Zion, a stone of stumbling and an offense to the disobedient, but the unshakable salvation of the faithful.[^150]

Manifestly bearing the express image of Him Who begat Thee before the ages, out of compassion Thou hast now clothed Thyself with the weakness of earthly men Unto the Son of the Most High, the Son of the Virgin, unto God Who became a child, having bowed down before Thee, [the elder cried:] Now dismiss me in peace.

*Another Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing to Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most-Merciful One![^151]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Adorned with discerning words like a priest, thou didst spiritually judge between soul and body, O God-bearer, and didst wisely subject the worse unto the better, O wondrously praised one.

Powerfully wounding Egypt with passionate pleasures, O boast of monastics, through the wounds of temperance, O glorious one, examining, thou didst afflict it, smoothing the passage for the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
He Who carries all things by divine power and upholds them as compassionate, being incarnate in thine embrace, O all-undefiled one, is carried as an infant, Who is co-everlasting in essence with the Father.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Thou Who didst sanctify the virginal womb by Thy birth, and didst bless the hands of Symeon as was meet, having anticipated now also hast Thou saved us, O Christ God; but make peaceful our commonwealth in wars, and strengthen the King whom Thou hast loved,[^152] O only Lover of mankind.

*Ikos:*
Let us run to the Birthgiver of God, wishing to see her Son carried to Symeon; Whom seeing from heaven, the bodiless hosts were amazed, saying: Marvelous things we see now and most glorious, incomprehensible and ineffable! For He Who created Adam is carried as an infant; the Uncontainable is contained in the arms of the elder; He Who is in the uncircumscribed bosom of His Father is willingly circumscribed in the flesh, and not in the Godhead, the only Lover of mankind.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thee, Who didst bedew the Children theologizing in the fire, and didst dwell in the incorrupt Virgin, God the Word, we piously hymn, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^153]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Desiring to make known unto Adam dwelling in hades, and to bring the good tidings unto Eve, Symeon cried aloud, keeping festival with the prophets: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

God, delivering the earthly race, shall come even unto hades; and unto all the captives He shall grant remission, and sight unto the blind, so that even the mute shall cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^154]

A sword shall pierce through thine own heart also, O incorrupt one, Symeon proclaimed unto the Birthgiver of God when thou seest thy Son upon the Cross, unto Whom we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^155]

*Another Heirmos:*
The angel made the furnace a source of dew for the venerable Children, but the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^156]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Upon the firmament of the faith, O father, the Master set thee as the sun, to enlighten with spiritual radiance the souls of those who cry fervently: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Thou didst wash away carnal and spiritual defilements, O blessed one, with outpourings of tears; and having become a light unto those on earth, O God-bearer, thou didst teach all to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Standing upon the unshakable foundation of the faith, we confess thee as the Mother of God, O pure Birthgiver of God; for thou didst bear for us without seed God the Word: Blessed, O all-undefiled one, is the Fruit of thy womb![^157]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Standing together for piety in unendurable fire, yet unhurt by the flame, the youths sang a divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^158]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
O people of Israel, beholding Thine Emmanuel, the Child born of the Virgin, dance now before the face of the divine ark: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

Behold, Symeon cried aloud, this Child, being God, shall be a sign spoken against! Unto Him let us, the faithful, cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^159]

Being Life, this God the Word, having become an infant, shall be the fall of the disobedient, and the resurrection of all who sing in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos:*
Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew upon the venerable ones, and by water didst burn the sacrifice of the righteous man; for Thou dost accomplish all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee do we exalt above all unto all ages![^160]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
As a spiritual sacrifice, O father, thou didst offer all thy life for a sweet-smelling savor unto Christ Who was hanged upon the Cross for thy sake; Whom we exalt above all unto all ages.

Thou multipliest wealth even after death, having thy living words as a costly heritage left unto all the faithful, who exalt Christ above all unto all ages.

Thou wast adorned, O most blessed one, with the beauty of thy words; for thou didst appear as a pen of the Holy Spirit,[^161] writing piously divine understanding for those who exalt the Lord above all unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
Unto thy protection do I flee, O all-immaculate one, and thee do I now set forth as the protectress of my life, who beyond understanding didst bear God the Word: Whom we exalt above all unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
In the shadow of the law and the scriptures, we the faithful behold an image: Every male child that opens the womb is holy unto God.[^162] Therefore the firstborn Word of the unoriginate Father, the Son Who is the firstborn of an unwedded Mother, do we magnify.

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Of old a pair of newly born turtledoves was offered, and two young pigeons;[^163] in their stead, the divine elder and the chaste prophetess Anna minister, magnifying the Son, born of the Virgin and only-begotten of the Father, as He is brought into the temple.

Thou hast given back unto me, cried Symeon, the joy of Thy salvation, O Christ;[^164] receive Thy servant, who is worn out with the shadow, the secret sacred preacher of the new grace, magnifying Thee in praise.

Anna the prophetess made confession in holy manner, chaste and venerable, and an aged woman of good repute, fitly in the temple; and preaching the Birthgiver of God unto all present, she magnified her.[^165]

*Another Heirmos:*
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate became visible unto mortals: Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.[^166]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Like a ray of light, the word of grace shone into thy soul, O venerable one, and through thee the whole earth was filled with its radiance; partaking of which, O father, we marvel at the treasury of thy wisdom.

Following the teachings of the fathers, thou didst teach us with godly wisdom to worship three Persons in the unity of nature, and to honor, while theologizing, the uncreated and unoriginate Trinity, Who pours forth enlightenment upon all the faithful.

Having found in the heavens the recompense of thy labors, and received life without end: entreat salvation, O all-wise and blessed Isidore, for those who sing of thy memory, as thou standest now with the angels before the throne of God.

*Theotokion:*
Taking a corruptible image, the incorruptible Word of God clothed men in incorruption,[^167] having dwelt within thee, O favored one, by the goodwill of the Father; therefore, O all-pure one, together with the heavenly hosts we magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion of the Feast:*
Having appeared in the sanctuary by the Spirit, the elder received into his hands the Master of the Law, crying: Now release me from the bonds of the flesh in peace, as Thou hast said; for mine eyes have seen the revelation of the Gentiles, and the salvation of Israel.[^168]

*Glory..., both now...: the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 2. Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Without defilement * Christ is born * of the Virgin, * as incorruptibly of the Father, * the Son before the morning star,[^169] * Who delivers Adam.

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master, according to Thy word, in peace.*[^170]

Open, * ye gates of heaven; * for Christ, * as an infant, * is brought into the temple * by the Virgin Mother * unto God and the Father.

*Verse: A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*[^171]

The Coal that was revealed * to the divine Isaiah,[^172] * Christ, as by tongs, * through the hands of the Birthgiver of God * is now given unto the elder.

*Glory..., both now..., Tone 7:*
A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, Thou hast come down upon earth from heaven, O our Savior; and proceeding from the Virgin, Thou hast rested in the embrace of righteous Symeon: for it was fitting for Thee, the Life-giver of all, to be recognized by the elder, that Thou camest to release him according to Thy word, as Thou hast great mercy.

*On the Fifth Day of the Same Month: The Holy Martyr Agatha.*[^173]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Martyr, 3. Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1. Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Today we celebrate the saving grace * of His ineffable revelation: * for Christ God, having unchangeably become an infant of the Virgin Maiden, * is now brought into the temple unto God and the Father * by His Mother; * Whom Symeon, having received, embraced in his arms.

In the prophets Thou wast seen of old, O Jesus, * as far as it was possible to see Thee; * but now, O Word of God, * Thou hast willingly appeared in the flesh unto all the world * from the Virgin Mary, O Christ: * Thou hast manifested Thy salvation unto all that are of Adam, * as the Lover of mankind.

Having ordained the Law on Mount Sinai of old,[^174] * now fulfilling the requirements of the Law in the city of Zion, * as an infant, O Christ, with whole burnt offerings, * through the hands of the Birthgiver of God, * Thou didst will to be carried in the flesh into the temple * in the arms of Symeon.

*Other Stichera, of the Saint, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Incorruptibly didst thou preserve thy body * for Christ thy Bridegroom, * adorned with the beauties of virginity, * O Agatha, bride of God, * and enlightened with the splendors of martyrdom; * and thou didst enter into the divine bridal chamber: * therefore we celebrate thy worldwide solemnity, * glorifying the Savior * Who ever glorifies Thee.

The severance of thy breasts, * and the burning with fire, * and the scraping of thy body didst thou endure, O glorious one; * beholding with the eyes of thy heart the eternal recompense, * and supreme blessedness, * and the unfading crown,[^175] * which Christ hath now bestowed upon thee, * for Whose sake thou didst suffer, * O much-suffering passion-bearer.

The uncontrollable surging * of the fierce fire didst thou check * by thy prayers, O namesake of goodness,[^176] * and didst save the city * that honors thy precious relics, O martyr; * from which rivers of healings abundantly flow * by the Holy Spirit: * for having suffered in this, thou didst cast down the enemy, * and didst receive the crown of victory, O all-praised one.

*If there is a fast: Glory..., both now...: Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, written on the 28th day. If there is no fast, we sing the Idiomelon of the Saint.*

*Glory..., Tone 8: By Symeon:*
A most marvelous wonder took place in the suffering of all-praised Agatha, the martyr of Christ God, like unto Moses: for while giving the Law unto the people, he received upon the mountain the God-inscribed letters written upon tablets;[^177] but here an angel from heaven brought a tablet inscribed unto her tomb: A holy, voluntary mind, honor from God, and deliverance unto her homeland.[^178]

*Both now..., of the Feast, the same Tone: By Andrew of Crete:*
He Who is carried upon the Cherubim, * and hymned by the Seraphim, * today being brought into the divine sanctuary according to the Law, sits upon the elder's hands as upon a throne; * and from Joseph He receives gifts in godly manner, as a pair of turtledoves of turtledoves, the undefiled church and newly chosen people from the Gentiles; and two young pigeons,[^179] as the Author of both the Old and New Testaments. Receiving the fulfillment of the promise made unto him, Symeon blessed Mary the Virgin Birthgiver of God, and foretold the images of the Passion that should proceed from her; and asking of Him release, he cried: Now lettest Thou me depart, O Master, as Thou didst announce unto me before; for I have seen Thee, the Light before the ages, and the Lord and Savior of the people that bear the name of Christ.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6. Podoben: "On the Third Day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Receive in thine arms, said Symeon, * the Creator of all; * O elder, take up Christ, * Whom the Virgin Maiden bore * without seed, unto the joy of our race.

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master, according to Thy word, in peace.*[^180]

Having assembled, O ye people, * let us sing unto the Creator of the Law and God, * before Whom the angelic multitudes tremble: * the only Benefactor * and Lawgiver of the Law, * unto the salvation of our souls.

*Verse: A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*[^181]

Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, * as Thou hast said; * for I have seen Thee, O Christ, the Light before the ages, * [releasing] me from the bonds of the flesh, unto the revelation of the Gentiles and Israel.

*Glory..., both now..., Tone 2: By Andrew Pyrrhus:*[^182]
He Whom the ministers on high entreat with trembling, Symeon now receiving below in material arms, preached that God is united unto men; and seeing heavenly man and God, filled with awe, he cried with joy unto those on earth: O Thou Who revealest the unwaning Light unto those sitting in darkness,[^183] Lord, glory to Thee!

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 4:*
Thy lamb Agatha, O Jesus, cries with a loud voice: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee I endure suffering; and I am crucified with Thee and buried with Thee in Thy baptism;[^184] and I suffer for Thy sake, that I may also reign in Thee; and I die for Thee, that I may also live with Thee: but as an immaculate sacrifice accept me, who have offered myself unto Thee with love. Through her intercessions, as Thou art merciful, save our souls.

*Glory..., both now..., of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, full of grace, for from thee hath shone forth the Sun of righteousness, Christ our God,[^185] enlightening those in darkness; be glad also, O righteous elder, who didst receive in thine arms the Deliverer of our souls, Who grants unto us resurrection.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Automelon (Самоподо́бенъ):*
Let the angelic choir be astonished at the wonder, and let us mortals cry aloud a hymn with voices, beholding the ineffable condescension of God: for He before Whom the heavenly hosts tremble, the elder's hands now embrace, the only Lover of mankind.

*Glory..., both now...: the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Overcome by the Pastoral Pipes" (Свирѣ́лей па́стырскихъ):*
Thou wast born upon earth, Who art co-unoriginate with the Father; Thou wast brought into the temple, Who art incomprehensible; and rejoicing, the elder received Thee into his arms, crying: Now lettest Thou depart him whom Thou hast visited according to Thy word, O Thou Who hast been pleased to save, as God, the human race.

*Glory..., both now...: the same.*

*Canon of the Feast, with 8 troparia; and of the Saint, with 4.*

*Canon of the Feast, Tone 3. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The sun of old traversed the dry land that had brought forth the deep; for the water grew firm on both sides like a wall,[^186] while the people traversed the sea on foot and sang in a manner pleasing unto God: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Let the clouds drop down water, for the Sun, borne upon a light cloud, hath come:[^187] upon incorrupt arms, Christ as an infant into the temple. Therefore let us, the faithful, cry aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

Be strengthened, ye hands of Symeon that are weakened by old age, and ye tired legs of the elder, move swiftly and straightway to meet Christ, forming a choir with the bodiless hosts: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

Rejoice, ye heavens stretched out with understanding, and be glad, O earth:[^188] for from the super-divine bosom Christ the Artificer hath come forth, and as an infant Who is before all things He is brought by the Virgin Mother unto God the Father: for He is gloriously glorified!

*Canon of the Saint.*
*Whose Acrostic is: With good hymns I will sing of the good.*
*By Theophanes, Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
