Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt; for He is glorified![^189]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Having desired the primal Cause of all good, O martyr, thou wast accounted worthy of the namesake title;[^190] for ineffable Providence foreknew the goodness of thy disposition.

Thou didst soar aloft, O pure one, unto the most radiant life, scorning the wisdom of the flesh,[^191] and steadfastly looking toward thy most desired Bridegroom.

As a bride of Christ, Thou wast adorned with divine goodness and with the beauties of virginity, and with the divine blood of honorable martyrdom, crowned with double crowns, O glorious one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast a dwelling fit for God, O pure one, more spacious than the heavens and most pure, O Virgin Birthgiver of God; and now the choir of virgins rejoices in thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, the Confirmation of those who hope in Thee, confirm the Church which Thou hast purchased with Thy precious Blood![^192]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Firstborn of the Father before the ages, the Firstborn Infant from the incorrupt Virgin hath appeared, stretching out His hand unto Adam.[^193]

God the Word, Who restores the first-created man who had become foolish through deception,[^194] hath appeared, having become an infant.

The Creator, having unchangeably manifested Himself as an infant, hath made the offspring of the earth that had returned unto it[^195] into a nature conformed unto the Godhead.

*Another Heirmos:*
Confirm us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death; and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who praise Thee![^196]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
O Agatha, radiant and most beautiful flower and glory of divine martyrs: heal the affliction of my soul and adorn it, O martyr!

By noble virtues thou didst make thyself clear, O Agatha, and wast proclaimed a martyr, disregarding temporary things and loving the kingdom of God.

Christ didst thou prefer above all fair things, O Agatha, drawn by divine desire; and the savage rages of the tormentors didst thou trample underfoot with good courage and manliness.

*Theotokion:*
O pure Bride of God, pray unto God Whom thou didst bear, to deliver those who praise thee from temptations, tribulations, passions, and distresses, O all-blessed one.

*Kontakion, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast Appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*
Let the Church be adorned today with a glorious purple robe, dipped in the pure blood of Agatha the martyr, crying aloud: Rejoice, O boast of Catania!

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 3. Podoben: "Of the Divine Faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Adorned with the glory of faith and piety, with singing, virginity, and the fervor of suffering, thou wast named a holy, voluntary mind, truly honor unto God, and deliverance unto Thy homeland; praying as a bride unto Christ to grant us great mercy.

*Glory..., both now..., of the Feast, the same Tone:*

...The sacred Symeon rejoicingly took Thee into his hands, incarnate of the Virgin, O Christ, crying: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master![^197] And the blameless and glorious prophetess Anna now offers thanksgiving and singing unto Thee.[^198] And we, O Giver of life, cry unto Thee: Glory to Thee Who wast thus well pleased!

*If there is a fast: Glory..., both now...: Theotokion. If Wednesday or Friday: Stavrotheotokion, written on the 18th day of this month.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thy virtue hath covered the heavens, O Christ; for coming forth from the ark of Thy sanctification, from Thine incorrupt Mother, Thou didst appear in the temple of Thy glory as an infant carried in arms: and all things were filled with Thy praise![^199]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Rejoice, O Symeon, initiate of ineffable mysteries, cried the Birthgiver of God: Receive into thine arms Christ the Word, Who hath become an infant, as was revealed unto thee of old by the Holy Spirit,[^200] crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

Take up Christ rejoicingly in the age of childhood, O Symeon, upon Whom thou didst hope: the divine Consolation of Israel,[^201] the Creator and Master of the Law, Who fulfills the ordinance of the Law, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

Seeing the unoriginate Word carried in the flesh by the Virgin as upon a cherubic throne, the Cause of the existence of all things, Symeon marveled at Him as an infant and cried unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Another Heirmos:*
I sing to Thee, for I have heard Thy report, O Lord, and was afraid; for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who had gone astray:[^202] therefore I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O greatly merciful One![^203]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
The folly of the deceptive serpent was put to shame and boasting ceased; for maidens now trample underfoot his fury, since Christ hath shone forth upon the earth from the Virgin and illumined all.

Having lawfully and chastely maintained unstained purity, O passion-bearer, rejoicing thou didst cast down the spirits of wickedness,[^204] having armed thyself with the grace of Christ, and having taken His Cross upon thy shoulders.

Be a protectress unto me, O all-wise one, delivering me from cruel things, and the storm of temptations, and afflictions, and deliver me from the violence of the enemy; that being delivered I may hymn thee as a good protectress, O namesake of goodness.

*Theotokion:*
Immeasurably didst thou surpass the angelic splendors, O all-pure one, having ineffably given birth to Christ, the intelligible Sun, Who illumines the minds of the faithful with the rays of the Godhead.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
When Isaiah saw God typically exalted upon a throne, escorted by angels of glory, he cried: Woe is me, wretched man![^205] For I have foreseen God becoming incarnate, the unwaning Light, Who rules over the world in peace.[^206]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Understanding the glory revealed of old unto the prophet, when the divine elder saw the Word held by motherly hands, he cried: Rejoice, O pure one, for as a throne thou holdest God, the unwaning Light, Who rules over the world in peace!

The elder bowed down and divinely touched the footsteps of the unwedded Mother of God, and said: Thou bearest fire, O pure one; I fear to embrace an infant Who is God, the unwaning Light, Who rules over the world in peace!

Isaiah was cleansed when he received the coal from the seraph;[^207] and the elder cried unto the Mother of God: Thou, as with tongs, dost enlighten me with thy hands, handing over Him Whom thou bearest, the unwaning Light, Who rules over the world in peace!

*Another Heirmos:*
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, Lord: guide us in the light of Thy commandments,[^208] for we know no other God beside Thee![^209]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Guided by divine commandments, O glorious one, typically thou didst loose the bonds of carnal passion, appearing as a fruitful olive tree, O Agatha.[^210]

By this love of Christ, beholding most radiant splendors, thou didst despise pleasures that drag downward unto the earth, desiring only that single Beauty.

That thou mightest be accounted worthy, O glorious one, to see the ineffable life of the elect, thou didst endure torments steadfastly, rejoicing, O all-praised Agatha.

*Theotokion:*
Unto thee who didst bear Christ the Creator of all, we cry: Rejoice, O pure one! Rejoice, thou who hast caused the Light to shine upon us! Rejoice, thou who didst contain the uncontainable God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The elder cried unto Thee when he saw with his eyes the salvation that came from God unto the people: Christ, Thou art my God![^211]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
Thou wast laid as a Stone for Zion, a stumbling stone and a rock of offense for the disobedient,[^212] but the indestructible salvation of the faithful.

Bearing manifestly the express Image of Him Who begot Thee before the ages,[^213] through tender mercy Thou hast now clothed Thyself with the weakness of mortals.

...Unto the Son of the Most High, the Son of the Virgin, God Who hath become an infant: release now in peace him who has bowed down before Thee.[^214]

*Another Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I invoke the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: from corruption, O God, raise me up![^215]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
The tormentor, being carnal-minded, hoped to rob thee of the beauty of virginity; but he was put to shame as shameless, having dashed himself against thy steadfast mind.

Grace was poured forth upon thy lips, O namesake of goodness and martyr;[^216] for clearly didst thou refute the shameless lion, being enriched with a God-taught word.

Thou didst appear as an all-honorable stone, O all-praised one, having a holy, voluntary mind, and becoming a firm deliverance unto thy homeland, O precious Agatha.

*Theotokion:*
Praising thee in psalmody, we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O rich mountain of Christ God,[^217] in which He was pleased to dwell for our salvation, O Bride of God!

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Thou Who didst sanctify the virginal womb by Thy birth, and didst bless the hands of Symeon as was meet, having anticipated now also hast saved us, O Christ God; but give peace unto the commonwealth in wars, and strengthen the King whom Thou hast loved,[^218] O only Lover of mankind.

*Ikos:*
Let us run unto the Birthgiver of God, wishing to see her Son borne unto Symeon; Whom seeing from heaven, the bodiless hosts were amazed, saying: We see now marvelous and most glorious things, incomprehensible and ineffable! For He Who created Adam is carried as an infant; the Uncontainable is contained in the hands of an elder; He Who is in the uncircumscribed bosom of His Father is willingly circumscribed in the flesh, not in the Godhead: the only Lover of mankind.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Let us hymn God the Word, Who in the fire bedewed the youths who theologized,[^219] and dwelt in the incorrupt Virgin, singing piously: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^220]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
I go to announce unto Adam living in hades, and to bring good tidings unto Eve, cried Symeon, dancing with the prophets: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

God, Who delivers the earthly race, shall come even down unto hades; and unto all captives He shall grant release, and recovery of sight unto the blind,[^221] so that even the mute shall cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

...And a sword shall pierce through thine own heart, O incorrupt one,[^222] Symeon proclaimed unto the Birthgiver of God, when thou seest thy Son upon the Cross, unto Whom we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Another Heirmos:*
When the golden image was worshipped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the godless decree;[^223] and cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^224]

*Troparia of the Saint:*
Speaking very radiantly, thou didst openly terrify the tormentor with words of wisdom and grace; and enduring the coals of fire painlessly through patience under torments, thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Held by savage frenzy, the evil-hearted one was terribly perplexed, seeing thy youth and the blossoming of thy breasts; for rejoicing, O martyr, thou didst cry aloud, calling unto thy Bridegroom: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Christ's disciple [Peter] stood radiantly before thee in the prison, healing thy grievous wounds which thou didst endure, O invincible martyr and maiden of the Savior,[^225] as thou didst rejoice and say: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Adam was put to death when he transgressed the commandment of the Almighty;[^226] but thou, O all-immaculate Virgin, having given birth to eternal Life, didst deliver him from death, as he cries unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Standing together for piety in unendurable fire, yet unhurt by the flame, the youths sang a divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^227]

*Troparia of the Feast:*
O people of Israel, beholding Thine Emmanuel, the Child born of the Virgin, dance now before the face of the divine ark: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

Behold, Symeon cried aloud, this Child, being God and an infant, shall be a sign spoken against![^228] Unto Him let us, the faithful, cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

Being Life, this God the Word, having become an infant, shall be the fall of the disobedient, and the resurrection of all who sing in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos:*

Unto God, Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew: sing, ye works, unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

Having been accounted worthy to dwell in transcendent joy and gladness, pray thou unto the Lord, O Agatha, that those who honor thee may be illuminated by thy ineffable glory and enjoy incorruptible life.

Having loved Christ above word with mind, and heart, and soul, thou wast winged unto Him, crying aloud: I run after Thee, and I shall dwell with Thee unto the ages.

Adorned with supernatural fringes and beautiful apparel as the King's daughter,[^229] thou standest in honor before Christ, O Agatha, crying aloud: We supremely exalt Thee, the Lord, unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
Bearing Christ in thy womb, the Myrrh poured out for us, O Birthgiver of God, thou hast made the world fragrant with divinely inspired scents. Therefore we cry aloud unto thee, O Virgin: Rejoice, receptacle of the myrrh-fragrant Myrrh!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
In the law, the shadow, and the scriptures, we, the faithful, behold an image: every male that opens the womb is holy unto God.[^230] Therefore, the firstborn Word of the Father without beginning, the Son firstborn of a Mother who knew not man, we magnify.

*Troparion:*
In the place of the ancient pair of turtledoves and two young pigeons for the newborn,[^231] the divine Elder and the chaste Anna the Prophetess serve, magnifying Him Who was born of the Virgin, the only-begotten Son of the Father, brought into the temple.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast granted unto me, Symeon cried aloud, the joy of Thy salvation, O Christ; receive Thy servant who is weary with the shadow, the secret sacred preacher of the new grace, magnifying Thee in praise.

*Troparion:*
In a holy manner Anna prophesied and confessed, the chaste, venerable, and good aged woman, openly in the temple; and proclaiming the Birthgiver of God unto all present, she magnified Her.

*Another Heirmos:*
God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who had grievously fallen into corruption through eating, and Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin: with one accord, let us, the faithful, magnify Him with hymns.

*Troparion:*
Having been adorned with the crowns of victory, O divinely wise Agatha, by the Life-originating right hand pray now that we may be delivered from the storm, even as thou didst previously stop the rush of fire for thy fatherland, that we all may unceasingly magnify thee with hymns.

*Troparion:*
Providing for all things by divine providence, Christ, now rewarding thy valor, O martyr, settles thee in the heavenly mansions as His all-immaculate bride, and has accounted thee worthy of rejoicing with the Angels and the martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Make Christ, the Judge of all, merciful unto me, O honorable passion-bearer, by thy supplications, O all-glorious one, and cease not ever to beseech the Compassionate One to cleanse the multitude of my transgressions, O adornment of martyrs.

*Theotokion:*
Having become ineffably incarnate of thy pure blood, the Son, Who is co-unoriginate with the Father before all ages, appeared unto us as the sun, O most pure unwedded Mother, and having driven away the darkness, He enlightened all things.

*Exapostilarion of the Martyr, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жєны̀ оу҆слы́шите):*
Thou didst endure the cutting off of thy breasts, O good maiden, and all other torments for the sake of Christ, O passion-bearer; therefore, thy Bridegroom, O glorious one, has settled thee in the beautiful bridal chamber, rejoicing with the Angels, O virgin martyr Agatha.

*Glory..., both now... of the Feast:*
Having stood in the sanctuary in the Spirit, the Elder received the Master of the Law into his arms, crying aloud: Now release me from bodily bonds, as Thou hast said, in peace; for with mine eyes I have seen the revelation of the Gentiles, and the salvation of Israel.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
With fear and joy holding the Master in his arms, Symeon asked for release from life, hymning the Birthgiver of God.[^232]

*Stichos: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.*

Now release me unto true life, Symeon cried aloud, O my Savior; for I have seen Thee, the good Light of the world.

*Stichos: A light for revelation to the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*

Anna the chaste and venerable prophetically confessed Him Who sprouted from the Virgin for the deliverance of those on earth.

*Glory..., both now... Tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Rejoice, thou who didst receive joy for the world, Christ the Giver of Life, and didst put an end to the grief of our ancestral mother, O Virgin.

*On the same month, on the 6th day: Our Venerable Father Bucolus, Bishop of Smyrna.*[^233]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
The Creator of all things and our Deliverer is brought into the temple by the Virgin Mother; therefore, the Elder, having received Him, cried aloud with joy: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Good One, as Thou hast willed.

Having received onto his arms from the Virgin Him Who was born before all ages, Symeon cried aloud: I have seen the Savior, the illumination of Thy glory unto the ends of the earth; now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Good One, for I have seen Thee today.

Born in the latter times for the salvation of mankind, Symeon, bearing the Savior in his arms, cried aloud rejoicing: I have seen the Light of the Gentiles and the Glory of Israel; now lettest Thou depart, as Thou hast said as God, from those who are here by Thy command.

*Other Stichera of the Venerable One, Tone 2, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Shining with the light of God-working virtues, O most sacred one, thou hast been mystically set as a light upon the candlestick of the divine Church, illuminating it, O father, with thy sacred teachings.

The Holy Spirit, having holily sanctified thee, O Bucolus, through thee grants both illumination and deliverance unto those who approach with holy faith, O God-bearer.

Proclaiming the supreme goodness of our God, thou dost make blessed the afflicted people by grace, O wise one, bringing them unto the Savior as a most sacred priest.

*Glory..., both now... of the Feast, Tone 6:*
Search the Scriptures, even as Christ our God said in the Gospels;[^234] for in them we find Him being born, and wrapped in swaddling clothes, laid in a manger, and nourished with milk, receiving circumcision, and borne by Symeon, appearing unto the world not in semblance nor in phantom, but in truth. Unto Him let us cry aloud: O eternal God, glory to Thee!

*If it be a fast, Glory..., both now... of the same. But if it be Wednesday or Friday, Stavrotheotokion, written on the 1st day of this month.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*

The pure one, the all-immaculate Virgin, bearing the Creator and Master in her arms as an infant, enters into the temple.

*Stichos: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.*

Receive, O Symeon, the Lord of glory, even as Thou wast informed by the Holy Spirit: for behold, He is come.

*Stichos: A light for revelation to the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*

Now have mine eyes seen Thy promise, O good God: quickly release me, Thy servant, O Lover of mankind.

*Glory..., both now... Tone 5. By Germanus:*
The Ancient of Days,[^235] having become an infant in the flesh, is brought into the temple by the Virgin Mother, fulfilling the promise of His own Law; Whom Symeon, having received, said: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, according to Thy word, for mine eyes have seen Thy salvation, O Holy One.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A rule of faith and a model of meekness, a teacher of abstinence has the truth of things revealed thee unto thy flock.[^236] Therefore, thou hast acquired through humility what is sublime, and through poverty what is rich, O father Bucolus: entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Glory..., both now... of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, full of grace,[^237] for from thee has shone forth the Sun of Righteousness, Christ our God, Who enlightens those in darkness. Rejoice thou also, O righteous Elder, who didst receive in thine arms the Deliverer of our souls, Who grants us resurrection.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The stone having been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́тану):*
Thou hast been brought into the temple, O Life of all, having become an infant for my sake; Thou wast under the Law, Who of old didst inscribe the Law upon tablets on Mount Sinai, that Thou mightest deliver all from the ancient bondage of the Law. Glory to Thy compassion, O Savior! Glory to Thy kingdom! Glory to Thy dispensation, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!

*Glory..., both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезна́чалное сло́во):*
Thou hast been brought into the temple, fulfilling the custom, O Savior, Creator and Master and Giver of the Law; and by Thy good pleasure Thou, Who art fearsome unto all and dost uphold all creation, didst desire to be borne in the flesh in the arms of Symeon, O Thou Who alone art compassionate, to reveal Thy light unto all of us.

*Glory..., both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast with the Heirmoi to 8, and of the Saint to 4.*

*Canon of the Feast, Tone 3.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The sun once traversed the deep-producing dry land; for the water solidified like a wall on both sides for the people who walked on foot across the sea, and who sang in a manner well-pleasing unto God:[^238] Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Let the clouds drop water, for the Sun, borne upon a light cloud, has arrived: Christ upon incorruptible arms in the temple as an infant. Therefore, let us, the faithful, cry aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Symeon's hands, weakened by old age, are strengthened; and the weary aged legs of the Elder move straight and swift to meet Christ, joining the choir with the bodiless hosts: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Ye heavens spread out by wisdom, be glad, and rejoice, O earth! For Christ, the Artificer, having come forth from the transcendent divine bosom, is brought by the Virgin Mother unto God the Father, an infant Who is before the ages: For He is gloriously glorified!

*Canon of the Saint, the Acrostic whereof is:*
I shall hymn the glory of great Bucolus. Joseph.[^239] *Tone 6.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory!

*Troparion:*
Ever illuminated by divine splendors and sacred, light-bearing rays, illumine my soul, O father, as I honor thy radiant feast.

*Troparion:*
The noetic Sun, having shown thee forth as a radiant star, enlightened the earth with the splendors of thy virtues, O initiate of Christ, all-wise Bucolus.

*Troparion:*
Having ever shown forth a sanctified and pure life, admitting no defilement, thou wast known on earth as a sacred vessel of Christ, O wise and all-glorious Bucolus.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is the only-begotten Son of the Father, having ineffably become incarnate in thy womb by good pleasure, deified mankind, O pure one, being all-good in the multitude of His compassions.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establishment of those who hope in Thee: establish, O Lord, the Church which Thou hast acquired with Thy precious blood.

*Troparion:*
Firstborn of the Father before the ages, firstborn infant of the incorrupt Virgin, unto Adam unto Adam He appeared with outstretched hand.

*Troparion:*
God the Word, correcting again the first-formed who had become foolish through deception, appeared having become an infant.

*Troparion:*
Earth's offspring, having run back into it: the Creator, having unchangeably become an infant, revealed a nature conformed to the Godhead.

*Another Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.[^240]

*Troparion:*
Having become a son of the day and of light through divine labors, O hierarch, thou hast manifestly settled now in the splendors of the saints, shining with grace.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst piously preach the one essence of the Trinity, and didst uproot the deception of polytheism from the earth, as an honorable hierarch, as a true minister of the Word.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast shown the path of life unto all who were deceived by the enemy's assault; and having saved them, thou hast led them, as a spiritual shepherd, unto the Word, the Chief Shepherd.

*Theotokion:*
Be thou my haven and guardian, O pure one, who am ever relentlessly tossed by the tempest of the fierce swelling of passions in the sea of life, O only ever-virgin!

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Shining with the light of priesthood, thou didst enlighten the people, O hierarch, and didst destroy idol darkness; and having dispelled the mist of passions with the radiance of healings, thou hast passed unto the unsetting brightness, fervently praying for us who honor thee, O blessed Bucolus.

*Glory..., both now... of the Feast, the same Tone, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ їѡ́сифъ):*
Thou art become an infant for my sake, O Ancient of Days; Thou dost partake of purification, O most pure God, that Thou mightest assure me of the flesh which is from the Virgin; and instructed by these things, Symeon recognized Thee as God manifested in the flesh, and embraced Thee as life, and rejoicing in old age cried aloud: Release me, for I have seen Thee, the Life of all!

*If it be a fast, Glory..., both now... Theotokion. If Wednesday or Friday, Stavrotheotokion, written on the 17th day.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thy virtue has covered the heavens, O Christ,[^241] for having come forth from the Ark of Thy sanctification, Thine incorrupt Mother, Thou hast appeared in the temple of Thy glory as an infant carried in arms, and all things are filled with Thy praise.

*Troparion:*
Rejoice, O Symeon, initiate of ineffable mysteries, cried aloud the Birthgiver of God cried aloud: Receive onto thine arms Christ, the Word Who has become an infant, as was made known unto thee of old by the Holy Spirit, crying aloud unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Troparion:*
Having hoped in Him, O Symeon, lift up rejoicing Christ in infant stature, the divine consolation of Israel, the Creator and Master of the Law Who fulfills the ordinance of the Law, crying aloud unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Troparion:*
Beholding the Word without beginning, the Cause of the existence of all things, borne in the flesh by the Virgin as upon a cherubic throne, Symeon, marveling at Him as an infant, cried aloud unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Another Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church holily sings, crying aloud from a pure mind, feasting in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Foreknowing the beauty of thy soul and the purity of thy mind, O divinely blessed one, God sacredly ordains thee as a wise sacred preacher.

*Troparion:*
By the illumination of the Spirit, thou dost enlighten those who are in the darkness of deception; and through baptism thou makest them sons of the day, O all-glorious hierarch.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thee shining with grace, the glorious Theologian laid his hand upon thee by divine guidance,[^242] and consecrated thee a sacred priest, O all-wise Bucolus.

*Theotokion:*
Moses noetically beheld thee in the bush in sacred prefiguration, mystically revealing the Fire that was to be in thy womb, O Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin.[^243]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
When Isaiah symbolically beheld God exalted upon a throne, escorted by angels of glory, he cried aloud: Woe is me, wretched one![^244] For I have foreseen God becoming incarnate, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over peace.

*Troparion:*
The divine Elder, understanding the glory formerly revealed unto the Prophet, seeing the Word held in motherly arms, cried aloud rejoicing: O pure one, thou holdest God as a throne, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over peace.

*Troparion:*
The Elder bowed down, and divinely touching the footsteps of the unwedded Mother of God, said: Thou bearest Fire, O pure one; I fear to embrace the Infant God, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over peace.

*Troparion:*
Isaiah was cleansed, having received a coal from the seraph, the Elder cried aloud unto the Mother of God: As with tongs, thou dost illuminate me with thy hands, handing unto me Him Whom thou bearest, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over peace.

*Another Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray, the souls of those who dawn early unto Thee with love, to know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, calling them from the darkness of sin.

*Troparion:*
Having holily worn the holy hierarchal vesture, thou didst enlighten the ungodly, O venerable hierarch, preaching the uncreated Unity in three Persons, O Bucolus.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst snatch the people from the mouth of the noetic wolf by the teachings of thy God-proclaiming mouth; and the unclosed heretical mouths thou didst shut with thy theologies, O father.

*Troparion:*
Having overcome Marcion by thy sacred words, that abominable one was cast down, who spewed forth the foul-smelling mire of every absurdity, O glorious Bucolus.[^245]

*Theotokion:*
Jesus, the transcendent God, renews the laws of nature, O all-immaculate one, being born of thy womb, having alone shared all things by His will.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The Elder, having seen with his eyes the salvation that came from God unto the people, cried aloud unto Thee: O Christ, Thou art my God!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast been placed as a stone for Sion,[^246] a stumbling-block and a rock of offense unto the disobedient, but unshakable salvation unto the faithful.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly bearing the express image of Him Who brought Thee forth before the ages, Thou hast now wrapped Thyself in the weakness of earthly men out of compassion.

*Troparion:*
Unto the Son of the Most High, the Son of the Virgin, God Who has become an infant, having bowed down unto Thee, now release me in peace.

*Another Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One!

*Troparion:*
Having uplifting humility, purity of mind, unhypocritical love, and pure faith and hope, glorious Bucolus was revealed as a dwelling of the Trinity.

*Troparion:*
The Spirit mystically directs the sacred flock of the Smyrneans, which adorned the seven Churches, O all-praised one, that Polycarp, the shepherd after thee, should be consecrated by thy hand, O Bucolus.[^247]

*Troparion:*
Having perceived thy calling by the Spirit, O all-wise one, through grace he was brought unto the throne thou bringest a leader for the spiritual sheep unto the throne, shining with the brightness of virtues.

*Theotokion:*
O gate of grace, who didst open the gate of heaven unto mankind, brightly open unto me the doors of repentance, O Birthgiver of God, and deliver me from the gates of death.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Thou Who didst sanctify the virginal womb by Thy birth, and didst bless the hands of Symeon as was fitting, having anticipated us, hast now saved us, O Christ God; but make peace among civil powers in wars, and strengthen the king whom Thou hast loved,[^248] O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!

*Ikos:*
Let us run unto the Birthgiver of God, wishing to see her Son borne unto Symeon; Whom the bodiless hosts beholding from heaven marveled, saying: We behold now marvelous and glorious things, incomprehensible and ineffable! For He Who fashioned Adam is borne as an infant; the Incomprehensible is contained in the arms of the Elder; He Who is in the uncircumscribed bosom of His Father is willingly circumscribed in the flesh and not in the Godhead, the only Lover of mankind.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Unto Thee Who in the fire didst bedew the youths who spoke of God, and didst dwell in an incorrupt Virgin, God the Word, we sing, piously chanting: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Wishing to announce unto Adam who dwelt in hades, and to bring good tidings unto Eve, Symeon cried aloud, dancing with the prophets: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
God, delivering the earthly race, shall come even unto hades; and unto all captives He shall grant remission, and sight unto the blind, so that even the mute shall cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
A sword shall pierce thine own heart also, O incorrupt one,[^249] Symeon proclaimed unto the Birthgiver of God, beholding thy Son upon the Cross, unto Whom we cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Another Heirmos:*
The Angel made the furnace sprinkle dew for the venerable youths, while the command of God, burning the Chaldeans, persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Instructing the foolish and correcting those who had stumbled through deception, O father, thou hast saved them and brought them pure unto God, piously hymning Him: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
All-blessed one, thy cheeks were adorned as thou didst preach the incarnation of Him Who has shone forth upon us through ineffable compassion, unto Whom unto Whom thou didst fervently cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having mastered the passions of the body through temperance, O glorious one, thou wast shown to be unmastered by the assaults of the deceiver, and now thou singest without deceit: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered from the ancient condemnation by thy birthgiving, O all-immaculate one, we who have been saved for thy sake hymn thee as the Birthgiver of God, O Virgin, crying aloud and saying in faith: Blessed is she who gave birth to God in the flesh!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
United to the unbearable fire, the youths standing for piety, yet unharmed by the flame, sang a divine song: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Ye people of Israel, beholding your glory, Emmanuel, an Infant from the Virgin, dance now before the face of the divine Ark: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Behold, Symeon cried aloud, this Child shall be a sign that is spoken against, being God and an Infant; unto Him let us, the faithful, cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Being Life, this God the Word, having become an infant, shall be the fall of the disobedient, and the resurrection of all who sing in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos:*
Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and with water Thou didst burn up the sacrifice of the righteous one;[^250] for Thou dost all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst flourish like a palm tree in the courts of God;[^251] thou hast fallen asleep in the sleep due unto the righteous, and thy divine tomb pours forth delight unto those who behold the marvel, O Bucolus, preacher of God!

*Troparion:*
God the hypostatic Word of God established thee as a holy hierarch, sacredly anointing the people, O hierarch, instructor of the Smyrneans, luminary of the universe!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst theologize the incarnate Word, and didst save many people from irrationality, having become a divine disciple of him who shone forth in theology, O God-bearing Bucolus!

*Theotokion:*
The divine choir of the prophets sacredly taught thy mysteries, O Birthgiver of God: having understood from afar and clearly setting forth the divine tokens of thy divine childbearing.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
In the shadow and letter of the Law, we the faithful behold an image: every male that opens the womb is holy unto God;[^252] therefore the firstborn Word of the unoriginate Father, the Son firstborn of a Mother who knew not man, do we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Unto the newborns of old there was a pair of turtledoves, and two young pigeons;[^253] in whose stead the divine Elder and the chaste prophetess Anna serve, magnifying Him Who is born of the Virgin, the only-begotten Son of the Father, as He is brought into the temple.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast rendered unto me the joy of Thy salvation, O Christ, cried Symeon; receive Thy servant who is wearied by the shadow, the sacred preacher of the new grace, magnifying Thee in mystical praises.[^254]

*Troparion:*
Sacredly giving thanks, Anna prophesied, chaste and venerable, aged in grace, openly in the temple; and proclaiming the Birthgiver of God unto all present, she magnified her.

*Another Heirmos:*
It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O most pure one, the incarnate Word was revealed unto men, Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts, O Birthgiver of God, we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
As light, as dawn, as a great sun, as lightning thou didst shine upon the Church of Christ, and didst enlighten the minds of the faithful; and thou hast taken up thy abode in the unsetting places, singing with thrice-holy voices unto the uncreated Trinity.

*Troparion:*
Having been outside the flesh and the world, O sacred celebrant, thou hast rejoicing passed unto the otherworldly ranks, deified by most radiant adoption; therefore we celebrate thy memory on earth with faith, O all-wise Bucolus.

*Troparion:*
The earth covers thy blessed body, manifestly pouring forth healings and gifts; while heaven bears thy spirit with the chosen from the ages, adorned with the divine saints, in truth, O glorious Bucolus.

*Theotokion:*
By the angel's voice, O pure one, thou didst bear Christ Emmanuel, the Angel of Great Counsel,[^255] Who unites those below with the angels, O only blessed one, only all-hymned, the only cause of our divine deification.

*Exapostilarion of the Feast:*
Standing in spirit in the sanctuary, the Elder received into his arms the Master of the Law, crying aloud: Now loose me from the bonds of the flesh in peace, as Thou hast said; for mine eyes have seen the revelation of the gentiles, and the salvation of Israel.[^256]

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Today the Savior * was brought as an infant * into the temple of the Lord, * and with aged arms * the Elder takes Him up.

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.*
An infant in the flesh, * the Ancient of Days, * is seen most gloriously; * on this day, * He is brought into the temple.

*Verse: A light for revelation unto the gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*
Receive, O Symeon, * cried the all-immaculate one * in the sanctuary, * as an infant, * the Lord of glory and salvation of the world.

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
A great and awesome mystery * and strange dispensation: * He Who encompasses all things * and fashions infants, * is carried as an infant in arms!

*On the Seventh Day of the Same Month: Our Venerable Father Parthenius, Bishop of Lampsacus; and our Venerable Father Luke of Hellas:*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Hierarch, 3; and of the Venerable One, 3.*

*Stichera of the Hierarch, Tone 1. Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Named after virginity,[^257] * the luminary of Lampsacus, * the radiant morning star of wonders, the great Parthenius, * convokes us all by his memory * to celebrate a spiritual feast, * praising Christ the Savior.

Bearing God within thyself from youth, * thou didst burn up the demonic hosts * with the fire of thy prayers, O most blessed one; * thou didst wisely guide the flock of Lampsacus; * having cast down the altars of idols, * thou didst renew them for God, O preacher O preacher! Thy wonder is proclaimed by the cast-forth fish, * that revived though not breathing; * the fruit of the barren field * and vineyard; * the abundant rain, * the promise of those who were languishing: * marvelous are thy works, O sacred celebrant; * therefore pray that we may be saved.

*Other Stichera of the Venerable One, Tone 6. Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всю̀ ѿло́жше):*
Wholly following the Lord from youth, * thou didst leave the attachment of thy parents, O glorious one, * and worldly love; * and thou didst dwell in the deserts, * keeping the life of fasting in humility, * which thou didst also finish, * burning in spirit, O venerable one; * having made a good bargain, * having dispersed thy possessions unto the poor, * and bought Christ, the precious Pearl;[^258] * having attained the object of thy desire, * thou hast fallen asleep, O worthy of glory.

Thou didst endure being beaten * and reproached, O father, * with harshness by thy parents for the sake of the Lord, * Whom thou didst desire, O glorious one; * wounded with desire for Whom, * thou didst account fair things as nothing; * but thou didst love the loss of thy mother * and pilgrimage; * and wast clothed in the monastic habit, O most sacred one; * but God reveals thee even against thy will, * being inclined unto thy mother's prayers, * O venerable Luke, * as the Lover of mankind.

Having finished thy sacred life, O all-venerable one, * thou didst work wonders, O holy one, * both visible and all-glorious; * and God having given Thee a divine pledge, * even before the reward of thy divine labors, * gives Thee also an incorrupt crown * and eternal glory * when He shall come as Judge; * Him therefore entreat, that those who ever hymn Thee * may be delivered from all affliction, * O most marvelous Luke.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, the same Tone. Idiomelon:*
*By Germanus:*
Upon aged arms on this day, * as upon a cherubic chariot, * Christ God was pleased to recline; * and from the tyranny of passions * deliver us who hymn Thee, when we call upon Thee, * and save us, as the Lover of mankind!

*If it be a fast, Glory... Both now... Theotokion. But if on Tuesday or Thursday evening, Stavrotheotokion, written on the 3rd day.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6. Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Receive with thine arms, said Symeon, * the Fashioner of all things; * O Elder, take up Christ, * Whom the Virgin Maiden * brought forth without seed, * unto the joy of our race.

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.*
Having assembled, ye peoples, let us hymn the Creator and God of the Law, * before Whom the angelic multitudes tremble, * the only Benefactor * and Lawgiver of the Law, * unto the salvation of our souls.

*Verse: A light for revelation unto the gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*
Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, * as Thou hast said; * for I have seen Thee, O Christ, the eternal Light, * [loose] me from the bonds of the flesh, * for revelation of the gentiles and of Israel.[^259]

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*
*By John the Monk:*
Let the heavenly gate be opened today: for the unoriginate Word of the Father, having taken a beginning under time without departing from His Godhead, is willingly brought by His Mother into the legal temple as a forty-day-old infant from the Virgin; and the Elder receives Him into his arms, crying aloud: Release Thy servant, O Master, for mine eyes have seen Thy salvation;[^260] O Thou Who didst come into the world to save the human race, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Troparion of the Saints, Tone 4:*
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness, take not Thy mercy away from us; but by their prayers direct our life in peace.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, full of grace: for from thee has shone forth the Sun of Righteousness, Christ our God,[^261] enlightening those who are in darkness; rejoice thou also, O righteous Elder, who didst receive in thine arms the Liberator of our souls, Who grants us resurrection!

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, twice;*
*Glory... of the Saints; Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ їѡ́сифъ):*
Thou becomest an infant for my sake, O Ancient of Days; Thou dost partake of purification, O most pure God, that Thou mightest assure me of the flesh which is from the Virgin; and instructed by these things, Symeon recognized Thee as God manifested in the flesh, and embraced Thee as life, and rejoicing in old age cried aloud: Release me, for I have seen Thee, the Life of all!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй):*
He Who sits with the Father upon the holy throne, having come to earth was born of the Virgin, and He Who is uncircumscribed became an infant in time; Whom Symeon, having received into his arms, rejoicing said: Now lettest Thou depart O Compassionate One, having magnified Thy servant!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos to 6; and of the Saints to 8.*

*Canon of the Feast, Tone 3.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The sun once shone upon the dry deep-yielding land, * for the water curdled like a wall on both sides * for the people walking dry-shod through the sea, and singing pleasingly unto God: * Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Let the clouds drop down water, for the Sun carried upon a light cloud has arrived;[^262] upon incorrupt arms, Christ is in the temple as an infant; therefore let us, the faithful, cry aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Be ye strengthened, O hands of Symeon weakened by old age, and the wearied knees of the Elder, move swiftly and directly to meet Christ, forming a choir with the bodiless hosts: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Be glad, ye heavens spread out by understanding, and rejoice, O earth; for from the most divine bosom, Christ the Craftsman, having come forth, is brought by His Virgin Mother unto God the Father, an Infant Who is before all things: for He is gloriously glorified!

*Canon of the Hierarch, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Israel of old, having traversed the deep of the Red Sea with dry feet, by Moses' hands stretched out in the form of a Cross conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.[^263]`

*Troparion:*
By the grace of virtues, they enlighten us all today unto the radiant celebration of the wise hierarch, sending forth as a dawn the radiances of light-bearing wonders.

*Troparion:*
Unto the mountain of dispassion thou didst raise up thy mind, O venerable one, and having entered into the darkness of the Scriptures, O Parthenius, thou wast revealed as filled with the immaterial light, O glorious and venerable one!

*Troparion:*
Having truly drawn the weapon, which is the word of God,[^264] thou didst wound the enemy's strength, O hierarch Parthenius; therefore thou hast received the tokens of victory over demons.

*Theotokion:*
Envy was the begetter of death unto men in paradise;[^265] but the Son of God the Father, having been born of thee, O Virgin, utterly destroyed it with a crash, granting immortality unto our race.

*Another Canon of the Venerable One, Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Israel, having traversed the deep with dry-shod steps as on dry land, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparion:*
Grant me a word, O beholder of God, who standest before the throne of thy Master, to hymn thy bright and light-bearing life, O Luke, dispelling the mist of my mind.

*Troparion:*
Firmly having loved Thee, O Savior, the sacred one entered steadfastly upon asceticism, enduring afflictions, labors, and illnesses, through which he found unending delight.

*Troparion:*
Ever wearing out the body by temperance, O father Luke, with frequent vigil and material hardship, thou didst wisely stay the surges of fierce passions.

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin Bearer of the Lord, who didst bear in the flesh God Who is inseparable from the Father, incarnate out of compassion for the earthly: show me to be above the torment of passions.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establishment of those who hope in Thee: establish, O Lord, the Church which Thou hast acquired with Thy precious blood.

*Troparion:*
Firstborn of the Father before the ages, firstborn infant of the incorrupt Virgin, unto Adam He appeared stretching forth His hand.

*Troparion:*
God the Word, correcting again the first-formed who had become foolish through deception, appeared having become an infant.

*Troparion:*
Earth's offspring, having run back into it: the Creator, having unchangeably become an infant, revealed a nature conformed to the Godhead...

*Another Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and establishment!

*Troparion:*
Like a flowering palm tree, O venerable one,[^266] thou didst hide the fruits of immortality in the heavens, having labored in virtues upon the earth.

*Troparion:*
The grace dwelling in thee, O father, showed thee forth unto all as a temple of God, for whose sake thou wast glorified, O Parthenius.

*Troparion:*
Adorned by God with the most honorable vesture of the high priesthood, O glorious one, thou wast revealed as a celebrant worthy of His goodness.

*Theotokion:*
The dividing wall of our curse was broken down, O pure Birthgiver of God, beyond understanding by thy childbearing, and we have been united unto the divine nature.

*Another Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion:*
Inflamed with fervent love for the Savior, thou didst despise the love of thy parents; for Whose sake also thou wast beaten, O Luke, estranged from all delights, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Wishing to make thyself a temple of the Lord, thou didst settle in the deserts thou didst settle, as Elijah of old,[^267] battling with demons; therefore thou didst find a blessed end.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast revealed as a well-obedient servant unto every divine commandment, O all-blessed one, fulfilling them well and humbling thyself; therefore thou hast received heavenly height.

*Theotokion:*
As the Mother of God, who didst bear the Word beyond words Who appeared for our sake, pray fervently to deliver me from all irrationality.

*Kontakion of Parthenius, Tone 3. Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
Thou didst receive the divine grace of wonders, O divinely wise, sacred Parthenius, wonderworker, God-bearer, cleansing all the passions of the faithful, casting out spirits of malice, O father; therefore we hymn thee as a great initiate of God's grace.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Being full of the divine Spirit, thou didst blossom with deeds of piety, O sacred wonderworker Parthenius; thou didst turn men from deception, healing the passions of the sick; O venerable father, pray unto Christ God that He may save our souls.

*Glory... Sessional Hymn of the Venerable One, Tone 8. Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having loved the virtue of monasticism, thou didst withdraw from all sweet things, O wise one, showing forth fasting and temperance with certainty, and accounting thy mother's desire as nothing, thou didst secretly give thyself unto pilgrimage; and again God makes thee known unto thy mother who wept for the loss of thee, O most excellent righteous one; with whom now dwelling, pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love.

*Both now... of the Feast, the same Tone. Podoben: "Overcome by the pastoral pipes" (Свирѣ́лей па́стырскихъ):*
Thou wast born on earth co-unoriginate with the Father, and wast brought into the temple, O Incomprehensible One; and the Elder, receiving Thee into his arms, cried aloud rejoicing: Now lettest Thou depart him whom Thou hast visited according to Thy word,[^268] O Thou Who wast well pleased as God to save the human race!

*If it be a fast, Glory... Both now... Theotokion. If Wednesday or Friday, Stavrotheotokion, written on the 11th day of this month.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thy virtue has covered the heavens, O Christ,[^269] for having come forth from the Ark of Thy sanctification, Thine incorrupt Mother, Thou hast appeared in the temple of Thy glory as an infant carried in arms, and all things were filled and all things were filled with Thy praise.

*Troparion:*
Rejoice, O Symeon, initiate of ineffable mysteries, cried the Birthgiver of God; receive into thine arms Christ, the Word Who has become an infant, as was made known unto thee of old by the Holy Spirit, crying aloud unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Troparion:*
Having hoped in Him, O Symeon, lift up rejoicing Christ in infant stature, the divine consolation of Israel,[^270] the Creator and Master of the Law Who fulfills the ordinance of the Law, crying aloud unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Troparion:*
Beholding the Word without beginning, the Cause of the existence of all things, borne in the flesh by the Virgin as upon a cherubic throne, Symeon, marveling at Him as an infant, cried aloud unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Another Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of Righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, crying aloud as was meet: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^271]

*Troparion:*
Enjoying the ineffable mysteries of God, O thrice-blessed one, being divinely taught His secret things, therefore also thou didst pour forth rivers of understanding, O Parthenius.

*Troparion:*
Wholly taken up in mind unto divine heaven, O God-bearer, thou didst mentally behold ineffable glory; therefore thou wast also revealed as equal to the angels upon earth, O Parthenius.

*Troparion:*
All creation rejoices with the angels, beholding the signs beyond understanding wrought through thee, O father; therefore it hymns the Cause of these things as the mighty God.

*Theotokion:*
How all-glorious and ineffable, O Birthgiver of God, are the awesome and marvelous mysteries of thy new childbearing, O pure one, in which mind and intellect are vanquished, O all-immaculate one!

*Another Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church holily sings, crying aloud from a pure mind, feasting in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Guileless, venerable, righteous, meek, and whole wast thou revealed, O father Luke; therefore thou wast called blessed by the Creator and Master of all, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
He that envied thee, beholding thee leading a good life, was inflamed and raised up a multitude of temptations against thee; but the ravenous wolf was exposed as empty, gaping in vain against the word.

*Troparion:*
By uprightness of mind thou didst direct thy life toward better things, O all-blessed one desiring, O father, to see the ultimate of all desires, which thou didst also happily attain.

*Theotokion:*
Having found thee fair in beauty above all women, O pure Virgin, the Beautiful One settled in thy womb, and delivered me from the ugliness of passions and from darkness.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
When Isaiah symbolically beheld God exalted upon a throne, escorted by angels of glory, he cried aloud: Woe is me, wretched one![^272] For I have foreseen God becoming incarnate, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over peace.

*Troparion:*
The divine Elder, understanding the glory formerly revealed unto the Prophet, seeing the Word held in motherly arms, cried aloud rejoicing: O pure one, thou holdest God as a throne, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over peace.

*Troparion:*
The Elder bowed down, and divinely touching the footsteps of the unwedded Mother of God, said: Thou bearest Fire, O pure one; I fear to embrace the Infant God, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over peace.

*Troparion:*
Isaiah was cleansed, having received a coal from the seraph;[^273] the Elder cried aloud unto the Mother of God: As with tongs, thou dost illuminate me with thy hands, handing unto me Him Whom thou bearest, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over peace.

*Another Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as a light, a holy light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith.[^274]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast truly anointed with the oil of the Spirit, O Parthenius, worthy to serve the Lord Who adorned thee with glory.

*Troparion:*
Having searched the depth of the Scripture, O all-glorious one, having chosen Christ the Pearl of great price,[^275] thou didst distribute it unto the flock.

*Troparion:*
As having inexhaustible grace from God, O wise wonderworker Parthenius, thou pourest forth most glorious wonders unto all.

*Theotokion:*
Let heaven drop down gladness and grace upon earth, for gladness on high was now prepared for the only Bearer of God.

*Another Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray, the souls of those who dawn early unto Thee with love, to know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, calling them from the darkness of sin.

*Troparion:*
Having received the reward of thy labors from the Rich Giver, O venerable father, thou wast revealed unto all as an ocean of wonders, driving away diseases from those who come unto thee unto thee.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast not shaken, O father, by the enemy's very many assaults; thou didst not fear his haughtiness, but didst stand as a firm, unshakable pillar, clad in humility as in a breastplate.

*Troparion:*
Having well accomplished thy sacred life, O all-blessed one, and received the grace of healings from God the Ruler of all, thou didst enlighten the blind and make the lame and the paralytic whole.

*Theotokion:*
Ye peoples, hymn her who truly gave birth unto the Word of God ineffably, and remained an incorrupt Virgin after childbearing, as our protectress and salvation.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The Elder, having seen with his eyes the salvation that came from God unto the people,[^276] cried aloud unto Thee: O Christ, Thou art my God!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast been placed as a stone for Sion,[^277] a stumbling-block and a rock of offense unto the disobedient, but unshakable salvation unto the faithful.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly bearing the express image of Him Who brought Thee forth before the ages, Thou hast now wrapped Thyself in the weakness of earthly men out of compassion.

*Troparion:*
Unto the Son of the Most High, the Son of the Virgin, God Who has become an infant, having bowed down unto Thee, now release me in peace.

*Another Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy from Thy side.[^278]

*Troparion:*
Thy God-bearing life trumpets unto all the universe, O Parthenius, with gifts of beautiful wonders, through the utterance of the Spirit, O all-glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst sprinkle the water of adoption upon thy flock, washing away the defilement of uncleanness by thy teachings, O Parthenius, and enlightening them with the light of grace.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst produce the Fruit of incorrupt understanding unto the world, O Bearer of God, shown forth as the ever-living Mediatress of incorruption unto those who honor thee with faith and love.

*Another Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One![^279]

*Troparion:*
Broken is thine ancient strength against us, O crafty-minded one, and gone without trace are thy many subtleties, O malicious one: for behold, a simple and guileless youth has cast thee down!`

*Troparion:*
Thou gavest no slumber unto thine eyes, O venerable one, nor rest unto thine eyelids,[^280] until thou didst cast down the demons thou didst cast down the craftiness of the deceiver, who each day stirred up diverse tribulations against thee.

*Troparion:*
Chastely didst thou complete thy life, and kindly receiving strangers always, with prayer rich and abundant: therefore didst thou also obtain incorruptible glory and delight.

*Theotokion:*
One of the Trinity hast thou borne in the flesh, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, beyond mind and word: therefore I cry aloud: by thy prayers make me a dwelling place of the divine Trinity.[^282]

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 4:*
Thou Who didst sanctify the virginal womb by Thy birth, and didst bless the hands of Simeon as was meet, having anticipated us, hast now also saved us, O Christ God; but give peace to the commonwealth in wars, and strengthen the King,[^281] whom Thou hast loved, O only Lover of mankind.

*Ikos:*
Let us hasten to the Birthgiver of God, we who desire to see Her Son borne unto Simeon; Whom the bodiless hosts seeing from Heaven were amazed, saying: Marvelous and most glorious things do we behold now, incomprehensible and ineffable! For He Who fashioned Adam is borne as an infant; He Who cannot be contained is contained in the arms of the Elder; He Who is in the uncircumscribed bosom of His Father is willingly circumscribed in the flesh, and not in the Godhead: the only Lover of mankind.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thee, Who didst bedew the pious youths in the fire and didst take up Thine abode in the incorrupt Virgin, God the Word do we hymn, chanting piously: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Desiring to give good tidings unto Adam dwelling in Hades, and to bring glad news unto Eve, Simeon cried aloud, dancing with the prophets: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
God, delivering the earthly race, shall come even unto Hades; and He shall grant remission unto all the captives, and sight unto the blind, so that even the mute cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
And a sword shall pierce thine own heart, O incorrupt one, Simeon proclaimed unto the Birthgiver of God, when thou seest thy Son upon the Cross, unto Whom we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Another [Canon, to St. Parthenios]. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamite youths, fired rather by the love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
The divine grace of Christ took up its abode in Thee, O wise one, truly as a palace of glory truly fashioning a palace: wherein the Father, and the Son, and the Comforter Himself took up Their abode.

*Troparion:*
With the divine fire hast thou burned up all the thorny deceit of impiety upon the earth, O venerable one, and hast sown divine teachings for thy flock, which bring forth ever-living fruits.

*Troparion:*
With the hook of the tongue, from the depth of deceit's downfall, didst thou of old draw thy flock unto life; and now, O father, by thy prayers thou dost preserve them blessed from all harm of fish,[^283] by grace.

*Theotokion:*
The angels were astonished, O Birthgiver of God, at how through thy marvelous childbirth the corruptible nature of the earthly race ran again unto incorruptible life and heavenly glory.

*Another [Canon, to St. Luke]. Heirmos:*
The youths in Babylon feared not the flame of the furnace, but cast into the midst of the flame, being bedewed, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
The shrine of thy relics, O Luke, is shown to be a fountain of healings, unto which everyone who hastens is delivered from all passions, blessing thee, O venerable one, and praising thy God.

*Troparion:*
With the greatest signs, O father, did the Lord glorify thee, Whom thou didst first glorify by the labors of abstinence; for thou didst cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Both most radiant in life, and greatest in miracles, and unswerving in all virtues wast thou shown to be, O venerable one; therefore hast thou found the recompense of thy labors: the Kingdom of God.

*Theotokion:*
Save thy people and thy city, O all-immaculate Birthgiver of God, our protection, as with unhesitating faith we cry unto thee: Blessed, O all-pure one, is the fruit of thy womb.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
United with the unbearable fire, yet standing firm in piety, the youths, unharmed by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.[^284]

*Troparion:*
Ye people of Israel, beholding Thine Emmanuel, Thy glory, a child from the Virgin: now dance before the face of the divine ark! Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Behold, Simeon cried aloud: A sign that shall be spoken against shall He be, being God and a child! Unto Him, ye faithful, let us cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

...Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Being Life, this shall be the falling of the disobedient, God the Word having become an infant: and as a resurrection unto all who chant in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Another [Canon, to St. Parthenios]. Heirmos:*
Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the yawning mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
With the rod of faith, O wise one, Thou didst cast down the pagan temples, and didst erect temples, O venerable one, unto our Savior and God, wherein Thou didst offer pure sacrifices, chanting unto Him: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
As a sweet-smelling sacrifice didst thou offer thyself, O wise one, by the fire of abstinence sending up a blameless fragrance unto the divine and unapproachable altar of God, chanting, O Parthenios: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having divine grace from God, O father Parthenios, thou dost pour forth healings unto those who draw near unto thee, O venerable one, cleansing infirmities and stoutly driving away the legions of demons, constantly teaching them to praise the Cause of these things as God.

*Theotokion:*
God, Who is unapproachable by nature, the Son and Word of God, took up His abode in thee, O pure one, and having clothed Himself in our image, O all-holy one, as He is merciful, He appeared well-pleasing in the flesh and lived among us. For this cause, with much love do I honor thee, and hymn and praise thee.

*Another [Canon, to St. Luke]. Heirmos:*
Standing in peril for the laws of their fathers, the blessed youths in Babylon spat upon the foolish command of the ruler; and united with the fire wherein they were not consumed, they sang a worthy hymn unto the Sovereign: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou dost drive away the harm of every passion, O venerable father, blessed Luke, cleansing lepers, strengthening the infirm, loosing the bonds that held the paralyzed, and healing the lame and the blind together, crying aloud: Sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Painless glory hast thou found, O venerable one, having endured many painful labors and ascetic feats: vigilance, and exhaustion of the body, fasting and affliction, and casting down[^285] and casting down, and every other torment of the flesh, for the sake of the love of thy Creator, with Whom now living thou dost rejoice; Him therefore pray thou to save all who with faith and love magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
By reason of diverse pains and virtues, O venerable one, thou hast entered into the Heavenly Kingdom, and art now settled there eternally, ever delighting again in beauties, singing with the angels the thrice-holy hymn: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
Him Who departed not and is inseparable from the Father's bosom, O pure one, hast thou borne without knowing man and ineffably, being perfect man. Pray Him to grant me strength to cast down utterly every assault of evil thoughts, and to do His divine will always, that I may magnify thee.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
In the shadow and letter of the Law, we the faithful behold a figure: every male that opens the womb is holy unto God; therefore the firstborn Word of the unoriginate Father, the Son Who is the firstborn of a Mother that knew not man, do we magnify.[^286]

*Troparion:*
Unto the ancients there was a pair of newborn turtledoves, or two young pigeons; and in their stead the divine Elder and the chaste Prophetess Anna minister, magnifying Him Who was born of the Virgin and brought into the church as the Only-begotten Son of the Father.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast restored unto me, Simeon cried aloud, the joy of Thy salvation, O Christ; receive Thy servant who is weary with the shadow, the sacred and mystic preacher of the new grace, who magnifies Thee in praise.

*Troparion:*
In a manner becoming sacred things, Anna, the chaste, venerable, and aged prophetess, openly gave thanks unto the Birthgiver of God in the temple; and proclaiming the Mother of God unto all present, she magnified Her.

*Another [Canon, to St. Parthenios]. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, the uncut mountain, O Virgin: Christ, Who joined together the divided natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Having truly shaken off the fluctuation of fleshly passions, O thrice-blessed one, thou didst transfer thy whole self unto divine life and immaterial delight, where, dancing in choir, thou dost watch over us.

*Troparion:*
Reaping the beauty of miracles as noetic flowers in incorruptible delight upon thy holy memory, O God-bearer, we carry about a diversely woven crown of thine actions.

...Thou didst pass beyond the things of the world, and dancing in choir with the heavenly bodiless ranks, O God-bearing father Parthenios, pray thou for us who celebrate thy memory in faith.

*Theotokion:*
I hymn thy grace, O Virgin, and I magnify thy glory; for thou art truly the enlightenment and consolation of my soul, a speedy help, and salvation, and protection.

*Another [Canon, to St. Luke]. Heirmos:*
It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-immaculate one, the Word incarnate was shown to men, Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Who of earthly men can recount thy labors and ascetic feats, O father? By reason of them hast thou received a worthy reward from God; for thou wast a vessel of the Holy Spirit: venerable, upright, true, righteous, and blameless.

*Troparion:*
Since thou hast settled in those desired mansions, being filled with divine beauties and now delighting unto fullness in the flowers of the divine Paradise, rejoicing, make remembrance of us unto God, O all-blessed one.

*Triadikon:*
O single nature in three Hypostases, thrice-radiant essence and indivisible power: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, one Godhead: save those who in faith magnify Thee, the one God in three Persons.[^287]

*Theotokion:*
An earthly mind cannot comprehend thy mystery, O Virgin, all-hymned Birthgiver of God; for before childbirth thou didst conceive without man, and thou didst remain a virgin after childbirth, and didst bear the Word Who is co-everlasting with the Father.

*Exapostilarion of the Feast:*
Present in the sanctuary in spirit, the Elder took into his arms the Master of the Law, crying aloud: Now loose me from the bonds of the flesh in peace, as Thou hast said; for mine eyes have seen the revelation of the Gentiles and the salvation of Israel.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Bearing the Creator and Master as an infant in her arms, the pure and all-immaculate Virgin enters into the church.

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.*

*Sticheron:*
Receive, O Simeon, the Lord of glory, as it was revealed unto thee by the Holy Spirit; for behold, He is come.

*Verse: A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*

...Now have mine eyes seen Thy promise, O good God; quickly loose me, Thy servant, O Lover of mankind.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Rejoice, thou who didst receive the Joy of the world, Christ the Giver of life, and didst cause the sorrow of our foremother to cease, O Virgin.

*On the eighth day of the same month: the holy Great-Martyr Theodore the Stratelates; and the holy Prophet Zechariah who saw the sickle.[^288]*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Martyr, 3; and of the Prophet, 3.*

*Stichera of the Martyr, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
A valiant sufferer, an invincible soldier, wast thou shown to be by the Holy Spirit, having cast down the adversary by the wisdom of thy words and by the patient manifestations of thy deeds, O wise one; therefore hast thou received the crowns of victory, and art joined with the choirs on high, O Great-Martyr Theodore.

*Sticheron:*
A divine pillar of piety wast thou, O exceedingly rich one, abhorring the temples of the impious and turning them to foolishness utterly; and as an acceptable sacrifice art thou honorably brought unto Him Who was sacrificed for thy sake, Who hath bestowed thee upon the world as a treasury of miracles for those who glorify thy holy memory, O Theodore.

*Sticheron:*
Lifted up upon a cross, and scraped upon thy flesh, and pierced with sharp arrows, and stretched upon the tree, and severed with swords, and encompassed with all manner of torturous contrivances, thou wast shown to be unyielding and unconquerable by the power of Him Who was nailed to the Cross, O Theodore, glory of martyrs.

*Other Stichera, of the Prophet, Tone 2, Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что̀ ва́съ нарече́мъ):*
Having an angel-like life, thou didst openly converse with the angels, O divinely blessed one, having thine intellect illuminated by them with things beyond mind: a purification of marvelous figures, a revelation of great mysteries, a prophetic illumination and spiritual uplifting; pray thou that our souls may be saved.

*Sticheron:*
An earthly angel wast thou shown to be, having on earth the heavenly hosts as thy companions, having become a beholder of supermundane visions, a spiritual beacon flashing forth spiritual light, an olive tree, to speak with the Psalmist, fruitful, dropping divine oil upon the pious: pray thou that our souls may be saved.

*Sticheron:*
Delighting in the hoped-for deification as a most true prophet of God, and clearly filled with ever-existing sweetness, beholding ineffable glory, and adorned with crowns of beauty: pray thou fervently, O God-receiver, for us who praise thee, that our souls may be saved.

*If it be a fast: Glory... Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, written on the 14th day.*

*If there be no fast, we sing also the Idiomelon of the Saint.*

*Glory... Tone 8:*
As one whose name bespeaks the gifts of God,[^289] and an heir of His beatitude, let all of us the faithful praise, and worthily bless, Theodore the noble Great-Martyr, champion of the universe; for he prays unto Christ God for our souls.

*Both now... of the Feast, the same Tone:*
He Who is borne upon the Cherubim and hymned by the Seraphim, today being brought into the divine sanctuary according to the Law, sits as upon a throne in the arms of the Elder, and from Joseph receives gifts in a manner becoming God: as a pair of turtledoves, the undefiled Church and the newly chosen people from the Gentiles; and two young pigeons, as Author of both the Old and the New. Simeon, having received the fulfillment of the promise made unto him, blessed Mary the Virgin Birthgiver of God, and proclaimed the figures of the sufferings that would come from Her; and he asked release from Him, crying aloud: Now dismiss me, O Master, as Thou didst tell me beforehand; for I have seen Thee, the Pre-eternal Light, and the Savior Lord of the people named after Christ.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Today the heavenly gates are opened: for Christ, as an infant, is brought into the church by the Virgin Mother unto God and the Father.

*Verse: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.*

*Sticheron:*
Christ, the Live Coal that was revealed unto divine Isaiah, is now given unto the Elder by the hands of the Birthgiver of God as with tongs.

*Verse: A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.*

*Sticheron:*
With fear and joy holding the Master in his arms, Simeon asked for release from life, praising the Mother of God.

*Glory... Tone 5:*
Today hath shone forth more brightly than the morning star the honored memory of the Passion-bearer of Christ, invisibly illuminating the hearts of the faithful and driving away driving away the clouds of the soul by the operation and grace of the Spirit; unto whom let us, lovers of the martyrs, cry aloud: Rejoice, thou who wast revealed unto the faithful as grace bestowed by God, and who dost extend multitudes of miracles unto those who flee unto thee, O blessed Theodore, unceasingly and abundantly praying Christ that those who celebrate thy most honored memory in faith may obtain eternal good things.

*Both now... of the Feast, the same Tone:*
The Ancient of Days, having become an infant in the flesh, is brought into the church by the Virgin Mother, fulfilling the promise of His Law; Whom Simeon receiving, said: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, according to Thy word, for mine eyes have seen Thy salvation, O Holy One.[^290]

*Troparion of the Martyr, Tone 4:*
