By true soldierly enlistment, O passion-bearer, wast thou an excellent general of the Heavenly King, O Theodore; for thou didst wisely arm thyself with the weapons of faith, and didst vanquish the hosts of demons, and wast shown to be a victorious athlete; therefore in faith we ever bless thee.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast:*
Rejoice, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin full of grace; for from thee hath shone forth the Sun of Righteousness, Christ our God, Who enlightens those who are in darkness. Rejoice thou also, O righteous Elder, who didst receive in thine embrace the Liberator of our souls, Who grants us resurrection.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory... of the Martyr; Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "The beauty of virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дв҃ства):*
Holding Thee incarnate, O Christ, holy Simeon rejoiced: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, O Master, he cried; and Anna, the blameless and glorious prophetess, now offers thanksgiving and praise unto Thee. And we, O Giver of life, cry unto Thee: Glory be to Thee Who wast thus well pleased!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The shepherds' pipes" (Свирѣ́лей па́стырскихъ):*
Thou wast born on earth, Who art co-unoriginate with the Father; Thou wast brought into the church, Who art incomprehensible; and rejoicing, the Elder took Thee into his arms, crying aloud: Now lettest Thou him whom Thou hast visited depart according to Thy word, O Thou Who as God wast well pleased to save mankind.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*The Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6; and the two Canons of the Saints, on 8.*

*Canon of the Feast, Tone 3. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The sun once shone upon dry land produced from the deep produced from the deep; for the water grew firm like a wall on either side for the people traveling on foot across the sea and singing in a manner well-pleasing to God: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified.[^291]

*Troparion:*
Let the clouds drop down water; for the Sun borne upon a light cloud hath appeared: Christ in the church as an infant upon an incorrupt hand; therefore let us the faithful cry aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*
Be strong, ye hands of Simeon, weakened by old age; and ye weary legs of the Elder, move swiftly and straightway to meet Christ, forming a choir with the bodiless hosts: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*
Ye heavens stretched out with wisdom, be glad, and rejoice, O earth; for coming forth from the most divine bosom, Christ the Craftsman is brought unto God the Father by the Virgin Mother, an infant Who is before all: for He is gloriously glorified.

*Canon of the Martyr, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The rod of Moses, performing wonders of old, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, drowned the chariot-driver Pharaoh, but saved fleeing Israel on foot, who sang a hymn unto God.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly adorned with divine splendors, sacredly crowned with the wreath of suffering, and radiant with the glory of the Holy Trinity: remember those who praise thee, O Theodore, great among martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Full of light, full of divine grace, the sacred memory of thy honorable struggles hath shone upon us, with most radiant brightness illuminating all who piously honor thee, O martyr, passion-bearer Theodore.

*Troparion:*
While a cruel tempest held mankind, the sacred martyr, steered by the divine grace of the Cross, sailed through without storm and rejoicing reached the heavenly havens.

*Theotokion:*
O Door of God, open unto me the doors of repentance, O Virgin; enlighten my soul with divine thoughts, and drive away the heavy clouds of passions, that rejoicing I may honor thee and duly call thee blessed.

*Canon of the Prophet, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*
Having the Giver of light listening to thy supplications: grant me enlightenment, O Prophet Zechariah O most marvelous prophet; adorned with the beauties of divine loveliness, thou beholdest the beauty of the angels in purity of soul.

*Troparion:*
Filled with the holiness of angel-like radiance as thou art, O divinely wise one, thou didst have the angels conversing with thee and illuminating thee, O beholder of God.

*Theotokion:*
The curse of our foremother Eve was abolished, O all-immaculate one; for having given birth to Christ, thou didst enrich the world with blessings.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, the Confirmation of those who hope in Thee, confirm the Church which Thou hast acquired with Thy precious Blood.[^292]

*Troparion:*
Firstborn from the Father before all ages, firstborn as an infant from the incorrupt Virgin, He was shown extending His hand unto Adam.

*Troparion:*
God the Word, correcting again the first-created man who became childlike in mind through deceit, was shown having become an infant.

*Troparion:*
The offspring of the earth that had run back unto it, the Creator, being unchangeable, showed to be a nature conformed to the Godhead, having become an infant.

*Another [Canon, of the Martyr]. Heirmos:*
Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for there is none holy save Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Daring like a lion, thou didst stand in the midst of the tribunal, proclaiming the divine and saving word, confounding the impious, preaching the truth, and rebuking the falsehood of godlessness, O Theodore.

*Troparion:*
The tormentor marveled at thy courage, O wise one; moved with beast-like rage, with ox tendons he unsparingly wounded thee, O Theodore, who didst accomplish the destruction of the abominable idols.

*Troparion:*
With manly purpose thou didst endure the drops of thy wounds as waves of the sea, O divinely wise one, being strengthened by the grace of the Cross, and wast shown to be a crown-bearer by the power of the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
He Who was born of thee, the Lord, hath truly done great things unto thee: and all generations glorify thee as the Mother of God.

*Another [Canon, of the Prophet]. Heirmos:*
There is none holy like the Lord, and none righteous like our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Grace from on high overshadowed thee, the divine guidance of ineffable providence, teaching thee by holy angels, O all-wise and blessed one, and enlightening thee.

*Troparion [of the Prophet]:*
Being instructed in divine goodness, thou wast taught, O exceedingly rich one, as pure, to live fruitfully in divine holiness in the all-holy city.

*Troparion:*
An unshakable wall, a confirmation unto God-wise people wast Thou, O Lord, and salvation unto souls, as of old the marvelous prophet showed unto Thee.

*Theotokion:*
Thy childbearing is incorrupt and pure, O Mother of God, thy carrying in the womb delivered the world from corruption and death: for thou didst bear the Savior, the Co-eternal Word.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Thou Who didst sanctify the virginal womb by Thy birth and didst bless the hands of Symeon, as was meet, having anticipated us, hast now also saved us, O Christ God: but make peace among the nations in wars, and strengthen the king,[^293] whom Thou hast loved, O only Lover of mankind.

*Ikos:*
Let us hasten unto the Mother of God, we who desire to behold her Son borne unto Symeon, Whom when the bodiless powers beheld from heaven, they were astonished, saying: Marvelous and most glorious, incomprehensible, and ineffable things do we now behold! For He Who fashioned Adam is carried as an infant, the Incomprehensible is contained in the hands of the elder. He Who is in the uncircumscribed bosom of His Father is willingly circumscribed in the flesh, and not in Godhead, the only Lover of mankind.

*Sessional Hymn of the Martyr, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Прему́дрости):*
Having taken up the divine whole armor and cast down the deceit of idols, thou didst raise up angels to praise thy struggles: for having kindled thy mind with divine love, thou didst valiantly dare to face a death by fire. Therefore, fittingly named, thou dost give divine gifts unto those who ask thee, O passion-bearer Theodore. Therefore we cry aloud unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate thy memory with love.

*Glory... of the Prophet, Tone 3, Podoben: "To the Beauty of Virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́тва):*
Thou didst behold harnessed chariots, the bodiless ministers guided by the hand of the God of all, O marvelous Zechariah: rejoicing with them as an ever-memorable prophet, pray that those who with unhesitating faith celebrate thy most sacred memory may be directed unto the path of divine understanding.

*Both now... of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "The Angelic Host" (Ли́къ а҆́ггелскїй):*
He Who is with the Father upon the holy throne, having come upon the earth was born of a Virgin: and He Who is uncircumscribed in years became an infant. Whom Symeon, having received into his arms, rejoicing, said: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart, according as Thou hast been well pleased.

*If it be a fast, Glory... Both now..., Theotokion. If Wednesday or Friday, Cross-Theotokion. They are written on the 28th day.*[^294]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thy virtue covered the heavens, O Christ: for proceeding forth from the ark of Thy sanctification, of Thine incorrupt Mother, in the temple of Thy glory Thou wast seen as an infant borne in arms, and all things were filled with Thy praise.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, O Symeon, initiate of ineffable mysteries, the Mother of God cried aloud: Receive into thine arms the Word Who has become an infant, Christ, concerning Whom it was declared unto thee of old by the Holy Spirit, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, O Symeon, receive Christ in childhood's age, upon Whom thou didst set thy hope, the divine consolation of Israel, the Maker and Master of the Law, Who fulfills the ordinance of the Law, crying unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Troparion:*
When Symeon beheld the beginningless Word, with flesh borne by the Virgin as upon a cherubic throne, the Cause of the existence of all things, marveling at Him as an infant, he cried unto Him: All things are filled with Thy praise!

*Another [Canon, of the Martyr]. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, and didst visit our poverty: therefore with the Prophet Habbakuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Having Christ within thyself through faith, O blessed one, thou didst cast down deceitful pride by thy wise words; for, O martyr, thou didst truly convict their exceeding folly, and by most glorious deeds thou didst astonish the onlookers, O valiantly wise sufferer Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Having been suspended by the iniquity of the tormentor, and torn with iron scrapers, and stained with the drops of thy blood unjustly shed, O martyr, thou didst put off the garments of grossness and mortal corruption, O most blessed Theodore.

*Theotokion:*
Tear asunder with the divine lance the wicked handwriting of my sins, O most pure one, which pierced the divine side of Him Who was pleased to become incarnate of thee, O Virgin, and pray that I, who have foolishly departed from God, may be written in the book of the saved.

*Another [Canon, of the Prophet]. Heirmos:*
From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, the prophet foresaw Thee in divine vision as desiring to become incarnate of the only Mother of God, and with fear he glorified Thy power.

*Troparion [of the Prophet]:*
Clearly dost thou teach us, O all-blessed one, to honor one illuminating Essence, defined in three Persons, multiplied in radiant processions.

*Troparion:*
Having become the chariot of God, O prophet, beholding the diverse angelic chariots, thou wast counted worthy to encompass all who walk with good order.

*Troparion:*
Hierarchically dost thou behold the all-radiant lampstand, the image of the divine radiance; and thou lookest upon the All-Seeing One, O all-blessed one, by Whom all things are saved.

*Theotokion:*
The God-beholding prophets prefigured the type of thy pure childbearing, having prefigured it in many forms, and in diverse enigmas, O divinely wise ones.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
When Isaiah beheld in a figure God exalted upon a throne, borne aloft by angels of glory, Woe is me, wretched one! he cried aloud: for I have foreseen God becoming incarnate, the Unwaning Light, and ruling over the world.

*Troparion:*
The divine elder, having understood the glory manifested of old unto the prophet, beholding the Word held by motherly hands, cried aloud: O rejoice, pure one! For as a throne dost thou hold God, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over the world.

*Troparion:*
The elder bowed down, and divinely touching the footsteps of the unwedded Mother of God, said: Pure one, thou carriest fire, fearing to embrace God as an infant, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over the world.

*Troparion:*
Isaiah is cleansed, having received a coal from a seraph, the elder cried aloud unto the Mother of God: Thou, as with tongs, dost enlighten me with thy hands, having bestowed Him Whom thou carriest, the Unwaning Light, Who rules over the world.

*Another [Canon, of the Martyr]. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has enveloped me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my paths, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
Enlightened by the ray of the Divine Spirit, O martyr, and kindled with love for Christ, thou didst not care for thy scorched flesh, nor didst thou feel pain, nor consider torments, being nourished by eternal hopes.

*Troparion [of the Martyr]:*
Bearing again upon thy neck the lightest yoke, though weighed down by a burden of lead, O most blessed passion-bearer, thou wast by no means bent down, nor shaken, nor overcome, O valiant warrior Theodore.

*Troparion:*
The most good Word Who called thee from heaven made thee spiritual, by Whose power thou didst endure the bitter pains of the lawless, O martyr of Christ, and torments, and a violent death, O glorious one.

*Theotokion:*
Entreat Him, having borne the Good Word Who delivered us from unreason, that I may abide whole, unshaken in the better stance, but walking uprightly and performing good deeds well-pleasing unto God.

*Another [Canon, of the Prophet]. Heirmos:*
From the night of ignorance, having enlightened the ends of the earth with divine knowledge, enlighten me with the morning light of Thy loving-kindness, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
With a pure heart, and foreseeing by the keenness of the mind the passion of the Savior, he is worthily blessed.

*Troparion:*
Supernaturally, rejoice, O holy Zion! the prophet beforehand commands: behold, thy King comes, righteous and saving in meekness.

*Troparion:*
The glory of the prophets, the splendor of God-proclaiming men, illumines the world with the radiance of prophecy.

*Theotokion:*
Through the Virgin [or: By the Tree][^295] renewing us, He freed us from the first transgressions, having become man for our sake.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Having seen with his eyes the salvation that came from God unto the people, the elder cried aloud unto Thee: O Christ, Thou art my God!

*Troparion:*
Unto Zion Thou wast laid as a stone, a stumbling-block unto the disobedient, and a stone of offense: the indestructible salvation of the faithful.

*Troparion:*
Clearly bearing the impress of Him Who brought Thee forth before the ages, through compassion Thou hast now clothed Thyself in the weakness of earthly men.

*Troparion:*
Unto the Son of the Most High, the Son of the Virgin, God Who has become an infant, having now bowed down unto Thee, let me depart in peace.

*Another [Canon, of the Martyr]. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my griefs, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades; and like Jonah I pray: From corruption, O God, lead me up!

*Troparion:*
Accomplishing the passion of the Passionless One, thou wast crucified, O glorious martyr, having been nailed upon a tree, O most blessed one, and wast an imitator of the Master, Who loosed by the Cross the painful passions of men.

*Troparion:*
Suspended like a lamb upon a tree, and shot through with arrows, O blessed one, and the pupils of thine eyes gouged out, and wounded in all thy body, thou didst wound the hearts of the enemies with the lance of thy suffering.

*Troparion:*
The torrents of immeasurable torments, dashing against the dwelling of thy soul, O martyr, shook it not; for it was established upon the immovable Rock, Who shone forth from the Virgin and enlightened the human race.

*Theotokion:*
O Mary, Mistress of the whole world, as having given birth unto the Lord of all, deliver me from the slavery of the enemy, and snatch me from his torment and save me, who unhesitatingly set my hope upon thee.

*Another [Canon, of the Prophet]. Heirmos:*
*The same [as the preceding]:* "I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord..."

*Troparion:*
A son full of prophetic inspiration, having learned beyond nature the understanding of future things, thou wast sent to proclaim the coming grace, the known day of the Cross of the Lord, O God-receiver.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with spiritual understanding, thou wast counted worthy of gifts beyond the mind, having seen angelic apparitions and ranks, and utterances and glories, O exceedingly rich one, teaching marvelous and divine mysteries.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired the lamp of virtues, thou shinest with the illumination of divine knowledge, mystically proclaiming beforehand the lamp by which, beyond word, the incarnation of the Word should appear in divine beauty from Zion, O prophet Zechariah, receiver of God.

*Theotokion:*
He Who established the heaven showed thee, O Mother of God, to be a spiritual heaven, having loved the beauty of Jacob; and from thy womb the Sun of the Godhead shone forth in the flesh, and illumined the world by grace.

*Kontakion [of the Martyr], Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having clad thyself in faith with courage of soul, and having taken the word of God into thy hand as a spear, thou didst conquer the enemy, O Theodore, greatest among martyrs: with them, cease not to pray unto Christ God for us all.

*Ikos:*
The most radiant beauty of the sufferers, come, all ye faithful, let us crown Theodore with songs as with wreaths! For a great gift of God has appeared unto the world with the radiance of miracles; having conquered the enemy Belial by honorable sufferings, instead of drops of blood he rains down streams of healings; with all these he rejoices in Christ and gives abundant peace: therefore we cry aloud unto him: Pray unceasingly for us all!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thee Who in the fire didst bedew the Children who spoke of God, and didst dwell in an incorrupt Virgin, we hymn, God the Word, devoutly singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion [of the Feast]:*
Desiring to declare unto Adam, for I go unto him living in Hades, and to bring glad tidings unto Eve, Symeon cried aloud, dancing with the prophets: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
God, delivering the earthly race, shall come even unto Hades; and He shall grant remission unto all captives, and sight unto the blind, so that even the mute shall cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
A sword shall pierce thine own heart also, O incorrupt one, Symeon proclaimed unto the Mother of God, when thou seest thy Son upon the Cross, unto Whom we cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Another [Canon, of the Martyr]. Heirmos:*
The fire of old in Babylon was ashamed before the condescension of God; therefore the Children, dancing in the furnace with joyful step as in a meadow, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
He proclaimed rejoicing the greatness of the Almighty; therefore the onlookers, marveling, abandoned the tyranny of deceit and cried aloud in faith: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
With the cords of thy most wise words, O most blessed one, rejoicing thou didst catch those who approached from the depth of ignorance, and didst make them divine martyrs, O Theodore, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
A holy angel, having come, loosed thee when bound from the prison unto the tree, granting boldness and strengthening thee to cry with a loud voice, O Theodore: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Upon me, who have lived in slothfulness and despised Thy holy laws and Thy precious commandments, O Christ, have compassion and save me for the sake of her who bore Thee, for Thou art a beneficent God and abundant in mercy.

*Another [Canon, of the Prophet]. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew Children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Throughout all the earth went forth thy God-inspired utterance, O prophet, and the power of thy words, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Enlightened throughout with rays from on high, thou didst behold future things as present: therefore, O exceedingly rich one, thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
With songs I honor thee, O Virgin, who didst honor the human race, which before was condemned to grievous dishonor: blessed is the fruit of thy womb, O all-immaculate one!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Standing together in the unendurable fire, championing godliness, the youths, unharmed by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
People of Israel, beholding your glory, Emmanuel, the Child born of the Virgin, dance now before the face of the divine ark: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Behold, Symeon cried aloud, this Child shall be a sign spoken against, being God and an Infant; unto Him, ye faithful, let us cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
This Child, being Life, having become an infant, God the Word, shall be the fall of the disobedient, as He is the resurrection unto all who sing in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Another [Canon, of the Martyr]. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having valiantly struggled, thou didst conquer the enemy and destroy his armies with the weapons of faith; therefore thou wast crowned with the wreath of victory, singing unto the Master: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
After the crucifixion, O glorious one, after the blessed passion, after the immeasurable wounds, O blessed one, thou didst bow thy neck unto God, O wise martyr; thy precious head was cut off by the sword, as thou didst cry unto the Master: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thy sacred and much-suffering body, O Theodore, became a fountain of healings for the faithful, from which those who devoutly draw obtain release from passions and salvation of souls, crying aloud unto the Master: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Quicken my deadened soul, raise up the fallen, heal the wounded, calm my mind, still the waves of misfortunes, O Virgin, and save me who cry aloud: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt the Lord above all unto all ages!

*Another [Canon, of the Prophet]. Heirmos:*
Hymn the Lord Who was glorified upon the holy mountain, and Who in the bush by fire revealed the mystery of the Ever-Virgin unto Moses; and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Rejoice greatly, O Zion, mountain and holy city! the divine Zechariah, companion of angels, commands; and be glad, O Jerusalem, unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Be glad, O prophet Zechariah, beholding the fulfillment of the words which thou didst speak beforehand, being enlightened by the Spirit, unto those who hymn the Lord and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
In the midst of the overshadowed mountains thou didst behold standing angels, who taught thee the providence that is over all, O glorious prophet, as they hymn Christ unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, fiery throne of the Lord! Rejoice, portal of immaterial radiance! Rejoice, light cloud who didst cause the Sun of Righteousness to shine forth upon the world, O pure Mother of God!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
In the law of the shadow and the scriptures we the faithful perceive a figure: every male that opens the womb is holy unto God. Therefore the firstborn Word of the beginningless Father, the firstborn Son of an unwedded Mother, do we magnify.

*Troparion:*
A pair of turtledoves was the ancient custom for the newborn, and two young pigeons; in place of which the divine elder and the chaste prophetess Anna minister unto Him Who was born of the Virgin, the only-begotten Son of the Father, when He is brought into the temple, magnifying Him.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast granted unto me, Symeon cried aloud, the joy of Thy salvation, O Christ; receive Thy servant who is wearied by the shadow, the mystical sacred proclaimer of the new grace, who magnifies Thee in praise!

*Troparion:*
Anna, prophesying, chaste and venerable, the aged woman, divinely acknowledged the Master openly in the temple; and proclaiming the Mother of God unto all present, she magnified her.

*Another [Canon, of the Martyr]. Heirmos:*
Heaven was astonished at this, and the ends of the earth marveled, that God appeared in the flesh unto men, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens: therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst sanctify thyself, O God-wise martyr, unto the Lamb and our God Who was sacrificed; thou didst ascend unto Him as a sweet-smelling whole-burnt offering, and a sacred oblation, perfect and pure, an acceptable and pure sacrifice: therefore we praise thee, and duly call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Like the dawn, like a radiant day hast thou appeared, O Theodore, with the bright rays of thy suffering and miracles, O all-blessed one, devoutly enlightening the world under the sun, and driving away the darkness of delusion and of our passions, O Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst join thyself in the flesh unto the angels, having conquered bodiless enemies, and dost ever rejoice with the choirs of sufferers; and with them thou dost raise the divine song unto the Trinity, O thrice-blessed Theodore, luminary of the Church, and adornment of passion-bearers.

*Troparion:*
The earth rejoices at the laying down of thy precious body, O Theodore, and heaven rejoices having acquired thy sacred spirit; as we men ever keep thy honorable memory, suppliantly we entreat thee, O most blessed one, to be an intercessor for us.

*Theotokion:*
O Bride of God, Virgin, pray unto the Good One to save me from all defilements, having washed the wounds of my heart with pure repentance and weeping with tears, that I may call thee blessed and magnify thee, O all-immaculate one.

*Another [Canon, of the Prophet]. Heirmos:*
Thee, the unwedded Mother of God the Most High; thee, who beyond understanding didst truly give birth unto God by the Word, who art higher than the all-pure Hosts, with unceasing hymns of glory do we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Having abandoned heavy sensual perception, thou standest radiant before the divine and purest Mind, O all-wise one, making prayers for the world; therefore we all call thee blessed, O Zechariah.

*Troparion:*
Following the words of thy lips, O God-revealed one, we call upon the Lord, by Whom being saved, we devoutly call thee blessed, O Zechariah, as a prophet of truth and a proclaimer.

*Troparion:*
Make the Master merciful through thy prayers, O God-inspired one, unto those who celebrate in faith thy sacred and glorious feast, and who honor thee with love as a prophet and truest proclaimer of God.

*Theotokion:*
Knowing thee, O pure Mother of God, as an abyss of wonders and an ocean of gifts, and clearly understanding this, hoping in thy protection we have recourse unto thee, and speedily flee beneath thy shelter.

*Exapostilarion of the Martyr, Podoben: "In Spirit in the Sanctuary" (Дꙋ́хомъ во ст҃и́лищи):*
The regiments of the enemies and the insolence of demons thou didst utterly overthrow, and thou didst receive the end, O blessed Theodore, for the faith of thy Creator, as a witness of truth, saving all from all afflictions and alien harm.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast:*
Having stood in spirit in the sanctuary, the elder received into his arms the Master of the Law, crying aloud: Now release me from the bonds of the flesh in peace, as Thou hast said; for mine eyes have seen the revelation of the Gentiles, and the salvation of Israel.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "In Praise" (Восхвале́нїи):*
As truly the all-radiant ark of God, the pure Virgin, having brought Christ today as our cleansing, carries Him into the temple, and reverently places Him into the arms of the God-wise Symeon; therefore the Holy of Holies has now been sanctified, and rejoices together in the One Holy God.

*Verse:*
Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word.

*Sticheron:*
Symeon was glorified today, having received Christ divinely as a live coal, and he kisses the eyes and lips; and he confesses and rejoices, and asks for his release. Him therefore do we all now devoutly call blessed, honoring him with unceasing songs.

*Verse:*
A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.

*Sticheron:*
The all-animate pure Virgin today carries the King and Lord, Whom she had within herself, into the temple, as He enters through the doors of old mystically shut on earth; therefore, dancing before her face, all of us praise her with divine songs.

*Glory... of the Saint, Tone 2:*
Having accomplished the contest of sufferers, thou didst act manfully against delusion, O Theodore; with thy fiery-radiant tongue thou didst trample down the inhumanity of Licinius. Therefore, rejoicing with the angels, O blessed one, pray unto Christ God that He may be merciful unto us in the day of judgment.

*Both now... of the Feast, Tone 5:*
Search the Scriptures, as Christ our God said in the Gospels: for in them we find Him being born, and wrapped in swaddling clothes, laid in a manger, and nourished with milk, receiving circumcision, and carried by Symeon, not in fantasy nor in phantom appearance, but in truth appearing unto the world; unto Whom let us cry aloud: O Pre-eternal God, glory to Thee!

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 8; and of the Saint, Ode 6.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall set his hope upon Him.

*Verse: Give ear, O God, unto my voice,* when I pray unto Thee.

*Epistle to Timothy, pericope 292.*[^296]

*Alleluia, Tone 4:*
*Verse: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree; he shall be multiplied like a cedar that is in Lebanon [LXX Ps. 91:13].*

*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 36.*[^297]

*Communion Hymn:*
The righteous man shall be in everlasting remembrance [LXX Ps. 111:6].

*In the same month, on the ninth day: The Holy Martyr Nikephoros.*

*On this day the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord is leavetaken. If, however, the Apodosis of the feast does not occur on this day, it is sung thus:*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Submitting to the laws of Him Who for our sake received flesh upon the earth and endured the honorable Passion, O martyr, divinely blessed one, thou didst preserve love toward thy neighbor, which is the depth of the Law and the Prophets; therefore thou didst also receive a blessed end, now standing before the Wellspring of love, O Nikephoros.

Thou bowest thy neck unto God, before Whom every knee bows, O marvelous martyr, being beheaded in the head, and separated from thy noble body, but united with Christ, the Head of all, with a most pure mind; drawing near unto Whose light now, O Nikephoros, ask thou enlightenment for all of us who praise thee.

With the streams of thy blood shed for Christ, O wise one, thou didst wash the earth, and in spirit thou didst gladden the spirits of the bodiless hosts, and didst enlighten all the flocks of the martyrs, being united with them, O blessed one, as a valiant armor-bearer, as an unconquerable martyr, and praying unto God for us.

*Glory..., Tone 6. By John the Monk:*
Thou hast plainly shown unto all, O sufferer Nikephoros, that he who does not love his neighbor cannot love God either. Therefore thou didst purely love Saprikios thy neighbor himself; from thence, drawn toward divine desire, thou didst lay down thy life for the confession and faith of Christ. But Saprikios of evil name, having acquired an implacable hatred toward thee, showed himself an apostate from Christ the Master; before Whom thou now standest, pray that our souls may be saved.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
A sword, as Symeon said, pierced thy heart, O most pure Sovereign Lady, when thou beheldest Him Who shone forth from thee by an ineffable Word, being lifted up upon the Cross as one condemned by the lawless, given vinegar and gall to drink, His side pierced, and His hands and feet nailed; and sobbing thou didst cry aloud, weeping maternally: What is this sweetest wonder, this new mystery?

*Troparion of the Martyr, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr, O Lord, Nikephoros, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the tormentors and crushed the powerless audacities of the demons. By his supplications save our souls.

*Canon of the Saint.*
*Whose acrostic is: "The glorious among passion-bearers, Nikephoros, I praise in hymns." A creation of Theophanes. Tone 8.*[^298]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The chariot-driver Pharaoh did the wonderworking rod of Moses once submerge, striking the sea in the sign of the cross and dividing it; but it saved fleeing Israel as a foot-traveler, who sang a song unto God.

*Troparion:*
Raised up by the sea of life, tempestuously tossing the ship of my soul with temptations and passions, O Author of peace, as Benefactor, translate me unto a deep calm, O Christ, by the prayers of the martyr.

*Troparion:*
With prudent mind, O all-wise one, thou didst piously subdue the passion of wrath in thy rational soul, and didst hasten unto thy neighbor Saprikios, striving to be reconciled, O marvelous one, in obedience to the laws of the Savior.

*Trinity Troparion:*
Equal in power, of one nature, enthroned together, and consubstantial: the tri-radiant dawn of the one Godhead do we honor and confess in three Persons, proclaiming undivided the Word with the Father and the Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
No heavenly mind is truly able to comprehend thy unfathomable mystery, O all-hymned one;[^299] for He Who is enthroned together with the Father took up His abode within thy womb, and was well pleased to be born of thee, acknowledged in two natures.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments;

establish me, for there is none holy save Thee, O Thou only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Being named Nikephoros, thou didst show thyself a trophy-bearer in deeds; for destroying the darkness of delusion by the light of grace, [delivered] from the fire, thou didst cry aloud: There is none holy save Thee, O Thou only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Believing in the Master Who was put to death for thy sake, thou didst hasten willingly unto death, having desired His voluntary deadness, strengthened by divine power as a trophy-bearing martyr.

*Troparion:*
A contender upon earth, a crowned bearer in the heavens: Nikephoros, the well-pleasing servant of Christ, was clearly shown forth, crying aloud with the bodiless ranks: There is none holy save Thee, O Thou only Lover of mankind.

*Trinity Troparion:*
From the great Intellect, the Father, the Word proceeds forth, and the Holy Spirit goes forth; being faithfully enlightened by divinely inspired commands, we confess one uncreated Godhead.

*Theotokion:*
Having conceived without husband within thy womb Him Who was born of the Father before the ages, O Virgin, thou didst give birth for us unto God, together both God and man, perfect in each nature, undivided.

*Kontakion, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй):*
Bound by the bond of love, O Nikephoros, thou didst plainly destroy the malice of hatred; and beheaded in thy head by the sword, thou becamest a divine martyr of the incarnate Savior; Whom pray thou for us, who praise thy glorious memory.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*
Thou didst steep a purple robe in thy blood, and didst clothe thyself with it as an unconquered warrior, and holding the Cross as a scepter, thou didst ascend from earth unto the heavens, O blessed Nikephoros, and standest before the King of all. Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who through thee works healings for all!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thy divine hands, by which thou didst bear the Creator, Who became incarnate in goodness, stretch forth, O all-pure Virgin, and entreat Him to deliver from temptations, and passions, and afflictions, us who praise thee with love and cry aloud: Glory to Him Who dwelt within thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who delivered us by thy childbearing!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Christ, all-immaculate Mother, dead and stretched out upon the Cross, she cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, by which Thou hast saved the creation of Thy most pure hands, O Compassionate One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power; Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who hast not left the bosom of the Father, yet hast visited our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habbakuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Divine life hast thou obtained, O Nikephoros, counted worthy of the divine radiance, O blessed one; with divine light thou now luminous-like illuminest those who keep thy memory, O divinely wise one, and faithfully cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Joined unto the martyrs of Christ,[^300] thou didst destroy the polytheistic delusion, being beheaded in thy head by the sword, and with the streams of thy blood thou didst drown all idolatrous error, crying aloud unto the Master: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Saprikios, having not kept Thy laws, is stripped of Thy divine grace, O Savior, and turning his back unto the enemies, the wretched one is deprived of the glory of Thy martyrs; therefore, marveling at Thy hymn-worthy providence, we cry:[^301] Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Trinity Troparion:*
Light is the Father, unoriginate and Cause of all; Light is the Word; Light is the Holy Spirit: from the Father as from a wellspring shining forth ineffably before all ages, the tri-solar Godhead is acknowledged in one essence, power, and glory.

*Theotokion:*
This man He renews in thee, being wholly united unto all, O all-pure one, not having left the Father's bosom, yet being pleased to dwell within thy womb: He Who through the richness of His tender compassion willingly became poor, and enriched the world with Godhead.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O never-setting Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
With the streams of thy blood thou didst water the paradise of the Church, divided into four heads of divine virtues, O blessed one; from which, O Nikephoros, we draw divine gifts for our souls.

*Troparion:*
The sower of malice through remembrance of wrongs carried off the wretched one; but the Author of goodness and peace drew in his stead Nikephoros, the divinely wise martyr, adorned with divine light-bearing beauty.

*Troparion:*
By his beheading,[^302] O Savior, assimilating himself unto Thy voluntary Passion—for the divinely wise one, sprinkled with the drops from Thy side, brings the streams of his own blood unto Thee, being cut down by the sword—he came unto Thee.

*Trinity Troparion:*
Knowing Orthodoxy to be the foundation of salvation, we preach the Word and the Holy Spirit as co-unoriginate with the Father: one Origin in three Persons, one Essence, one Will, one Operation.

*Theotokion:*
Spiritually was the choir of the Prophets taught thy mysteries; for the Master showed thee forth upon earth as the door of heaven, and having become incarnate of thee, O Virgin, the Sun of righteousness shone upon those who were in darkness.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
An abyss of sins and a tempest of transgressions trouble me, and cast me into the depth of dire despair; but stretch forth Thy strong hand unto me, as unto Peter, O Steersman, and save me!

*Troparion:*
Heal the contrition of my soul, O divinely wise Nikephoros, and destroying the darkness of passions, drive it away by the rays of the divine brightness that is within thee, praying unto Christ the Redeemer, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Having calmly sailed across the tempest of idolatrous frenzy by the sail of the Cross and by spiritual breezes, O sufferer Nikephoros, unconquerable martyr, thou didst reach the calm harbor of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst suffer after the manner of martyrs, and in truth becamest a trophy-bearer, powerfully destroying the wiles of the serpent; and having become a partaker of the passions of the Master, O blessed one, thou art now deservedly glorified together with Him.

*Theotokion:*
The spiritual angelic ranks are struck with awe and marvel, beholding Him Who before the ages was with the Father and the Spirit being born in the flesh as an Infant from thy womb, O most pure Mother of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
Inflamed, O glorious one, with the love of the Lord, and taking His glorious Cross upon thy shoulder, thou didst put to shame the devil's snares; thou didst suffer even unto death, and wast revealed as a trophy-bearer of truth, an armor-bearer, and an initiate of the grace of God.

*Ikos:*
Thou didst plainly love the teaching of Paul, and didst plant it in thy breast, O glorious one, crying aloud: Love does not behave unseemly [1 Cor. 13:5]; this love bestowed upon us the Creator as perfect man; for love's sake He endured all things: the nails and the Cross, vinegar and spittings, and was pierced with a spear in His holy side, whereby He poured forth for us blood and deifying water. Having desired Him, thou wast revealed as a trophy-bearer, and by calling an armor-bearer, and an initiate of the grace of God.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire was abashed before the condescension of God in Babylon of old; therefore the children, dancing in the furnace with joyful step as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Death became for thee a departure from things here below, O blessed Nikephoros; and unto heaven and heavenly things thou hast now ascended rejoicing, singing with the angels: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the passions of the Almighty, thou didst cast down to the earth the haughty eye of weakness and the exalted brow, O divinely wise one, crying aloud unto thy Creator: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Running the course of his testimony, Nikephoros finished it; having enlarged his heart by the working of the Holy Spirit, he now fervently cries unto Thee, O Master: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Trinity Troparion:*
Knowing Thee as the Father, the first and unoriginate Mind, Who didst beget the co-unoriginate Word dispassionately and timelessly, and from Whom the all-holy Spirit proceeds, we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast an image, O Virgin Mother, pure Birthgiver of God, having ineffably conceived within thy womb God, Who became incarnate of thee, acknowledged in two forms, Who lived on earth as God-man.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously heat the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a greater Power, he cried aloud to the Creator and Redeemer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Marked with the grace of the Savior, uncalled didst thou valiantly array thyself for the contests, and having won the victory over the adversaries, O martyr of Christ, thou wast adorned with crowns of victory, divinely singing: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Cleansed by water and the Spirit, O Nikephoros, and stained with the blood of martyrdom, thou hast acquired, as a true martyr of Christ God, light-bearing splendor, O all-blessed one, in which thou now criest aloud: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Trinity Troparion:*
With the bodiless ranks, the one ever-flowing wellspring of goodness in three Persons, the unbegotten, unoriginate, and all-perfect Godhead, Creator and King of all: ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast brighter, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God, than the transcendent angelic order; for thou didst give birth unto their Creator and Lord from a virginal, unwedded womb: God incarnate in two essences, unconfused and immutable, but in one Hypostasis.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
At this heaven was struck with awe, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens. Therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst present thyself as a bridegroom adorned with vesture woven from the blood of martyrdom, through participation in the divine brightness of the Benefactor of all, Who adorned and beautified Himself on the Cross by His honorable Passion, O glorious and all-blessed Nikephoros.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast attracted by the beauties of the Master, than which nothing is more fair, O glorious one; and striving to enjoy His unceasing and majestic brightness, inflamed with love for thy Lord, O Nikephoros, thou didst yield unto the torturers to have thy head cut off with the sword.

*Troparion:*
Having brightly contended against delusion, O martyr of Christ, and having conquered it as a victor, thou didst receive upon thy head a crown of gifts, O glorious one; and now thou dost dance as a martyr, with the martyric choirs, O all-blessed Nikephoros, before the throne of the King of all.

*Trinity Troparion:*
Divinely glorifying the Son begotten of the unbegotten Father, and the upright Spirit proceeding before the ages, we honor the three Hypostases of the transcendent and primordial Trinity, united in unconfused unity, and praising God we magnify Them.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth in the flesh beyond nature unto Him Who appeared, O Virgin Mother of God, the all-good Word, Whom the Father as good poured forth from His heart before all ages, Whom we now understand to be higher than bodily forms, even though He clothed Himself in flesh.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "He Who adorned the heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*[^303]
Thou hast shown thyself true to thy name of victory, O Nikephoros, having conquered the tormentors by love and martyrdom; therefore thou hast also received a crown of victory from the Lord.

*Theotokion:*
By thy sovereign protection preserve all of us, thy servants, unharmed from the assault of enemies, O pure one; for thee have we acquired as our only refuge in necessities.

*Order concerning the Apodosis of the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord.*
*It should be known that if the Apodosis of the Meeting should occur before the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee on a weekday, on whatever day, except Sunday:*
*In the evening, after the usual Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the feast on 6, sung as on the feast itself. Glory... Both now... of the feast, Idiomelon.*
*If it be on Friday evening: Glory... of the feast; Both now... Theotokion of the current tone. Prokeimenon of the day. There is no Entrance and no readings.*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the feast, with their verses. Glory... Both now... of the feast.*
*After the Trisagion, Troparion of the feast. Then litany and dismissal.*
*At Compline, after the Trisagion, Kontakion of the feast.*
*We sing Midnight Office as usual, with the Kathisma and the usual troparia.*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the feast, thrice.*
*After both Kathismata, Sessional hymns of the feast, and readings.*
*Canon of the feast, with the Heirmos on 14; the Heirmos twice, Katavasia the same Heirmos.*
*After Ode 3, Sessional hymn of the feast, twice.*
*After Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the feast, and reading in the Prologue.*
*At Ode 9 we do not sing "More honorable," but we sing the refrains of the feast, as on the feast itself.*
*Exapostilarion of the feast, twice.*
*At the Praises, Stichera of the feast on 4; Glory... Both now... Idiomelon of the feast; after this, Great Doxology.*
*After the Trisagion, Troparion of the feast, once. Then litanies, and dismissal, and the usual departure into the narthex, and the First Hour.*
*At the Hours, Troparion and Kontakion of the feast.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes, Ode 9 with the Heirmos on 6.*
*After the Entrance, Troparion of the feast; Glory... Both now... Kontakion of the feast.*
*Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Hymn of the feast. Epistle and Gospel of the day.*

*(Note:)* And of the saint that has occurred we sing the service at Compline, or as the superior shall determine.[^304]

*If however the Apodosis of the feast of the Meeting of the Lord should occur on a Sunday,[^305] before the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee:*
*On Saturday evening, at Little Vespers, Resurrectional Stichera as usual. At the Aposticha, one Resurrectional Sticheron, and the Aposticha of the feast with the verses of Great Vespers: Glory... Both now... of the feast. Resurrectional Troparion; Glory... Both now... of the feast.*
*At Great Vespers, after the Introductory Psalm, we sing the whole Kathisma "Blessed is the man." At "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3, and one Anatolian; and of the feast, 6, sung as on the feast itself: Glory... of the feast; Both now... Theotokion of Tone 1.*
*At the Litiya, Stichera of the feast: Glory... Both now... of the feast.*
*At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera: Glory... Both now... of the feast.*
*At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion: "Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God," twice; and of the feast, once; and reading in the Apostolic Epistles.*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Resurrectional Troparion, twice; Glory... Both now... of the feast; after both Kathismata, Resurrectional Sessional hymns, with their Theotokia, and reading in the Commentary Gospel; and Polyeleos, and the Troparia "The Angelic Host," Hypakoe of the tone, and reading.*
*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi] and Prokeimenon of the tone. "Let every breath." Resurrection Gospel; and the rest Resurrectional up to the Canon.*
*Resurrectional Canon with the Heirmos on 4, and of the Birthgiver of God on 2, and of the feast on 8. Katavasia of the feast: "The sun once shone upon the dry land of the deep..."*
*After Ode 3, Kontakion of the feast and Ikos, and Sessional hymn of the feast, twice.*
*After Ode 6, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos; and we read the Prologue.*
*At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable."*
*Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory... Both now... of the feast.*
*At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, 4, and of the feast with the Doxastikon on 4, with festal verses. Glory... Gospel Sticheron; Both now... "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God"; and Great Doxology.*
*After the Trisagion, the Resurrectional Troparion only. Litanies, and dismissal, and the Studite Catechesis; and the usual departure into the narthex, and the First Hour.*
*At the Hours, Resurrectional Troparion; Glory... of the feast; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the feast, and final dismissal; likewise at the other Hours we say the troparia and kontakia.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the tone on 6, and of the feast, Ode 4 on 4. After the Entrance, Resurrectional Troparion, and of the feast: Glory... Resurrectional Kontakion; Both now... Kontakion of the feast. Prokeimenon of the tone, and of the feast; Epistle and Gospel of the Sunday in course, and of the feast. Communion Hymn: Praise the Lord from the heavens [Ps. 148:1];*

*another [Communion Hymn] of the feast. And of the daily saint who has occurred on this Sunday we sing the service at Compline, or as the superior shall determine.*

*Order concerning the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord: on whatever day the Meeting shall be, or on whatever day its Apodosis shall occur.*
*If the feast of the Meeting should occur on the Sunday of the Prodigal Son, or on Monday, or on Tuesday of Meatfare Week, the feast is leavetaken on Friday of that same week, since on Meatfare Sunday there is no Apodosis.*
*(Note:)* *If the Meeting of the Lord should occur on Wednesday, or on Thursday, or on Friday, or on Saturday of Meatfare Week, it is leavetaken on Tuesday of Cheesefare Week.*
*If it should occur on Meatfare Sunday, or on Monday of Cheesefare Week, it is leavetaken on Thursday of that same week.*
*If it should occur on Tuesday or on Wednesday of Cheesefare Week, it is leavetaken on Saturday of that same week.*
*If it should occur on Cheesefare Thursday, or on Friday, or on Saturday, it is leavetaken on Cheesefare Sunday.*
*If the Meeting should be on Cheesefare Sunday, then we celebrate for one day.*

*(Note:)* *If the Apodosis of the Meeting of the Lord should occur on the Sunday of the Pharisee [and Publican], or of the Prodigal Son, or on Cheesefare Sunday:*
*On Saturday, at Little Vespers, Resurrectional Stichera of the tone, as usual. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Sticheron, and the Aposticha of the feast from Great Vespers.*
*At Great Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and of the Triodion, 3; and of the feast with the Doxastikon, 4. Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... Theotokion 1 of the tone. If it be the temple of the Meeting, Both now... of the feast.*
*At the Litiya, Stichera of the feast, and of the Triodion from the Praises, without the Doxastikon: Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast.*
*At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast.*
*At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion: "Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God," twice; and of the feast, once.*
*At "God is the Lord," Resurrectional Troparion, twice; Glory... Both now... of the feast.*
*After the Kathismata, Resurrectional Sessional hymns, and their Theotokia; and reading. And we sing the Polyeleos, the Psalm: "Praise ye the name of the Lord" [Ps. 134], and the second Psalm: "Give thanks unto the Lord" [Ps. 135].*
*(Note:)* *If it be the Sunday of the Prodigal Son, or Cheesefare Sunday, we sing also the third Psalm: "By the rivers of Babylon" [Ps. 136], with Alleluia; and the rest Resurrectional, up to the Canon. Resurrectional Canon on 4; and of the Triodion on 4;*

...to 4, and of the feast to 6. Katavasia of the feast. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Sessional hymn of the Triodion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the feast and Ikos. At Ode 9, we sing the More Honorable [Magnificat]. Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... of the feast.

At the Praises, Resurrectional stichera, 4; and of the feast with the Doxastikon, 4; Glory... of the Triodion; Both now... "Most blessed art thou..."

*If it be Cheesefare Sunday:*

*and after the stichera of the feast we say the refrains: "Arise, O Lord my God" [Ps. 9:33]; and we sing the Idiomelon sticheron of the Triodion for the day; Glory..., another sticheron; Both now... "Most blessed art thou..."; and after the dismissal, Glory... Both now..., the Gospel sticheron. And the First Hour. At the Hours, Resurrectional troparion; Glory... of the feast; Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," the Kontakion of the feast and of the Triodion, saying them alternately. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone to 4, and of the Triodion Ode 3 to 4, and of the feast Ode 4 to 4. After the Entrance, Resurrectional troparion, and of the feast; Glory..., Kontakion of the Triodion; Both now..., of the feast. Prokeimenon of the day, and of the feast.*

*(Note:)* *On Meatfare Saturday, however, the Apodosis of the Meeting does not take place: see likewise also on Meatfare Sunday.*

*If the Apodosis of the Meeting of the Lord fall on Tuesday or Thursday of Cheesefare Week, we sing thus:*

*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the feast to 6; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion for the day, twice, and the Martyrikon, with the customary refrains; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Troparion of the feast, and dismissal. But the service of the saint of the day we sing at Compline, or as the superior shall determine. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," troparion of the feast, thrice. After the Kathismata, Sessional hymns of the feast. Canon of the feast with the heirmos to 14. But where there is a triodion [three-ode canon], we sing of the feast with the heirmos to 6, and of the Triodion to 8. After Ode 3, Sessional hymn of the Triodion, written after the second Kathisma; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the feast and Ikos. At Ode 9, we sing the More Honorable. Exapostilarion of the feast, twice. At the Praises, stichera of the feast to 4; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion for the day, twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... Both now... of the feast; troparion of the feast; litany, and dismissal. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the feast to 6, Ode 4. After the Entrance, troparion of the feast; Glory... Both now... Kontakion of the feast; Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion hymn of the feast; Apostle and Gospel of the day. On Wednesday and Friday, however, the Apodosis of the Meeting does not take place does not take place.*

*If the Apodosis of the Meeting fall on Cheesefare Saturday:*

*On Cheesefare Friday evening, at "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the feast, 3; and of the Fathers, 3[^306]; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon of the day, and the reading [paremia]; and after it, the prokeimenon: "Let Israel hope in the Lord" [Ps. 130:3].*

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion for the day, twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. Troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," troparion of the feast, twice; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. After the Kathismata, Sessional hymns of the feast. Canon of the feast with the heirmos to 8, and of the Fathers to 6. Katavasia of the feast. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of the Fathers; and their Sessional hymns, two; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the feast and Ikos. At Ode 9, we sing the More Honorable. Exapostilarion of the feast; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. At the Praises, stichera of the feast, 3; and of the Fathers, 3; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the feast. Great Doxology; troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the feast; and the customary exit into the narthex, and the First Hour, and the final dismissal. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the feast, Ode 4, to 4; and of the Fathers, Ode 6, to 4. After the Entrance, troparion of the feast and of the Fathers; Glory..., Kontakion of the Fathers; Both now..., Kontakion of the feast. Prokeimenon of the feast and of the Fathers; Apostle and Gospel of the day and of the Fathers; Alleluia of the feast and of the Fathers; Communion hymn of the feast and of the Fathers.*

*In the same month, on the 10th day: The Holy Martyr Charalampes.*[^307]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*

*Sticheron:*
Having dedicated thyself wholly from youth unto the Master, thou didst desire Him, and didst follow in His footsteps; and having cleansed thyself from all defilement of passions, thou wast enriched by divine grace to work healings, and, renowned in performing miracles, thou becamest a martyr, remaining unmoved by every assault of tortures through the power of Him Who was sacrificed upon the Cross, Whom do thou fervently entreat for our souls.

*Sticheron:*
When thy blessed and patient body was pierced with nails, O sufferer Charalampes, keeping an unyielding soul and an unassailable mind, though broken: for divine desire richly inflamed thee, and urged thee to endure every form of tortures, O martyr, passion-bearer, partaker of the sufferings of Christ, Whom do thou entreat with boldness for our souls.

*Sticheron:*
A radiant joy and all-honorable glory didst thou inherit having suffered, and everlasting rejoicing, O venerable captive,[^308] honorable sufferer, valiant warrior, who by the streams of thy blood didst manifestly drown the army and host of the dragon by grace; and by divine prayer didst truly raise the dead, praying with boldness for our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Wounded by the bandit assaults of demons, and lying completely helpless on the path of this present life, O all-immaculate one, and in need of mercy, visit me speedily, pouring wine and oil upon my incurable wounds, and restore me unto health, that I may glorify thee and with love dutifully hymn thy great deeds, O pure and only Virgin Mother.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
A sword pierced thy heart, as Simeon said [Luke 2:35], O most pure Lady, when thou didst behold Him Who shone forth from thee by an ineffable word, lifted up upon the Cross by the lawless as one condemned, tasting vinegar and gall, pierced in His side, and His hands and feet nailed; and weeping, thou didst cry out, lamenting maternally: "What is this sweetest wonder, this new mystery?"

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr Charalampes, O Lord, in his suffering received an imperishable crown from Thee, our God; for possessing Thy might, he cast down the tormentors and crushed the powerless insolence of demons. By his prayers save our souls.

*Canon, Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, and beholding Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, Israel cried aloud: "Let us sing a song of victory unto God!"

*Troparion:*
Walking joyfully with the choirs in the spiritual heavenly habitations, and shining with unwaning light, deliver me from passions by thy prayers, O wise one, that I may hymn thee.

*Troparion:*
Thy light-bearing memory flashes forth sun-like gifts and enlightens those who faithfully honor thee therein, O martyr truly namesake of joy.[^309]

*Troparion:*
By the drops of thy blood thou didst drown the turbulent[^310] sea of unbelief; thou wast a river of piety, truly watering the Church of Christ continually.

*Theotokion:*
A tempest of sins, contrary waves of thoughts, and assaults of passions have come upon me; but by thy mighty prayer, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, save me.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion:*
It is utterly impossible to recount the multitude of miracles which thou didst perform while yet walking in life and having passed unto God, O passion-bearer, martyr worthy of wonder.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by divine power, thou didst cast down the feeble insolence of the lawless, like a mighty soldier, O sufferer Charalampes, as an armored warrior of the divine host.

*Troparion:*
The streams of blood pouring forth from thy body extinguished all the flame of impiety and its cruel burning, and procured for thee a torrent of delight.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth to the Holy of holies, God, O all-holy one, shown forth as a pure temple and the gate through which Christ alone passed, saving mankind.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Thou wast revealed as an unshakable pillar of the Church, an unquenchable light of the universe, O passion-bearer Charalampes; and having shone brighter than the sun, thou drivest away the darkness of idols, O glorious martyr. Entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only all-immaculate Virgin Mother, who in purity didst surpass the angels. Cleanse me, who above all have become dust, defiled by carnal transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, granting, O pure one, great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding hanging upon the Cross the Word, her painless offspring, the undefiled Ewe-lamb, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, maternally weeping, cried aloud: "Woe is me, my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in a manner befitting God, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping feast in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast fair, being anointed with the blood of honorable martyrdom, and adorned with the beauties thereof, O Charalampes, adornment and confirmation of sufferers.

*Troparion:*
By thy courage, O passion-bearer, thou didst shatter demonic shrines, and didst show forth the bodily temple of the Holy Spirit, and a pillar and pattern of unfeigned endurance.

*Troparion:*
Lifting up their hands on high, O glorious one, the rulers of darkness scraped thy ribs with iron claws, truly procuring for thee everlasting delight.

*Theotokion:*
Christ God Who shone forth from thee, O pure one, is my strength and my song; by the abundance of His goodness, He delivered us from the first transgression.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray, the souls of those who dawn early unto Thee with love, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, to be the true God, crying out from the gloom of sins.

*Troparion:*
Firmly imitating the divine passion of Him Who endured suffering for our sake, O martyr of Christ, thou dost valiantly endure throughout thy whole body the piercing of nails, O glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Correcting the unreason of the persecutors, O all-wise one, the Word co-beginningless with the Father manifestly puts speech into the irrational beasts,[^311] commanding them to loose thee from bonds.

*Troparion:*
Pierced with iron spikes, thou didst wound the heart of the fierce adversary of earthborn men with the spear of thy courage and endurance, O God-wise passion-bearer worthy of wonder.

*Theotokion:*
With thy divine radiance, O gracious Lady, enlighten my soul darkened by pleasures, and guide me unto the paths of salvation, O thou who alone didst bear Christ the Savior.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: "Lead up my life from corruption, O greatly Merciful One!"

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a beacon, enlightening the ends of the earth with the light of thy sufferings; confessing the name of Christ before tormentors, O most glorious passion-bearer of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Inflamed with the divine fire of love, by the dew of faith thou didst extinguish the conflagration of the wicked, showing forth the wonders of Christ unto the unbelieving, O worthy of wonder.

*Troparion:*
Desiring immortal life, O Charalampes, thou didst put to death the passionate movements of the flesh; and by prayer thou didst raise the dead, who hymn Him Who glorified thee among all.

*Theotokion:*
Strengthen me who am shaken by the hostile assaults of passions, O Mother of God, as thou didst bear the Wellspring of dispassion; for unto thee have I fled, the great refuge of men.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
Having been conformed unto the grace of the priesthood, O glorious one, thou didst brightly adorn the Church with divine suffering, which thou didst valiantly receive for Christ, rejoicing, O honorable Charalampes, universal beacon who enlightenest the ends of the earth as one unconquered.

*Ikos:*
The Pastor, Christ our God, Who was pleased to come upon earth as a lamb, taking away sins and bearing the infirmities of all, didst thou fittingly imitate, O holy martyr: thou didst lay down thy life for the sheep, proclaiming His holy wonders; therefore today we all sing unto thee with hymns as one unconquered.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Children in Babylon feared not the flame of the furnace, but cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!"

*Troparion:*
Partaking of tortures, scorched by fire, O passion-bearer of Christ, thou becamest like pure gold and a holy offering of Christ.

*Troparion:*
By the dew of thy prayers, O wise one, the furnace among the lawless was quenched, and thou wast delivered, singing unto the Creator: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!"

*Troparion:*
By the array of thy sufferings thou wast shown forth as a high-grown tree, overshadowing the faithful from fiery sin, as they cry: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!"

*Theotokion:*
Thou who didst receive Joy into thy most pure womb, O pure Birthgiver of God, fill my heart with gladness, driving away the grief of passions, O Ever-Virgin.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable, and with water didst burn up the sacrifice of the righteous one; for Thou dost accomplish all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Bedewed with the streams of blood, adorned with the beauty of wounds, adorned in a manner befitting God with the crown of victory, thou didst stand before God, O martyr.

*Troparion:*
As clouds did not overshadow thy courage, O martyr, and thy valiant deeds, so the opposition of persecutors did not darken thy most radiant endurance, O God-wise one.

*Troparion:*
An unconquered priest and martyr, a valiant warrior, an unshakable pillar, the confirmation of the faithful, an antagonist of impiety wast thou truly revealed, O God-wise passion-bearer.

*Theotokion:*
He Who created human nature by divine will, being pleased, took up abode in thy womb, O pure one, desiring to renew us who were corrupted; Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-immaculate one, the incarnate Word was revealed unto men, Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts, we bless thee.

*Troparion:*
Neither fire, nor sword, nor even death, nor persecution, nor affliction, nor famine, nor peril, nor the contrivance of any tortures was able truly to separate thee from the love of God Who made thee [Rom. 8:35–39], O sufferer worthy of wonder.

*Troparion:*
Having put off the garments of mortality by the scraping of iron claws, thou didst adorn thyself with vesture of blood, O passion-bearer; therefore with boldness, O unconquered martyr, rejoicing, thou standest before the Master and God of all.

*Troparion:*
Full of joy and of divine brightness from the Giver, O Charalampes, thy memory hath shone forth upon the world, driving away the winter of passions, enlightening all; celebrating which, we obtain cleansing of transgressions through thy prayers.

*Theotokion:*
A tempest of passions troubles me, and the surging swell of pleasures drowns me, O all-immaculate one; O thou who didst bear the Helmsman, Christ, stretch forth a hand of help unto me, and save me, the only salvation of those who faithfully bless thee.

*In the same month, on the 11th day: The Holy Hieromartyr Blaise.*[^312]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the Octoechos, 3; and of the saint, 3; Tone 4, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нб҃ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Sticheron:*
The height of virtues, the foundation of the faithful, the glory of hierarchs, and the praise of martyrs, brightly calls all the faithful together to his honorable memory, to hymn him as a victor who glorified God on earth.

*Sticheron:*
Having shone forth with the manifestation of Christ, the Head of the assembly, and having seen the congregation of the lawless, O martyr, thou didst zealously come unto the judgment seat, O wise one. Having taken the victor's prizes of glory, thou now dwellest in the heavens, O most sacred one.

*Sticheron:*
By thy drops of blood, O martyr Blaise, the vesture of the Church was beautifully woven, being adorned with varied anointings of grace and twofold dyeings of sufferings; wherewith she is clothed, as with bridal beauty, hymning Christ the Bridegroom.

*Glory..., Tone 4:*
As a good tutor and teacher of piety, by the effective power of thy divine words thou didst lead God-loving women unto the contests of martyrdom,[^313] strengthening the weakness of their nature in Christ; having well completed the course of sufferings with them, thou brightly rejoicest in the heavenly bridal chamber: thou art adorned with a twofold crown of divine glory; entreat with them to grant us peace and great mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Beholding Christ the Lover of mankind being crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the most pure one, weeping, cried aloud: "What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for all the good things Thou didst unto them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O Compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!"

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos; Glory..., Tone 6:*
Having sprouted forth in the fasting of divine virtues, fittingly namesake of sprouting,[^314] O Blaise, thou didst flourish Davidically like a palm tree in the courts of the Lord [Ps. 91:13], and didst multiply like a cedar in righteous deeds; and like a fruitful vine in the house of God [Ps. 51:10], in the time of martyrdom pruned by the torments of tortures, from the fruit of thy labors thou didst pour forth spiritual wine for us; drinking whereof, we fill our hearts with divine gladness, and coming harmoniously together on the honorable memory of thy demise, blessing thee, we praise thee, entreating to receive through thee peace and great mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ):*
Beholding the most lawless people unjustly nailing Thee to the Tree, O Savior, the Virgin and Thy pure Mother, as Simeon foretold [Luke 2:35], was wounded in her heart.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A sharer in the ways and a successor upon the thrones of the Apostles, thou didst find action as an ascent unto contemplation, O God-inspired one; therefore, dispensing the word of truth, thou didst suffer for the faith even unto blood, O hieromartyr Blaise. Entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Canon, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, ancient Israel of old, by the cross-shaped hands of Moses, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
Today we bring harmonious praise unto Christ, celebrating the glorious memory of wise Blaise, musically singing a hymn unto our God as lovers of feasts, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparion:*
A despiser of things below and a lover of Christ, Blaise, as a hierarch, martyr, and shepherd, showing this forth in deed, hath set forth a divine banquet and convokes us to eat thereof.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst cleanse thy life with virtues, and wast revealed as a fit vessel for receiving the divine myrrh of honorable service, O wise one; and thou didst variegate thy vesture with the blood of martyrdom.

*Theotokion:*
All mankind was raised from the ancestral fall, O pure Mother of God, and was manifestly delivered from the grandmother's curse through thy most holy childbearing.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: "Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation!"

*Troparion:*
Known unto God, thou wast anointed and taught by the Spirit to serve as priest unto Christ; and thou didst sanctify thyself by blood.

*Troparion:*
By thy teachings and by the brightness of thy wise deeds, thou wast truly a martyr and teacher unto the Church, O Blaise.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a pattern of piety unto the Church of Christ, and didst make bright thy sacred vesture with the blood of thy sufferings.

*Theotokion:*
Having borne on earth the all-rich God, O pure one, thou didst raise the fall of mankind unto heaven.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
A divine sprout, an unfading flower, a much-suffering branch of the vine of Christ, O God-bearer Blaise: fill with thy gladness those who honor thy memory with faith, praying unceasingly for us all.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Being an honorable sprout of piety, thou wast revealed as an uprooter of impiety by spiritual power, O all-praised Blaise; for upon the judgment seat thou didst expose the deception of idols, and with good boldness didst proclaim that we hymn the Trinity; therefore, delivered unto the lawless, O passion-bearer, thou didst slay the beast, the serpent that is the author of evil. Therefore we cry unto thee: entreat Christ God to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
