O cloud of the spiritual Sun, gold-wrought lamp of the divine Light: O immaculate, blameless, all-immaculate Lady, enlighten, I pray, my soul darkened by the blindness of passions with the ray of dispassion; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction and tears of repentance; and cleanse me from the mire of my deeds, that with love I may cry unto thee: O Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin, entreat Christ God to grant me forgiveness of transgressions, for I, thy servant, have thee as my hope.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried out, weeping, and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my heart burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest through the tender compassion of Thy mercy, O longsuffering Lord, Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fountain! Have compassion, and grant forgiveness of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine passions with faith!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her place, worthily crying aloud: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the true staff, thou didst martyrlike cast down the serpent of deception, O Blaise O Blaise, servant of Christ, having exposed the tormentor, and having guided a new people.

*Troparion:*
Thy closeness unto God, O blessed one, demonstrated thee to be a benefactor of the rational and the irrational,[^315] for the sake of thy righteous deeds; therefore thou didst receive the gifts of healings.

*Troparion:*
From the dale where pain was laid upon thy sheep that are in Christ, O father, thou didst utterly choke out all the thorns of lawlessness by the word of grace.

*Theotokion:*
Shown forth as a paradise planted in the midst of the Church, O all-hymned one, having borne the Tree of Life, tasting whereof we have known Christ our God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, art come into the world as Light, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
Having extended the borders of thy flock in divine pasture, O hierarch, thou didst God-wisely drive away deception from all the earth.

*Troparion:*
By teachings, O wise hierarch, thou didst establish thy flock in the faith; for behold, even women trampled upon deception.

*Troparion:*
Established by the love of Christ, the women attain unto the likeness of thy contests, O father, zealously slaughtered for Christ.

*Theotokion:*
The assault of death is truly put to death by thy childbearing, O Lady; for darkness is seen to be destroyed by light.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
"I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord," the Church cries unto Thee, cleansed from demonic blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion:*
With the beauty of diverse bodily wounds thou didst adorn the loveliness of thy mystical vestures; and from both sides, O hierarch, thou wast crowned by grace.

*Troparion:*
As the women who suffered with thee came together in the faith of Christ, O father, having zealously died, O Blaise: preserve those who celebrate thy memory with love.

*Troparion:*
Inflamed with the zeal of Christ, the women martyrs, casting away the deception of flattering deceit, extinguished the torments of tortures by their blood.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art truly my strength and my song, O Parent of God; for upon thee have I set my hope, and through thee have I exalted my horn against my enemies [LXX 1 Kings 2:1].

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
Adorned with the anointing of the priesthood and the blood of martyrdom, O glorious Blaise, thou shinest everywhere triumphing in the highest, and looking down upon us who have entered thy temple, and who in it unceasingly cry unto thee: Preserve us all!

*Ikos:*
Gather together, O brethren, with eagerness, and reach the temple of the martyr, that we may hymn him today, that we may delight in his grace; for he saves souls and bodies from manifold passions, as a martyr, as a first-fruit, as a faithful hierarch and champion, unto those who cry unto him from a pure soul: Preserve us all!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamic Children, burned by love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Angelically we cry unto thee with love, O father Blaise: thou wast shown to be an imitator of the Lord, and thy footsteps were not known; for thy paths in the Lord were manifested upon the waters.[^316]

*Troparion:*
Delighting in twofold grace, O holy women martyrs, ye truly accounted the torment of trials to be joy; and a wonder exceeding human nature occurred, when your blood was dreadfully changed into milk.[^317]

*Troparion:*
Beholding the motherly boldness unto martyrdom, the children, in the faith of Christ, also delight in his fair beauty, eagerly hastening rejoicing unto the Master.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, sanctified divine habitation of the Most High! For through thee was joy given, O Birthgiver of God, unto those who cry: "Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Lady!"

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, stretching forth his hands, stopped the gaping mouths of lions in the den; and the Children, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Stretching forth thy hands psalm-like before the face of God, O father, serving as a priest, thou didst offer thy prayers unto Christ with thy blood; therefore, O wise one, thou didst drink the saving cup: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Fervently emulating in piety, we faithfully celebrate thy divine memory, O God-gladdened Blaise; and do thou thyself, according to the promise that thou didst make unto it, and with it unto us, be our help with good gifts.[^318]

*Theotokion:*
Human nature, which had previously been condemned by the fall of transgression, found true blessing through thy childbearing, O Birthgiver of God; and it sings joyfully: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, O virgin, the unhewn mountain: Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures [Dan. 2:34, 45]. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
As if preparing for the sweetness of paradise and the kingdom within it, O wise hierarch Blaise, preparing enjoyment, thou didst run unto the slaughter, rejoicing in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
With the spiritual sword thou didst cut down the pride of the godless who insolently exalted themselves against thy flock, O blessed one, having adorned piety with thy blood.

*Troparion:*
O Blaise, wise hierarch, together with the women who suffered with thee, and likewise with the divine children, pray that those who praise thee with blessings may be crowned, as from the beginning they have sung a word unto thee.

*Theotokion:*
In childbearing thou wast shown to be a Virgin even as before childbearing, O Birthgiver of God, and after childbearing likewise, having surpassed the established laws of nature, O Lady; therefore we all magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Having adorned heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
A hierarch and martyr, venerable and guileless wast thou revealed, O Blaise, bearing the wounds of the passions of Christ, Who gave thee grace to heal all infirmities.

*Theotokion:*
Daniel prefigured thee as the unhewn mountain [Dan. 2:34, 45], from which the precious Stone was cut out, crushing the idol temples, O Parent of God, Mary.

*On the Twelfth Day of the Same Month: Our Father among the Saints Meletius, Archbishop of Antioch the Great.*[^319]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Octoechos, 3; and of Saint Meletius, 3; Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
*Sticheron:*
Of the bodiless and co-unoriginate Word of the Father, Who united Himself hypostatically unto flesh, O all-wise one, without change or commingling, thou didst wisely teach: operating twofold in both natures, whereof He is composed and in which He is seen, being One and undivided in essence, understood in this and in that being One and undivided in essence, understood in this and in that.

*Sticheron:*
Having meditated, O blessed one, O hierarch Meletius, upon the law of the Lord [Ps. 1:2], as it is written: thou wast shown to be a tree standing beside the waters of fasting [Ps. 1:3], offering the fruits of virtues through the grace of Him Who enlightened thy holy soul, and made it receptive of all spiritual radiances.

*Sticheron:*
Thy cheeks were scripturally adorned, O all-wise one [Song 1:9–10], like a turtledove that loves self-control and spurns food through desire, O Meletius, of spiritual food, which Christ grants unto thee, having accepted thy many labors and inflamed zeal, which thou hadst when standing in the forefront of danger for the sake of the faith.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As thou hast sleepless prayer and abiding supplication unto the Lord, O most pure one: lull temptations to sleep, calm the waves of my wretched soul, and comfort my heart that is in affliction, O Maiden, I pray thee; and endow my mind with grace, that I may worthily glorify thee.

*Stavrotheotokion, same Tone and Podoben (*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ)*):*
When the Ewe-lamb who bore Thee beheld Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she wept and with maternal lamentation spoke unto Thee: "O most desired Son! How art Thou hung upon the Tree of the Cross, O Longsuffering One? How are Thy hands and Thy feet pierced with nails by the lawless, O Word? And how hast Thou poured forth Thy blood, O Master?"

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A rule of faith and an image of meekness, a teacher of temperance, the truth of things revealed thee unto thy flock; therefore, thou didst acquire by humility the high things, by poverty the rich things: O father Meletius, entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "Thy praises I declare, O blessed one."[^320] Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The chariot-driver Pharaoh did the wonderworking rod of Moses drown of old, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, but it saved fleeing Israel the foot-traveler, as he sang a song unto God.

*Troparion:*
Standing all-radiant before the Giver of light, enlighten with the light of understanding the darkened eye of my soul, and drive away the gloom of passions by thy prayers, O venerable hierarch Meletius, for thou hast boldness.

*Troparion:*
He Who sits in the bosom of the Father, the co-everlasting and consubstantial Word of Him Who begot Him, the only [Begotten], sends thee forth now as a preacher of His Divinity, equal in character unto the Apostles, O God-manifest Meletius.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with divine radiance, O venerable one, thou didst proclaim the Only-begotten Word from the unoriginate Father as uncreated and eternal; and protected by divine armor, thou didst scatter the like-minded champions of Arius.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bear Him incarnate, O most holy one, Who was previously bodiless, the divinely originating Word; for thou alone from eternity wast worthy, O all-immaculate one, through the rays of purity, the beauty of virginity, and all-blameless gifts.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for there is none holy save Thee, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Having a soul firmer than adamant, enlightened by the teachings of piety, O all-blessed one, thou didst spit upon the rotten opinion of heresies; therefore all we faithful honor thee with hymns, O God-wise one.

*Troparion:*
Having likened thyself in virtues unto the Apostles of Christ, thou didst manifestly inherit their authority and throne, O all-glorious Meletius, having become a pillar of Orthodoxy and a champion of piety.

*Troparion:*
Adopted as son unto God,[^321] thou didst not foolishly bring down unto the level of a creature God the Word Who is from God; but thou didst glorify Him as co-unoriginate and co-enthroned with the Father, the Maker and Creator of all ages, O God-receiver.

*Theotokion:*
Taking up His abode within thy holy womb, God became incarnate as He alone knows, O Mother of God, and saved us by His life-bearing passions; therefore we know thee, O Virgin, as the door of salvation.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Meditating day and night upon the law of the Lord, O wise father, thou wast revealed like a fruitful tree planted beside the spiritual waters [Ps. 1:2–3]; therefore, having served as priest unto God blamelessly, thou didst openly abhor the blasphemies of Arius and the evilly-famed disposition of Sabellius with an Orthodox mind, O hierarch Meletius.[^322] Entreat Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Finding through thee, O all-immaculate one, healing from manifold bodily diseases and diverse spiritual passions, and release from sorrows, a ready change and speedy deliverance from misfortunes and tribulations: with undoubting faith and a warm heart, I confess thy mercies, O Lady, and thy many wonders unto the sons of men; and I pray: by the shelter of thy wings ever watch over and preserve me always.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried out, weeping, and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my heart burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest through the tender compassion of Thy mercy, O longsuffering Lord, Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fountain! Have compassion, and grant forgiveness of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine passions with faith!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, and didst visit our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: "Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!"

*Troparion:*
Manifestly pre-eminent, O sacred initiate, thou didst inherit the throne of Peter the supreme, ordained by Christ; having emulated his character, thou didst glorify the Son of the living God, the Savior, following his teaching.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with diverse gifts, thou wast a preacher of the Monarchy known in the Father, and in the Only-begotten Word, and in the Divine Spirit, O all-wise one; for thou didst teach the Unity in essence subsisting in three Persons, O venerable one.

*Troparion:*
Nurtured by the beauties of divine instruction, shaped by its dawn, and enlightened by divine splendors through participation, thou becamest a second light unto the Church, O God-manifest one, radiating the illumination of piety unto those who flee unto thee with faith.

*Theotokion:*
That Thou mightest seek out the image buried by passions through the richness of Thy goodness, Thou didst take up Thine abode within a virginal womb, wherefrom Thou, the Wisdom of God, didst build Thyself a temple, wherein Thou didst dwell among men, O Compassionate One, and didst save the ends of the world.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy countenance, O never-setting Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and guide my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!

*Troparion:*
While every heresy of wickedness lived lawlessly, thou didst surpass them, O father, by the nobility of thy soul, and by thy brightness, thy most holy character, and thy firm piety, O God-manifest and all-blessed Meletius.

*Troparion:*
Converting with all zeal those of like mind with Arius, thou didst establish dominion with the Orthodox faith, O God-bearer, preaching unto us the one God in Trinity, O all-blessed father Meletius.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast the truest initiate of the all-divine Trinity, honoring the Word as consubstantial and co-enthroned with the Father, and the Divine Spirit as of one nature with the Father and the Word, O all-honorable father Meletius.

*Theotokion:*
The hosts of the powers on high bless thee, O all-pure one, and all generations of men worthily glorify thee; for through thee alone those on earth were joined with the heavenly ones, singing of thy childbearing, O Virgin.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst dry up the streams of heresy, O God-wise one, and didst open the fountains of the dogmas of uprightness, having become an unconquerable champion of Orthodoxy, O God-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Having fled the turbid and godless stream of Arius, thou didst richly give drink to the assemblies of the faithful with gracious waters, and didst adorn the fair beauty of the Church.

*Troparion:*
Having lived fittingly in divine undertakings, thou wast an all-radiant laborer, O father, an unyielding bulwark of piety, and a guide of pastors.

*Theotokion:*
The prophetic utterances enlighten the souls of those who hymn thee in an Orthodox manner, foreannouncing thy most pure conception and thy childbearing beyond speech, O Bride of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
Fearing thy spiritual boldness, the apostate Macedonius flees;[^323] and celebrating our prayerful service, we thy servants run unto thee with love, O companion of the angels, Meletius, fiery sword of Christ our God, slaughtering all the godless: we hymn thee as a beacon who hast enlightened all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire was ashamed of the divine condescension in Babylon of old; therefore the Children in the furnace dancing with joyful foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

*Troparion:*
The holy Council had thee as its primate, O God-receiver,[^324] which the Holy Spirit gathered together into one from the ends of the earth, as they sang praise unto His Divinity: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

*Troparion:*
With equal honor thou didst obtain the presidency of the Apostles of Christ; and thou didst inherit their divine teaching, and imitating their life, thou didst sing: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

*Troparion:*
Having a most keen mind extended unto contemplation of God, thou didst mock the dull sight of the heretics, and teaching in piety, didst cry: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

*Theotokion:*
Like a pure mirror of thy purity shining with grace, O Virgin, Parent of the Light and all-immaculate Birthgiver of God wast thou. Therefore in harmony we sing unto thy childbearing: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant madly kindle the furnace for the pious; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: "Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Troparion:*
All-radiantly thou didst teach to honor the one Godhead in the Father, and the Son, and the Spirit, and the immeasurable power and one kingdom, while the property of each Hypostasis is firmly preserved; unto Whom also singing, thou didst cry: "Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Troparion:*
Having become a temple of holiness through holiness, thou sanctifiest all who strive to call upon thy name, and who depict thine image with love toward thee, O blessed father, and who sing with faith: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!"

*Troparion:*
By mystical instructions thou didst enlighten thine excellent flock; and now thou hast augmented the church of the firstborn [Heb. 12:23], having become an addition unto those who shone forth from the ages, by hierarchic and mystical services; with whom thou unceasingly criest: "Ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt the Lord unto the ages!"

*Theotokion:*
Being instructed, O all-pure one, in the mystery wrought upon thee, and having received through thee renewal and salvation for our souls, we cry unto thee with the Archangel Gabriel: "Rejoice!" crying aloud, O Birthgiver of God, and faithfully hymning thee, and supremely exalting [Him] unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was astonished at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God was manifested unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens. Therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee, the Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Incorporeally standing before the Incorporeal One, Whom as Creator of all and Lord, and as Word and Wisdom of the Father, essentially and consubstantially sanctifying, thou didst radiantly theologize: cease not to entreat Him to save those who praise thee with faith, O God-proclaimed one.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thee toiling with spiritual labors and still zealously fighting for Him against the Pneumatomachians,[^325] the good and Holy Spirit, having crowned thee, received thee worthily as a victor, and numbered thee with the choirs on high, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Having finished the contests of piety, and completed the course most excellently [2 Tim. 4:7], and having taught the upright faith in all piety, thou didst receive the righteous crown from the Maker of all, O God-proclaimer, sending forth a song of songs unto the assembly of teachers.

*Theotokion:*
Grant me easily to pass through the tempest of this life, O Birthgiver of God, calming the assaults of temptations and passions, as thou art good, and guiding me unto virtue and the paths of uprightness, that I may unceasingly magnify thee as my Benefactress.

*On the Thirteenth Day of the Same Month: Our Venerable Father Martinian.*[^326]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera; Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
*Sticheron:*
Come, let us crown with hymns the sufferer of Christ, as with flowers of the field and fair-sprouting blossoms of the divine paradise, in common with all the Orthodox; for he was manifestly revealed to the world as the beauty of purity, the sacrifice of faith, and the glory of abstinence; therefore he received an immutable crown of the kingdom.

*Sticheron:*
Having in thy heart the fire of the Divine Spirit, O God-blessed Martinian burning therewith the image of the maiden's impurity, the adversary was found wounded by his own sword;[^327] therefore he that assaulted thee was shown forth lying truly as a laughingstock, utterly put to shame, the all-polluted one.

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst kindle sensible fire against the enemy's battle-arrays, O all-blessed one; having acquired the thought of eternal fire, thou didst greatly wither the burning of thy passions. Setting thy feet upon a rock, O venerable one, thou didst build a dwelling upon it;[^328] wandering throughout lands, O father, thou wast crowned by thine endurance.

*Glory..., Tone 2:*
Rejoice, honorable and marvelous boast of Palestine! For thou hast shone forth upon us like a most radiant sun, and having burned up all the power of the enemy, thou didst scorch thy limbs with fire, O blessed one; for in the mountains, the wildernesses, and the islands, he brought every temptation against thee: in the wilderness he set a woman before thee, and upon the rock in the sea the tempter attempted to tempt thee, O wise one. But, O thrice-blessed Martinian, cease not to pray unceasingly unto Christ on behalf of us who celebrate thy memory with faith!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When thou didst behold upon the Tree the fully ripened Cluster, O pure one, Whom thou didst carry as a child in thy womb without cultivation, hanging thereon, weeping thou didst cry aloud, exclaiming: "O Child, drop down sweetness, whereby all the drunkenness of passions is taken away, O Benefactor, gladdening me who bore Thee with divine consolations, as Thou art compassionate!"

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos; Glory..., Tone 2. Automelon:*
Diversified with the garment of purity, and enlightened by divine prayer, having Christ Who was born of the Virgin dwelling within himself, he was not carried away by bridal adornment, nor did he accept carnal pleasures; but he zealously stepped upon the servant-like fire, having the divine fire in his heart. And by the sensible and temporal fire thou didst burn up passionate fire, and didst quench the flame of Gehenna. Therefore pray, O blessed Martinian, that we also may be delivered from all-destructive and eternal fire.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ):*
Beholding the most lawless people unjustly nailing Thee to the Tree, O Savior, the Virgin and Thy pure Mother, as Simeon foretold, was wounded in maternal heart, O Savior.[^329]

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
The flame of temptations thou didst extinguish with streams of tears, O blessed one; and having bridled the waves of the sea and the assaults of wild beasts, thou didst cry aloud: "Most glorified art Thou, O All-powerful One, Who hast saved me from fire and tempest!"

*Canon, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The overwhelming power laid Pharaoh's whole host in the deep of old; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin, the most glorified Lord, for gloriously is He glorified.

*Troparion:*
With mystical hymns in harmony, come, let us praise with faith today Martinian, the ascetic of piety, as one who fasted well and lawfully; therefore he received also a crown of victory from Christ.

*Troparion:*
Unswervingly hedged about with the fear of Christ, O father, thou didst drive away the terrors of the enemy like lightning; for thou wast fearful unto them by the endurance of abstinence, and thou didst remain unshaken by demonic fantasies, O all-praised one.

*Troparion:*
By the saving power of Christ, the demonic temptation that came upon thee easily passed by—the assault of the woman, O venerable one;[^330] whom having saved, thou didst wound because of her the author of evil.

*Theotokion:*
Having carried in thy womb without a man, O pure one, thou art an incorrupt Virgin Mother among women, having conceived the Son of God in thy womb by the Holy Spirit; Whom according to the good pleasure of the Father thou didst bear without seed.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies; for my spirit rejoiced to sing: "There is none holy like our God, and none is righteous more than Thee, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
By abstinence of the flesh thou didst wash away the filth of malice like mire, and by purity of mind thou didst find, O venerable one, the smooth paths of the righteous, having fled from the ways of sin, O wise one.

*Troparion:*
Enlivening Thy heart, and kindling it with fear and love of the Lord, Thou didst willingly scorch Thy flesh with material fire, O wise one, thereby prefiguring for us the unquenchable fire of Gehenna, O venerable one.[^331]

*Troparion:*
Wounding thy body with all-consuming fire, thou didst appear as a pursuer and a victor, wounding the adversary by endurance, and cleansing the defilement of thoughts, O venerable one, and becoming radiant by the Spirit, though acting in the flesh.

*Theotokion:*
A conception without knowledge of man, a divine ineffable birthgiving, for it is beyond the mind; and God Who is born is above nature. O Thy birthgiving! O thy purity! For through thee God appeared unto me in the form of a servant [Phil. 2:7].

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предвари́):*
Having shown forth thy struggles in thy fasting, O Lord, with great zeal, O Christ our God;[^332] for having Thy strength, he set at nought the demons, and cut down their frail pride by the Word; therefore we pray Thee: save our souls!

*Glory..., Both now... Theotokion, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предвари́):*
O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst bear the transcendent God, with the bodiless hosts unceasingly pray Him to grant us remission of transgressions and amendment of life before the end, unto us who with faith and love hymn thee as is due, O only all-praised one.

*Or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предвари́):*
O all-immaculate Virgin Mother of Christ God, a sword passed through thy most holy soul when thou didst see Him crucified willingly, Thy Son and thy God; cease not to pray Him, O blessed one, to grant us remission of transgressions.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou didst come from the Virgin, not an intercessor nor an angel, but Thyself, O Lord, having become incarnate, and Thou didst save the whole of me, man. Therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
The invention of the tormentor's deceit was put to death, and he suffered defeat; for by the very one through whom he hoped to conquer, by her he was cast down, and is valiantly trampled underfoot firmly.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst endure the Lord with a valiant disposition, and didst direct thy paths; and thou didst take up residence upon a rock, O venerable one, lying without a shelter, as though having ascended into heaven upon a small island.[^333]

*Troparion:*
Valiant and beautiful feet didst thou stretch forth unto the course, O all-praised one, passing from land to land;[^334] for thou didst flee from the city of passions, and didst attain unto dispassion.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst remain a Virgin after giving birth, O all-hymned one, for the beginningless God appeared coming forth from thee; and having become man, we venerate the likeness of His form.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for besides Thee we know no other God.

*Troparion:*
Having found the door of virtues against the craft of the enemy, O venerable one, thou wast revealed also as the protector of my life, and guide of my soul when grievously submerged in the storm.

*Troparion:*
Since thou didst receive the gift of God, the strength of thy prayers, thou dost accomplish all things most gloriously, O wise one, and dost save us from the deep, and deliver our souls from the tempest of passions.

*Theotokion:*
In two natures and one hypostasis Thou didst exist, being perfect man. The unconfused form or appearance suffered no alteration, O Word, when becoming incarnate, O Lord.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Unto the Lord from the whale Jonah cried aloud: "Bring me up from the deep of hades, I pray, that as unto the Deliverer, in the voice of praise and in the spirit of truth, I may sacrifice unto Thee!"

*Troparion:*
Strengthening thy soul by divine power, O all-praised one, hedged about by hope, thou wast not terrified to pass through the untraversable deep, O wise one; but thou wast saved, borne along by dolphins.[^335]

*Troparion:*
Thy life truly astonished the angels, put the demons to shame, and enlightened men; and moreover, thou didst save the woman, and draw her onto the path of fasting.

*Troparion:*
Corresponding to thy labors and thy honorable struggles, O wise one, thy departure was truly worthy; and thou didst take the victory of chastity over the enemies, having slain the serpent through abstinence.

*Theotokion:*
The laws of nature truly grew weak concerning thee, O pure Virgin; for beyond nature thou dost act, having given birth unto the Word, Who is co-enthroned with the Father and the Divine Spirit, being both God and man.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Firm and God-bearing" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
As a skilled ascetic of piety, an honorable voluntary sufferer, and an inhabitant and settler of the wilderness, let us worthily praise in hymns Martinian ever-honorable; for he trampled down the serpent.

*Ikos:*
Unto the ends, even unto the ends of the earth went forth the proclamation of thy beautiful virtues and thy divine struggles; being young in age, thou didst desire to live in the wilderness, ever singing songs, psalms, and prayers unto Christ. Day and night toiling in labors and tears, thou didst finish thy life in purity, and wisely put to shame the author of evil; for thou didst trample down the serpent.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Unto the golden image served upon the plain of Dura, Thy three youths paid no heed of the godless decree; but cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed they sang: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

*Troparion:*
Setting aside peaceful life, be a monk, flee firmly, traversing all cities and lands worthily; for Martinian teaches to sing without sorrow: "Blessed is the God of our fathers!"

*Troparion:*
By thy firm walk thou wast an image of Paul's course, O wise one, pursuing with love Him Whom thou didst attain by the course of thine abstinence; therefore as a stranger to the world thou wast known, O all-praised one, unto the heavenly realm, and thou wast gloriously glorified in faith.

*Troparion:*
Finishing the course of thy fasting, as a disciple of Christ knowing beforehand by the Divine Spirit thy departure from the body, thou didst sing with unwavering soul: "Into Thy hands, O Master, I commit my spirit and my soul today!"[^336]

*Theotokion:*
Christ Who gives living water from the fountain did source it forth; He dwelt in thy holy and most glorious womb, remaining without corruption, watering all with incorruption who cry with faith: "Blessed art thou who didst bear God in the flesh!"

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew, sing works as the Lord, and exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having stripped off visibly the frail garment of the flesh, and taken up the garment of courage and wisdom, the maiden cried out, fasting: "Thee do I exalt, O Jesus, unto all ages!"[^337]

*Troparion:*
She truly rejoices eagerly, established upon the rock of Christ, and having armed herself firmly with fasting and faith, the maiden cried out in hymns: "Thee do I exalt, O Jesus, unto all ages!"

*Troparion:*
As one translated from corruptible things, and become a partaker of celestial incorruption, O God-bearer, having finished the divine course and received a crown, thou didst cry aloud: "Thee do I exalt, O Jesus, unto all ages!"

*Theotokion:*
Thou Who didst descend from heaven, O compassionate God of the good, through tender mercy, and wast born of the Virgin, save those who sing: "Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
What a great and most glorious mystery concerning thee, O daughter of Adam and Mother of the Most High, the only sure bridge of the faithful unto God! With unceasing hymns, we the faithful magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Transcendently surpassing the laws of fasting, O father, thou didst extend thyself in abstinence of every kind, and being watchful in prayer, together with fasting, purity, and reverence; therefore Christ, thy Crowner, proclaims thee at the end of thy sufferings.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown worthy of God, since thou didst contest well; for having cast off every burden, thou didst lightly sail across the great and heavy gulf of this life, O father; and thou didst arrive at a calm harbor, having accomplished thy course, O wise one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art truly the only Ever-virgin Birthgiver of God, the guidance of Christians, the protectress of sinners; for from thee devoutly shone forth redemption; together with Him, I finish my hymn unto thee in faith, as is due.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "He Who adorned the heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
Desiring the angelic life, thou didst withdraw thyself into the wildernesses; and having subdued the passions of the flesh, thou wast revealed as an equal of the angels, O God-bearing Martinian.

*Theotokion, Podoben: "He Who adorned the heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
The mountain which the divine Habakkuk foresaw of old, shadowed and densely wooded [Hab. 3:3], thou wast revealed to be, O Virgin; and David names thee the curdled and fertile mountain [Ps. 67:16].

*On the Fourteenth Day of the Same Month: Our Venerable Father Auxentius.*[^338]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Octoechos, 3; and of the Saint, 3.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
*Sticheron:*
As a reward of fasting, Auxentius, thou didst show forth the blossoming of gifts: from God thou didst receive the grace to heal infirmities and drive away demons, being faithful in the invocation of Christ. And thou wast filled with the divine grace and power of the Spirit, O all-blessed one; guided thereby in heaven, thou didst reach the harbor.

*Sticheron:*
Thou wast enriched with the grace of healings and miracles, having purified thyself in mind; for thou didst shake off the infirmities of passions and the darkness and tempest of the flesh, and didst make radiant the disposition of thy soul. Whence also thou didst shine forth among the ranks of monastics, praying the Lover of mankind on behalf of those who praise thee.

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst multiply the talent entrusted to thee before, laboring richly, and having sown with tears, in joy now thou reapest, O father [Ps. 125:5], reaping truly manifold joy and consolation. Having boldness unto the Master, pray for those who sing of thee, O God-bearing Auxentius!

*Glory..., Tone 8 (By Anatolius):*
The pure wisdom of the Holy Spirit, which took up its abode in thy pure heart, O all-blessed Auxentius, venerable father, showed thee forth as a fearful expeller of the spirits of wickedness, and not only fearful, but a physician of secret infirmities. Therefore, having acquired boldness unto God the Lover of mankind, by thine unceasing prayer deliver us from spiritual and bodily passions.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
When the Ewe-lamb beheld the Lamb stretched out willingly upon the tree of the Cross, she cried out maternally, grieving with weeping: "O my Son, what is this fearful sight? O Thou Who givest life unto all as Lord, how art Thou put to death, O Long-suffering One, granting resurrection unto earthly men? I glorify Thy great condescension, O my God!"

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
A dweller of the wilderness, an angel in the flesh, and a wonderworker thou wast revealed to be, O our God-bearing father Auxentius; having received heavenly gifts through fasting, vigilance, and prayer, thou healest the sick, and the souls of those that run unto thee with faith. Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who through thee works healings for all!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing of the divine Auxentius, O venerable one, wisely." A composition of Theophanes.*[^339]
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The ancient Israel, having traversed the Red Sea's deep with dry feet, by Moses' hands outstretched in the form of a cross, vanquished Amalek's power in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
Having turned thy love unto God, O father Auxentius, and forsaken worldly love, O God-bearer, thou wast revealed as a receptive vessel of the gifts of the Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst take upon thee by thine own will, O father Auxentius, the good yoke of the Lord [Matt. 11:30]; and thou didst cultivate new ground, watering the virtues with tears, O worthy of wonder.

*Troparion:*
Having estranged thyself from worldly tumult, and zealously united thy soul and mind unto God by rigorous fasts, thou didst live upon earth as one bodiless, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
In thee was seen now, O blessed Birthgiver of God, the incomprehensible God-manward descent of Christ; for being both God and man and man was born of thee, O pure one, renewing mine essence.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Not in wisdom, and power, and riches do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for none is holy more than Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Enlivened by the power of the Cross, thou didst abolish the assaults of demons and destroy their wiles, vanquishing their onset through fasting.

*Troparion:*
Emulating on earth the angelic life, pure in prayers and strong in vigils, thou didst traverse it zealously, O father Auxentius.

*Troparion:*
Understanding carnal pleasure to be deadly poison coming forth unto souls, Auxentius, by abstinence thou didst put to death its motions, O God-wise and venerable one.

*Theotokion:*
He Who in the beginning fashioned me as man from the dust [Gen. 2:7], is fashioned for my sake in thy womb, O all-immaculate one, setting right the ancient fall.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having forsaken all earthly things, and being in the world bodily, in spirit thou wast a companion of the angels; for having put to death carnal passions, thou wast shown forth as a servant of the Trinity, O blessed one; therefore thou healest the passions of the ailing, and by a word expellest spirits by grace, O God-bearing Auxentius; pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love!

*Glory..., Both now... Theotokion, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
O Cloud of the noetic Sun, golden-wrought Lamp of the divine Light, undefiled, spotless, all-immaculate Sovereign Lady: enlighten my soul, darkened by the blindness of passions, with the radiance of dispassion, I pray thee; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction and tears of repentance, and cleanse me from the mire of my deeds, that with love I may cry unto thee: O Birthgiver of God, Ever-virgin, pray Christ God to grant me remission of transgressions, for I thy servant have thee as my hope!

*Or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Beholding the Lamb, the Shepherd, and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud, weeping and bitterly lamenting, exclaiming: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee; but my maternal heart burns, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest through the tender compassion of mercy, O Long-suffering Lord, Abyss of mercy, and Inexhaustible Fountain! Have mercy upon me and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine passions with faith!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
A conqueror over the spirits of malice by the co-operation and grace of the Holy Spirit thou wast shown to be, O father, crying aloud, Auxentius: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by prayer against the torrents of iniquity, O all-blessed God-bearer, thou didst flee confusion; and thou didst worthily drink of the torrent of delight, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Increasing faith and love toward the Trinity and toward God, unto the celestial height of divine adoption thou wast exalted, crying aloud: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Theotokion:*
Knowing that thou didst clearly bear God the Word in the flesh, we the faithful all truly name thee Birthgiver of God, bringing the title corresponding to each nature of things.[^340]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning from gloomy ignorance those who sing of Thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
The grievous stumbling-blocks lying near thy path thou didst pass through unhurt with sharp mind and purity of soul.

*Troparion:*
Thy life was radiant, and thy faith orthodox, thine abstinence marvelous, and thy speech seasoned with grace [Col. 4:6], Auxentius.

*Troparion:*
With evenness of thoughts thou didst pass through life, traversing temporal things, but ever clinging unto the eternal.

*Theotokion:*
Possessing an upright mind filled with dogmas, we all name thee, O all-pure one, the Birthgiver of God, and with love we call thee blessed.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
"I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord," the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the blood that flowed through mercy from Thy side.

*Troparion:*
Thy life showed thee forth as a son of light and of the day, O blessed one [1 Thess. 5:5], walking truly becomingly, and holding forth the word of life as a luminary in the world [Phil. 2:15–16].

*Troparion:*
Having made thy life a meditation on death, and understanding exceedingly the boundary of wisdom, O father, thou didst pass over unto the Hypostatic Wisdom.

*Theotokion:*
The temple of God and bridal chamber we know thee to be, the jar of manna, the lampstand, and the tablet having written within the Word Who became incarnate through tender compassion, O all-immaculate Virgin!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "In prayers" (Въ мл҃твахъ):*
Delighting in abstinence, O God-wise one, and having bridled the desires of thy flesh, thou wast revealed shining with faith; like a tree in the midst of paradise didst thou blossom forth, O sacred father Auxentius!

*Ikos:*
Who can now recount thy struggles, or thy pains, O father, which thou didst endure upon earth for divine enjoyment, having followed the laws of the Lord, and served His commandments? A new Job thou wast shown unto us by thy contests; thou didst appear as a stranger to the world and a pilgrim to all the earth; by fasting and faith thou didst flourish, and thou didst abound in vigilance and purity, O sacred father Auxentius!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, burning with love of piety rather than with flame, cried aloud: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
As one wounded with a dart of divine love, O father, thou didst turn all the unflagging desire of thy heart unto Him, crying aloud: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!" *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Enlightened by the Savior's dawn, O all-wise one, thou didst patiently expose the leader of darkness, and didst cast down the ruler of the world, crying aloud: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"

*Theotokion:*
Desiring to renew human nature, which of old had decayed, unto a better restoration, the Creator and God dwelt within thy womb, O all-pure one, only all-hymned Lady.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
O All-powerful Redeemer of all, descending in the midst of the flame unto those who practiced piety, Thou didst bedew them, and teach them to sing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works, sing unto Him!"

*Troparion:*
Wholly turned unto God, Thou didst shake off carnal susceptibility to passions, and didst receive the operation of miracles, singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!" *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Fearful wast thou revealed unto demons, having acquired Christ as a helper; taking up Whose Cross thou didst follow Him, singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Theotokion:*
The Archangel appeared, bearing the sacred message, and proclaims concerning thy giving birth, O all-pure one, the conception which is beyond word and saving unto the world: "Bless the Lord, all ye works, sing unto Him!"

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A stone uncut by human hands was cut off from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin [Dan. 2:34, 45], even Christ the corner-stone, Who hath joined together the divided natures. Therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast illuminated unto all the Godhead by thy baptism, O father; and thou didst purely preserve the dignity of the image, O blessed one; into the thrice-radiant splendor thou wast received with rejoicing.

*Troparion:*
Now not in an enigma, O father, neither as in a mirror dost thou behold the illumination of the Godhead, but face to face, O all-wise one [1 Cor. 13:12], being counted worthy of the light that is beyond mind, and of the knowledge of God.

*Troparion:*
Having great boldness unto the King of all, O father, pray that those who now celebrate thy memory may be delivered from every distress, that we all may call thee blessed.

*Theotokion:*
Loose the cords of my transgressions, O Virgin Birthgiver of God [Prov. 5:22], who didst bear the Fountain of tender compassion, O only God-blessed Lady; and fill me with consolation, that I may worthily magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
Turning away from worldly luxury as from defilement, and having withered the flesh with fasting, thou didst renew the strength of thy soul, O venerable one, and wast enriched with heavenly glory, O glorious one; therefore cease not to pray unto the Lord on behalf of us all.

*Theotokion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
The Word on high unites Himself hypostatically in the flesh here below, noetically and perfectly; and One from two comes forth from thee, unconfusedly, O all-pure one, having deified that which He assumed; therefore the world truly glorifies thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*On the Fifteenth Day of the Same Month: The Holy Apostle Onesimus.*[^341]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the celestial ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
*Sticheron:*
Adopted as a son by grace unto God for the sake of faith, and born through Paul's faith [Philem. 1:10], the divine recorder, Onesimus the God-bearing martyr: celebrating his sacred memory today with hymns, let us praise him!

*Sticheron:*
Conformably fleeing from servitude to deceitful things, O wise one, a child a child of God thou didst become by grace, through the Holy Spirit and faith in Christ, numbered among the divine and all-praised disciples of Paul, O profitable Onesimus.

*Sticheron:*
Having become a disciple of the Word through the preacher and divine apostle, the all-praised Paul, O thrice-blessed Onesimus, thou receivest from Christ a threefold crown, being adorned as a priest, as a preacher, and as a sufferer, O all-praised one.

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion, tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
With the staff of thy intercession, O pure Birthgiver of God, drive away the bestial passions of my wretched soul, directing me peacefully unto life, and number me among the flock of thine elect sheep.

*Stavrotheotokion. Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*
When the blameless Virgin saw the Lamb lifted up upon the Cross, weeping she cried aloud: "O my sweet Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How art Thou, Who holdest all things in Thy fist, nailed in the flesh upon the Tree?"

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy Apostle Onesimus, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "Deliver me who am a slave to passions, O blessed one. Theophanes."[^342] Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as if upon dry land, Israel, beholding Pharaoh the persecutor drowning, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the divine and light-bearing grace of the Savior, O blessed Onesimus, entreating Christ the Lover of mankind, our God, do thou illumine my darkened soul.

*Troparion:*
Illumined by the radiant rays of Paul, O glorious one, thou didst easily escape the mist of ignorance, crying aloud a song of thanksgiving unto Christ, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst show thyself a courageous sacred minister, O most blessed one, and shining brightly with the Divine Spirit, thou didst run everywhere, piously proclaiming Christ, O divinely blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Adorned with the beauty of virtues, O pure Mother of God, beyond understanding thou didst conceive the true God, Who illuminated us with divine virtues.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst draw near thy lips to the fountain of divine waters, and receiving therefrom the torrent of delight, O glorious one, thou didst water the face of the honorable Church.

*Troparion:*
By communion with the noetic radiance, thou didst appear most bright, brightly illumining the hearts of the faithful with the lamps of piety and the light-bearing dawn of thy preaching.

*Troparion:*
Through Paul's bonds thou wast loosed from the deception of slavery, and wast honored with the freedom of grace, having become a son of God and shown forth as a divine heir.[^343]

*Theotokion:*
By deceit the serpent at the first took me captive, having captured me through guile; but through thee I was delivered from his slavery, O Mother of God, and with hymns I bless thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предвари́):*
Having truly been loosed from temporal slavery, O Onesimus, thou becamest a servant of Christ God, and a profitable vessel; therefore by the divine word thou didst show those enslaved by the enemy to be free, making them citizens of heaven, with whom we faithfully glorify thy holy memory.

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion:*
By thy divine childbearing, O pure one, thou didst renew the mortal nature of earthborn men that was decayed in passions, and didst raise all from death unto the life of incorruption; therefore as is due we all bless thee, O all-glorified Virgin, as thou didst foretell.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When Thy most pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, lamenting maternally she spoke: "What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death, desiring to bring the dead to life, as Thou art merciful?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church divinely sings, crying aloud from a pure understanding, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
The Lord, desiring to save all, delivers thee from the yoke of slavery, and appoints thee a sacred minister, O blessed one, to minister the honorable Gospel, O divinely blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a son of God by grace, proclaiming His co-eternal Only-begotten Word, Who most gloriously drew near in the flesh unto those upon earth by the word of judgment, and by the divine anointing of the priesthood, thou didst live as a hierarch, O all-wise one, and through endurance of cruel torments thou becamest a divine martyr.

*Theotokion:*
The Son, beginningless and timeless by nature, willingly accepts a beginning in time from the Virgin Maiden, providing to renew from corruption those who are under time.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine with love, I pray, the souls of those who awake early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them from the gloom of sins.

*Troparion:*
By keenness of mind and purity of soul, O blessed one, having flown over the snares of the enemy, thou didst ascend unto the heavenly mansions, having received the wings of the knowledge of God.

*Troparion:*
Thy disciple, O Good One, proclaimed Thy coming in the flesh unto mankind, guiding the erring unto knowledge, and enlightening them with the dawn of faith.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as an all-comely temple, having within thee as a priest the radiance of divine grace, founded upon the edifice of the Holy Spirit, O blessed Onesimus.

*Theotokion:*
Tear asunder the cords of my sin by thy intercession and protection, O Virgin; for thou art the hope of the despairing, of those who flee with faith beneath thy divine protection.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing to Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One!

*Troparion:*
Shining like the sun, he who was called from heaven left unto thee the dawn with the illumination of grace,[^344] driving far away with goodly power the darkness of ignorance, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Brightly didst thou rebuke the rotten and evildoing raging of the lawless, proclaiming in truth the divine teachings of piety, O all-honorable hierarch of the Lord.

*Theotokion:*
Guilty of sins and wounded with wounds, wretch that I am, I flee unto thee, O merciful Mother of God, praying thee to cleanse the arrows of my transgressions.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
As a ray thou didst shine forth upon the universe, shining with the beams of that most radiant sun Paul, who enlightened the world; therefore we honor thee, O glorious Onesimus.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Angel made the furnace pour forth dew for the venerable children, but the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tormentor to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst receive a blessed end, O Onesimus, and imperishable immortality through pure hope, O blessed one, and rejoicing thou singest unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
By thy firm mindset, O Onesimus, thou didst trample down the craft of deception, having cast it down with the weapons of piety, O divinely wise one, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
As being all-immaculate, O Bride of God, thou didst conceive the Word of God, Who is inseparably in the bosom of the Father, yet held by thy hands: Blessed art thou, O Mother of God, who hast not known wedlock!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable youths, and the sacrifice of the righteous one Thou didst consume with water [1 Kgs. 18:38]; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, solely by willing them. Thee do we exalt supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing with the angels, O blessed Onesimus, adorned with the crown of martyrdom, thou standest before the throne of the radiant Master, Whom we exalt supremely unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Performing the divine service in a godly manner, O Onesimus, thou didst possess the operation of ineffable wonders, having become a true preacher of Christ, O blessed one, Whom we exalt supremely unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Having been enriched by the grace of healings from the ungrudging fountain of the Savior, thou didst impart it unto all, obeying His divine commands, O blessed one, Whom we exalt supremely unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
Having been instructed by the wonders of thy childbearing, we sing that the mystery is divine and beyond understanding, O holy, pure, and all-immaculate Mother of God, exalting thee supremely unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word was manifested unto men, Whom magnifying with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a most radiant lamp, shining with bright spiritual rays, illuminating the ends of the world with the light of the knowledge of God, O blessed Onesimus; therefore with the apostolic choirs, we magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with spiritual fruits, rejoicing thou wast taken up unto the heavens, O divinely revealed one; thou didst show thyself an honorable apostle of Christ, all-wise, a hierarch, an invincible martyr, a true preacher of things surpassing the mind, O divinely blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Ever stand by those who constantly sing of thee and praise thy festival, O Onesimus, as thou standest before the Master, entirely illumined, entirely radiant, entirely shining with the splendors from above.

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered through thy childbearing, O Mother of God who hast not known wedlock, from the ancient deadness of the skins,[^345] and having shaken off the curse, corruption, and death that came from sin, with the heavenly hosts I ever glorify thee.

*On the 16th Day of the same Month: Memory of the Holy Martyr Pamphilus and those with him.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Pamphilus and Seleucus together, with Valens and with Paul in faith, Porphyrius also and Theodulus with them, Julian and the five-numbered assembly of the Egyptians, let them be hymned in song as a twelve-stringed harp.

*Sticheron:*
Brightly elevated in the faith of Christ and divine glory, O glorious Pamphilus, thou didst show thyself the goodly adornment of Caesarea, having become an all-beloved hierarch therein according to thy name,[^346] an all-honorable sufferer, and a faithful martyr.

*Sticheron:*
A company didst thou gather, O mighty Pamphilus, of valiant sufferers, twelve in number; ye showed yourselves a divine army, wherewith having conquered the enemies, ye were taken up manifoldly unto the Lord, having finished your course by martyrdom.

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion, tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
With the staff of thy intercession, O pure Birthgiver of God, drive away the bestial passions of my wretched soul, directing me peacefully unto life, and number me among the flock of thine elect sheep.

*Stavrotheotokion. Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*
When the blameless Virgin saw the Lamb lifted up upon the Cross, weeping she cried aloud: "O my sweet Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How art Thou, Who holdest all things in Thy fist, nailed in the flesh upon the Tree?"

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I will hymn the twelve-numbered choir of the passion-bearers. The work of Theophanes."[^347] Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing with unwetted feet the Red Sea's deep, ancient Israel of old, by Moses' hands stretched out in the form of the cross, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
By the power of the All-Holy Spirit the twelve-numbered company assembled, and valiantly, as a trophy-bearer, conquered the godless and wicked host of the ungodly.

*Troparion:*
The enemy was put to shame, having dashed himself against the passion-bearing martyrs; for having brought to nought his deceitful pride, he was not able to cast down the strength of the most valiant.

*Troparion:*
Ye trampled down the fury of the Hellenic raging, O martyrs of Christ, adorned with divine understanding and wisdom, which the adversaries of piety were not able to resist.

*Theotokion:*
Piously glorifying the Cloud that made the Sun of Righteousness shine forth upon us, the Only-begotten Son of the Father, O all-hymned Mother of God, we continually call thee blessed.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, my refuge and my firm support!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst piously shine forth in deed and word, and by the honorable anointing, O martyr of Christ Pamphilus, divinely wise hierarch.

*Troparion:*
Like an all-comely harp attuned by confession, singing unto the Benefactor with your mouths, O martyrs, ye were gathered together.

*Troparion:*
Running toward the understanding of the heavenly and honorable calling, as victors ye received crowns from the Master.

*Theotokion:*
He Who dwells in heaven, living on high above nature, O all-pure one, took flesh from thee and robed Himself.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was astonished" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*
The twelve-numbered choir of divine martyrs, truly arrayed firmly against the persecuting enemies, conquered them with the weapons of faith; and adorned with a crown from God, they were numbered with the angelic choirs, with whom they ever pray that those who bless them may be delivered from temptations, afflictions, and eternal torments.

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion:*
Joseph was astonished, beholding that which was beyond nature, and he considered the rain upon the fleece [Judg. 6:37–40] in thy seedless conception, O Mother of God, the bush unburned by fire [Ex. 3:2], Aaron's rod that budded [Num. 17:8]; and thy betrothed and guardian, bearing witness, cried unto the priests: "A Virgin gives birth, and after giving birth remains still a Virgin!"

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The Virgin and Ewe-lamb, beholding upon the Cross the Lamb born of her without seed, pierced with a spear, wounded with the arrows of grief, cried aloud in pain, exclaiming: "What is this new mystery? How dost Thou die, Who art the only Lord of life? Therefore arise, raising up the fallen forefather!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of Righteousness, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the divine gifts of the apostles and prophets, the choir of Thy martyrs appeared, equal in number unto them, O Master,[^348] having been manifested by Thy providence.

*Troparion:*
Gathered from diverse nations and places of dwelling, as an image of the Church in miniature, ye were counted worthy to be saved, crying in unison: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Guided by the gift of the Master, the all-praised assembly of martyrs, having willingly accepted death for His sake, cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Instructed by the God-inspired utterances concerning thee, O Mother of God, and beholding their fulfillment, we cry unto thee, "Rejoice!", O all-immaculate Virgin, who are delivered by thy childbearing.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, didst come as a light into the world, a holy light turning from the gloomy darkness of ignorance those who sing of Thee in faith.

*Troparion:*
Not enduring to serve the things upon earth, O Pamphilus, thou didst illuminate by suffering the divine life that thou didst have from infancy.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with manly gray hair and with wisdom, Valens, hastening toward the holy city, was crowned as a martyr.

*Troparion:*
Having the mind of Christ [1 Cor. 2:16], as namesaked unto Paul, O all-praised one, having suffered lawfully thou wast adorned with a martyr's crown.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst set upright Eve's slipping, O pure Mother of God, having given birth unto God the Word, Who corrected the fall of the cast-down.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demonic blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy ribs.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the hopes of eternal life, the valiant ones rejoicing endured the tearing asunder of their bodies, being namesakes of Thy divine prophets, O Savior.

*Troparion:*
By death they purchased a better life: Samuel and Elias, Daniel, and the divine Jeremias, and with them Isaias of great name.[^349]

*Troparion:*
Egypt, which of old was covered with the deep darkness of ignorance, being enriched with the light of the knowledge of God, now causes bright luminaries to shine forth unto God.

*Theotokion:*
Daniel beheld thee as the mountain, O all-immaculate one, from which was cut out the stone [Dan. 2:34–35] that crushed every image of deception, and filled all the ends of the earth with the knowledge of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast raised up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
When fearful torments lay before them, the valiant sufferers of the Lord, rejoicing in fearless wisdom, conversed with them, having no care for the flesh; therefore they inherited future glory, ever praying for us who praise their struggles.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, consumed rather with love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
With radiant countenance and purity of mind foreseeing the future joy in the midst of torments, thou didst despise the torments while unmercifully scraped, O all-blessed Porphyrius.

*Troparion:*
God Who saved the three youths in the furnace: He Himself, O blessed passion-bearer, manifestly receives thee, burned by fire for His sake, as a sweet-smelling sacrifice, O Porphyrius.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the fortitude of thy soul and body, thou didst cast down the might of the striker, O Seleucus, all-praised one, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O my God and Lord!

*Theotokion:*
As Mother of God, thou didst give birth unto the most comely of all creation, for thou wast truly the all-holy dwelling-place of God: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Mother of God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Stretching out his hands, Daniel shut the lions' gaping mouths in the pit [Dan. 6:22]; and girded about with virtue the children who loved piety, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Yielding obedience unto the laws of Christ, thou didst cast away the false gods, O all-wise Seleucus; for thou wast a soldier and a teacher of piety, having care for orphans and widows, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Shining with noetic radiance, O glorious elder and martyr Theodulus, thou wast a true servant, imitating the Master, Whose cross desiring, rejoicing thou didst endure, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a temple of the Holy Spirit, O Julian, adorned with goodness of character, full of reverence and brightness, and full of faith thou didst show thyself when burned entirely by fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
The Word was first Son from the Father without a mother, and secondly from thee was without a father, O all-honorable one, having become incarnate, Who before was bodiless, desiring out of tender mercy to save those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, an uncut mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who united the distant natures [Dan. 2:34, Eph. 2:14–20]. Therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Desiring to possess only the heavenly life, O passion-bearers, with noble mind ye despised that which is temporary and corruptible; and now ye enjoy the better and blessed sweetness.

*Troparion:*
Within the divine Paradise ye now zealously dance, standing before the Fountain of illumination, O glorious martyrs, drawing therefrom and being illumined by its rays.

*Troparion:*
Now remembering before the Master Christ all who celebrate thy most radiant memory with faith, O twelve-numbered choir, pray that we may be counted worthy of a dwelling with thee.

*Theotokion:*
As the Mother of the only Master, thou wast higher than all creatures, O Virgin Mary; therefore all we the faithful, rejoicing and making glad, now magnify thee, O all-immaculate one.

*On the 17th Day of the same Month: Memory of the Holy Great-martyr Theodore the Tyro.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Christ gave thee as an enriching gift unto the universe, as the Benefactor God, having accepted as a gift thy precious blood, O Theodore, shed for His sake and offered unto Him with zeal for piety; before Whom now standing with pious boldness, O martyr, do thou save all who flee unto thee.

*Sticheron:*
Thou art a firm pillar repelling the assaults of the adversaries, an invincible champion for those who praise thee: a most speedy defense, a fervent protection, a diligent deliverance, common aid, a most powerful intercessor, O blessed one, a most ready deliverer unto all who faithfully pray unto thee, O Theodore.

*Sticheron:*
Possessing the torrent of sweetness and the water of remission, Christ the Gracious, having been His truest witness: deliver me from the torrents of sin, calm for me the surging water, and guide me unto the calm on high, O ever-blessed Theodore.

*Other Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^350]
Moved by divine zeal, and kindled in soul with love for the Savior Christ, O glorious one: together with her temple thou didst burn the falsely named goddess,[^351] proclaiming the one God and His Name; and thou didst put to shame the raging of the tormentors, O passion-bearer, and wast counted worthy of eternal joy, O Theodore.

*Sticheron:*
Having ascended unto the height of the knowledge of Christ, O Theodore, thou didst pass beyond visible things, and Him alone didst thou seek, and having found Him, thou didst love Him, and with a valiant soul didst suffer for His sake. Pray Him that they may be delivered from corruption and dangers, who celebrate thy most glorious memory with faith.

*Sticheron:*
Girded with divinely bestowed strength and the wounds of struggle, O passion-bearer Theodore, thou didst stand before Christ God, the King of all creation, receiving from Him crowns of honor. Pray Him that they may be delivered from corruption and dangers, who celebrate thy most glorious memory with faith.

*Glory..., Tone 6. Self-noted [Idiomelon]:*
As a gift of healings and a richness of divine life hast thou appeared unto the world, O Theodore; for Christ hath glorified thy memory through thy wisdom, wherein with one accord we the faithful rejoicing sing the struggles of thy sufferings.

*Both now..., Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid all aside" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
A sword pierced thy heart, as Simeon spoke, O most pure Lady,[^352] when thou didst behold Him Who shone forth from thee by the ineffable Word, condemned by the lawless as a criminal, lifted up upon the Cross, given vinegar and gall to drink, His side pierced, and His hands and feet nailed; and lamenting thou didst cry aloud, weeping maternally: "What is this, O my sweetest Child, this new mystery?"

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*
*Glory..., Tone 8. Self-noted [Idiomelon]:*
Having piously girded thyself with the courage of suffering, O passion-bearer of Christ, and mystically contending for the rational service by His power, thou didst show the idolatrous impiety and the persecutor's threat to be powerless, having disregarded the torments and the temporal fire; but from the divine gifts, both in deed and in name,[^353] save by thy prayer from every adversity those who celebrate thy memory.

*Both now..., Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
The unblemished heifer, beholding the calf willingly nailed upon the Tree, lamented piteously and cried aloud: "Woe is me, O most beloved Child! What hath the ungrateful congregation of the Hebrews rendered unto Thee, desiring to make me childless of Thee, O all-beloved?"

*Troparion, Tone 2:*
Great are the achievements of faith! In the fountain of flame, as upon the water of rest, the holy martyr Theodore rejoiced; for having been wholly consumed by fire, he was offered as sweet bread unto the Trinity.[^354] By his prayers, O Christ God, save our souls.

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos; and the Saint's, to 6.*
*Canon, whose acrostic is: "Save those who call upon thee, O all-blessed one." Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the water as if dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion:*
Having striven in the divine contest, O martyr of Christ, and having finished the course, thou didst keep the divine faith, and hast found the crown of righteousness.[^355]

*Troparion:*
Having received supernatural glory from Christ, O passion-bearer, thou dost traverse the whole earth, delivering all who hasten unto thee with faith and reverence.

*Troparion:*
O member of the choir of heavenly glory, being with the bodiless hosts, deliver from afflictions and adversities those on earth who call upon thee.

*Theotokion:*
Having ineffably borne the Wisdom and Word of the Father, heal the cruel wound of my soul, and calm the pain of my heart.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those who hasten unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.

*Troparion:*
The fire of torments did not terrify thee; therefore thou dost extinguish the fire of adversities for those who flee unto thee, O Theodore.

*Troparion:*
By thy prayers deliver me from the griefs that hold me fast, O martyr of Christ, smoothing all the crookedness of worldly life.

*Troparion:*
Lo, unto thee do I stretch out my heart, my mind, and my soul, O passion-bearer, requiring thy help.

*Theotokion:*
Still the unstable confusion of my thoughts, O Mother of God, directing their motion unto thy Son.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Adorned with the beauties of martyrdom, rejoicing before the Heavenly King, O crowned martyr Theodore, thou dost stand; for upon a cross wast thou lifted up for His sake,[^356] and the driving in of nails, the ox-hide thongs, the scrapings with claws, and the gouging out of the pupils of thine eyes didst thou endure steadfastly; therefore with faith we honor thee, O great victor. *Twice.*

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion, tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
He Who sits upon the cherubic throne and rests in the bosom of the Father, sits as upon His holy throne in thy womb, O Lady; for God, having truly become incarnate, is become King over all nations; and understandingly we now sing unto Him: Whom do thou entreat, that thy servants may be saved.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, she who in latter times bore Thee in the flesh, Who wast begotten of the beginningless Father, cried aloud: "Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How art Thou Who art glorified as God by the angels, now willingly crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thee, O Long-suffering One."

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I understood Thy works, and glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
I set thee forth as a helmsman, O favored one of God; do Thou guide me by the sail of the Spirit and by divine winds, O most glorious one.

*Troparion:*
From every threat deliver, we pray, O all-blessed Theodore, those who with faith call thee blessed and honor thy divine memory.

*Troparion:*
Neither life nor death, nor things present nor things to come, nor fire, nor wounds, have separated thee from Christ, O divinely wise one.[^357]

*Theotokion:*
Him Who was under time, Who timelessly shone forth from the Father, hast thou borne for us, O Mother of God; Whom do thou entreat, to save those who sing of thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Awaking early, we cry unto Thee, O Lord: Save us! For Thou art our God; besides Thee we know none other.

*Troparion:*
Having become the adornment of martyrs, O martyr of Christ, be thou a help and an unbroken wall unto the faithful.

*Troparion:*
Enjoying the better union, which is of good things beyond the mind, O Theodore, save those who honor thee.

*Troparion:*
Overcome by love for the Creator, thou didst shake off the attachments of life, and didst well-please God.

*Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst bear God the Word, diligently pray for us who sing of thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the depths of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:*
Having been a most fervent champion of piety and a reprover of the idolatrous deceit: destroy the phantoms of demons and the images of passions of my soul.

*Troparion:*
Be thou my confirmation and an invincible wall, O martyr Theodore, confirming the weakness and corruption of my mind by thine intercessions, and preserving me unharmed.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst renounce the corrupting and corruptible warfare, and didst love unto the end the warfare of life, O all-blessed Theodore, wherein thou wast shown forth as a victor, O worthy of wonder.

*Theotokion:*
