Splendor shone forth from Sion on high by the assumption of flesh according to the ineffable union, having clothed Himself from thee, O unwedded Maiden, and having enlightened the world.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
Having received the faith of Christ within thy heart as a shield, thou didst trample down the adversary powers, O much-suffering one, and hast been eternally crowned with a heavenly crown, O Theodore, as one invincible.

*Ikos:*
Thee Who art borne upon the throne of light, do we with thanksgiving and faith hymn; for Thou didst grant as a divine gift the valiant Theodore in sufferings, thrice-blessed in life, as a champion of truth, who with pious mind desired Thee, O Christ, a mighty victor over the deceiver, as one invincible.[^358]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The youths having arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled down the flame of the furnace, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thee do I have as the intercessor of my life, the protector of my salvation, and my guardian, O divinely wise one, who grantest confirmation unto those who call aloud with faith: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
As a fair and most sacred slaughter-victim didst thou offer thyself unto the Lord, O all-blessed one; and in the fire of torments thou wast wholly consumed, O glorious one, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having been filled with the life-giving inspiration of the Divine Spirit, O blessed one, thou drivest away spirits, healest diseases, and callest aloud, rejoicing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
From thy womb came forth the Lord of divine things, incarnate, O Virgin; therefore, orthodoxly knowing thee to be the Birthgiver of God, unto thy Son we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindle the furnace for the godly ones; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Surrounded by worldly tempests, O all-praised one, and held fast by the adversities of life, as a most speedy intercessor, helper, and champion, we the faithful bring thee before the Master as an intercessor, crying aloud: Cease not to pray for those who hymn thine all-honorable memory!

*Troparion:*
Bound with the chains of sins, O most enriched one, and fettered with the bonds of my transgressions, I flee unto thy protection, O crowned one, asking for release but having marvelous boldness before the Master of all, O divinely wise one, pray that they may be saved who sing of Christ unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the brightness of martyrdom, O all-praised one, thou hast been numbered among the ranks of angels, with whom rejoicing and ever dancing in chorus, O martyr, by wonder-working healings thou dost make radiant those who honor thee with love and faithfully sing: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Having come in the flesh unto men, as He is merciful, the Creator and Maker of creation received it from thee, O unwedded Mother of God; therefore we the faithful truly glorify thee as the Birthgiver of God, crying aloud and faithfully singing: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thee, the unwedded Mother of God Most High, thee who beyond understanding didst bear by the Word the true God, higher than the most pure powers, with unceasing doxologies do we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired thee as a vigilant guardian, O great-martyr, easily driving away the malice of the enemies and granting peace unto the churches, O passion-bearer, we unceasingly call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
The Master granted thee strength against demons, and to heal the passions of soul and body, O blessed one, praying unto the Savior with most pious boldness; therefore we all call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Beholding the three-sun and single radiance, the one Godhead in three Persons, the Origin above all origin, and the Fountain of goodness, O passion-bearer, preserve those who sing of thee.

*Theotokion:*
Deliver me, O all-immaculate Lady, from noetic captivity, granting as deliverance the crucifixion of thy Son, which was deliverance for all, and peace and salvation unto those who sing of thee.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "The heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
Having been tried like gold by fire and torments, thou hast appeared as a worthy gift of God Most High, O passion-bearer Theodore, now praying for us.

*Theotokion:*
By thy sovereign protection preserve all of us thy servants unharmed from the assault of enemies, O pure one; for thee alone have we acquired as a refuge in necessities.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Octoechos. Glory..., Tone 2:*
Thee who art named after divine gifts,[^359] O thrice-blessed Theodore, do I honor; for having appeared as an unsetting luminary of the divine light, thou didst enlighten all creation by thy sufferings and having appeared mightier than fire, thou didst quench the flame and crush the head of the deceptive serpent. Therefore after thy sufferings Christ inclined and crowned thy divine head, O great-martyr sufferer. As thou hast boldness before God, diligently pray for our souls!

*Both now..., Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the pure one beheld suspended upon the Tree the fully ripened Cluster,[^360] Whom she had borne in her womb untilled, weeping she cried aloud and called out: "O my Child, drop down sweetness, whereby all drunkenness of the passions may be taken away, O Benefactor, being entreated by Thy tender mercy for the sake of me who bore Thee!"

*At the Liturgy, Beatitudes of the Octoechos to 4; and of the Saint, Ode 3 to 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him [LXX Ps. 63:11]. Verse: Hear, O God, my prayer, when I make supplication unto Thee [LXX Ps. 63:2]. Epistle to Timothy, Pericope 292 [2 Tim. 2:1–10]. Alleluia, Tone 4: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, and shall be increased like the cedar in Lebanon [LXX Ps. 91:13]. Verse: They that are planted in the house of the Lord, in the courts of our God shall flourish [LXX Ps. 91:14]. Gospel of John, Pericope 52 [John 15:17–16:2]. Communion Verse: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be [LXX Ps. 111:6].*

*On the 18th Day of the same Month: Our Father among the Saints, Leo, Pope of Rome.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что̀ вы̀ нарече́мъ):*
What shall we name thee, O divinely inspired one? The head of the Orthodox Church of Christ?[^361] An eye of piety, clearly beholding with the heart spiritual understanding? Pouring forth the word of life unto all ends of the earth: a divinely inscribed scroll of the right faith. Pray that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron:*
What shall I now call thee, O marvelous one? A preacher of truth, and a firm foundation? The primate of the honorable supreme council?[^362] An exact rule of teaching exact, and possessing an upright balance,[^363] cutting off both the division of Nestorius and the confusion of Eutyches by divinely woven manifestations.[^364]

*Sticheron:*
What shall I now call thee, O marvelous one? A ruler and king over bodily passions, guiding spiritual virtue brightly? A vessel of divine mercy; an abode of perfect love; waiting for the conversion of those who sin against the love of mankind. Pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion, tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Unto whom hast thou become like, O wretched soul, in no wise looking up unto repentance, nor fearing the fire that awaits the wicked? Arise, and call upon the one who is speedy unto protection, and cry aloud: "O Virgin Mother, pray thy Son and our God, to deliver me from the snare of the deceitful one!"

*Stavrotheotokion, Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*
When the ewe-lamb beheld the Lamb willingly stretched out upon the Tree of the Cross, weeping maternally and suffering with tears, she cried aloud: "O my Son, what is this strange sight? How art Thou Who grantest life unto all as Lord, put to death, O Long-suffering One, granting resurrection unto earthborn men? I glorify Thy great condescension, O my God!"

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Guide of Orthodoxy, teacher of piety and purity, luminary of the universe, divinely inspired adornment of hierarchs, O all-wise Leo, by thy teachings thou hast enlightened all, O spiritual flute: pray Christ God that our souls may be saved![^365]

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I weave hymns unto all-wise Leo. The work of Theophanes." Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the anointing of the priesthood, O all-blessed one, thou didst embellish it with forms of virtue, O most enriched one.

*Troparion:*
Like a ripe cluster of grapes, O father, pressing out understanding, a chalice of gladness of thy wisdom didst thou set before all, O thrice-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast the successor to the throne of Peter the Primate, having his disposition and his divinely wise zeal for the faith.[^366]

*Theotokion:*
Having borne the Savior, O pure Mother of God, thou didst free the first-formed man who was condemned through the transgression of the law.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Supreme Creator of the celestial vault and Founder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
As a pillar of the Orthodoxy of Christ's Church, O all-wise Leo, thou didst zealously raise up thy feet; holding fast to which, she destroys the regiments and assemblies of godless heretics.

*Troparion:*
Having been filled with heavenly and divine grace, O father, thou didst set forth the dogmas of the Church; wherewith every babble of heretical tongues is combated, O champion of piety, most enriched Leo.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the brightest light, O venerable one, thou didst make clear the word of ineffable and divine teaching: having declared the dual nature and dual operation of God incarnate.[^367]

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast been adorned like a bride with maidenly beauties, O Maiden full of grace, unwedded Mother; for above every creature hast thou been exalted far higher, having borne the Word Who is the Cause of all.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Thou didst still insolent boldnesses, and by deeds didst convince of Orthodoxy, having hoped like a lion, O righteous one;[^368] thou declarest true teachings and exposest the hiding places of deceit, O venerable father. Entreat Christ God, that He grant us great mercy!

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion, tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Without separating from the divine nature, becoming flesh in thy womb, yet God having become man remained, Who preserved thee as a Virgin Mother after childbearing, even as before childbirth, all-immaculate, the only Lord: Him do thou diligently pray, that He grant us great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The immaculate ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding hanging upon the Cross Him Who sprouted forth from her without travails, lamenting as becomes a mother, cried aloud: "Woe is me, O my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, the prophet divinely foresaw thee, the only Birthgiver of God, of whom He was to become incarnate, and with fear he glorified Thy power.

*Troparion:*
A lion in truth didst thou appear, O all-blessed one driving away the ravaging foxes, terrifying with a kingly roar the evil designs of the ungodly.[^369]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst conceal the traces of God-abhorred heresies by the application of thy dogmas, having searched out the hidden truth, O all-honorable sacred initiate.

*Troparion:*
As the dawn from the West didst thou shine forth, O thrice-blessed one, emitting the scroll of godly dogmas like beams of the Church, O divinely wise one, and enlightening our souls.[^370]

*Theotokion:*
Put to death the surgings of my passions, O Virgin; still the tumult of transgressions, O Good One, destroying the storm of sins by thy calm, O Mother of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O Unsetting Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and guide my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a preacher of the dual operation of Christ the Savior; for thou didst say, O blessed hierarch, divinely wise, that each nature acts in communion with the other, according to that which it had.[^371] *Twice.*

*Troparion:*
Equal in power unto the Father Who begat Him didst thou know the Word to be; and that He became flesh, O thrice-blessed one, thou didst confirm: saying that fitly, each form performs its own proper properties, unconfusedly and unalterably, both the one and the other.[^372]

*Theotokion:*
The mystery known unto God alone before all ages: that the Good One and Lord of all should become man, and be united with the flesh without alteration, hath appeared from thee, O Virgin, receiving a transformation.[^373]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my griefs; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades; and I pray like Jonah: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:*
Successor to the honorable Peter, and enriched with his primacy, having acquired fervent zeal, thou didst write the divinely moved scroll,[^374] casting down the troubled command of heretics, uniting without confusion the unmingled mixture.

*Troparion:*
One Son, Christ and Lord, begotten of the Father before the ages, and born for our sake of the Virgin, and having appeared unto us as consubstantial, didst thou supernatural proclaim, O God-receiver, minister of ineffable things!

*Theotokion:*
Confessing thee, O all-pure one, as the mediatress of salvation crying aloud: "Rejoice, O pure one; rejoice, O Bride-Virgin! Rejoice, O overshadowing mountain of God; for thou didst pour forth ever-existing joy unto the world!"

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
Seated upon the throne of the priesthood, O glorious one, and having stopped the mouths of noetic lions,[^375] with the divinely inspired dogmas of the honorable Trinity thou didst illumine the light of the knowledge of God for thy flock. Therefore thou hast been glorified as a divine initiate of God's grace.

*Ikos:*
Having proclaimed the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the three-sun Light, the three-hypostatic Power, the one Godhead, and the one Essence: thou didst stop to the end the deceitful mouths of heretics, O divinely wise Leo. Therefore, having confessed the pure Mother of God to be the Birthgiver of God, thou didst cast down the folly and babble of Nestorius.[^376] For this cause thou wast known in the world and in Rome as a divine initiate of God's grace.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trampled down the flame and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst not give sleep unto thine eyelids,[^377] until thou didst uproot the deceit of Eutyches who had lost his mind, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Having confessed Christ as one Hypostasis in two essences, operating doubly and willing doubly,[^378] thou now singest: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
Now I flee unto thee, O Mother of God, bound with the bonds of transgressions; in thy tender mercy, O Mother, deliver me who cry aloud: Blessed, O most pure one, is the Fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindle the furnace for the godly ones; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having enlightened like the sun, thou didst shine forth from the West, a manifest, most glorious wonder, O divine speaker! Clearly having exposed the mixture and confusion of Eutyches, and cutting short the division of Nestorius, teaching all to honor the one Christ in two essences, indivisibly, unalterably, inconfusedly.[^379]

*Troparion:*
Moved by God, the teachings of piety, as upon divinely inscribed tablets, didst thou depict didst depict, appearing as a second Moses unto the divine people and the assembly of honorable teachers, crying: "Children, bless; priests, sing praises; people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!"[^380]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst recognize the incarnate One, O venerable father, Who was previously bodiless, the only-begotten and co-unoriginate Word of the Father, in time, Who is timeless; circumscribed indeed in the flesh, yet as Creator knowing no circumscription, crying unto the disciples: "Priests, sing praises, O people, and highly exalt Him unto ages!"

*Theotokion:*
By the clothing of the body from thy blood was God clothed, O most pure one, He Who enlightened the heaven with the beauties of the luminaries, the sun and the moon, showing thee forth as another animate heaven; Whom the children bless, the priests praise in song, and the people highly exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every hearing was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from a virgin's womb; therefore, O most pure Mother of God, we the faithful magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Thou shinest now, O initiate of Christ's mysteries, adorned with the crown of majesty; and as a faithful hierarch, thou hast truly been vested with righteousness, and dancing in the paradise of sweetness, O marvelous one, diligently pray for thy flock unto the Good One.

*Troparion:*
Where now are the precedences, thrones, and orders of the patriarchs, O all-blessed Leo, there hast thou worthily dwelt, O father, as a true patriarch, radiant with faith and grace; therefore do we all ever call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Having fled the storm of life, thou didst come unto Christ, O most eminent Leo, resting in a place of green pasture,[^381] where is the torrent of sweetness,[^382] the unwaning light, the ineffable gladness, and the abiding joy.

*Theotokion:*
Let us reap mature life, unharmed by the tree of knowledge; for thou, O all-immaculate one, didst bud forth Christ, the Tree of Life, revealing the entrances of life unto all; therefore, O spotless Mother of God, we the faithful magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
The glory of hierarchs and defender of the faithful hast thou appeared, O divinely wise father, and a pillar of virtues; therefore ever remember those who sing of thee with love.

*Theotokion:*
All of us sinners having thee as an intercessor, O most holy Virgin, do thou make thy Son gracious unto us by thy maternal prayers.

*On the 19th Day of the same Month: The Holy Apostle Archippus.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "Thy martyrs, O Lord" (Мꙋ́ченицы твоѝ гдⷭ҇и):*
With the light of words did Archippus enlighten the people, and delivered them from the darkness of ignorance; and having suffered, and trampled down the enemy, he ascended unto the unwaning radiance, and now rejoices with the angels. By his prayers, O Master, grant great mercy unto all.[^383]

*Sticheron:*
Urged with flatteries, O blessed one, yet pierced, and beaten, surrounded by other forms of cruelties, thou didst not deny Christ, nor didst thou render honor unto carved idols; therefore thou hast been crowned, ever praying that great mercy may be granted unto all.[^384]

*Sticheron:*
With the divine streams of thy blood, O divinely wise martyr, thou didst sanctify the earth, and with thy wounds didst wound the wicked multitude of demons; and pouring forth healings unceasingly, thou dost heal the most grievous passions. Therefore by thy prayers, O blessed one, entreat great mercy for all.

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion:*
To snatch me from the hands of the man-slaying serpent, who desires through cunning to swallow me up utterly, break his jaws, I pray thee, O Sovereign Lady, and destroy his snares, so that having escaped from his claws, I may magnify thy protection.[^385]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Not enduring to behold Thee fallen asleep upon the Tree, O Child, Who grantest vigilance unto all, so that unto those who of old fell asleep with a deadly sleep through the fruit of transgression, Thou mightest give divine and saving vigilance, the Virgin, weeping, said, whom we magnify.

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy apostle Archippus, entreat the merciful God, that He may grant remission of sins unto our souls.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing of the wise and divinely speaking Archippus." A creation of Joseph. Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the Red Sea's deep with unwetted feet, ancient Israel of old, with Moses' arms outstretched in the likeness of the Cross, conquered Amalek's power in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
Having shone forth with the enlightenment of grace, O all-wise Paul, he sent thee forth as a radiant beam, O blessed one, driving away by the Spirit the deepest darkness of polytheism, O Archippus.

*Troparion:*
By the divine cords of the words of Paul the preacher wast thou caught, and from the depth of the enemy's malice didst thou most gloriously catch the people, and brought them as rational food unto the divine table.[^386]

*Troparion:*
Clothed in the vesture of divine grace, O Archippus, thou didst clothe in the garment of incorruption and the divine robe of salvation, O glorious one, those who had been stripped of the divine glory.

*Theotokion:*
Having seen from afar with prophetic eyes the dreadful mystery of thy divine childbearing which is beyond understanding, O all-honorable one, the divinely speaking prophets explained it with various figures.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: "Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation!"

*Troparion:*
The divine Paul testifies of thee, O Archippus, as a faithful servant of Christ, and a sacred preacher, and a fellow soldier,[^387] O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Noetically kindled by the fire of the Comforter, thou didst burn up the bitter matter of the madness of idolatry, O Archippus, all-praised one.

*Troparion:*
Thy radiant life shone forth with the lightnings of virtues, enlightening those who piously honor thee, O Archippus.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast been shown forth as a temple capable of holding God, O all-praised, all-immaculate one, a pure dwelling and a godlike habitation.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "The choir of angels" (Ли́къ а҆́ггелскїй):*
Having become a disciple of Paul, O godbearer, thou didst enlighten the people to sing unto the Trinity; and by grace thou didst destroy destructive temples, and having suffered patiently, thou didst joyfully receive crowns, praying for us, O blessed Archippus.

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion:*
O unwedded, pure Virgin Mother of God, the only protection and shelter of the faithful: deliver from perils, afflictions, and all cruel circumstances those who place their hope in thee, O Maiden, and save our souls by thy divine prayers.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee stretched out dead upon the Cross, O Christ, the all-immaculate Mother cried aloud: "O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thine all-pure hands, O Compassionate One?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of Righteousness, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
As thou didst proclaim divine things, and didst openly convict the godless, the lawless persecutor delivered thee over to beating, and unto wounds, O Archippus, and unto manifold torments.[^388]

*Troparion:*
Leading thy life well, O blessed one, thou wast truly accounted worthy to find a blessed end; for thou wast Christ's martyr most valiantly, having endured bodily pains.

*Troparion:*
The festering corruption of godlessness, the fulfillment of the pious, didst thou arrest with the sweet salt of thy words,[^389] and rejoicing didst cry aloud: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Theotokion:*
As an overshadowed mountain did Habakkuk of old foresee thee, O all-immaculate one,[^390] bearing the Word Who overshadows all from the flame of transgression and from the burning of sins, O Maiden.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as a Light, a holy Light turning from dark ignorance those who praise Thee in faith.

*Troparion:*
With the divine lightnings of all-wise preaching thou didst enlighten those held fast by the night of ignorance, O divinely wise, all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
The divine river proceeding from thy heart drowned the streams of deception, and gave to drink unto the minds of the faithful, O most blessed one.[^391]

*Troparion:*
Animated by good hopes, thou didst endure being dragged by four,[^392] and didst sanctify the earth with thy blood, O thou who art acceptable unto God.

*Theotokion:*
Thee alone from among all generations did the Lord choose, O Mother of God; and from thee He became incarnate, deifying humanity.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
"I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord," the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demons' blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion:*
In no wise weakened by delusions, the deceitful one unmercifully smote thee with wounds, O divinely wise one, as thou didst look toward the glory which Christ will give thee, glorifying thee.

*Troparion:*
The hosts of the invisible enemies hast thou wounded with the wounds of thy flesh, O blessed Archippus, and exalting, thou didst rend the torturer's heart with lacerations.

*Troparion:*
Those who serve stones with an unfeeling heart stone thee with stones, O blessed one, as thou dost confess the Stone of Life,[^393] and destroying the confirmation of error, the torment destroying the confirmation of error.

*Theotokion:*
Having put off mortality, Adam was clothed in life in paradise, and was made immortal by thy life-bearing childbearing, O all-immaculate Bride of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Having thee as a great star, O Archippus, enlightened by the rays of thy wonders, the Church cries unto thee: "Save those who honor thy memory in faith!"

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, ignited by love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Attested by divinely spoken and divinely wise words, O all-blessed one, with true understanding thou didst drive away the darkness of ignorance from the souls of those who obeyed thy honorable word, by the grace of wisdom.

*Troparion:*
In a deep pit the crowds of the impious bury thee, O martyr, and mercilessly put thee to death with stones as thou dost sing: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"[^394]

*Troparion:*
The earth displays thy buried body, O martyr, pouring forth waters of healings unto the faithful; and heaven, O blessed Archippus, numbers thy spirit with the souls of the passion-bearers.

*Theotokion:*
While the Lord clothes the earth with mist and the heaven with clouds, O Maiden, He willingly clothes Himself in mortal flesh from thy blood, so that He might clothe man in the garment of immortal glory.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Stretching out his hands, Daniel stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the children, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all you works of the Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Extolling thee with sacred praises, O glorious Archippus, Paul depicted thee as a fellow soldier,[^395] an accomplisher of divine deeds, serving him, and singing in faith: "Bless the Lord, all you works of the Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Anointed with honorable words for martyrdom, the marvelous Apphia, loving thee most wisely as an intimate son, with thee now dwells in the highest kingdoms, crying: "Bless the Lord, all you works of the Lord!"[^396]

*Troparion:*
Those who were infants in understanding, though thou hadst wisdom, gave thee over as a sport unto children, O Archippus, as thou didst suffer most patiently, and mercilessly by them mercilessly by them wast pierced, crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all you works of the Lord!"

*Theotokion:*
Adam became dead because of a single eating, grasping the death of the tree of knowledge, O pure one; but through thee he was restored to life, and received again the delight of paradise, singing: "Bless the Lord, all you works of the Lord!"

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out without hands from an uncut mountain, which is thee, O Virgin, even Christ, Who joined together the natures that had stood apart; therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
That thou mightest inherit everlasting glory, O passion-bearer, thou didst despise earthly glory, which is unstable and passing away; and thou hast drawn near unto Christ, adorned with His glory.

*Troparion:*
Thy head was bound with a crown of victory, O divinely wise one, and thou wast clothed in a purple robe dipped in thy blood, O Archippus; and rejoicing with Christ, thou reignest, O most blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Having become a disciple of the foremost of the apostles, the wise and divinely speaking Paul, O all-blessed one, thou rejoicest with him, having inherited everlasting delight, the adornment of the Colossians.[^397]

*Troparion:*
The people have gathered on Thy light-bearing memory to sing of the Savior Benefactor, and of Thy valiant sufferings, O Archippus, which thou didst manfully endure, casting down the crafty one.

*Theotokion:*
Bearing in thine arms Christ, Who holds all things by His nod: pray Him as thy Son, O most pure one, to deliver me from the hands of the enemy, and through the abyss of His tender mercy, to embrace and save me.

*On the 20th Day of the same Month: Our Venerable Father Leo, Bishop of Catania.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "Lord, even if at the judgment seat" (Гдⷭ҇и, а҆́ще и҆ на сꙋди́щи):*
Lord, Thou hast shown forth the hierarch in the world, the successor of the apostles, in deeds and words, a preacher of upright doctrines; and therefore hast Thou appointed him a guide of hierarchs, and a partaker of Thine incorruptible glory.[^398]

*Sticheron:*
As a living pillar and a proclaimer of the words of upright doctrines does the Church of Christ hold thee, O Leo; beholding this immovable truth, she ever abides glorifying the Master of all and Lord Who glorified thee.[^399]

*Sticheron:*
Lord, Thou hast shown forth the hierarch's word as a sharpened sword against those who act impiously against Thy Orthodox faith; and thereby cutting off false opinions, Thou hast multiplied the doctrines of piety in the world, O Master.

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion, tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
The distress of my contrite heart, and the groanings, O Bride of God, do thou accept, O pure Virgin; and do not cast away the lifting up of my hands, O all-immaculate and pure one, since thou art a lover of goodness; that I may praise and magnify thee who hast magnified our race.[^400]

*Stavrotheotokion, Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*
When the ewe-lamb and virgin beheld Thee, the Lamb, led unto slaughter, weeping she followed Thee, O Word, and cried aloud: "Whither dost Thou hasten, O my sweetest Child, at Thy departure? For I cannot endure not to see Thee, O my most merciful Jesus!"

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A rule of faith, and an image of meekness, a teacher of temperance did the reality of things show thee forth unto thy flock; therefore thou didst acquire through humility the highest things, through poverty the richest, O father Leo: entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.[^401]

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I honor Leo, the sacred source of wonders." Joseph. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the water as if dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: "Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!"

*Troparion:*
Having acquired thee, O wise Leo, as a divine luminary, the radiant Church of Christ is richly enlightened by the radiances of thy virtues and wonders.

*Troparion:*
From infancy thou didst dedicate thyself unto the Master of all; and by the mortification of pleasures, thou wast His living sacrifice, slaying thyself without blood, O blessed one.[^402]

*Troparion:*
Thy life shines, adorned with virtues and beautified with the lightnings of wonders, O godbearer, making thee all-praised unto all.

*Theotokion:*
A temple of God wast thou, O undefiled Maiden, for Him Who dwelt beyond understanding in thee, O pure Mother of God Mother of God, and Who drove away deception from the souls of men.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Maker of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Preserving unharmed the hierarch's doctrines of piety and Orthodoxy, O father, thou didst shepherd the flock in life-bearing pastures, and didst lead them into the heavenly fold.

*Troparion:*
Having ascended unto the height of virtues, O venerable one, thou didst receive the sacred anointing, and didst extol the Most High from the highest throne, O all-wise one, having served Him by grace as an angel.[^403]

*Troparion:*
Having endured adversities for the sake of Christ, O venerable one, thou hast partaken of the immortality of deification; and unceasingly pouring forth fragrant myrrh, thou dost sanctify those who hasten unto thee in faith.[^404]

*Theotokion:*
O tongs of the divine and radiant coal,[^405] who wast not consumed by the fire of the Godhead: burn up all my brushwood passions, and snatch me from the eternal fire!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Боже́ственныя вѣ́ры):*
Thou didst shine forth as a great sun unto the universe by the radiances of thy virtues, and didst enlighten the assemblies of the faithful with the brightness of wonders; and having driven away the darkness of the passions, O venerable Leo, entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory..., both now..., Theotokion:*
Without parting from the divine nature, He became flesh in thy womb, yet remained God incarnate, the only Lord, Who preserved thee an all-immaculate Virgin Mother after birth-giving even as before birth-giving; Him diligently entreat to grant us great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding suspended upon the Cross Him Who sprouted forth from her without pain, lamenting maternally, cried aloud: "Woe is me, O my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparion:*
Having tilled the field of thy mind, thou didst yield a hundredfold ear of corn, O exceedingly rich one,[^406] of divine virtues and healings.

*Troparion:*
This thy shrine, O glorious one, continually pours forth holy oil, sanctifying the souls of those who hasten unto thee in faith.[^407]

*Troparion:*
A cleanser of diseases, a driver-away of cunning demons hast thou been, O hierarch, and a refuge of faithful men.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is uncontainable by all, O most pure one, is contained within thy womb, saving me by His goodness, who am submerged in many sins.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy presence, O Light unsetting, and a strange darkness has covered me, wretched one? But convert me, and guide my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!

*Troparion:*
With a most pure mind beholding the Mind, the Cause of all: from Him, O divinely inspired one, thou didst receive the beam of gifts, the sacred outpourings of light of healings, whereby thou dost dispel the darkness of the passions.

*Troparion:*
Unto the fiery trial didst thou deliver in dispute, O blessed one, him who was deceiving all who believe in Christ by demonic deceptions; and thou didst deliver souls from this all-ruinous harm, as a true and saving shepherd.[^408]

*Troparion:*
When the fire was kindled, thou didst stand in the midst unharmed, O most glorious one; for the divine dew of the Holy Spirit sprinkled Thee as an initiate of sacred mysteries, as a minister of divine glory, as a partaker of the light on high.[^409]

*Theotokion:*
Beholding of old most divinely, O thou who art full of divine joy, the ineffable depth of the mystery of thy honorable childbearing, O most pure one, the venerable prophets proclaimed it in advance under various figures, as receivers of this mystery.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my supplication unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my griefs, for my soul is full of evils, and my life has drawn near unto hell; and like Jonah I pray: "Raise me up from corruption, O God!"

*Troparion:*
The blind received sight by thy prayers, O father, O divinely wise one; for having Christ as fellow-worker, and having the eyes of thy soul ever fixed upon Him, thou didst richly receive requests.[^410]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast awesome unto the emperor, O marvelous one, when going unto his courts thou didst carry burning coals in thy garment; for God glorified thee, Whom thou didst glorify by thy life, O Leo.[^411]

*Troparion:*
The shrine of thy relics is fragrant, and ever pours forth, O all-blessed one, as a pure and sweet-smelling fountain, the divine oil; whereby diseases are driven away from the faithful, and health is granted unto those in need.[^412]

*Theotokion:*
Sanctify my soul, O Virgin, who didst bear the Holy Word; for truly He rests most purely as the Holy One among the saints, our only God; and grant me, as thou art merciful, a shower of compunction, O all-immaculate one.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "As firstfruits" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
As a very great luminary does the Church possess thee, O all-blessed one, shining more brightly than the sun; which Church do thou preserve by thy prayers, O blessed one, invincible and unshakable from every heresy, and undefiled, as one ever-memorable.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!"

*Troparion:*
Equaling in the flesh the divine incorporeal ranks, O divinely wise one, thou didst serve God, unceasingly singing: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!"

*Troparion:*
Deaf ears didst thou open by thy divine prayers, O all-blessed one; and unto the lame thou didst grant good motion in walking, crying: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!"[^413]

*Troparion:*
By the divine nod of the Master thou didst remain unburnt, having entered into the midst of the fire; but he who had no part in this, Heliodorus, was burned by just judgment through thy prayer.[^414]

*Theotokion:*
The fire of the Godhead did not consume thy womb, O Virgin Mother, as He became incarnate from thee, bedewing the ends of the earth with divine splendors; therefore do we sing of thee.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Having become conquerors of the tyrant and of the flame by Thy grace, cleaving earnestly unto Thy commandments, the children cried aloud: "Bless the Lord, all you works of the Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Grace was poured forth upon thy lips, O father, initiate of sacred mysteries; therefore did God anoint thee a hierarch, O Leo, while the people cry aloud: "Bless the Lord, all you works of the Lord!"[^415]

*Troparion:*
Having pleased the eternal King, O all-blessed one, thou didst stand before the earthly emperor, carrying burning coals, O Leo; and thou didst astound his mind, O father, by most glorious wonderworking.[^416]

*Troparion:*
Having been planted as a fruitful olive tree in the house of the Lord, O father, thou bringest forth fruit after thy repose from thy divine body, the divine oil, ever driving away every disease from the faithful[^417] unto those who have recourse to thee with love, O wise one.

*Theotokion:*
He Who accomplishes all things by His will, O all-immaculate and blessed one, having willed, dwelt in thy womb and appeared in the flesh; and He deified me, who was before corrupted by the evil counsel of the deceiver.[^418]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every hearing was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from a virgin's womb; therefore, O most pure Mother of God, we the faithful magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
That we may bless thee with divine voices, who hast lived venerable, and ascended unto the heavens, and rejoicest: from passions, afflictions, and tribulations save us by thy prayers, O hierarch and most wonderful shepherd.[^419]

*Troparion:*
Having been a branch of the true Vine, O father, thou didst put forth clusters of virtues, dripping the sweetness of miracles; drinking of which with faith, those who ever praise thee, O all-wise Leo, receive health and joy.[^420]

*Troparion:*
Thy body was a temple of the Holy and honorable Spirit, O father, and now it lies within the divine temple which thou didst build, O Leo, unto the glory and honor of the Martyr Lucy, pouring forth rivers of miracles for us who piously venerate thee.[^421]

*Troparion:*
Like a radiant sun, ever illuminating the ends of the earth with the torches of virtues, thou hast now departed unto the unwaning Light; therefore, O exceedingly wealthy Leo, deliver from the gloom of all-grievous evils those who celebrate thy light-bearing memory.

*Theotokion:*
I was terrified of Thy dread second coming, O only King, having sinned immeasurably against Thee, and not being established according to understanding; but as a good Son, turn me and save me, O Jesus, through the acceptable prayers of her who gave birth to Thee.

*On the 21st Day of the same Month: Our Venerable Father Timothy of Symbola; and our Father among the Saints Eustathius, Archbishop of Antioch.*[^422]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чудесѐ):*

*Sticheron:*
O divinely wise father Timothy, through much abstinence and frequent prayers thou didst utterly consume the stirrings of passions, and having received the grace of dispassion, thou wast revealed as a receptacle of the Divine Spirit; therefore thou ever drivest away evil spirits, while living and after death, O divinely blessed one.

*Sticheron:*
O divinely wise father Timothy, thou wast a true imitator of Abraham, healing everywhere those who had recourse unto thee; thou didst acquire Job's fortitude in sicknesses, possessing David's meekness; thou didst live an angel-like life upon the earth; thou hast attained the ultimate desire, praying for us.[^423]

*Sticheron:*
O divinely wise father Timothy, thou wast revealed as an image of chastity, a model of abstinence, a countenance of piety, and a fountain of compunction, an unshakable confirmation of Orthodoxy, an ever-flowing stream of healings, an unwaning sun, a child of divine grace, and the adornment of monastics, O most honorable one.

*Other Stichera of the Hierarch, the same Tone, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones" (Что́ вы нарече́мъ):*

*Sticheron:*
Having a heavenly life, thou wast truly appointed by God to perform sacred service; and thou didst offer up services unto Him, honored in mind; thou didst endure perils and afflictions, O blessed one, for the sake of the divine preaching and of steadfast truth, which thou didst establish by thy words, mystically rebuking the senseless.

*Sticheron:*
By the flood of thy sacred streams, thou didst manifestly stop up the blasphemies of Christ-hating Arius the impious, who could not comprehend that the Son is called equally honorable by nature with the Father, O all-sacred Eustathius; and thou didst enlighten the hearts and strengthen with truth those who celebrate thy memory with faith.[^424]

*Sticheron:*
Pasturing with the staff of thy words, O all-sacred shepherd, thou didst thereby preserve the rational flock of God unharmed from all wolves; therefore the Good Shepherd led thee into the heavenly sheepfold, where the chosen ancestral spirits of the righteous dwell, O divinely-bearing and all-blessed Eustathius.

*Glory... both now..., Theotokion, the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Unto whom hast thou become likened, O wretched soul, in no way rousing thyself unto repentance, and not dreading the fire that awaits the wicked? Arise, and call upon the only one who is swift to help, and cry aloud: O Virgin Mother, entreat Thy Son and our God, to deliver me from the snare of the deceiver.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
When the Ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb stretched out willingly upon the Tree of the Cross, she cried aloud maternally, grieving with weeping: My Son, what strange sight is this? Thou Who givest life unto all as Lord, how art Thou put to death, O Long-suffering One, granting resurrection unto earthly beings? I glorify Thy great condescension, O my God!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness, take not Thy mercy away from us, but by their prayers guide our life in peace.[^425]

*Canon of the Venerable One, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, all ye peoples, Who drowned Pharaoh in the Red Sea, singing a triumphal song, for He hath been glorified.[^426]

*Troparion:*
We ever praise thee, O blessed Timothy, as an intelligible star shining brightly upon the height of abstinence, O father, and illuminating the hearts of the faithful.

*Troparion:*
Having the eye of thy mind intently fixed upon God, thou didst shake off the sleep of despondency from thy soul, O father; and thou wast a temple of the Divine Spirit and a place of sanctification, O Timothy.

*Troparion:*
Fenced about with humility, O father, thou didst pass through the wiles of the evil one unharmed, and wast lifted up unto God; and thou ever enjoyest His glory, O blessed Timothy.

*Theotokion:*
We honor with hymns the Mother of God, the maiden who remains a virgin, who ineffably conceived without seed in her womb God the Creator of all things, and truly gave birth to Him in the flesh.

*Another Canon of the Hierarch, Tone 7.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto God Who aided Moses in Egypt to lead forth Israel, unto Him alone let us sing, let us sing, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Let us praise with hymns the crown of the fathers, the great Eustathius, as one vested with the grace of the Word.

*Troparion:*
Revealed as a champion of the faith, thou didst preserve the Church of Christ from assaults through battling against heresy, O venerable one.

*Troparion:*
Having become the mouth of the Word, thou didst cast forth a gulf of teachings, O venerable one, wherein thou didst drown the impiety of Arius.[^427]

*Theotokion:*
Having beyond word given birth in the flesh unto the Prince of life by the Word, unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art my establishment, O Savior, my refuge and strength; establish my wavering heart in Thy fear, for there is none holy as Thou, our God.[^428]

*Troparion:*
Showing bounty unto all through compassion, thou wast another Abraham, receiving those who came from all sides, O Timothy, and thereby pleasing God of all.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst mortify thy members upon the earth, O father, as the Apostle says; thou wast shown forth as a partaker of the heavenly life, wherein ever remember those who honor thee.[^429]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast an unwaning sun from the orient of thy deeds, O father, ever illuminating the ends of the earth with the rays of thy struggles, and destroying the demonic gloom.

*Theotokion:*
O holy Virgin, birthgiver of the King of kings, break asunder the bonds of my transgressions, and guide me unto the paths of repentance by thy prayers.

*Another Heirmos:*
My heart is established in the Lord, my horn is exalted in my God; my mouth is enlarged over mine enemies, I have rejoiced in Thy salvation.[^430]

*Troparion:*
Having drawn the weapon of truth, thou wast revealed as a leader of piety, piercing those who opposed the faith, O Eustathius.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst laboriously cultivate the Church, O blessed one; with the sickle of thy teachings thou didst thereby cut down all tares from the root.[^431]

*Troparion:*
Presiding with glory over the true assembly, thou didst truly strike down the leader of falsehood, Arius, O divinely blessed one, with the sling-stones of thy words.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast born ineffably, and didst appear as Thou wast well-pleased, O our Savior; and Thou didst renew the world, O Lord.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having been instructed in the Word Who for our sake was born of the Virgin without corruption, thou didst proclaim His divinity unto the godless; and thou didst bring many unto God by faith through many manifestations of sacred wonders; therefore, O God-bearer, as we celebrate thy holy memory, we enlighten our souls by thy grace and cry out unto thee with one accord: O God-bearer Timothy, entreat Christ God, that He may grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory..., Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
Thou didst proclaim the Word to be consubstantial with the Father and the Spirit, O father Eustathius; and with the steadfast sling-stones of thy teachings thou didst crush senseless Arius, who blasphemed against orthodox doctrine; therefore thou didst rejoice to be numbered among the choirs of the divinely inspired preacher-fathers, with whom do thou remember us.[^432]

*Both now..., Theotokion:*
As an uncultivated vine, O Virgin, thou didst bud forth the most beautiful cluster of grapes, pouring forth for us the wine of salvation, making glad the souls and bodies of all; therefore, ever blessing thee as the cause of good things, with the Angel we cry unto thee: Rejoice, full of grace![^433]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The unwedded pure one and Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee dead, hanging upon the Cross, wept maternally, saying: What has the lawless and ungrateful Jewish assembly repaid Thee, which enjoyed Thy many and great gifts, O my Son? I praise Thy divine condescension![^434]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, the Prophet divinely foresaw Thee about to become incarnate from the only Mother of God, and with fear he glorified Thy power.[^435]

*Troparion:*
Ever strengthening thyself by prayers, thou didst account the bows and arrows of the noetic enemies as the darts of children, O most glorious father Timothy.[^436]

*Troparion:*
Venerating with love the icon of Christ's dispensation toward us, O divinely wise one, thou didst endure the trial of torments from those who rejected it, O Timothy.[^437]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast like an unshakable pillar, ever praising God; in no way resting in sleep, thou didst remain a partaker of heavenly beauties, O Timothy.

*Theotokion:*
As an animate heaven upon earth, O pure Mother, thou didst give birth unto the Child Who was incorruptibly begotten of the Father before all ages, the restoration of Christians.

*Another Heirmos:*
Thy virtue hath covered the heavens, O Lord, and Thou hast filled all things with Thy praise; glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^438]

*Troparion:*
Gathering the trophies of the faith, with the arrows of words thou didst divinely and wisely cast down impious Arius, O father.

*Troparion:*
The brightness of thy words, going forth like lightning, consumes the evil glory of Arius, O all-wise Eustathius.

*Troparion:*
By thy words thou didst set a hedge around the Church, as for a vineyard of the Lord, O venerable one.[^439]

*Theotokion:*
In the flesh, O pure Virgin, thou didst give birth unto the Word Who was without beginning before thee, and Who in thee became man beyond word from thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Dispel the darkness of my soul, O Christ God, Giver of light, having driven away the primordial darkness of the abyss; and grant me the light of Thy commandments, O Word, that rising early I may glorify Thee.[^440]

*Troparion:*
Standing before the Trinity with a purified mind, O blessed one, and illumined with the rays from thence, O father, thou ever enlightenest us who are in darkness and suffering from spiritual passions.

*Troparion:*
Having adorned thy flesh with abstinence and thy soul with dispassion, O wise one, thou wast revealed as a pleasing servant of the King of all, and from Him thou didst manifestly receive the gifts of miracles, O Timothy.

*Troparion:*
The barren woman, having approached and asked not with a deceitful mind but with a simple heart, was blessed through faithful prayer by thy intercessions from barrenness, singing of Christ our God.[^441]

*Theotokion:*
He Who dwells in the highest, having dwelt in thy womb, made thee wider than the heavens, O Mary, Mother of God; as thou art good, cease not to pray unto Him for all of us, O Bride!

*Another Heirmos:*
My spirit seeks Thee early in the morning, O God, for Thou art light, and Thy commandments are healings for Thy servants, O Lover of mankind![^442]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst dispel the gloom of heresies, and didst make the dawn of thy teachings to shine forth unto all ends of the earth, O blessed Eustathius.

*Troparion:*
*Twice:*
Fenced about with the weapons of thy teachings, O Eustathius, we conquer the heresies that assault the divine faith.

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin, cease not to pray unto God Who was born of thee in the flesh, in the flesh, that thy servants may be delivered from every tribulation.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee, for unto Thee have I cried; and hear me, O God of my salvation![^443]

*Troparion:*
Well guarding the dignity of thy virginity throughout all thy life, thou didst never look upon the face of a woman, utterly repelling the harm arising therefrom.[^444]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst hasten unto the noetic haven, not being wetted by the waves of pleasures, O all-blessed Timothy; but by the life-bearing winds of the Spirit thou didst sail well across, O all-glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst withdraw from human society, dwelling in the mountains, ever abiding in the wilderness, O wise one, until thou didst enter into the divine heavenly city, O blessed Timothy.[^445]

*Theotokion:*
He Who invisibly sits upon the cherubic shoulders dwelt circumscribably in thy womb, in the flesh and not in the Godhead, and came forth from thee, saving me, O all-immaculate one.

*Another Heirmos:*
Jonah cried out from the belly of hades: Lead up my life from corruption; and we cry unto Thee: O almighty Savior, have mercy on us![^446]

*Troparion:*
By the wind of thy divinely inspired words thou didst calm the storm of the heretics, directing the Church into the haven of piety, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
*Twice:*
A blameless son of virtue, O venerable one, thou wast revealed as glorious in word and life, the adornment of the Church of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Him Whom thou didst ineffably bear, our God, O Mother of God, cease not to entreat, that those who hymn thee may be delivered from perils, O pure Ever-virgin!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Having shone forth from the east like a most radiant star, thou didst illuminate in the hearts of the faithful the virtues of thy miracles, O wonderworker and divinely blessed Timothy.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who didst save the Children from the fire through an Angel, and turned the roaring furnace into dew: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^447]

*Troparion:*
Having quenched the flame of passions with the dew of tears, thou pourest forth the grace of miracles, whereby thou cleansest the defilements of passions, O Timothy.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast truly attained divine blessedness; thou didst inherit the land of the meek, O venerable one, and thou enjoyest the joy of the chosen.[^448]

*Troparion:*
As one who hast found boldness, O blessed one, entreat remission of sins for those who celebrate with love the sacred memory of thy departure.

*Theotokion:*
Having all been delivered from the primal curse by thy most holy childbearing, O only blessed one, we call thee blessed, O undefiled Mother of God.

*Another Heirmos:*
In the burning furnace of the Chaldeans, the Abrahamite Children danced, saying with the Angel: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^449]

*Troparion:*
Having wisely conquered the warfare of the heretics with the weapon of the Spirit, thou didst receive incorruptible rewards, being crowned by Christ.

*Troparion:*
Thy mouth was shown forth as a two-edged sword against the impious, O father, covered under the hand of the Word, as having cut down their warfare.[^450]

*Troparion:*
With the winnowing-fan of doctrine thou didst scatter the deceit of Arius like chaff, O father, gathering the wheat of the faith into the living granary.[^451]

*Theotokion:*
Let us all honor the Mother of God, for she gave birth unto the Savior, crying aloud in faith: Rejoice, handmaiden and Mother of Christ our God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who coverest Thy chambers with waters, Who settest the sand as a boundary for the sea, and upholdest all things: the sun sings of Thee, the moon glorifies Thee, all creation brings a song unto Thee, as the Creator of all unto ages![^452]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a slayer of passions through much abstinence and the intense labors of vigilance, ever meditating in thy heart upon the ascents of virtues, O venerable one, and passing from glory unto the glory of unending life and incorruption.[^453]

*Troparion:*
As a sacrifice, O venerable one, thou didst offer up thy pure prayers unto the most pure God from thy soul; from Him thou didst receive the divine operation of miracles, to drive away demons and heal the infirmities of men.

*Troparion:*
Guileless in speech and free from malice, merciful and meek, and adorned with righteousness wast thou, O father, venerable, blameless, having departed from all unrighteousness, a pillar of monastics; therefore we the faithful honor thee unto ages.

*Theotokion:*
The Word of the Father chose thee alone as the beauty of Jacob, and dwelt in thy womb as He was well-pleased; He Who is more beautiful than all the sons of men came forth, O all-pure one, cleansing our darkened nature.[^454]

*Another Heirmos:*
The only King of glory without beginning, Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and the angelic ranks tremble: sing, ye priests; people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
With the rod of thy wisdom having divided the divine deep of the Scriptures, O hierarch of Christ, thou didst lead the new Israel across unto the land of Orthodoxy, and didst drown the Arians like the Egyptians.[^455]

*Troparion:*
Having come down from heavenly vision upon the Chalanaean tower of Arius, thou didst confound the tongues of the heretics, O wise Eustathius, preaching the single-voiced faith unto all ages.[^456]

*Troparion:*
Through thy true confessions thou didst end thy life in the West; yet thou didst shine forth again in the East unto thy flock, O wise one, sending forth the radiance of piety unto all ages.[^457]

*Theotokion:*
Praise Him Who created Adam according to His image, and came forth from his lineage, and saved the human race, ye priests, and people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Blessed is the Lord God of Israel, Who hath raised up a horn of salvation for us in the house of His servant David; wherein the Dayspring from on high hath visited us, and hath guided us into the way of peace.[^458]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast revealed, O wise one, as a temple of sanctification, a fountain of compunction, a stream of compassion, an immeasurable abyss of love, a true pleasing servant of the Lord, a heavenly citizen, a fellow-kinsman of the angels, and a gushing stream of miracles.

*Troparion:*
Knowing, O father, that mercy boasts against judgment, thou didst not despise the stranger; but as one good unto all, thou didst open the bowels of compassion, O venerable one, having been a father of orphans, an intercessor for widows, clothing for the naked, and food for the hungry.[^459]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst depart from earth unto heaven, where thou hadst thy citizenship while yet walking in the flesh, O venerable one, entreating that cleansing of transgressions may be granted unto us who faithfully honor thy holy memory.[^460]

*Troparion:*
Thy precious shrine ever pours forth for us a stream of healing, O father, drying up all the outpourings of passions, and watering every soul unto divine fruitfulness, O sacred Timothy, pleasing servant of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Following thy words, all generations call thee blessed, and we ever bless thee; for thou, O most holy and pure one, didst truly give birth unto the blessed God, Who truly makes blessed all those who serve Him.[^461]

*Another Heirmos:*
Her who is a Mother beyond nature, and according to nature a Virgin, the only one among women blessed among women, we the faithful magnify with hymns as Mother of God.[^462]

*Troparion:*
Having skillfully pressed out the cluster of grapes in the Scriptures, O father, thou didst give the faithful to drink of the wine of Christ's truth, which gladdens the heart.[^463]

*Troparion:*
Bearing thy theological tongue as a two-edged weapon, thou didst cut down the wicked wrath of Arius, who poured out innocent and spiritual blood, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
As we spiritually celebrate the renowned holy day of thy holy memory, we pray thee: Fervently entreat, O hierarch, that we may be saved!

*Theotokion:*
Her who conceived in her womb the uncircumscribable Word, and who revealed unto us the gate of divine life, the truly most pure Mother of God, we magnify.

*On the 22nd Day of the same Month: The Finding of the Relics of the Holy Martyrs in Eugenius.*[^464]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*

*Sticheron:*
Through manifold kinds of tortures having put off the garments of mortality, O glorious martyrs, ye have been clothed in the divine garment of incorruption; and now ye dwell in the heavens, ever standing before the divine throne, radiant and divinely blessed ones; therefore in faith we celebrate your all-radiant memory and sacredly kiss the shrine of your relics.

*Sticheron:*
In the weakness of men, through divine power healing is granted unto those who draw near: the small bodily dust of the passion-bearers pours forth a fountain of miracles by grace; let us draw near, O ye people, and draw health of soul and health of body, crying aloud with a thankful voice and saying: O Savior of the world, for Whose sake the honorable passion-bearers suffered most patiently, by their prayers deliver us from every harm!

*Sticheron:*
Those who were hidden for many years, the fair-victorious martyrs, have now appeared like a precious treasure, enriching the imperial city of all cities, borne by the hands of a wise hierarch and reverently brought into the divine church, and distributed unto those who ask for every kind of health, unto benefit, unto enlightenment, and unto the protection of us[^465] receiving the petitions of those who draw near, as faithful pleased-ones of God.[^466]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*In the same Tone, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́ко до́бля):*
From every assault of the enemy save those who piously worship thy birthgiving, O all-immaculate Mother of God, for thou art the protectress of our whole race; for her help have we all now acquired, as our refuge and confirmation, and our mediatress before Christ our good God; pray thou unto Him, we pray thee, to grant peace unto the world and remission of sins unto those who flee beneath thy protection.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Weep Not for Me, O Mother" (Не рыда́й мене́ ма́ти):*
Weep not for Me, O Mother, beholding hanging upon the Tree thy Son and God, Who without restraint hung the earth upon the waters and created all creation; for I shall arise and be glorified, and I shall shatter the realms of Hades with power, and shall destroy his might, and shall deliver the bound from his malice, as I am tender-hearted, and unto My Father I shall lead them, as the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors, and shattered the powerless audacity of the demons. Through their prayers, save our souls.

*Canon, Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The chariot-driver Pharaoh did the wonderworking rod of Moses once drown, striking crosswise and dividing the sea; but Israel, the fleeing wayfarer, it saved, as he sang a song unto God.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with martyr’s gifts, and standing in the great light, radiant with love, O martyrs,[^467] disperse the clouds of our souls and the winter of passions by the grace of our almighty God.

*Troparion:*
Having dedicated themselves unto the all-seeing God, and having bound their souls by His love, the choir of martyrs loosed the bond of wickedness, and grant remission of sins unto those who bless them in faith.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with divine fire, O passion-bearing martyrs, and having set your souls ablaze with mental power, you became as burning coals, consuming the substance of polytheism and enlightening the ends of the earth with the radiance of piety.

*Theotokion:*
Deliver me from enemies visible and invisible, O Virgin Mother, who hast given birth unto God the Word, visible though invisible in essence; and drive away the tempest of my passions by thy tranquility, O only restoration of mankind.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Supreme Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, firm foundation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with divine love, O martyrs, you extinguished the fire of godlessness with the streams of your blood; and shining radiantly like stars, you illumined the whole world under the sun.

*Troparion:*
The radiant relics of the martyrs shine forth, the light of healings unto those who hasten unto them with faith; for they drew grace from the Savior's fountains, having emulated His voluntary passion.

*Troparion:*
Pillars of the faith, the slaughtered of Christ, though hidden for a long time in the waters, have now appeared; and by hierarchal hands they were piously and venerably translated.[^468]

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O only one who hast given birth unto the Lord of all; rejoice, who hast mediated joy unto men; rejoice, overshadowed and uncut mountain, firm foundation of the faithful; rejoice, all-immaculate one.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Яви́лся еси́ дне́сь):*
Having shone forth from the earth like luminaries of righteousness, you drove away all the gloom of impiety; and you enlighten the faithful, O divine martyrs, champions of the Trinity.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Спа́се):*
Having plainly cast down the deception of the enemy, and having been hidden enviously for many years, you have appeared unto us, O all-ever-memorable martyrs, healing the passions of our souls and curing bodily diseases, unto the glory of our God.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Direct my wretched soul, O pure one, and have compassion upon it from the multitude of transgressions, having slipped into the depth of perdition, O all-immaculate one; and at the fearful hour of death, do thou snatch me away from the accusing demons and every torment.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Christ, hanging dead upon the Cross, the all-immaculate Mother cried out: O My Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thine immaculate hands, O Compassionate One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who without leaving the bosom of the Father hast visited our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Thy slaughter and voluntary death, O Giver of life, did the choir of passion-bearers desire, and having endured multiform afflictions, they passed over into painless and divine gladness, singing in thanksgiving: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Protected by the breastplates of the Cross, and strengthened by divine love, the divine martyrs cast down the weapons of the enemies and destroyed their legions through faith, singing fervently: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the beauties of virtues, and adorned with the scarlet mantle dyed by your blood, O martyrs, bearing the victory of the Cross as a scepter, you were accounted worthy truly to reign with Christ,[^469] having received the blessed hope.

*Theotokion:*
O blessed, all-immaculate Mother of God, divine adornment of the passion-bearers, who hast renewed our nature which was cast out by the counsel of the serpent: save me from every worldly stumbling-block, and grant that I may attain salvation.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray.

*Troparion:*
Having preserved the bones of Thy saints hidden for a sufficient time, O Savior, Thou hast now revealed them as Thou wast well pleased, O Word, unto Thy people for cleansing and salvation, and for the shame of the enemies that blaspheme Thee.

*Troparion:*
You destroyed the frost of deception by the warmth of faith, O glorious ones, having passed through the intricate snares of many torments; and winged with love toward the city on high, you have reposed, filled with glory.

*Troparion:*
By the endurance of stripes, having cast down all the haughtiness of the enemy, O steadfast champions, being torn and hanged, and approaching material fire, you consumed all the substance of godlessness by grace.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast higher than the hosts on high, O woman full of the grace of God, having conceived the Word Who made all things by a word, and having given birth unto Him Who was begotten of the Father before the ages, incorruptibly, O all-pure one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparion:*
Blossoming like lilies in spiritual valleys, you fill all with divine fragrance, O all-praised ones, and drive away every foul stench from souls, O wonderfully renowned passion-bearers.

*Troparion:*
Being cut into pieces and multiformly tormented, by the sword of true endurance your noble passion-bearers, O Word, cut down the enemies that know Thee not, singing unto Thee.

*Troparion:*
The wounds of the martyrs heal the wounds of souls; for having sorely wounded the enemy thereby, by the divine Spirit they became gratuitous healing unto those who fervently ask.

*Theotokion:*
From thee, God was manifested unto mortals by taking flesh; being rich, O all-immaculate one, He took upon Himself my poverty from thee, granting me the entrances to immortality.

*Kontakion, Tone 6, Podoben: "As first-fruits" (Я́ко нача́тки):*
The steadfast pillars of faith, the fair-victorious martyrs, having endured diverse torments for Christ, were hidden for many years; and now you have appeared like a precious treasure, and being venerably borne into the church of God by the hands of the hierarch of God,[^470] you distribute what is profitable unto those who ask; therefore pray for our souls.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire was ashamed before the condescension of God in Babylon of old; therefore the youths in the furnace, dancing with rejoicing foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Known of old unto your Creator by your martyrdom, and hidden from knowledge, you have now been made known unto us who sing:[^471] Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Draw, O men, from the fountain pouring forth saving water, from the immaterial streams, today for the sake of the relics of the martyrs, and sing unto Him Who glorifies the saints: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having become dwellings of light by reason of your many torments, though concealed for many years in dark hiding, like luminaries from unwaning realms Thy passion-bearers have shone forth, O Christ, enlightening us.

*Theotokion:*
The prophets foretold the depth of the mystery of thy birthgiving, for from thy pure blood, O Maiden, thou didst bear God in thy womb, and in two natures didst bring Him forth for our salvation and deliverance.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Being as branches of the immaterial vine, you put forth for us clusters of divine knowledge, and poured forth wine of immortality manifestly unto all, warding off the harm of spiritual drunkenness, O martyrs, crying: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
With streams of blood, the passion-bearers dried up the rivers of idol madness, and by the glory of Christ extinguished the blaze of the godless decree; and every heart that cries out in faith they richly watered: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The elect among hierarchs, moved by holy zeal, accomplished your sacred translation, O all-blessed ones,[^472] and honors it yearly, singing with the whole sacred assembly: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Sanctified by the Spirit, O all-immaculate Mother of God, thou didst give birth unto the Holy One Who rests in the saints, the only God and Benefactor, Who illumines all that cry out in faith: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was astonished at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee as the Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Urging one another on toward life, and bidding yourselves to be bold to endure wounds, the passion-bearers cried: Behold, now is the acceptable time; let us stand and conquer the enemy! For Christ, the Chief of contestants, Who was pleased to suffer for our sake, holds forth the crowns.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, O Word of God, and didst draw unto Thyself flocks of martyrs, who portrayed Thy passion and slaughter which Thou didst voluntarily accept, O Master, out of compassion for our sake. Therefore, having loved Thee, O noble passion-bearers, they are slaughtered like sheep.

Having been gladly accounted worthy to reign always with Christ in the heavens, O all-praised martyrs, entreat for the King of victors and Chief Shepherd salvation of soul: and for all who praise you today in faith.

*Theotokion:*
O cloud of the spiritual Sun, disperse the clouds of my soul; O gate of God, open unto me the gates of righteousness, I pray thee, O good one; and lead me into good entrances, O Virgin, delivering me from the manifold paths of the evil one.

*On the 23rd Day of the Same Month.*
*The Holy Hieromartyr Polycarp, Bishop of Smyrna.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чино́въ):*
Living upon the earth, thou didst unite thyself with the bodiless hosts, and with them thou dost unceasingly serve the Trinity, O divinely blessed father; therefore living ever on high as a heavenly man, thou dost mystically instruct those upon earth in divine mysteries.

A divinely planted branch of Christ's husbandry, offering spiritual clusters of divine grapes, hast thou appeared, O divinely blessed one; from which we faithfully draw honor and sweetness, and in our hearts offer thy teachings throughout all years.

Illumined with divine radiance, while thy body was consumed by material fire, thou didst pass over, O father, unto the immaterial dawn; and grant remission through thy prayers, enlightening the dark night of our passions, O wise one of our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*In the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Held fast by the abyss of transgressions, having fled unto the tranquil haven of thy most pure prayer, O Mother of God, I cry unto thee: Save me, stretching forth thy strong right hand unto thy servant, O all-immaculate one.

*Stavrotheotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чино́въ):*
When the immaculate Virgin beheld the Lamb lifted up upon the Cross weeping, she cried: O My sweet Child, what is this new and glorious sight? How art Thou, Who holdest all things in Thy hand, nailed in the flesh upon the Tree?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
As a sharer in their ways and a successor to the thrones of the Apostles, thou didst find action, O divinely inspired one, as an ascent to contemplation; therefore, rightly dividing the word of truth, thou didst also suffer for the faith even unto blood, O hieromartyr Polycarp: pray thou unto Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*At Matins, the Canon, the acrostic whereof is: "I praise the divine Polycarp in song." By Theophanes.*[^473]
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt, for He has been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Shining with the effusion of Christ's light, and filled with His illumination, O father, enlighten my darkened soul by thy prayers, O divinely wise one.

*Troparion:*
As an unblemished mirror, O father, thy pure heart flashed forth the illumination of the saving preaching, extending rays unto all.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast an inscribed pillar of the New Law, O father, having written upon thee, not with ink, but by the divine Spirit, the divine Gospel of grace.

*Theotokion:*
Knowing God to be incarnate of thee without fleshly desire, Who was before creation and before all ages, we truly confess thee to be the Mother of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
