Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who didst put sin to death by the Tree, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing unto Thee.

*Troparion:*
Like an olive tree bearing sacred fruit according to the Psalm, thou wast in the house of thy Lord, O divinely blessed Polycarp, bearing the joy of the preaching.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst fervently direct thy mind unto the commandments of the Savior, O divinely blessed one; and worthily wast thou an excellent shepherd of His Church.

*Troparion:*
As a whole offering thou didst bring thyself unto Christ as a living sacrifice through martyrdom, having previously suffered by the witness of conscience through asceticism, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast shaken off the dust of our mortality, having truly given birth unto Immortality; and thou hast woven for us garments of incorruption, O Virgin, by thy birthgiving.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of the Wisdom" (Прему́дрости):*
Having truly cultivated in thy soul the cluster of grace, O wise father, as wine thou didst pour forth the word of faith, gladdening the minds of all the faithful; and thou hast appeared as an impassable abyss of wonders.[^474] Therefore thou hast also appeared as the adornment of martyrs, finishing thy course in fire, and accounted worthy of the unwaning light, O Polycarp: entreat Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having slipped through evil thoughts, I have fallen into the depth of sin, and groaning I cry unto thee from the heart, O all-pure one: make marvelous thy rich mercy upon me, and the immeasurable depth of thy compassion, and the unnumbered wealth of thy compassions; and grant me repentance, and forgiveness of transgressions, that with love I may cry unto thee: pray thou unto Christ God to grant me remission of transgressions; for I, thy servant, have thee as my hope.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried out weeping, and lamenting bitterly, exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee; but my heart burns, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou dost endure out of the compassion of mercy, O long-suffering Lord! O Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fountain, have compassion and grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing in faith of Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I have glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
A garden rich in fruit of virtues, rich in good fruit unto the Master, hast thou appeared, O father Polycarp.

*Troparion:*
A perfect whole-burnt offering and a pure sacrifice wast thou offered unto the Savior of all, O father Polycarp.

*Troparion:*
We praise in hymn, O glorious one, the steadfast endurance of thy soul and the valiant courage of thy contests.

*Theotokion:*
Enlighten my darkened soul with thy light, O pure one, who hast given birth unto the unwaning Light.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments, for beside Thee we know no other God.

*Troparion:*
With thy mind enlightened by the spiritual dawn, O venerable one, figurative burning by fire wast thou visibly taught, O divinely wise one, through the fire of thine end.

...the stream of life hast thou received, as from a torrent of delight, O all-wise one; having been taught by the beloved disciple, who drew from the abyss of wisdom.[^475]

Riches and radiant glory, and length of days, and life incorruptible did Christ, the true Wisdom, grant unto thee, O father, as unto one that loved Him exceedingly.

*Theotokion:*
The Ancient of Days, like dew upon the fleece, O all-pure one, came down into thy sanctified womb; and the Lover of mankind appeared as the New Adam.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:*
Having died unto the whole world, O divinely speaking one, and abiding alive unto Christ alone, Thou didst pass over unto Him, receiving vital incorruption.

*Troparion:*
Walking with goodly form, O blessed one, and appearing as a wonder of light and peace, thou didst recognize Marcion as the first-born of the night and the defamer.[^476]

*Troparion:*
Instructed by thy words, O father, we overcome every foul heresy and the wicked synagogues that introduce two godless principles.[^477]

*Theotokion:*
Having conceived the pure Lamb Who takes away the sin of the world, O all-immaculate one, pray thou that forgiveness of transgressions be granted unto thy servants.

*Kontakion, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ликъ а́нгельскій):*
Offering spiritual fruits unto the Lord, O wise Polycarp, sacrifices of virtues; therefore also by God wast thou glorified as a worthy hierarch; therefore today, enlightened by thy words, we hymn thy praiseworthy memory, glorifying the Lord.

*Ikos:*
Having drawn the goodness of wisdom, therefrom, O father, didst thou give drink to the flock of divine knowledge; and thou didst proclaim the three-sunned deity of the all-holy and ineffable Godhead: clearly teaching the unbegottenness of the Father, the generation of the Son, and the procession of the Spirit, one Godhead, one glory; and the godlessness of idols didst thou cut down from the root, bringing the ripe fruits of the souls of the faithful unto Her, O glorious one; in Whom we were baptized, in Whom also we believe, glorifying the Lord.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
When the golden image was worshiped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the godless decree; and cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed they sang they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
With steadfast resolve thou didst pass through the burning flame, O glorious one, like the three youths of old who were bedewed by immaterial fire; and crying out in the midst of the fire, thou didst remain unconsumed: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Blessed art thou, and well is it with thee, who hast now taken up thine abode where the dwellings of the righteous are, where the hosts of martyrs and where the choirs of the wise Apostles cry out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Souls barren before, thou didst bring fruitful unto Christ, having leveled them by spiritual husbandry, and offering unto them well-headed grain as seed, and instructing those who sing with thee: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone, unto God Who made all things wisely, didst appear as Mother without knowing man, O all-pure Virgin Mother; therefore piously crying out unto thee, O all-immaculate one, we sing: Blessed is she who gave birth unto God in the flesh!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto God, Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children and transformed the flame into dew: hymn Him, ye works, as Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst celebrate the sacred mysteries, O glorious one, the mystical service of Christ; as a reasonable sheep thou didst bring thyself unto God as an acceptable and well-pleasing fruit-bearing sacrifice, O thrice-blessed Polycarp.

*Troparion:*
A youthful discourse and deepest courage didst thou display; by the power of the Cross thou didst raise thyself up unto divine contests, O divinely wise one, glory of the hieromartyrs of Christ, O venerable one.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the Cross, and worthily robed in hierarchal beauty, O father, thou didst enter into the Church of God by thy blood, to appear before Christ the Chief Shepherd.

*Troparion:*
Slaughtered for Christ like a notable ram,[^478] O sacredly revealed one, thou hast appeared as an emulator of His sufferings, and didst become a partaker of His glory and a co-heir of His Kingdom.

*Theotokion:*
A luminous pillar of fire and a radiant cloud leading forward unto the heavenly heritage, O pure Mother of God, be thou unto me, O all-immaculate Virgin, who am lost in the wilderness of life.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
God the Word from God the Word from God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who by eating fell grievously into corruption, ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin for our sake, we the faithful magnify with one mind in hymns.

*Troparion:*
Thy light-bearing memory having shone forth, O father, enlightens the souls of those who celebrate it devoutly, O marvelous one, and makes them partakers of the divine light-manifestation; and it we worthily bless with hymns, O divinely wise one.

*Troparion:*
Standing with the choirs on high of holy angels before the Master's throne: pray thou unto Him that we may attain unto the saving Passion, and His Resurrection brightly, as we accomplish diverse forms of virtues.

*Troparion:*
Believing thee, O father, to be a crown-bearer, and that thou didst receive the victory of honor from the divine right hand, and didst finish thy struggles, and art filled with light, O wise Polycarp, we fervently offer thee as an intercessor unto Christ.

*Theotokion:*
As the Birthgiver of God, O Virgin, and Mother of the Word, and as our mediatress of eternal and divine life, as having shone forth upon us the Sun of righteousness, we the faithful magnify thee with one mind in hymns.

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "Thou hast visited us" (Посѣти́лъ ны̀):*
Having become a disciple of the disciple who spoke of God,[^479] O Polycarp, thou didst bring much fruit of the faithful unto the Lord; and thou didst offer thyself as a whole fruit-bearing offering for thy flock; and now pray thou for us all.

*Theotokion:*
Send down Thy compassions, O God, upon Thy people and Thy flock, through the intercessions of Thy most pure Mother and of all Thy saints, and grant unto us all Thy great mercy.

*In the same month, on the 24th day. The First and Second Finding of the Honorable Head of the Forerunner.*[^480]

*If it be not Fast: At Vespers we sing: "Blessed is the man," the first antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 6. But if it be Fast, we recite the kathisma as usual. And the service of the Forerunner is sung with the Triodion. Tone 5.*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice" (Ра́дуйся):*
Rejoice, O sacred head, radiant and revered by angels, which of old was severed by the sword, and by sharp reproofs cut down the shame of uncleanness; fountain of wonders, watering the faithful; which also announced the saving coming of the Savior; which of old beheld the descent of the Spirit Who took up His abode in Him; which didst mediate between the old and the new grace: entreat Christ to grant great mercy unto our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Hidden of old in an earthen vessel, the head of the Forerunner was manifested,[^481] visibly coming forth from the bowels of the earth and pouring out streams of wonders; for it washed with waters the head of Him Who was baptized in the waters, and Who clothes men with divine forgiveness. Let us therefore truly bless this most glorious head, and joyfully celebrate in its finding, praying Christ to grant great mercy unto our souls.

*Sticheron:*
The head that proclaimed the Lamb of God manifested in the flesh, and made known unto all the saving paths of repentance by divine commandments; which formerly rebuked the lawless transgression of Herod; and for this was severed from the body, and for a time endured concealment, has shone forth unto us like a light-revealing sun, crying aloud: Repent, and with compunction of soul turn now unto Christ, Who grants great mercy unto the world.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
A treasury of divine gifts, thy God-preserved head, O Forerunner, has shone forth from the bowels of the earth; receiving it with faith and bowing down before it, O glorious one, we are enriched by thee, O Baptist of Christ, with most glorious wonders and the forgiveness of sins.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Who will not bless thee...*
*Entrance.*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the Readings:*
*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah. (Chapter 40):*
Thus says the Lord: Comfort, comfort My people, says God. Ye priests, speak unto the heart of Jerusalem; plead with her, for her humiliation is multiplied, for her sin is loosed, for she has received from the hand of the Lord double for her sins. The voice of one crying in the wilderness: Prepare the way of the Lord, make straight the paths for our God. Go up into the high mountain, thou that bringest good tidings to Zion; lift up thy voice with strength, thou that bringest good tidings to Jerusalem; lift it up, be not afraid. I am the Lord God, I will hear the poor of Israel, and will not forsake them. But I will open rivers upon the mountains, and fountains in the midst of the field. I will make the wilderness a meadow, and the thirsty land into fountains of waters. Let the heaven rejoice from above, and let the clouds drop down righteousness. Let the earth shine forth, and let mercy spring forth, and let righteousness shine forth together. Announce with a voice of joy, and let it be heard; announce it even unto the ends of the earth; say ye: The Lord has redeemed His servant Jacob. And if they thirst in the desert, He will bring forth water for them out of the rock. Rejoice, thou barren that barest not; break forth and cry, thou that didst not travail; for many more are the children of the desolate than of her that has a husband.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Malachi. (Chapter 3):*
Thus says the Lord Almighty: Behold, I send My angel before Thy face, who shall prepare Thy way before Thee; and the Lord Whom ye seek shall come into His temple. And who shall abide the day of His coming? For He comes like a refiner's fire, and like the herb of the washers; and He shall purify, melting and refining like silver and gold. And He shall come unto you in judgment, and will be a swift witness against the wicked, and against adulterers, and against those who swear falsely by My name, and against those who defraud the hireling of his wages, and who oppress the widows, and buffet[^482] the fatherless, and who turn aside the judgment of the stranger, and who fear Him not, says the Lord Almighty. For I am the Lord your God, and I change not. And ye, sons of Jacob, have turned aside from the law and have not kept it. Therefore return unto Me, and I will return unto you, says the Lord Almighty; and all nations shall call you blessed. And know that I am the Lord, Who looks between the righteous and between the lawless on that day wherein I provide for those who love Me. Know therefore and remember the law of Moses My servant, according as I commanded you in Horeb for all Israel, both commandments and ordinances. And behold, I send you Elijah the Tishbite before the great and illustrious day of the Lord come; and he shall turn the heart of the father to the son, and the heart of a man to his neighbor, lest I come and smite the earth suddenly, says the Lord Almighty, the Holy God of Israel.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon. (Chapters 4 and 5):*[^483]
Though the righteous happen to die, he shall be at rest. And the dying righteous shall condemn the living ungodly. For they shall see the end of the righteous, and shall not understand what was counseled concerning him; for the Lord shall cast down the ungodly, speechless on their faces; and He shall shake them from the foundations, and they shall be utterly destroyed in sorrow, and their memory shall perish. For when they reckon up their sins, they shall come in terror, and their own iniquities shall convict them to their face. Then shall the righteous stand in great boldness before the face of such as afflicted him and made no account of his labors. When they see it, they shall be troubled with terrible fear, and shall be amazed at the strangeness of his salvation. For repenting and groaning for anguish of spirit, they shall say within themselves: This was he whom we once had in derision, and a proverb of reproach, fools that we were! We accounted his life madness, and his end without honor. How is he numbered among the children of God, and his lot is among the saints? Therefore have we erred from the way of truth, and the light of righteousness has not shone unto us, and the sun rose not upon us. We filled ourselves with the paths of lawlessness and destruction; yea, we have gone through deserts where there was no path, but as for the way of the Lord, we have not known it.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Come, ye faithful, let us today honor the honorable head of the Baptist severed by the sword, in its finding, meeting it with hymns, honoring out of love that which pours out upon us the grace of healings; which the lustful Herod severed of old, being entangled in frenzy with Herodias.

*Verse:* Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.

Like gold from ore, so from the earth shines forth the head of the Forerunner, shining in an earthen vessel, and speaking clearly, rebuking Herod's rebuking adultery and murder; as a ray enlightening our thoughts: kissing it, ye faithful, let us today honor it with hymns, as praying unto God for us.

*Verse:* The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, and shall be multiplied like a cedar in Lebanon.

*Sticheron:*
As a divine treasure hidden in a vessel in the earth of old, the head of the Baptist is today uncovered unto the ends of the earth: as wealth pouring forth fountains of healings, curing sicknesses, enlightening souls. Therefore receiving the grace of every comfort, let us devoutly praise it with hymns.

*Glory... Tone 2:*
As an all-venerable shrine[^484] of divine thoughts, thy head clearly foresaw the mystery of the ineffable Luminary; and as from a mother's womb, it has today shone forth from the earthly treasuries, O all-praised John, and has filled the whole universe with fragrance, pouring out the myrrh of sanctification, and mystically preaching the path of repentance, and praying unto the Savior of all for our souls.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having shone forth from the earth, the head of the Forerunner emits rays of incorruption, of healings unto the faithful; from on high it gathers the multitude of angels, and below it calls together the human race, to send up praise with one voice unto Christ God.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion, twice. Glory... Both now... Theotokion, according to the Tone of the troparion. After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
As a divine treasure hidden in the earth, Christ revealed thy head unto us, O prophet and Forerunner; having all assembled therefore at its finding, let us hymn the Savior with divinely voiced songs, Who saves our life through thy prayers. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
We hymn thee, O Bride of God, Mother of Christ God, glorifying thy incomprehensible childbirth, whereby we were delivered from the deceit of the devil and from every affliction, O Lady Birthgiver of God; and we faithfully cry out: Have mercy upon thy flock, O only all-hymned one!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast raised up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Coming forth like gold from the ore, thy sacred head most gloriously, from the hidden bowels of the earth, has enriched all the thoughts of the faithful with understanding divine understanding: crying out as was its custom: "Repent now!" Bowing down before it, O Forerunner, we loudly glorify Christ with faith. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Never shall we, thy servants, cease to speak of thy mercies, O Birthgiver of God, singing thankfully from our soul, O Lady, calling and saying: O most holy Virgin, anticipate and deliver us from visible and invisible enemies, and from every threatening; for thou art our protection.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "What was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́иннѡ):*
The Baptist's head, which was hidden like a pearl in the blessed earth, and adorned like a divinely radiant priest, shines forth, working wonders for the whole world; for like the dawn the Forerunner of the Sun has shone forth beautifully; and crying out again unto us with unwaning light: "Behold, the Lamb of God is taken away!"[^485] *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
We thank thee always, O Birthgiver of God, and we magnify thee, O pure one, and bow down before thee, hymning thy childbirth, O thou full of grace, crying out unceasingly: Save us, O all-merciful Virgin, as thou art good, and snatch thy servants from the dreadful trial of demons in the hour of examination, that we may not be put to shame.

*Gradual Hymns, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* There will I raise up a horn unto David, I have prepared a lamp for My anointed. *Verse:* Remember, O Lord, David, and all his meekness. *Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 31.*[^486]
*After Psalm 50, Idiomelon Sticheron, Tone 6:*
That which formerly on a platter rebuked the lawless uncleanness of Herod, and preached repentance unto all the faithful, the honorable head of the Baptist of Christ, having appeared from the earthly treasuries, lies before those who run to it with faith, and touch it with fear, and venerate it with spiritual love: granting remission of transgressions, by its petition it bestows things needful, and great mercy.

*Canon of the Birthgiver of God, with the Heirmos on 6, and of the Forerunner on 8. Tone 6.*
*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as if on dry land, and beholding the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, Israel cried out: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparion:*
Come, with a tongue of good praise, and clapping hands in the all-powerful Spirit, from unclean lips let us with hymns spiritual hymns, ye faithful, let us glorify the Forerunner, the voice of the Word!

*Troparion:*
The Church of Christ is quieted in peaceful order, and with immeasurable multitudes is nobly garlanded, crying out unto thee a yearly song, O Forerunner.

*Troparion:*
Having assembled together, ye faithful, let us worthily honor with hymns the God-bearing head of the Forerunner, granted unto us by God as an inalienable treasure.

*Theotokion:*
Having received in thy seedless womb thy Creator as He Himself willed, incarnate beyond understanding, O pure one, thou hast truly appeared as Sovereign Lady of creatures.

*Katavasia:*
*I shall open my mouth...*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy like Thee, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion:*
A barren womb brought thee forth divinely as a shoot, a glorious bridesman for the Church called from the Gentiles, betrothing her unto Christ the true God and Bridegroom.

*Troparion:*
The vile adulteress could not hold back thy divinely speaking voice even with the sword, O Forerunner John; for thou dost reveal unto us thy divine head from the earth.

*Troparion:*
Brightly celebrating, the city that yearned for thee[^487] is established, having found as a mystical treasure and an unfailing fountain of healings thy head, O Forerunner.

*Theotokion:*
The wonder of thy divine childbirth, O pure one, transcends every order of nature; for supernaturally didst thou conceive God in thy womb, and having given birth thou remainest ever-virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "What was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́иннѡ):*
Kept as a treasure in a storehouse, the blessed head of the Forerunner, the voice of the Word hiding in a water-jar,[^488] like wheat covered in the depth of the earth has blossomed forth, bearing the fruit of divine life. All we honor its finding, glorifying Christ Who granted it grace to pour out healings. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Taking into his mind what was commanded mystically, the bodiless one stood with haste in the abode of Joseph, saying unto the unwedded one: He Who bowed down the heavens by His descent is contained wholly and unchangeably in thee! And beholding Him in thy womb taking the form of a servant, I am amazed to cry unto thee: Rejoice, unwedded Bride!

...O unwedded Bride!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, my God and Lord, the venerable Church divinely sings, crying out with a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly wast thou the seal of the Old Covenant, O thou rich in God, and didst make known the end of the prophets; and as a divine adorner of the bridal chamber of the New Covenant hast thou appeared, O Forerunner.

*Troparion:*
With garments of camel's hair,[^489] as was thy custom, O Forerunner, thou didst endure to cover thy honorable and glorious head, rejoicing in it as in a royal purple robe.

*Troparion:*
By thy divine head, he that was poor in deification and wrapped in swaddling-bands shows forth by thy divine instructions, O John, an ungrudging wealth.

*Troparion:*
Pray thou that the city which ever honors thee, O Forerunner, may be made radiant by the ray of the divine Spirit, and may rejoice together with the bodiless hosts in thy divine memory.

*Theotokion:*
The tasting of the tree showed me to be dead; but the Tree of Life which appeared from thee, O all-pure one, has raised me up and made me an heir of Eden's delight.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, enlighten with love, I pray, the souls of those who rise early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them from the darkness of sin.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast not a reed, O Baptist, shaken always by the tempting winds of misfortune,[^490] but an unshakable pillar of the divine people; for thou didst appear in the power and spirit of Elijah.

*Troparion:*
He that rejects the Divinity and diminishes the glory of Christ Who was baptized by thee, and conceals thy divine grace, is justly driven away from the divine folds.[^491]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst open the doors of the ungrudging divine fountain unto those who sing unto thee with love, O glorious Forerunner, pouring forth the waters of divine grace and streams of healings which gladden all.

*Theotokion:*
Bearing the spiritual beauty of thy most fair soul, thou wast the Bride of God, sealed with virginity, O pure one, enlightening the world with the ray of purity.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, and fleeing to Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Bring up my life from corruption from corruption, O greatly Merciful One!

*Troparion:*
Christ the Truth proclaimed thee a prophet and greater than prophets, and the baptizer and forerunner of Life; for Him didst thou behold, Whom the Law and the prophets foretold.

*Troparion:*
The fountain of wonders, the treasury of grace, thy glorious head, O Baptist, prophet, and Forerunner, could not endure to be concealed; but having appeared, it pours forth fountains of healings.

*Troparion:*
As a singular boast and bright adornment and a saving shield, this Christ-loving and renowned city of thine offers thy honorable and divine head, O Forerunner.

*Theotokion:*
Moses, great among the prophets, prefigured thee prefiguratively as the Ark, the Table, the Lampstand, and the Jar, signifying the Incarnation of Him Who was born of thee, O Virgin Mother.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
O prophet of God and Forerunner of grace, having found thy head from the earth as a most sacred rose, we ever receive healings; for again, as before, thou preachest repentance in the world.

*Ikos:*
Having received witness from the Lord, O John, thou wast higher than all men; whence I tremble to offer thee songs of praise; yet compelled by love, I have dared to begin a hymn: therefore do thou vouchsafe to be a helper unto me,[^492] O all-praised one, that I may be able to crown thy holy head, O thrice-rich one! For thou preachest repentance in the world.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The angel made the furnace dew-bearing for the venerable children, but the burning command of God consumed the Chaldeans, persuading the tyrant to cry out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The Offspring of the Virgin declared thee greater than all born of women; for as one bodiless and equal to the angels didst thou live unto Him upon the earth, crying out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Him Who was contained in the virginal womb didst thou recognize from the barren womb, and using thy mother's voice, O Forerunner, leaping for joy thou didst cry out unto Him: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Taught in the wilderness by the All-Holy Spirit, thou didst appear like a light-bearing dawn, an angel of light, teaching us to cry out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Truly blessed among cities is this city now, O ever-memorable one, having acquired thee as a treasure O Forerunner of Christ: therefore celebrating it cries aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Through thee, O Virgin Mother, the radiant light has shone upon the whole universe, for thou didst give birth unto God, the Creator of all: entreat Him, O all-pure one, to send down great mercy upon us, the faithful.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable children, and the sacrifice of the righteous one Thou didst consume with water; for Thou, O Christ, dost do all things by Thy will alone: Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The Forerunner, the voice of one crying, was sent unto hearts that had grown gross,[^493] planting pious faith in the true God, the Son of God, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
"Prepare ye the path of the Lord," the Forerunner says even now; for having come for the sake of holiness, He will dwell with the Father and the Spirit in our hearts unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
By hearing the voice of his parents, by the eyes beholding the Holy Spirit, and in every way by touch, John the Forerunner, having laid his hand upon Thee, O Christ, becomes a God-bearer.

*Troparion:*
From the womb was he sanctified by the Holy Spirit, and appeared as a prophet while yet carried in the womb: and now as a delight has he appeared, O prophet, unto the city that holds thee in its bosom.[^494]

*Theotokion:*
The golden lampstand prefigured thee, who ineffably received the unapproachable Light, which by the knowledge of Him enlightens all things, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate was manifested unto men, Whom magnifying together with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly keeping the fulfillment of the ten commandments, O Forerunner, having been their strong guardian, thou wast fittingly honored by Christ with the decad of gifts; therefore, knowing thee as an initiate of the Spirit and a friend of the Word, we bless thee.

*Troparion:*
Christ's prophet and apostle, angel and forerunner, baptizer of the divine Incarnation, priest, and faithful martyr, preacher unto those in Hades wast thou, and a rule for virgins, and a shoot of the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
The unbelief of the soul is reproved by the wonder of the sanctified hand that was deadened:[^495] when it blossomed and when faith was again forgotten, upon the appearance of thy head, O blessed one, the swallowed-up hand is renewed.

*Troparion:*
Foretelling thy manifestation unto him that beheld it, the angelic ranks proclaimed without falsehood, speaking what was fitting for the divine Annunciation, which before thy birth the sacred Zechariah received.

*Troparion:*[^496]
Let us draw forth healings of passions, fleeing unto the glorious temple of the Forerunner; for into it come the angelic choirs, and today those of all the venerable ones, granting grace and healings together with John the Baptist.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "With the disciples let us assemble" (Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ):*
Radiant as light, thy honorable head has shone forth from the unsetting depths of the earth, O Forerunner John, lamp of the immaterial Light. As we celebrate its finding with love, we entreat thee: may we find release from fierce evils, and may we well accomplish the season of abstinence, through thy prayers, O all-praised one, baptizer of the Savior. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The God-wise assembly of the faithful is comforted, O all-honorable one, ministering and faithfully singing of thee as truly the Mother of God. Therefore, through thy prayers grant us to accomplish the course of the fast in a manner befitting God, and to worship the precious Cross and the saving passions of thy Son and God.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Opening the portals of abstinence, thy all-honorable head, O all-praised one, has set forth for all a most sweet nourishment of divine gifts; partaking whereof in faith, we sweeten the bitterness of the fast, and in faith we praise thee and cry aloud unto Christ God: O Jesus all-powerful, Savior of our souls! *(Twice)*

Pouring forth myrrh like ever-flowing waters, the Forerunner's head, placed today upon the mystical and divine table, breathes a spiritual fragrance, and drives away the bitterness of afflictions, and fills with gladness all who cry with love: O Jesus all-powerful, Savior of our souls!

Thy head, like a sharp sword, severed the heads of lawless enemies, and poured forth healings of divine gifts unto all who abide in righteousness. And on this account we faithfully praise thee, O John, friend and forerunner of the Creator, and we cry aloud unto Him diligently: O Jesus all-powerful, Savior of our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Having in faith received with the voice of praise the all-honorable head[^497] of Thy Baptist, O Lord, which today has appeared from the earth: we Thy sinful servants, bringing it forward as an intercession, pray Thee, O Lover of mankind, that for its sake we may obtain on the Day of Judgment cleansing[^498] from Thee, and great mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O God and Savior, Lover of the good, save Thy servants from every fierce circumstance, through the prayers of Thy Forerunner and of all the saints, and through the supplications of Thy Mother.

*Note: Great Doxology, and the dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the canon, Ode 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:* The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him. [LXX Ps. 63:11]
*Verse:* Hear, O God, my voice, when I make supplication unto Thee. [LXX Ps. 63:2]
*The Apostle to the Corinthians, pericope 176 [2 Cor. 4:6–15].*
*Alleluia, Tone 4:* The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree; like a cedar in Lebanon shall he be multiplied. [LXX Ps. 91:13]
*Verse:* Truth has sprung out of the earth,[^499] and righteousness has looked down from heaven. [LXX Ps. 84:12]
*The Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 40 [Mt. 11:2–15].*
*Communion Verse:* The righteous man shall be in everlasting remembrance. [LXX Ps. 111:6]

*It is fitting to know: If the Finding of the precious head of John the Forerunner occurs on Meatfare Sunday, or Cheesefare Sunday; for it may begin from Wednesday of Meatfare Week, and extend up to Tuesday of the Fourth Week of Great Lent.*
*At Small Vespers on Saturday: Resurrection stichera, 4; and the Theotokion of the Tone. At the Aposticha: one Resurrection sticheron, and the Aposticha of the Forerunner. After "Now lettest Thou depart", the Resurrection troparion; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, the Resurrection Theotokion; Small Litany; and the dismissal.*
*At Great Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried": Resurrection stichera, 3; and of the Triodion, 4; and of the Forerunner, 3; Glory, of the Triodion; Both now, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. If it be Cheesefare Sunday, we sing: Resurrection stichera, 3; and Anatolian, 1; and of the Triodion, 3; and of the Forerunner, 3; Glory, of the Triodion; Both now, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. At the Litiya: the sticheron of the Temple; and of the Triodion from the Praises, two stichera, if it be Meatfare Sunday: 1st sticheron, Tone 6: "I ponder on that day"; 2nd, the same Tone: "Woe is me, Adam!"*
*Note: What hour and day it then is.*
*If it be Cheesefare Sunday: We likewise say two stichera of the Triodion from the Praises at the Litiya: 1st sticheron, Tone 5: "Woe is me, Adam"* cried out with weeping; 2nd, the same Tone: "The arena of virtues is opened"; Glory, of the Forerunner, Tone 6: "A treasury of divine gifts"; Both now, of the Triodion, the Doxastichon written at the Litiya.*
*At the Aposticha: Resurrection stichera; Glory, of the Forerunner, Tone 2: "As a treasury of divine gifts"; Both now, of the Triodion. At the blessing of the loaves: Troparion, "O Mother of God and Virgin", thrice; and reading in the apostolic Epistles. At Matins, on "God is the Lord": Resurrection troparion, twice; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, the 1st Theotokion in the same Tone. After the Kathismata: Resurrection sessional hymns, with their Theotokia; and reading in the Commentary Gospel. Then: "Praise the name of the Lord", "Give thanks unto the Lord"; and we also say the third Psalm: "By the waters of Babylon", with Alleluia. Then we sing the Chosen Psalm of the Forerunner; and the troparia, "The assembly of angels"; and both sessional hymns, once each; Glory, sessional hymn after the Polyeleos; Both now, Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner. Hymns of Ascent, and Prokeimenon of the Tone; "Let every breath praise the Lord"; Resurrection Gospel; "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"; Resurrection canon with the heirmos on 4; and of the Triodion on 6; and of the Forerunner on 4; Katavasia of the Triodion.*
*If it be Cheesefare Sunday: Resurrection canon with the heirmos on 4; and of the Mother of God on 2; and of the Triodion on 4; and of the Forerunner on 4; Katavasia of the Triodion. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the Forerunner and Ikos, and his sessional hymn twice; Glory... Both now, of the Triodion; and reading of the Forerunner. After Ode 6: Kontakion of the Triodion, and Ikos, and the Synaxarion. At Ode 9: we sing "More honorable than the Cherubim". Resurrection Exapostilarion; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, of the Triodion.*
*At the Praises: Resurrection stichera, 4; and of the saint with the Doxastichon, 4, with his refrains. And after the stichera of the saint, Tone [refrain]: "Arise, O Lord my God"; and we sing the idiomelic sticheron of the Triodion, Tone 8: "Daniel the prophet"; Glory, Tone 1: "Let us purify ourselves beforehand, brethren"; Both now: "Most blessed art thou".*
*If it be Cheesefare Sunday: We likewise say the refrain: "Arise, O Lord my God"; and we sing the idiomelic sticheron of the Triodion, Tone 6: "Adam was driven from Paradise"; Glory, the same Tone: "The season has arrived"; Both now: "Most blessed art thou"; Great Doxology. After the Trisagion: Resurrection troparion; litanies, and the dismissal. And the customary exit into the narthex; Glory... Both now, the Gospel Sticheron; and the First Hour. At the Hours: Resurrection troparion; Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father", the kontakia of the Triodion and of the Forerunner we say alternately.*
*At the Liturgy: the Beatitudes of the Tone on 4; and of the Triodion, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Forerunner and of the Forerunner, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance: Resurrection troparion, and of the Forerunner; Glory, his kontakion; Both now, of the Triodion; Prokeimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Verse, first of the day, and of the Forerunner.*

*If the Finding of the precious head of John the Forerunner occurs on Meatfare Saturday:*
*The entire feast of the Forerunner is sung beforehand on Meatfare Friday without fail.*

*If the Finding of the precious head of John the Forerunner occurs on Monday or Tuesday of Cheesefare Week:*
*In the evening we sing "Blessed is the man", the 1st antiphon. But on the other days of Cheesefare Week the Kathisma of the day is said. At "Lord, I have cried", stichera of the Forerunner on 6; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, the Resurrection Theotokion in the same Tone; Prokeimenon of the day, and the Old Testament readings of the Forerunner. At the Aposticha, the idiomelic sticheron of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyr-sticheron; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, Theotokion. Troparion of the Forerunner; Glory... Both now, the Resurrection Theotokion; and litany.*
*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": troparion of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now, Theotokion; and the customary Kathismata. After the 1st Kathisma, sessional hymn of the Triodion, and reading of the day. After the 2nd Kathisma, both sessional hymns of the Forerunner, and reading of the Forerunner. Polyeleos: and sessional hymn of the Forerunner twice, and his Theotokion, and reading of the Forerunner. Hymns of Ascent, the 1st antiphon of Tone 4; Prokeimenon of the Forerunner; "Let every breath praise the Lord"; Gospel of the Forerunner; then Psalm 50; sticheron of the Forerunner. Canon of the Mother of God with the heirmos on 6, and of the Forerunner on 8; Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth". Where there is a triode, we sing the canon of the Forerunner with the heirmos on 6, and of the Triodion on 8. After Ode 3, sessional hymn of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now, Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner. After Ode 6, kontakion of the Forerunner, and Ikos, and the Prologue. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now, his Theotokion. At the Praises, stichera of the Forerunner on 4; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, Theotokion. At the Aposticha, idiomelic sticheron of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyr-sticheron; Glory, of the Forerunner: "That which formerly upon a platter"; Both now, Theotokion; and the rest as customary, and the dismissal. And at the First Hour, troparion of the Forerunner, and after the Trisagion his kontakion. Likewise at the other Hours. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Forerunner, Ode 3 and 6; Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse of the day and of the Forerunner.*

*If the Finding of the precious head of John the Forerunner occurs on Wednesday of Cheesefare Week: we sing the entire service of the Forerunner in the evening and in the morning on Tuesday of Cheesefare Week.*
*And if on Friday, we sing his service on Thursday.*
*If the Finding of the precious head occurs on Cheesefare Thursday:*
*And on Wednesday at the Hours, after "Pardon, remit" and after the prostrations, the bells are rung according to custom, and straightway we say: "Come, let us worship", thrice; and the customary Psalm; and litany, and stichera as prescribed. After the Entrance, Prokeimenon, the Old Testament readings of the day, and after these the Prokeimenon: "Let Israel hope in the Lord"; then three Old Testament readings of the saint; thereafter: "Vouchsafe,[^501] O Lord"; at the Aposticha, the idiomelic sticheron of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyr-sticheron; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, Theotokion. After "Now lettest Thou depart", Trisagion; after "Our Father", troparion of the Forerunner; Glory... Both now, the Resurrection Theotokion; and litany: "Have mercy on us, O God", and 3 great prostrations. Thereafter the prayer: "All-holy Trinity", and "Blessed be the name of the Lord", thrice, with prostrations, and Psalm 33; then "It is truly meet", and the dismissal; and we enter the refectory, and partake of cheese and eggs.*

*If the Finding of the precious head[^500] of John the Forerunner occurs on Cheesefare Saturday:*
*On Friday evening we sing "Blessed is the man", the 1st antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried", stichera of the Forerunner, prosomoia, 3; and of the Fathers, 3; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone; Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day, and the Old Testament reading; and after it the Prokeimenon, Tone 6: "Let Israel hope in the Lord"; then the Old Testament readings of the Forerunner; "Vouchsafe, O Lord, in this evening"; and the litany: "Let us complete our evening prayer"; at the Aposticha, the idiomelic sticheron of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyr-sticheron; then the refrain: "The righteous man shall be glad"; and we sing the idiomelic sticheron of the Forerunner, Tone 6: "That which formerly upon a platter"; Glory, of the Fathers, Tone 8: "The multitudes of monastics"; Both now, Theotokion in the same Tone. Troparion of the Forerunner; Glory, of the Fathers; Both now, Theotokion: "The mystery hidden from the ages". Thereafter the litany,[^502] "Have mercy on us, O God", and 3 great prostrations. Then "All-holy Trinity", and "Blessed be the name of the Lord", thrice; and Psalm 33: "I will bless the Lord"; and the dismissal; and at the refectory we partake of cheese and eggs. At Compline we sing the canon of the departed in the narthex from the Octoechos, of the Tone, or of the Mother of God. After "It is truly meet" and after the Trisagion, kontakion of the Forerunner; Glory... Both now, of the Triodion, of the Fathers.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": troparion of the Forerunner, twice; Glory, of the Fathers; Both now, the 1st Theotokion. After the Kathismata: sessional hymns of the Fathers, and their Theotokion, and readings of the Fathers. Polyeleos: and all the sessional hymns of the Forerunner, once each; Glory, sessional hymn after the Polyeleos; Both now, Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner. Hymns of Ascent, the 1st antiphon of Tone 4; Prokeimenon; "Let every breath praise the Lord"; and Gospel of the Forerunner; Psalm 50; sticheron of the Forerunner. Canon of the Forerunner with the heirmos on 6, and of the Fathers on 8; Katavasia: the heirmos of the Triodion. We also recite the Ninth biblical Ode from the Triodion, but in the Psalter we do not say that ode. After Ode 3: kontakion of the Forerunner, and Ikos, and his sessional hymn; Glory, of the Fathers; Both now, Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner. After Ode 6: kontakion of the Fathers, and Ikos, and the Synaxarion. After Ode 9: Exapostilarion of the Forerunner; Glory, of the Fathers; Both now, Theotokion. At the Praises: stichera of the Forerunner, 3; and of the Fathers, 3; Glory, of the Fathers; Both now, Theotokion; Great Doxology; troparion of the Forerunner; Glory, of the Fathers; Both now, Theotokion; and exit into the narthex, and the First Hour. At the Hours: troparion of the Forerunner; Glory, of the Fathers; Both now, the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father", the kontakia of the Forerunner and of the Fathers we say alternately. At the Liturgy: the Beatitudes of the Forerunner, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Fathers, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance: troparion of the Temple, and of the Forerunner, and of the Fathers; then the kontakion of the Temple, and of the Forerunner; Glory, of the Fathers; Both now, of the Temple of the Mother of God, or "Protection of Christians"; Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse: first of the Forerunner, and then of the Fathers. But the Epistle and Gospel of the day we read combined under one pericope.*

*If the Finding of the precious head of the Forerunner occurs on Monday of the First Week of Great Lent:*
*The entire service of the Forerunner is sung on Cheesefare Sunday.*

*If the Finding of the precious head of the Forerunner occurs on any day of the First Week of Great Lent:*
*His service is sung either on Cheesefare Sunday, or on Saturday of the First Week of Great Lent.*

*If the Finding of the precious head of the Forerunner occurs on Saturday of the First Week of Great Lent:*
*On Friday evening: the Kathisma of the day. At "Lord, I have cried", stichera on 10: and we sing the idiomelic sticheron of the day, twice; and stichera of the Forerunner on 4; and of Theodore on 4; Glory, of Theodore; Both now, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone; Entrance; Prokeimenon; and the Old Testament readings of the day, and three of the Forerunner, and after "Let my prayer be set forth", 3 great prostrations; and the rest of the Presanctified. After the Prayer behind the Ambo, we sing the canon of Saint Theodore over the kolyvo, as indicated in the Menaion in order; and the dismissal. Where there is no Presanctified: in the evening at "Lord, I have cried", stichera of the Forerunner, 3; and of Theodore, 3; Glory, of Theodore; Both now, the 1st Theotokion of the incoming Tone, without Entrance; Prokeimenon, and reading of the day, and of the Forerunner. At the Aposticha, the idiomelic sticheron of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyr-sticheron; then the refrain: "The righteous man shall be glad"; and we sing the sticheron of the Martyr, Tone 2: "Of divine gifts", written among the stichera; Glory, of the Forerunner, Tone 6: "A treasury of divine gifts"; Both now: "O Mother of God, thou art the vine"; after "Now lettest Thou depart", troparion of the Forerunner; Glory, of the Martyr; Both now, Theotokion: "All beyond comprehension"; litany, and 3 great prostrations; and we sing the canon of Saint Theodore over the kolyvo; and dismissal according to custom. At Compline we sing Great Compline, at which we sing the canon of the departed from the Menaion in order. Troparia after the 1st Trisagion we say of both saints, and after the final Trisagion the kontakion of the Forerunner. At Matins, on "God is the Lord": troparion of the Forerunner, twice; Glory, of Theodore; Both now, Theotokion: "All beyond comprehension". After the 1st Kathisma, both sessional hymns of the Forerunner; Glory... Both now, Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner. After the 2nd Kathisma, sessional hymn of Theodore; Glory... Both now, Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner, or of Theodore. Polyeleos: and sessional hymn of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now, Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner. Hymns of Ascent, the 1st antiphon of Tone 4; Prokeimenon; "Let every breath praise the Lord"; Gospel of the Forerunner; Psalm 50; sticheron of the Forerunner. Canon of the Forerunner with the heirmos on 6, and both canons of Theodore on 8; Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth". But the Seventh and Eighth Odes we say beforehand on Friday. After Ode 3, kontakion and Ikos, and sessional hymn of the Forerunner; Glory, of Theodore; Both now, Theotokion; and reading concerning Saint Theodore. After Ode 6, kontakion of Theodore, and Ikos; and we read the Prologue, and the Synaxarion. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion of the Forerunner; Glory, of Theodore; Both now, Theotokion. At the Praises: stichera of the Forerunner, prosomoia, 3; and of Theodore, prosomoia, 3; then the refrain: "The righteous man shall be glad"; and we sing the idiomelic sticheron of the Forerunner, Tone 6: "The all-honorable head"; Glory, of Theodore; Both now: "O Mother of God, thou art the vine"; Great Doxology; troparion of the Forerunner Glory, of Theodore; Both now, Theotokion; litanies, and dismissal. And the customary exit into the narthex; and the First Hour. At the Hours: troparion of the Forerunner; Glory, of Theodore; Both now, the Theotokion of the Hours; but the kontakia we say alternately. At the Liturgy: the Beatitudes of the Forerunner, Ode 3 on 4; and of Theodore, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance: troparion of the Forerunner, and of Theodore, and his kontakion; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, of the Temple of the Mother of God, or "Protection of Christians"; Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Verse: one of the Forerunner and of Theodore. But the Epistle and Gospel first of Saturday, and of Theodore according to pericope; then of the Forerunner.*

*If the Finding of the precious head of John the Forerunner occurs on the Second Sunday of Great Lent:*
*His service is sung likewise as indicated for the Third Sunday of Great Lent, except for the veneration of the Cross. Only at the Litiya, Glory, of the Forerunner, written at "Lord, I have cried"; and Both now, of the Triodion. At the Aposticha, Glory, of the Forerunner; and Both now, of the Triodion. At "God is the Lord", the Resurrection troparion, twice; Glory, of the Forerunner; and Both now: "Thy most pure image"; Katavasia of the Triodion.*

*If the Finding of the precious head of the Forerunner occurs on Monday of the Second, Third, or Fourth Week of Great Lent:*
*On Sunday evening we sing "Blessed is the man", the 1st antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried", stichera on 10: of the Triodion of the day on 4, repeating the 1st; and of the Forerunner on 6; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, the 1st Theotokion. Entrance; Great Prokeimenon of the Triodion; and three Old Testament readings of the Forerunner; then: "Vouchsafe, O Lord, in this evening"; "Let us complete our evening prayer"; at the Aposticha, the idiomelic sticheron of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyr-sticheron; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, Resurrection Theotokion according to the Tone. And after "Our Father", troparion of the Forerunner; Glory... Both now, the Resurrection Theotokion; litany: "Have mercy on us, O God", and 3 great prostrations; and the dismissal. The Midnight Office we sing according to custom, with the Kathisma and the customary troparia. After the reading, 3 great prostrations. At Matins, on "God is the Lord": troparion of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now, Theotokion. After the 1st and after the 2nd Kathisma, we say the sessional hymns of the Triodion; but litanies are not said; and reading in Ephraim. After the 3rd Kathisma the priest says the small litany; and both sessional hymns of the Forerunner; and reading of the Forerunner. Polyeleos: and sessional hymn of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now, Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner. Hymns of Ascent, the 1st antiphon of Tone 4; Prokeimenon, and Gospel of the Forerunner; then Psalm 50; sticheron of the Forerunner. Canon of the Forerunner with the heirmos on 8; Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth" Where there is a triode: we sing the canon of the Forerunner with the heirmos on 6, and the triode on 8; Katavasia of the Triodion. After Ode 3, sessional hymn of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now, Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner. After Ode 6, kontakion, and Ikos, and Prologue. Exapostilarion of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now, Theotokion. At the Praises, stichera of the Forerunner on 4; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, his Theotokion. Then the superior or the reader says: "Glory to Thee Who hast showed us the light"; thereafter the litany: "Let us complete our morning prayer"; then at the Aposticha, the idiomelic sticheron of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyr-sticheron;[^503] Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, Theotokion; thereafter: "It is good to give praise", we say once; after "Our Father", troparion of the Forerunner; Glory... Both now, the Resurrection Theotokion; litany: "Have mercy on us, O God", and 3 great prostrations, without dismissal; and the First Hour. At the Hours, we say the troparion of the Forerunner, and after the Trisagion his kontakion; and at the end, 3 great prostrations. And after the prostrations, we do not say the Trisagion, but we say: "O Christ, the true Light"; and the dismissal. But the Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours we sing with Kathismata, at their proper time, with reading. At the Sixth Hour the Old Testament reading of the day is read according to its order. But the prayers of Basil the Great after the Hours we say without fail, which are: "O Master God the Father", and "O God and Lord of hosts", and "O Master Lord Jesus Christ". Whenever we sing the Polyeleos, at the Hours we do not strike the great bell. After the Kathisma, Alleluia, thrice; then "Lord, have mercy", thrice; and the reader says: Glory, troparion of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion of the Hours; after "Our Father", kontakion of the Forerunner. After "More honorable than the Cherubim", 3 great prostrations. After the Ninth Hour we say the Beatitudes quickly without singing. After "Pardon, remit", kontakion of the Temple; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, of the Temple of the Mother of God. If there is no temple of the Mother of God: Both now, "Protection of Christians"; thereafter, "Lord, have mercy", 40; Glory... Both now, "More honorable than the Cherubim"; and 3 great prostrations; then the prayer: "All-holy Trinity"; and the customary dismissal. And we begin Vespers according to custom: the superior or reader says: "Come, let us worship", thrice; and we sing Vespers with prostrations according to the Lenten order. After the customary Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried", stichera on 10: the idiomelic sticheron of the day from the Triodion, twice, and the Martyr-sticheron, and prosomoia of the Triodion, 3, and of the Forerunner, 4; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, Theotokion according to the customary Tone. But if it be towards Wednesday or Friday, we say the Stavrotheotokion according to the Tone of the Tone of the Doxastichon. Entrance with the Gospel. "O Gladsome Light". Prokeimenon, and reading of the day; "Let my prayer be set forth", and 3 great prostrations; small litanies are not said; Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse of the Forerunner. Liturgy of the Presanctified according to custom. At the dismissal the priest commemorates first the celebrated saint, then the incoming one. But for the saint who happens in order, we sing his canon at Matins according to rule; and his stichera that were written at Vespers for "Lord, I have cried", we say at the Praises. But if on account of some great necessity, on the memory of great saints having a Polyeleos, it should happen that there is no Presanctified Liturgy: we sing the Hours as said above; after the Ninth Hour, and after the prayer: "O Master Lord Jesus Christ", we say Psalm 102: "Bless the Lord, O my soul, and all that is within me"; Glory: "Praise the Lord, O my soul"; Both now: "O Only-begotten Son"; and "In Thy Kingdom" quickly without singing. At the Beatitudes we say of the Forerunner, Ode 3 and 6; then the Epistle and Gospel of the Forerunner. Thereafter: "Remember us, O Lord"; after "Pardon, remit", and after "Our Father", kontakion of the Temple; Glory, of the Forerunner; Both now, of the Temple of the Mother of God. If there is no temple of the Mother of God: "Protection of Christians"; then "Lord, have mercy", 40, and 3 great prostrations. And at that time the paraecclesiarch strikes two bells; and after the three great prostrations, we say: "Come, let us worship"; and we sing Vespers with prostrations, and the customary Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried", stichera of the Triodion, prosomoia, 3; and of Saint Tarasius,[^504] 3; Glory... Both now, Theotokion. But if it be towards Wednesday or Friday, we say: Glory... Both now, Stavrotheotokion; then "O Gladsome Light"; Prokeimenon, and reading of the Triodion of the day; at the Aposticha of the day from the Triodion; after "Now lettest Thou depart", and after "Our Father", the troparion: "O Mother of God and Virgin, rejoice"; and the rest according to the Lenten order. After "More honorable than the Cherubim" and after the prostrations, we say the Trisagion; "Lord, have mercy", 12; "All-holy Trinity"; then "Blessed be the name of the Lord", and 3 prostrations; "I will bless the Lord"; "It is truly meet"; and dismissal: at the dismissal he commemorates the incoming saint. After the dismissal we enter the refectory, we eat two cooked dishes with oil, and drink wine. But on Wednesday and Friday we eat two cooked dishes without oil, and drink wine.*

*If the Finding of the precious head of the Forerunner occurs on one of the days of the fast we chant his service thus:*
*At the Hours, after "Pardon, remit", and after "Our Father", the Kontakia according to the typikon. Then, "Lord, have mercy" (40). Glory, and now, "More honorable than the Cherubim". And after the prostrations we say the Trisagion. "Lord, have mercy" (12), and "All-Holy Trinity". And immediately we begin Vespers, and the customary Kathisma; and little litanies are said after each antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 10: the Idiomelon of the day (2), and the Martyrion, and Prosomoia of the Triodion (3), and of the Forerunner (4). Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, "Who will not bless thee...". Entrance, Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day, and three readings of the Forerunner. Then we chant, "Let my prayer be set forth", and 3 prostrations; and in order, the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.*
*Where there is no Presanctified Liturgy: After "Pardon, remit", and after the great prostrations, the reader says: "Come, let us worship" (thrice), and 3 prostrations. Then the Psalm: "Bless the Lord, O my soul", and we chant Vespers without prostrations. After the customary Kathisma, Stichera on 6: of the Triodion (3), and of the Forerunner (3). Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion: "Who will not bless thee...". Entrance, and "O Gladsome Light". Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day, and of the Forerunner; and immediately, "Vouchsafe, O Lord". Then, "Let us complete our evening prayer". At the Aposticha, of the Triodion. Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion, according to the Tone. "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart". After the Trisagion, the Troparion of the Forerunner. Glory, and now: "The mystery hidden from the ages". Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God". Thereafter, three great prostrations, and the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity". And "I will bless the Lord", "It is truly meet", and the Dismissal.*
*Great Compline we chant without prostrations. After the 1st Trisagion, the Troparion of the Forerunner. Glory, and now, the Resurrectional Theotokion according to the Tone. After the second Trisagion, the customary Troparia: "Have mercy on us, O Lord". After "It is truly meet" and after the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Forerunner; but "O Lord of hosts, be with us" we do not chant. After "More honorable", three great prostrations. But the Trisagion we do not say, but the Small Dismissal.*
*Midnight Office we chant according to custom, with the Kathisma, as was prescribed on Monday of the second week of the Fast (see fol. 209v)[^505]. Likewise also Matins, and the entire service of the Forerunner.*

*If the Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner occurs on the second or third Saturday of the Fast:*
*On Friday evening, the Kathisma in course. At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 10: the Idiomelon of the Triodion (2), and the Martyria of the Oktoechos (4), and of the Forerunner (4). Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion: the 1st of the Tone. Entrance, Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day, and of the Forerunner (3). "Let my prayer be set forth" "Let my prayer be set forth"[^506], and the rest of the Presanctified.*
*If there is no Presanctified Liturgy: on Friday evening, at "Lord, I have cried", Stichera of the Forerunner on 6. Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion: the 1st of the Tone. Entrance, Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day, and of the saint. Then, "Vouchsafe, O Lord". At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion (2), and the Martyrion of the Oktoechos according to the Tone. Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion. After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart", the Troparion of the Forerunner. Glory, and now, the Resurrectional Theotokion of the current Tone. Litany, and three prostrations. Then the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity", "Blessed be the name of the Lord", and Psalm 33: "I will bless the Lord". "It is truly meet", and the Dismissal. But Great Compline we chant, at which we chant the Four-Ode Canon for Saturday. At Matins and at the Hours we chant of the Forerunner alone, as was prescribed above (see fol. 210r)[^507]. At the Liturgy, the service of the day, and of the Forerunner.*

*If the Finding of the Head of John the Forerunner occurs on the second Sunday of the Fast:*
*On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers, the Resurrectional Stichera, and of the Birthgiver of God according to custom.*
*At Great Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried", Resurrectional Stichera (3), and Anatolian (1), and of the Forerunner on 6. Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion: the 1st of the Tone. Entrance, and the readings of the Forerunner. At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the temple, and of the Forerunner, those from the Aposticha. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera, alphabetical. Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion. At the blessing of the loaves, "Birthgiver of God, Virgin" (thrice), and a reading from the Apostolic Epistles. At "God is the Lord", the Resurrectional Troparion (twice). Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion, according to the Tone of the Troparion. After the Kathismata, the Resurrectional Sessional Hymns, and their Theotokia; and a reading from the Gospel. After the Polyeleos, both Sessional Hymns of the Forerunner. Glory, the Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos; and now, the Theotokion. Graduals, and the Prokeimenon of the Tone; and the Resurrectional Gospel. Psalm 50. Glory, Sticheron, Tone 8: "Open unto me the doors of repentance"[^508], and the rest. The Resurrection Canon with its Heirmos on 4; and of the Triodion on 4; and of the Forerunner on 6. Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth". After Ode 3, the Kontakion of the Triodion. Then the Kontakion of the Forerunner and the Ikos, and his Sessional Hymn (twice). Glory, and now, the Theotokion; and a reading of the Forerunner. After Ode 6, the Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos. After Ode 9, the Resurrectional Exapostilarion. Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Resurrectional Theotokion. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera (4), and of the Forerunner of the Forerunner with the Doxastichon (4), with his refrains. Then the refrain: "Arise, O Lord my God", and we chant the Idiomelon Sticheron of the Triodion. Glory, the same; and now: "All-blessed art thou...". Great Doxology. Resurrectional Troparion. Litanies, and Dismissal. And the procession into the narthex: Glory, and now, the Evangelical Sticheron. And the First Hour. Resurrectional Troparion; Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father", the Kontakion of the Triodion; and the complete Dismissal. At the other Hours we say the Kontakia of the Triodion and of the Forerunner, alternating them. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone on 6; and of the Forerunner, Ode 3 on 4; and the rest of the service of the day and of the Forerunner.*

*If the Finding of the Head of John the Forerunner occurs on the third Sunday of the Fast, the Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross:*
*On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers, the Stichera and at the Aposticha, all of the Triodion, according to custom.*
*At Great Vespers: after the Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried", Resurrectional Stichera (3), and of the Cross (4), and of the Forerunner (3). Glory, of the Cross; and now, the Theotokion: the 1st of the Tone. Prokeimenon of the day; and the Old Testament readings of the Forerunner. At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the temple, and of the Forerunner, those which are at the Aposticha and the Praises. Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, of the Cross. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera. Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, of the Cross. At the blessing of the loaves, "Birthgiver of God, Virgin" (twice), and of the Cross (once): "O Lord, save Thy people".*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", the Resurrectional Troparion (twice). Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, of the Cross. After the Kathismata, the Resurrectional Sessional Hymns, and their Theotokia; and a reading from the Explanatory Gospel. Polyeleos of the Forerunner; and both Sessional Hymns. Glory, the Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos; and now, the Theotokion; and a reading of the Forerunner. Graduals of the Tone. Prokeimenon, and the Resurrectional Gospel. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ". Psalm 50, and "Open unto me the doors of repentance". The Resurrection Canon with its Heirmos on 4; and of the Forerunner on 4; and of the Cross on 6. Katavasia of the Cross: "Moses prefigured of old in divine fashion"[^509]. After Ode 3, the Kontakion of the Forerunner and the Ikos; and the Sessional Hymn (twice). Glory, and now, of the Cross; and a reading of the Forerunner. After Ode 6, the Kontakion of the Cross, and the Ikos. At Ode 9 we chant "More honorable". Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, of the Cross. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera (4), and Prosomoia of the Cross (3, chanting the 1st twice), with their refrains: "Remember Thy congregation", 2nd: "God is our King before the ages". Then the refrain: "Arise, O Lord my God", and we chant the Idiomelon Sticheron the Idiomelon Sticheron of the day of the Triodion; Glory, the same; and now: "All-blessed art thou...". Great Doxology, Trisagion by chanting. Thereafter, the Entrance; we say the Troparion: "O Lord, save Thy people" (thrice). And there is the veneration of the Precious Cross according to custom, as is set forth in the Triodion. Then the litanies, and Dismissal, and procession. Glory, and now, the Evangelical Sticheron; and the Catechesis of Theodore the Studite; and the First Hour in the narthex. At the Hours, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory, of the Cross; and now, the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father", the Resurrectional Kontakion. At the Third Hour, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father", we say the Kontakia alternating them. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone on 4; and of the Cross, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Forerunner, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Troparion, and of the Cross, and of the Forerunner; then, the Resurrectional Kontakion; Glory, of the Forerunner; and now, of the Cross. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse: first of the Cross, then of the Forerunner.*

*On the same month, on the 25th day:*
*Our father among the saints, Tarasios, Archbishop of Constantinople.*
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Come, ye lovers of things divine and honorers of spiritual matters: today let us behold a table having the sweetness of abstinence, which the divine nourisher Tarasios prepared for us by the Spirit with immaterial virtues, all crying aloud without doubting: Pray, we beseech thee, that we may obtain forgiveness of sins.

Having appeared as head of an ecumenical council[^510], O wise father, thou didst truly delineate the truth: thou didst prune away the foul heretical offshoot, and didst establish the beauty of the Church, following the teaching of the Apostles of Christ. Therefore, with them we all cry out to thee: O father worthy of all praise, pray that our souls may be saved!

Thou didst wholly receive Christ and the beatitude of mercy, which is love toward the poor; for having poured out mercy richly upon them, thou didst obtain the mercy of the promise, for mercy boasts over judgment, it cries out. Therefore thou hast departed unto life; pray, we beseech thee, that we may obtain forgiveness of sins.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Alone without being constricted in thy womb, O all-holy Bride of God, hast thou borne the uncontainable God Who became man for the sake of goodness; therefore I pray thee, deliver me from the passions that constrain me, that walking by the narrow and straight path I may attain unto that which leads unto life, O Virgin.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the pure one beheld Him hanging upon the Tree—the all-ripe Cluster Whom she had borne without tilling in her womb—lamenting she cried out, and exclaimed: O Child, distill sweetness, whereby all intoxication of the passions may be taken away, O Benefactor, having been entreated through Thine own tender-heartedness for the sake of me who bare Thee!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A rule of faith and image of meekness, a teacher of abstinence the reality of things showed thee forth to thy flock; therefore thou didst acquire high things by humility, and rich things by poverty, O father Tarasios: pray to Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Canon, Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song to Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt, for He hath been glorified.*

*Troparia:*
Deliver me, O bounteous One, from assaults, captivity, and the bondage of sin, and guide me unto repentance, leading me swiftly unto Thy love.

Having thy heart as streams of life, O wise father, thou wast like a river of God to the Church, filled with teachings and watering these furrows, O father.

Thy precious head was anointed with ointment by the divine Spirit, O Tarasios; like Aaron's upon the beard, it descended richly upon thy garments, like the dew of Hermon.

*Theotokion:*
Behold, Isaiah cries aloud, O pure Virgin: having conceived the Angel of Great Counsel without seed in thy womb, thou hast given birth, and after giving birth thou remainest a pure Virgin.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing to Thee.*

*Troparia:*
Thou wast the establishment for the hearts of all by the bread of thy words, O hierarch, anointing as with oil and gladdening our hearts as with wine with the warmth of understanding and zeal of faith; thou wast the chief of the fathers, and the heretical oppositions, being like the gates of hades, thou didst tear down.

Having lifted up thy venerable hands unto God, thou didst ask peace for thy flock; therefore having ascended unto the heritage on high, thou hast fallen asleep.

*Theotokion:*
Strange is the birthgiving, strange the procession in the womb of the Virgin made new; and human nature, having slipped into corruption, hath He raised up from deception.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
By deeds of the knowledge of God thou didst shine forth; as a radiant beam thou didst illumine the Church; heresies thou didst slay by thy words; love and love for the poor thou didst pour forth, imitating the hospitality of Abraham, O father Tarasios: entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only all-pure Virgin Mother, who in purity didst surpass the Angels: cleanse me, who above all have become dust, defiled by carnal transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, granting great mercy, O pure one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding hanging upon the Cross Him Who had sprouted forth from her without pain, the unblemished ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, lamented maternally and cried out: Woe is me, O my Child! How sufferest Thou willingly, wishing to deliver mankind from the dishonor of the passions?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I sing to Thee, for by hearing, O Lord, I have heard and was afraid, for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who had gone astray; therefore do I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Plenteous in mercy.*

*Troparia:*
By the sweat of piety thou didst sow the divine word of the Church, and thou didst reap the wheat-ear of Orthodoxy, O Tarasios; in the land of the meek thou hast taken up thy dwelling, and rejoicing with them, make glad!

As Isaiah said, O venerable one, thou didst receive divine fear in the womb through the Spirit, and didst bear it for the Church, whereby the sprouts of heresies are dashed against the rock of faith.

By the outpouring of tears, O venerable one, thou didst wash away defilements, and hast reached the fountain wherein the unrained drink of sweetness is poured forth for the ever-existing ones; thou hast attained unto the utmost desires.

*Theotokion:*
Having conceived without seed, O all-immaculate one, God the Word in thy womb in thy womb, proceeds changelessly after our likeness unto the human race; entreat Him unceasingly on behalf of thy servants, that our souls may be saved from perils.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments, for other than Thee we know no other God.*

*Troparia:*
By the power of thy words thou didst expose the weakness of the ungodly, and with the bond of divine love thou didst bind thy flock, and by hope and faith thou keepest them unharmed.

By streams of abstinence thou didst nourish thy soul with food, having pure prayers as bread, and divine study, and uplifting humility.

Thou, O wise one, didst fatherly weave a church garment, wherein Christ, having robed thee with a precious icon for the sake of the faith, thou didst make clear the teachings of Orthodoxy.

*Theotokion:*
Thee do we all have as an intercessor, O pure one, and a firm foundation of hope, and a support, and a shelter, and a wall, and a bridge leading unto the kingdom.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy lovingkindness; from corruption, O God, raise me up!*

*Troparia:*
Having become a sacrifice and an immolation, thou didst subject thyself unto God by faith, and thou preservest the completion of His unbloody services.

Having now passed over from the earth into immaterial glory, heal by thy fervent prayer my soul, defiled by material passions, O venerable one.

Thou didst pare away the grossness of the letter by the profound subtlety of understanding; thou wast the pen of a ready writer for the Church, O Tarasios.

*Theotokion:*
In perplexity is thy birthgiving veiled, for strangely, O Virgin, dost thou suckle the Nourisher, and with milk dost thou nourish the Immaterial One, and in thy hands dost thou hold the Creator.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва днесь):*
Having made clear the Church with Orthodox dogmas, and having taught all to honor and venerate the precious icon of Christ, O blessed one, thou didst expose the godless decree of the iconoclasts. Therefore we cry out to thee: O father, rejoice, wise Tarasios!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Serving the golden image on the plain of Dura, the three youths disregarded the godless decree; but cast into the midst of the fire* bedewed, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Thou wast shown forth as a cup of wisdom, filled with virtues, and having called them together by the proclamation of thy lofty life, thou didst delight the flock, O divinely wise one; and with them, O father, thou criest aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Thou didst surpass the dwellings there and the precious delights, and rejoicing with thy God, thou art truly glad; therefore honoring thee, O all-blessed one, with boldness we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Girded in the loins with courage of mind, O Tarasios, and robed in a life that leads those who enter into faith, thou didst ascend unto the table of the King on high, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
He Who without a mother proceeds from the Father was born from thee without a father; and having preserved the tokens of thy purity unharmed, He showed forth a most pure birthgiving and virginity undefiled; for in thee the law of nature is renewed.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
