*From the furnace for the youths Thou didst of old prefigure Thy Mother, O Lord; and this image rescued from the fire those who entered unconsumed; whom we sing as having appeared through Thee unto the ends of the earth today, and we supremely exalt Him unto all ages.*

*Troparia:*
Thou, as a morning cloud, and as a dropping drop, and as dew wast shown forth; thou didst gather a wealth of beneficence upon the earth: thou didst enrich the hearts of the hungry, and didst kindle the candle of the soul, O wise one.

Illumined with the vesture of the hierarchical office by the light of thy deeds, thou wast shown forth as the key of chastity, the nourishment of abstinence, a true teacher of prayer, the sweat of repentance, and the law of life.

Bring forth the fruit of the time of repentance and conversion, and fear the threat of cutting down the barren fig tree, O my soul, and its curse; but nourish Christ with the good fruits of virtues!

*Theotokion:*
Forget not the voice of the fearful intercession of thy servants, but from every affliction and from divine threat snatch them away by thy prayers, O mediatress; for thy maternal prayer inclines God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously into corruption through eating, Who from the Holy* Virgin was ineffably incarnate for our sake, let us the faithful magnify with one accord in songs.

*Troparia:*
Thou didst wither the craftiness and the arrows of the enemy, O father, with the fire of abstinence, and with the coals of purity thou didst truly burn up the material wrath of the flesh, having been vouchsafed that radiant illumination, O Tarasios.

Dwelling with the patriarchal choirs, thou singest the angelic song with them: Holy, Holy, Holy is the Father the Begetter, and the Son Who is begotten, and the Spirit proceeding indivisibly; O Holy Trinity, glory to Thee!

Having seen into the heights with Paul, we cry aloud, O all-blessed one: pray, O Tarasios, that divine blessedness, the establishment of the Church, victory for the king[^511], and purification be granted unto us who honor thee with love!

*Theotokion:*
Incarnate ineffably from thy precious blood, He appeared unto us like the sun, O all-pure Mother, Bride of God: the Son Who before all the ages is equally without beginning with the Father, having driven away darkness to enlighten all things.

*On the same month, on the 26th day:*
*Our father among the saints, Porphyrios, Archbishop of Gaza.*
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder!" (Пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
Venerable father Porphyrios, taking the Cross of Christ upon thy shoulder, thou didst forsake all, and didst reach the wilderness, and didst do battle with the prince of this world, arming thyself with prayers and fasting; therefore thou didst cast down carnal mindedness along with him, having become a temple of the Divine Spirit.

Even as of old God saved three youths from the fire by an Angel, so now through thee He saved three youths who had slipped into a pit; for as they were being carried in a basket, the light of a cloud overshadowed them, having formed crosses[^512] on their bodies, unto the refutation of those who oppose thee, O Porphyrios.

Who can worthily sing of thy tribulations and illnesses, thy afflictions and sorrows, which were thy struggles according to faith, O Porphyrios? For great was thy boldness which thou didst show forth before God; therefore we beseech thee, for thou hast boldness, pray to the Lord on our behalf, that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Having received flesh from thy blood, the God before all ages showed thee forth as an intercessor unto men, O pure one; therefore deliver thy servants from every affliction and distress, and from the snares of the deceitful enemy; and vouchsafe all who glorify and worship thee to partake of the radiance of the elect.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Seeing Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and accepting sufferings willingly, Thy Virgin Mother cried out, O Master: Woe is me, O sweet Child! How dost Thou unjustly endure wounds, O Physician Who didst heal human infirmity and deliver all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A rule of faith and image of meekness, a teacher of abstinence the reality of things showed thee forth to thy flock; therefore thou didst acquire high things by humility, and rich things by poverty, O father Porphyrios: pray to Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Canon, the work of George, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The ancient Israel traversed the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod steps, and with Moses' hands outstretched in the form of a cross, defeated the power of Amalek in the wilderness.*

*Troparia:*
Adorned today as with imperial purple by thy teachings, O hierarch Porphyrios, the Church is adorned with them, and sings to the Lord of all.

Having shone forth by thy life, O venerable one, and by thy divine words, thou wast shown forth as a beacon unto all men, ever shining by deeds and teachings, and illuminating the ends of the earth.

Cleansed by the sprinkling of the All-Holy Spirit, O Porphyrios, and robed in the garment of divine virtues, thou didst enter into the places that know no setting, to celebrate the sacred mysteries unto Him Who anointed thee.

*Theotokion:*
Wishing to declare unto those on earth His ineffable love for mankind which surpasses the mind, the Son of God was born of thee in the flesh, O Bride of God, granting regeneration unto the faithful.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thy Church makes glad in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and establishment!*

*Troparia:*
Thou didst subdue beneath thy feet the reasonings of the impious, which were raging against God the Lord of all raving against God the Lord of all.

*Troparia:*
With the fire of thy deeds thou didst burn up the brushwood of Mani, O wise one; and thou didst extend the enlightenment of teaching unto the faithful.

Having set the feet of the faithful upon a firm rock, thou didst shake from its very foundation the foundation of the impious, O Porphyrios.

*Theotokion:*
All the earth is filled with the true knowledge, and ineffable glory, and grace of thy Son and God, O pure one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*
Thou didst shine forth by deeds like the morning star; thou didst drive away every heresy from thy flock, O hierarch Porphyrios, and didst enlighten the hearts of the faithful to hymn and glorify the Lord. Therefore, celebrating thy memory today, we cry aloud to thee: Pray to Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Without departing from the divine nature when He became flesh in thy womb, yet God having become man, He remained the One Lord; Who preserved thee after childbirth as a Virgin, even as thou wast before birthgiving, all-blameless: pray to Him earnestly to grant us great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, seeing Him Who sprang forth from her without pain suspended upon the Cross, lamenting maternally as was meet, cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Child! How dost Thou suffer all things, wishing to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*The Church, having seen Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparia:*
Having in thy soul the Sun of righteousness of the all-radiant shining of faith, O Porphyrios, thou didst enlighten the whole fullness of the Church with words.

Establishing in the love of the Word of God, thou didst loathe all attachment to visible things, O Porphyrios; therefore we honor thee as an equal of the Angels and an honorable hierarch.

Having come to the communion of the ineffable mysteries, O Porphyrios, thou didst preserve them by honorable deeds, purely and ineffably, as a worthy initiate of these mysteries.

*Theotokion:*
The Church is gloriously adorned with thy divine splendors, O most pure one; and she is manifestly made beautiful by them, and with love she glorifies thee, hymning thy birthgiving.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light, turning back from gloomy ignorance those who praise Thee with faith.*

*Troparia:*
Thou didst tear apart the cords of the snares with true words, O Porphyrios, establishing the power of the faith in every way.

Having drawn upon earth from the Comforter, O venerable one, rivers of ungrudging teachings, thou didst pour forth piety, O Porphyrios.

Having consumed with the fire of thy words, O Porphyrios, the torrents of impiety, thou didst enlighten men by the proclamation of piety.

*Theotokion:*
Strengthened by thee with spiritual power, we conquer the adversaries who oppose the truth of Christ, O most pure one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.*

*Troparia:*
Spiritually possessing thee, O Porphyrios, as an animate image of virtues, and having thee as ever striving unto all divine works in truth: therefore we honor thee.

Clothed with the power of the Comforter, even as the eyewitnesses of the Word, thou wast shown forth unconquerable by enemies, driving away and cutting down their impieties.

Renewing men by the water of baptism of adoption, thou didst bring them to Him Who makes them sons, and didst present them pure and blameless, O Porphyrios.

*Theotokion:*
Having understood[^513] thy divine mysteries which are beyond word, and thine ineffable glory, we who boast in thee, O most pure one, truly and unceasingly glorify thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Adorned with thy most sacred character, thou wast radiant with the garment of the hierarchical office, O blessed, divinely wise Porphyrios; and thou art made beautiful by the loftiness of healings, praying unceasingly on behalf of us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace the Abrahamite youths, burned rather by the love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*

*Troparia:*
When the Church of Christ was shaken by contrary winds and assaults, O father, thou didst establish her powerfully, as she sang unto Him: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Valiantly thou didst conquer the heretical assemblies, O wise one, having robed thyself with spiritual weapons; and thou didst cry aloud gratefully unto Christ: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Thou didst enter into the paradise of spiritual virtues, from which thou didst pluck the true and fair flowers, O father Porphyrios, with which thou didst breathe fragrance in the world.

*Theotokion:*
The mighty Word of God, Who was born of thee, O Virgin, granted thee as strength and might unto all the infirm, as they cry aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Daniel, having stretched forth his hands, shut the lions' gaping mouths in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparia:*
Thou didst arm the Church of Christ with thy words, O wise one, and didst establish her; therefore she always conquers the assault of heresies, and victoriously sings unto Christ, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Ever having thy mind toward heaven, O Porphyrios, thou didst obtain the divine transformation ineffably and incomprehensible to the senses, beholding the mysteries of Christ, and in faith thou didst cry aloud unto Him: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Thou didst stop the mouths of the spiritual lions who evilly blasphemed, O Porphyrios, by thy prayers, and didst break their jaws with the firm arrows of thy divine words, crying aloud earnestly unto Him Who strengthens thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
He Who sits upon the throne of the Father's glory, His Son and Word, made thee, O pure one, His divine bearer and most glorious seat, born of thee in the flesh, the One Whom all earthborn praise as Sovereign Lady.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, even Christ Who joined together the divided natures. Therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.*

*Troparia:*
Thou didst bring honorable gifts, thy acceptable virtues, unto the bountiful Master, for those who were delivered from the deceit of heresies through thee, O venerable father, who magnify the Creator of all.

With the showers of thy tears thou didst quench the flame of passions, and with dispassion thou didst serve the Creator, and wast vouchsafed the better glory, O Porphyrios, singing[^514] and ever magnifying Him.

Dancing with the supreme Angels, and with the saints and the venerable, unceasingly pray to the Lord Benefactor on behalf of those who in faith honor thee and praise thee, O father Porphyrios.

*Theotokion:*
The Master, having become incarnate for our salvation, granted thee as mediatress unto all and protection and healing, O pure one, unto those who faithfully glorify thee and magnify Him with love.

*On the same month, on the 27th day:*
*Our venerable father and confessor Prokopios of Decapolis.*
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Я҆́кѡ до́бля):*
Having been in the image and after the likeness of God from the beginning of creation, O blessed one, thou didst strive to preserve the dignity of the image by a pious mind, by purity of mind and holiness, by temperance of passions, and by the keeping of Christ's commandments, O most rich in piety.

Having first received by fasting, and divinely wise having later pleased the Creator by both through suffering: cleansing unto the one, and spiritual nobility; glorifying Whose coming in the flesh, thou didst venerate the holy icon according to His incarnation.

Exposing those who senselessly rejected the incarnation of the Word, O venerable one, thou didst finish thy course with wounded tortures and all manner of afflictions, with bonds and imprisonment, establishing the truth; therefore thou didst become an heir of the kingdom of Christ, and of ineffable joy, and of ever-existing radiance.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
As thou hast unfailing prayer and enduring supplication unto God, O most pure one, put tribulations to sleep, calm the waves of my wretched soul, and comfort my heart that is in affliction, O Maiden, I pray thee, and enlighten my mind with grace, that I may worthily glorify thee.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Seeing Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, the ewe-lamb that bore Thee lamented and maternally spoke to Thee: O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How are Thy hands and feet nailed by the lawless, O Word? And how hast Thou shed Thy blood, O Master?

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
With the streams of thy tears thou didst cultivate the barren desert, and with sighs from the depths thou didst make thy labors fruitful a hundredfold, and didst become a luminary to the universe, shining with marvels, O our father Prokopios: pray to Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing thy struggles gloriously, O blessed one." The work of Theophanes, Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the deep as on dry land with his steps, Israel, seeing Pharaoh the persecutor drowning, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!*

*Troparia:*
Robed with the love of piety, and with divine desire, thou didst shake off worldly surges, O venerable one, crying a song of victory unto God: For He is glorified!

Thy light-bearing manner of life, and the radiant simplicity of soul, joined together in thee, and the formation of virtue mixed with piety, wrought wonders in thee, O venerable one.

Enlightened by supernatural teaching and the radiant promises of Christ, Thou didst wither the assaults of the passions by temperance, crying a song of victory unto the Creator.

*Theotokion:*
Having divinely conceived the incarnate Word, Who was previously bodiless, thou didst bear Him for us beyond nature, O pure one, remaining a virgin after childbirth.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.*

*Troparia:*
Thy God-pleasing soul, inflamed with zeal for the sake of faith, O blessed one, powerfully destroyed the bitter rage and fury of the lawless, O God-bearer.

Stained with thine own blood, thou wast likened to the sufferings of the martyrs, O thrice-blessed one, and with a courageous soul thou didst patiently endure the wounds of the impious, O Prokopios.

Thou didst display twofold struggles wisely, O venerable one; having refuted heresies and manfully endured the rushes of lions in a divinely wise manner, O all-wise Prokopios.

*Theotokion:*
The Wisdom and Word of the Father, Which was before all ages, having ineffably taken flesh in the end of times from a Mother who knew not man, made her the Birthgiver of God.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Я҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*
Adorned with the beauties of temperance, thou didst brighten thy soul with the blood of martyrdom, and didst shine forth brighter than the sun, O wonderworking venerable Prokopios.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
The fervent protection of those who are in distress, and our helper, and reconciliation with God, through whom we were delivered from corruption, the Birthgiver of God, we the faithful call blessed.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Seeing Thine uplifted Son upon the Tree, O most pure one, torn in maternal compassion, thou didst mournfully cry aloud: Woe is me! How hast Thou set, O my timeless[^515] Light?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Christ is my power, God and Lord, the pure Church divinely sings, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.*

*Troparia:*
Watering thy heart with tearful streams, O venerable, blessed one, thou didst cultivate the seed of virtues with a pure mind, O venerable Prokopios.

Thou didst subject earthly mindedness unto the Divine Spirit, O father, having embraced the ascetic life, and art joined unto the choirs of martyrs, O all-blessed one.

As a divine and most true martyr for piety, thou didst expose heretical folly; for thou didst manfully despise the fury of the godless.

*Theotokion:*
Apart from the ordinances of the law, O all-blameless one, having conceived the Creator, thou didst also bear Him without corruption; and thou wast truly and richly shown forth as the Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate, I pray Thee, the souls of those who dawn early unto Thee with love, to know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, calling them back from the darkness of sin.*

*Troparia:*
Thy struggles accomplished ascetically, and perfected in blood, O father, we worthily honor; for the memory of the righteous is ever celebrated with praise, and remembered with faith.

Suddenly transforming and arranging all things for the better, as the Lord wills: He transformed the foul murder of the impious through thy temperance and the glory of martyrdom.

Taking the whole armor of the Cross upon thy shoulder, O father, thou didst follow Christ; consigning all things behind unto oblivion, thou didst stretch forth in thy course unto the things ahead.

*Theotokion:*
We hymn, O most pure one, the Lord Who from thee clothed Himself in flesh both passible and mortal, and made it deified by an unconfused union according to hypostasis.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing to Thy calm haven, I cry aloud unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly Merciful One!*

*Troparia:*
Passing over, O ever-memorable one, from truly ascetic glory, thou didst ascend unto the glory and honor of the martyrs, robed in a purple mantle dyed with thine own blood.

Having transcended the instability of transient things with a divinely wise soul and mind, thou wast piously winged toward the ever-enduring and unending good things, O all-blessed one.

Seeing thee enriched with goodly power by temperance and meekness, Christ looked upon thee with love for mankind, and showed thee forth as a partaker of glory, O most glorious passion-bearer.

*Theotokion:*
All-blameless Sovereign Lady, heal the heavy and grievous afflictions of my soul with secret healings, applying[^516] the effective remedies of thy Son to my passions.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Я҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*
Possessing thee today as a morning star, the Church dispels all mist of evil fame, honoring thee, O most glorious heavenly initiate Prokopios.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Angel made the furnace pour forth dew unto the venerable youths, but the command of God burning up the Chaldeans persuaded the tormentor to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*

*Troparia:*
Even as thy life became most radiant through asceticism, O divinely wise one, so manifestly was shown forth thy steadfast suffering; for thou didst glorify Christ, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Girded with the purity of chastity, O marvelous father, and through courage having received by grace a radiant crown of martyrdom, thou didst call out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

The impious who do not venerate Thine all-pure icon, O Savior, cruelly scraped Thy servant with wounds, as he sang and said unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoicing in thy birthgiving, O all-blameless one, we all call thee blessed; for for this cause, having now been delivered from carnal corruption, we cry aloud unto Him: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew unto the venerable, and with water didst burn up the sacrifice of the righteous man; for Thou dost all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: we supremely exalt Thee unto all ages.*

*Troparia:*
Loosed from corruption and shown forth even before death as uncorrupted, thou didst pass away hence unto the incorrupt life, O venerable one, brightly receiving death as a ladder, O divinely wise Prokopios.

Filled with radiant grace thou wast shown forth, and unto the unsetting Light thou didst depart, O father, having put away all material desire; therefore we honor thee, O divinely wise Prokopios.

Armed with the power of the Divine Spirit, thou didst conquer the regiments of the spirits of malice. Therefore the gates of paradise were opened unto thee, O blessed one, as thou didst hymn Christ unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
All generations call thee blessed, O blessed Sovereign Lady, even as thou didst foretell, O only divinely blessed one, thee the true all-pure Birthgiver of God, whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word was manifested unto men, Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.*

*Troparia:*
Having chosen to suffer lawfully, O martyr, thou didst mortify the impulses of passions and carnal wantonness by temperance, O favorite of Christ; therefore thou hast now found the immortal delight of dispassion with the bodiless hosts.

Radiantly thou standest before the righteous Judge, having received unfading crowns as a victor, earnestly praying, O Prokopios, on behalf of those who celebrate thy sacred memory and the divine feast of thy passing.

Now illuminated by the three rays that ineffably proceed from the One Godhead, thou wast vouchsafed the most rich inheritance and fair rejoicing, glad with the transcendent hosts, O father Prokopios.

*Theotokion:*
Mystically, having received an incorrupt form, the Word of God clothed men in incorruption, having dwelt in thee, full of grace, by the goodwill of the Father; therefore, O most pure one, with the heavenly hosts we magnify thee.

*On the same month, on the 28th day:*
*Our venerable father and confessor Basil, fellow-ascetic of Saint Prokopios.*
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Desiring the blessedness which is beyond the mind, O marvelous one, thou didst account temperance as food, poverty as wealth, non-possession as abundant property, and humility as good renown. Therefore thou didst also obtain the desire according to knowledge, dwelling in the tabernacles of the saints, O Basil.

Thou didst unswervingly accomplish the course of temperance, and didst keep the faith; therefore thou wast adorned with the crown of righteousness, O father, which Christ prepared for thee, granting recompense according to worth, and bestowing honors, and the rewards of labors. Pray to Him, O divinely inspired one, that our souls may be saved.

Every voluptuousness, enslaving thy body, thou didst cast away, O divinely wise one, making bitter the sense by the pains of temperance, and by harsh dwelling[^517] and endurance of temptations, and patient waiting through afflictions, through which thou receivest unending delight, and uninterrupted sweetness, and ineffable joy.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Bent down by the assaults of demons, and cast into the pit of destruction, take compassion, O Sovereign Lady, and establish me upon the rock of virtues; and having destroyed the snares of the enemy, vouchsafe me to do the commandments of thy Son and our God, that I may obtain remission on the day of judgment.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Seeing Christ the Lover of mankind crucified, O most pure one, and His side pierced with a spear, weeping she cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for the good things Thou didst do for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O Compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion.

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
A dweller of the wilderness, and in the body an Angel, and a wonderworker thou wast shown forth, O our God-bearing father Basil: having received heavenly gifts by fasting, vigilance, and prayer, thou healest the sick and the souls of those who hasten to thee in faith. Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who works healings for all through thee!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I know thee as a foundation of the faith, O father." The work of Theophanes, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The ancient Israel traversed the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod steps, and with Moses' hands outstretched in the form of a cross, defeated the power of Amalek in the wilderness.*

*Troparia:*
Having set thy going upon the rock of the faith, O father, thou didst not fear the rush of temptations; but thou didst remain unshaken as a champion of virtue and as one named after a kingdom.

The grace of the All-Holy Spirit, having strengthened Thee, confirmed Thee to endure painful wounds, and showed Thee forth as a victor over the vain-minded[^518] tormentor.

Thou didst obtain a wise fellow-sufferer and fellow-warrior, O father, who ever advanced in virtues; with whom rejoicing, O most rich one, thou didst complete the struggle of suffering through martyrdom.

*Theotokion:*
As protectress, and mediatress of salvation, and an insurmountable wall, do I present thee for my life, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God, who didst bear God the Benefactor of all creation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thy Church makes glad in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and establishment!*

*Troparia:*
Earnestly thou didst direct thy heart unto God, O venerable one, through ascetic grace, and thou didst shine forth through confession.

Having received warfare and the whole armor of the Cross, thou didst conquer the spiritual serpent and the sensible lion, O father.

Having piously preserved what was according to the image of the soul, O venerable ones, venerating the all-pure icon of Christ, ye suffered.

*Theotokion:*
Through thee the primal curse was blessed, and our nature held by corruption received incorruption, O pure one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
Having forsaken corruptible wealth and transient glory, thou didst receive heavenly wealth and truly eternal glory, O blessed father; therefore we call blessed thy holy falling asleep, and celebrating in it we cry aloud to thee: Remember us unto God, as thou hast boldness, O Basil!

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Incomprehensible and unsearchable, O Birthgiver of God, full of grace unto us is the fearful divine mystery accomplished in thee; for having conceived the uncircumscribed One, thou didst bear Him enclosed in flesh from thy most pure blood; Whom do thou ever entreat, O pure one, as thy Son, that our souls may be saved.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The unwedded pure one and Thy Mother, O Christ, seeing Thee hanging dead upon the Cross, maternally weeping said: What hath the lawless and ungrateful assembly of the Jews rendered unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many and great gifts, O my Son? I hymn Thy divine condescension.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee uplifted upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her place, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparia:*
How fair is thy patience, O all-blessed one! In old age thou didst display youthful steadfastness, O venerable one, wherein thou didst cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

United by love unto thy Master, and clothed with His help, thou didst not feel the bodily pains, but crying aloud didst sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

As namesake of the true kingdom, thou didst journey on the path leading thereto, having found Prokopios as a fellow traveler, crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Without knowing wedlock thou didst give birth, O Virgin, and after childbearing thou wast shown forth maidenly again; therefore with unceasing voices, in doubt-free faith, we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as a light, a holy light turning back from gloomy ignorance those who praise Thee in faith.*

*Troparia:*
Thou didst brightly struggle, O venerable Basil, having reproached the untamed audacity of Leo,[^519] and his senseless temper.

Having found Prokopios equal in might in labors, who completed the suffering, through diverse advancements thou art divinely crowned.

Now thou beholdest mentally Christ, Whom thou didst desire, and Whose manifestations from afar thou didst actively see, O most blessed, most sacred one.

*Theotokion:*
Thee do we set forth as an invincible weapon against enemies; thee have we acquired, O Bride of God, as the confirmation and hope of our salvation.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

*I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.*

*Troparia:*
Having received the kingdom on high through desire, O God-bearer, thou didst valiantly despise the senseless temper, and commands, and precepts of the earthly king. *(Twice)*

As a needle indeed of those upon the earth,[^520] thou didst wisely struggle; and clothed with divine power, thou wast shown forth as a crown-bearer, having conquered the opposition of the godless tormentors.

*Theotokion:*
Behold, now the proclamations of the prophets are fulfilled, since thou, O Virgin, hast borne Him Who was of old proclaimed by the prophets, and Who fulfills the things foretold by the grace of the Spirit.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the things on high" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having received divine revelation from on high, thou didst depart, O wise one, from the midst of turmoils; and having lived monastically in venerable manner, thou didst receive the operation of wonders, and to heal diseases by grace, O Basil most blessed, most sacred.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace the Abrahamite youths, burned more by love of piety than by flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*

*Troparia:*
Strengthened by divine power, O father, thou didst rebuke all those who reject the honorable icon of Christ as utterly senseless, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Cultivated and purified by temperance, O father, and having patiently embraced fasting, Christ received thee as thou criedst out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
As the Birthgiver of God and Queen of all creation, all we the faithful hymn thee, O pure one; and we cry unto thee: Rejoice! Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Daniel, having outstretched his hands, stopped the gaping mouths of lions in the pit; and having girded themselves with virtue, the youths, lovers of piety, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparia:*
Body and mind, O God-bearer, thou didst offer unto Christ as a whole-fruit, as a whole burnt offering perfected by the fire of testimony, wholly consumed by the flame of wounds, and crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having pursued thy enemies, and being persecuted, O father, thou didst cast them down, and didst trample down, O venerable one, the madness of the high-minded, untamed foe, seeing those who tormented thee to be untamed, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

As an artist excellent in doctrines thou wast shown forth unto us, O most rich one; for first thou didst most manfully set forth temperance unto sufferings, having thy disciple Prokopios as fellow sufferer, who cried aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
A light-bearing heavenly gate do we name thee, through which God passed; and the formerly bodiless One was incarnate, O most pure one, as God, being consubstantial with the Father; unto Whom we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, O uncut mountain, O Virgin: Christ, Who joined together the divided natures. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.*

*Troparia:*
Having now transcended every sense, O father, rejoicing with love thou hast passed over unto the Master, having found the wealth of thy struggles, angelic beauty, and the unending sweetness of paradise.

Together with thy disciple and fellow sufferer, receiving the hope of the blessed and eternal gladness, O God-bearer, watch over those who celebrate your festival.

From the earth unto heavenly delight ye passed away, O crown-bearers, where are the assemblies of the saints and the divine hosts of Angels; do ye save the company of disciples by your prayers.

*Theotokion:*
Tear asunder the bonds of transgressions of those who hymn thee, O Mother of God; stay the rushes of tribulations, calm sinful turmoil, crush the storm of calamities, O Virgin Mother.

*(Woodcut tailpiece vignette) (Woodcut headpiece vignette)*
*On the 29th day of the same month:*
*Our venerable father and confessor Cassian the Roman.*
*When the month of February has 28 days, then the service of the saint is sung at Compline.*
*And if it is a leap year:[^521] Stichera, Tone 6.*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid all aside" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
Wisely didst thou foresee fleeting deception, O most glorious father, and didst show it unto those in need of temperance as in no wise abiding after departure hence unto the Lord—this transient and temporal life and constitution. Therefore thou didst join thyself unto the better things of life, as an overseer and wise in life and deed, O Cassian, having pleased Christ.

Having taken up the Cross from infancy, O father, thou didst diligently and most honorably tread the hard and narrow path, obeying, O glorious one, the commands of Him Who called thee. Therefore thou didst twofold receive the recompense of thy labors and a worthy reward: the heavenly Kingdom of Christ, and festival with all the saints in light, O Cassian; with whom do thou unceasingly pray for our souls.

The matter of wonders belongs to every virtue, O divinely wise one, wherewith also formerly in the flesh upon earth, observing silence, O venerable one, and speaking afterwards by words and deeds, humble, meek, and guileless, and an exceedingly eloquent prophetic herald; but all the more now, when having ascended unto God Thou wast made marvelous, and receivedst the grace of healings.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Slipping into evil ways and enslaved by crafty deceit, O Bride unwedded, unto thy most marvelous compassion and fervent protection, O all-holy Maiden, do I the wretched one flee. Deliver me from the bond of temptations and sorrows, and save me, O all-immaculate one, from the constraints of demons; that I may glorify thee, and sing in love, and worship, and magnify thee, O Sovereign Lady, who art ever-blessed.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
A sword, even as Symeon said, pierced thy heart, O most pure Sovereign Lady, when thou didst behold Him Who shone forth from thee by an ineffable Word, by the lawless condemned as a criminal, uplifted upon the Cross, given vinegar and gall to drink, His side pierced, His hands and feet nailed; and weeping thou didst cry aloud, maternally lamenting: What is this new mystery, O sweetest Child?

*Canon, Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The deeply armed power of old overthrew the entire host of Pharaoh in the deep; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin: the supremely glorified Lord, for gloriously is He glorified.*

*Troparia:*
Radiant with divine virtues, O venerable one, thy life, O Cassian, departed unto God; from Whom do thou ask for us deliverance from transgressions, we pray thee, who celebrate with faith thy holy falling asleep.

By a conformable beckoning toward God, O all-glorious one, Thou didst turn away from impassioned thoughts, and wast light, being deified by immaterial communions, singing unto the supremely glorified Lord Who glorified Thee.

Thou wast a physician unto souls by thy divine teachings, O Cassian, directing the thoughts of monastics by grace. And thou didst guide them unto eternal life; therefore we all honor thee with love.

*Theotokion:*
We all know thee, O pure one, who didst bear Him in thy womb; for beyond understanding thou didst bear God clothed in mortal flesh, Who enlightened the assembly of the venerable, the supremely glorified Lord; for gloriously is He glorified.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The barren church of the nations blossomed as a lily, O Lord, by Thy coming, wherein my heart is established.*

*Troparia:*
Christ showed thee forth, O all-praised one, adornment of fasters, O venerable one, as a star shining in the height of the Church, an illumination unto those in darkness.

Conformed unto Him Who was partaker of the Cross and enlightened the world, O Cassian, thou wast crucified unto worldly passions, having conquered the wiles of demons.

Thy heart was filled with wisdom, O all-wise one; thou didst pour forth a deep of teachings by the Holy Spirit, watering the flocks of monks.

*Theotokion:*
Heal my soul, I pray thee, O all-immaculate one, that is enfeebled by passions and by every assault of the most wicked demons, through thy divine mediation.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йся):*
Being made venerable,[^522] thou didst dedicate thyself unto God; and having been enlightened with goodly forms, O Cassian, thou didst shine forth as the sun, ever illuminating the hearts of all who honor thee with the radiance of thy divine teachings. But earnestly pray to Christ on behalf of those who warmly praise thee with love.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Being a lover of sin, I pray thee who didst bear the sinless God Who takes away the sins of the world, O most pure one, to have compassion upon my sinful soul, and to cleanse my many sins; for thou art the cleansing of sinners, and the salvation and protection of the faithful.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Him Who was willingly crucified upon the Tree for our sake, the ewe-lamb weeping said with tears: What hath the ungrateful assembly of the Jews rendered unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many and great gifts, O my Son? But I hymn Thy longsuffering, O my God.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou hast come from the Virgin, neither an ambassador nor an Angel, but Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and hast saved me, the whole man. Therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparia:*
By thy ascetic struggles thou didst subject bodily wantonness unto the spirit, O venerable one; and by thy sacred teachings thou didst expose all the wiles and snares of the deceitful one.

Being willingly mortified unto worldly deadness, thou didst receive the inheritance of the life to come, O most glorious Cassian; and thou didst write down rules for the guidance and perfection of monastics.

The grace of the Spirit took up abode in thy soul, and showed thee forth, O most rich one, as higher than all carnal mindedness and manifestly above the nets of the deceitful one.

*Theotokion:*
Without mother, timeless, in time thou didst bear Him ineffably, O all-immaculate one, Who enlightened those who lived in faith with the venerable, and humbled the chief of malice, the serpent.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou wast a mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, from the night of ignorance, we have access unto Thy Father, the Origin of Light.*

*Troparia:*
Thou wast caught up,[^523] being borne along by thy virtues, O God-received one; and rejoicing Thou didst attain unto the heavenly habitations, worthily receiving the honors of thy labors.

Contemplating the beauty of Christ with a pure mind, Thou didst stand unshaken night and day, O most glorious one, receiving divine manifestations from thence.

...watered by the streams of tears, O God-bearing father, as a high-flowering tree thou didst bear the fruits of correction, divinely gladdening the thoughts of all.[^524]

*Theotokion:*
Life having shone forth beyond understanding from thee, O Virgin, put to death the enemy who had slain us all, and brought to life the world that unceasingly hymns thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I am dragged down in the abyss of sin; the unsearchable abyss of Thy compassion do I call upon: lead me up from corruption, O God!*

*Troparia:*
By the narrow path, O father, thou didst journey, and by thy words thou didst show it unto all; walking well wherein, they are brought into the spaciousness of paradise.

Thy life was venerable, and thy end blessed and honorable, O Cassian, our father, companion of the holy Angels.

Thou didst cast away the slumber of despondency, and abiding in the contemplations and actions of God, thou didst live angelically upon earth, O God-bearing, all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Having loved thee as good and as fair, the Benefactor of all, O all-immaculate one, is born from thee in the flesh, and deifies me through His boundless mercy.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The god-opposing command of the lawless tormentor raised up a high flame; but Christ spread out spiritual dew unto the pious youths: He Who is blessed and supremely glorified.*

*Troparia:*
Ever deified by the most radiant visions, O divinely wise one, thou wast shown forth as an unsetting luminary; wherefrom thou didst shine forth the saving word, illuminating the minds of all us who praise thee.

Thy tongue was enriched by the Spirit, which clearly traced out the saving law, and set forth rules; whereby all the flocks of monastics are ever directed, rejoicing, O all-blessed one.

Saving understandings and words, which drive away spiritual ignorance, thou didst richly pour forth, O divinely wise father; therefore we faithfully honor thee, celebrating thy all-holy memory.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the bridal chamber of divine glory and a fiery throne, whereon rested the incarnate Word through surpassing goodness; do thou entreat Him, O pure one, to have compassion and to show mercy upon us.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

*The fiery furnace of old in Babylon divided its actions by the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful, who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparia:*
Shining as a star by word and life, O Cassian, thou enlightenest all the ends of the earth, taking away the mists of ignorance, and bidding all ever to sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Thou didst set, having died as a sun, O all-blessed one; yet as a radiance thou didst leave behind thy unsetting words, illuminating the souls of us who celebrate thy holy memory with faith, O venerable one.

Thou ineffably delightest in spiritual beauties, having left behind the corruptible body, and thou wast vouchsafed to behold what the angelic hosts behold, singing, O glorious one: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth wider than the heavens, O Virgin; for having ineffably contained Him Whom they did not contain, and having given birth beyond understanding unto Him Who is twofold in nature, yet possesses one hypostasis in godlike manner.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, was manifested unto us, to enlighten those in darkness, to gather the dispersed. Therefore we magnify the all-hymned Birthgiver of God.*

*Troparia:*
Strength was granted unto thee from God and power to drive away the principalities of the ruler of this world; therefore thou didst manfully prevail exceedingly, as truly a divinely wise father, and rejoicing thou wast numbered among the choir of the venerable.

As a sweet-scented rose, the God-inspired words of thy divine tongue breathe fragrance upon us; for thou wast a fragrance of Him Who was poured out for our sake through mercy, and clothed Himself in flesh, O all-honored God-bearer.

Thy memory having today shone forth in venerable manner, illumines the hearts of those who praise thy angelic life, O Cassian; wherein we pray thee: Intercede that forgiveness of sins may be granted unto all.

*Theotokion:*
We offer unto thee Gabriel's greeting, crying aloud: Rejoice, O table that didst bear the Divine Bread! Rejoice, O gate that cannot be passed through! Rejoice, O joy of all who are saved, praise of the venerable, O Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin!

*(Woodcut tailpiece vignette) (Woodcut headpiece ornament)*
*Resurrectional Theotokia in the Eight Tones.*[^525]

*Tone 1. Theotokion:*
The glory of the whole world, which sprang forth from men, and bore the Master, the heavenly gate, let us hymn Mary the Virgin, the song of the bodiless and the adornment of the faithful; for she was shown forth as heaven and the temple of the Godhead; she, having broken down the barrier of enmity, brought in peace and opened the Kingdom. Having her, therefore, as the confirmation of faith, we have as champion the Lord Who was born of her. Be of good courage, therefore, be of good courage, O people of God; for He will conquer the enemies, for He is almighty.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Behold, the prophecy of Isaiah is fulfilled; for a Virgin thou didst give birth, and after childbearing thou didst remain as before childbearing. For God was born; therefore He also made new the nature. But O Mother of God, despise not the entreaties of thy servants, offered unto thee in thy temple; but as carrying the Compassionate One in thy arms, have compassion upon thy servants, and pray that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 2. Theotokion:*
The shadow of the law passed away when grace arrived; for even as the bush burned without being consumed, so a Virgin thou didst give birth, and a Virgin didst remain. In place of the pillar of fire, the Sun of righteousness shone forth; in place of Moses, Christ, the salvation of our souls.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O new wonder, greater than all ancient wonders! For who hath known a mother to give birth without a husband, and to bear in her arms Him Who upholds all creation? That which was born is the counsel of God; Whom having carried as an infant in thy arms, O most pure one, and having maternal boldness toward Him, cease not to pray on behalf of those who honor thee, to have compassion and to save our souls.

*Tone 3. Theotokion:*
How can we not marvel at thy God-manbearing childbearing, O most honorable one? For having not received the trial of man, O all-immaculate one, thou didst bear in the flesh without a father a Son Who before the ages was born of the Father without a mother, Who in no wise endured change, or mingling, or division, but preserved intact the property of both essences. Therefore, O Virgin Mother, Sovereign Lady, pray to Him that the souls of those who confess thee as Birthgiver of God in orthodox manner may be saved.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Without seed from the Divine Spirit, by the Father's will, thou didst conceive the Son of God, Who was before the ages from the Father without a mother; and for our sake, having come from thee without a father, thou didst bear Him in the flesh, and didst nourish the Infant with milk. Therefore cease not to pray, that our souls may be delivered from tribulations.

*Tone 4. Theotokion:*
David the Prophet, who through thee was the forefather of God, melodiously proclaimed of thee, unto Him Who wrought great things *(Woodcut cross vignette)*
...in thee: The Queen stood at Thy right hand. For God, Who was pleased to become man from thee without a father, showed thee forth as the Mother and mediatress of life, that He might renew His image corrupted by passions; and having found the lost sheep wandering in the mountains, taking it upon His shoulder, He will bring it unto the Father, and according to His own will, unite it with the heavenly hosts, and Christ, Who hath great and rich mercy, will save the world, O Birthgiver of God.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Look down upon the entreaties of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, quenching the fierce uprisings against us, releasing us from every sorrow; for thee alone do we have as a firm and sure confirmation, and we have acquired thy protection; let us not be put to shame, O Sovereign Lady, who call upon thee. Make haste unto the supplication of those who faithfully cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, help of all, joy and protection, and salvation of our souls!

*Tone 5. Theotokion:*
In the Red Sea an image of the unwedded Bride was of old prefigured: there Moses was the divider of the water, and here Gabriel was the minister of the wonder. Then Israel traversed the deep dryshod; and now the Virgin hath borne Christ without seed. The sea after the passage of Israel remained impassable; the immaculate one after the childbearing of Emmanuel remained incorrupt. O Thou Who art, and Who wast before, and hast appeared as man, O God, have mercy on us!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Thou art a temple and gate, a palace and throne of the King, O all-honorable Virgin, through whom my Redeemer, Christ the Lord, appeared unto those who slept in darkness, being the Sun of righteousness, willing to enlighten those whom He created according to His own image with His own hand. Therefore, O all-hymned one, as having acquired maternal boldness toward Him, unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 6. Theotokion:*
Who will not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin? Who will not hymn thy all-pure childbearing? For the only-begotten Son, Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, the same came forth from thee, pure one, ineffably incarnate; being God by nature, and having become man by nature for our sake; not divided into two persons, but known in two natures without confusion. Do thou entreat Him, O pure and all-blessed one, that our souls may be shown mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
My Creator and Redeemer, Christ the Lord, O most pure one, having come forth from thy womb, clothed in me, freed Adam from the primal curse. Therefore unto thee, O all-pure one, as truly Mother of God and Virgin, we cry unceasingly: Rejoice with the Angel! Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, protection and shelter, and salvation of our souls!

*Tone 7. Theotokion:*
A Mother indeed thou wast known to be beyond nature, O Birthgiver of God, and thou didst remain a Virgin beyond word and understanding; and the wonder of thy childbearing the tongue cannot declare. For being glorious being pure in conception; incomprehensible is the manner of thy childbearing: for where God wills, the order of nature is overcome. Therefore, knowing thee to be the Mother of God, we all earnestly pray unto thee: Pray that our souls may be saved.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Fleeing beneath thy protection, O Sovereign Lady, all we earthborn cry unto thee: O Birthgiver of God, our hope, deliver us from immeasurable transgressions, and save our souls.

*Tone 8. Theotokion:*
The King of heaven, for His love of mankind, appeared on earth and dwelt with men; for having taken flesh from a pure Virgin, and having come forth from her with the assumed nature, He is one Son, twofold in nature, but not in hypostasis. Therefore, truly proclaiming Him to be perfect God and perfect man, we confess Christ our God; Whom do thou entreat, O unwedded Mother, that our souls may have mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O unwedded Virgin, who ineffably didst conceive God in the flesh, Mother of God the Most High: accept the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, granting unto all cleansing of transgressions; now receiving our entreaties, pray that we all may be saved.

*(Woodcut ornament)*

*Theotokia of the Eight Tones,*
*Sung when there is "Glory..." to the saint in the Menaion, and "Both now..." according to the tone, as follows:*

*Tone 1. On Sunday Evening, Theotokion:*
Joy of the heavenly ranks, strong protection of men on earth, O most pure Virgin: save us who flee unto thee, for after God, O Birthgiver of God, we have placed our hope in thee.

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*
More radiant than all the holy powers, more honorable than all creation, O Birthgiver of God, Sovereign Lady of the world: save us, who didst bear the Savior, from myriad transgressions and afflictions, as thou art good, by thy prayers.

*On Monday Evening, Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin, the mystery that was in thee Moses beheld with prophetic eyes: the bush burning and unconsumed; for the fire of the Godhead consumed not thy womb, O pure one. Therefore we pray thee, as the Mother of our God, to ask peace for the world, and great mercy.

*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
I have surpassed the harlot and the prodigal, and the robber, and the publican, and the Ninevites in transgressions that exceed measure. Woe is me! What shall become of me? How shall I, a wretched one, be condemned to torment? O pure one, I fall down before thee: show mercy unto me by thy favor, even as Thy Son saved them *(Woodcut headpiece vignette)*
...hath.

*On Wednesday Evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, joy of the forefathers, gladness of the apostles and martyrs, and protection of us thy servants, O Virgin!

*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin! Rejoice, praise of the whole universe! Rejoice, most pure Mother of God, blessed one!

*On Friday Evening, Theotokion:*
Truly beyond understanding are thy marvelous great things, O Bride of God, of thy childbearing, which all the prophets proclaimed, all most glorious! Thy conceiving and childbearing, O all-immaculate one, are unfathomable and ineffable, whereby He hath saved the world, as He is merciful.

*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Rejoice from us, holy Birthgiver of God, Virgin, pure vessel of the whole universe, unquenchable candle, container of the Uncontainable, unconquerable temple! Rejoice, from whom was born the Lamb of God, Who takes away the sins of the whole world!

*(Decorative divider band)*

*Tone 2.*
*On Sunday Evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mary, Birthgiver of God, indestructible temple, yea, most holy, even as the prophet cries: Holy is Thy temple, wonderful in righteousness![^526]

*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
We have placed our hope in thee, O Birthgiver of God; let us not fall away from our expectation; save us from afflictions, O helper of the perplexed, and destroy the counsels of the adversaries; for thou art our salvation, O blessed one.

*On Monday Evening, Theotokion:*
O impassable gate, mystically sealed, blessed Birthgiver of God, Virgin: accept our supplications, and offer them unto thy Son and God, that through thee He may save our souls.

*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mary, Birthgiver of God, indestructible temple, yea, most holy, even as the prophet cries: Holy is Thy temple, wonderful in righteousness.

*On Wednesday Evening, Theotokion:*[^527]
As a fruitful olive tree, the Virgin brought Thee forth, the fruit of life, to bear the fruit of great and rich mercy for the world.

*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
All my hope I place in thee, O Mother of God; preserve me beneath thy protection.

*On Friday Evening, Theotokion:*
Save thy servants from afflictions, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin; for next to God we all flee unto thee, as unto an unbreakable wall and protection.

*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Come, calling with unceasing hymns, let us all glorify the Mother of the Light, for she bore our Salvation; and let us offer "Rejoice!" unto her as the only one who bore the Most Supreme of all, Who was God before the ages. Rejoice, thou who didst restore fallen Eve! Rejoice, O most pure Virgin unwedded!

*(Woodcut headpiece vignette)*

*Tone 3.*
*On Sunday Evening, Theotokion:*
O Birthgiver of God, protectress of all who pray unto thee: in thee do we take courage, and in thee do we boast, and in thee is all our hope; pray unto Him Who was born of thee for thy unprofitable servants.

*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
Holy and supremely pure praise[^528] of the heavenly ranks, hymn of the apostles, and fulfillment of the prophets, O Sovereign Lady: accept our prayers.

*On Monday Evening, Theotokion:*
Holy among women, Birthgiver of God, unwedded Mother: pray unto Him Whom thou didst bear, the King and God, that He may save us, as He is the Lover of mankind.

*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Without seed thou didst conceive of the Holy Spirit, and giving glory we hymn thee: Rejoice, O all-holy Virgin!

*On Wednesday Evening, Theotokion:*
Exceedingly sinning as I am[^529], O Maiden, snatch me by thy great mercy from the dire flame; and direct me, O pure one, by thy supplications, guiding me unto paths of salvation by thy prayerful entreaties.

*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Without seed thou didst conceive: *(see fol. 226r)*

*On Friday Evening, Theotokion:*
Holy among women, Birthgiver of God, Mother: *(see fol. 226r)*

*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Without seed thou didst conceive: *(see fol. 226r)*

*(Decorative divider band)*

*Tone 4.*
*On Sunday Evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, cloud of light! Rejoice, bright candlestick! Rejoice, jar containing the manna! Rejoice, rod of Aaron! Rejoice, bush unconsumed! Rejoice, bridal chamber! Rejoice, throne! Rejoice, holy mountain! Rejoice, refuge! Rejoice, divine table! Rejoice, mystical door! Rejoice, joy of all!

*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
O Birthgiver of God, Queen of all, praise of the Orthodox: shatter the insolence of the heretics, and put to shame the faces of those who neither worship nor venerate thy precious icon, O most pure one.

*On Monday Evening, Theotokion:*
Deliver us from our necessities, O Mother of Christ God, who didst bear the Creator of all, that we all may cry unto thee: Rejoice, sole protection of our souls!

*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Preserve thy servants from all afflictions, O blessed Birthgiver of God, that we may glorify thee, the hope of our souls.

*On Wednesday Evening, Theotokion:*
Having thee as our hope and protection, O Birthgiver of God, we shall not fear the snares of the enemy, for thou savest our souls.

*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Thee have we acquired as a wall, O most pure Birthgiver of God, and a tranquil haven and confirmation. Therefore pray *(Woodcut headpiece vignette)*
...I pray who am tempest-tossed in this life: guide and save me.

*On Friday Evening, Theotokion:*
O unquenchable candle, righteous throne, all-pure Sovereign Lady: pray that our souls may be saved.

*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Sole pure and most pure Virgin, who without seed didst bear God: pray that our souls may be saved.

*(Decorative divider band)*

*Tone 5.*
*On Sunday Evening, Theotokion:*
Fearful and most glorious and great is the mystery: the Uncontainable was contained in the womb, and the Mother after childbearing remained a Virgin; for she bore God incarnate from her. Unto Him let us cry, unto Him let us say a hymn, singing with the Angels: Holy art Thou, O Christ God, Who for our sake didst become man; glory to Thee!

*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
O full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions and cleansing of many transgressions, we pray.

*On Monday Evening, Theotokion:*
Assuage the afflictions of my much-sighing soul, O thou who hast wiped away every tear from the face of the earth; for thou dost drive away the afflictions of men and dispellest the sorrows of sinners; for thee have we all acquired as our hope and confirmation, O all-holy Mother and Virgin.

*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
O full of grace, intercede: *(see fol. 226v)*

*On Wednesday Evening, Theotokion:*
We call thee blessed, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin, and as is meet we believers glorify thee: an unshakable city, an insurmountable wall, a firm protection, and the refuge of our souls.

*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
We call thee blessed, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin, for from thee hath shone forth the Sun of righteousness, Christ, Who hath great mercy.

*On Friday Evening, Theotokion:*
Unto thee we pray as to the Mother of God: O blessed one, pray for the salvation of our souls.

*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Woe, O wretched soul! What answer shalt thou say unto the Judge in that hour, when thrones are placed for judgment, and the Judge shall come from heaven, having descended with myriads of Angels? When He shall sit upon the judgment seat to make reckoning with unprofitable servants like unto me, what shalt thou answer? What, then, shalt thou bring? Truly nothing, having defiled thy mind and thy body. Therefore fall down before the Virgin, and cry unceasingly, that rich forgiveness of sins may be granted thee.

*(Decorative divider band)*

*Tone 6.*
*On Sunday Evening, Theotokion:*
In archangelic manner let us believers sing unto the heavenly bridal chamber and truly sealed door: Rejoice, for whose sake the Savior of all, Christ, the Giver of life and God, budded forth for us! Cast down the tyrant, O Sovereign Lady, even our godless enemies, by thy hand, O all-pure one *(Woodcut headpiece vignette)*
...the hope of Christians.

*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
Thou didst receive the archangelic word, and wast shown to be the cherubic throne, and upon thine arms thou didst bear, O Birthgiver of God, the hope of our souls.

*On Monday Evening, Theotokion:*
No one that flees unto thee goes away from thee put to shame, O most pure Birthgiver of God, Virgin; but he asks grace, and receives a gift according to his profitable petition.

*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Of great gifts, O pure Virgin Mother of God, wast thou accounted worthy; for in the flesh thou didst bear One of the Trinity, Christ the Giver of life, unto the salvation of our souls.

*On Wednesday Evening, Theotokion:*
The eye of my heart I direct unto thee[^530], O Sovereign Lady: despise not my little sighing in the hour when thy Son judges the world; be unto me a protection and helper.

*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Thou art the transformation of the afflicted, the relief of the ailing, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God: save the city and the people, O pacification of the embattled, calm of the tempest-tossed, sole protectress of the faithful.

*On Friday Evening, Theotokion:*
By the prayers of her who bore Thee, O Christ, of Thy martyrs, and apostles, and prophets, and hierarchs, venerable and righteous ones, and of all the saints, give rest unto Thy departed servants.

*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
We have known God Who became incarnate of thee, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin: pray unto Him for the salvation of our souls.

*(Decorative divider band)*

*Tone 7.*
*On Sunday Evening, Theotokion:*
"Rejoice!" we cry unto thee with the Angel, O Bride of God, calling thee bridal chamber, and door, and fiery throne, and uncut mountain, and bush unconsumed.

*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
Make peaceful our life by the prayers of the Birthgiver of God, who cry unto Thee: O merciful Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Monday Evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, cloud of the spiritual and ineffable Sun, O Sovereign Lady! Rejoice, all-radiant candle! Rejoice, all-golden candlestick! Through thee, O all-holy one, Eve was delivered from the curse; but as having boldness toward thy well-pleasing Son and God, fail not to pray with thy maternal prayer, O all-pure one.

*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
A Light hast Thou shone forth from the Virgin, O Christ, and hast enlightened mankind: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Wednesday Evening, Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the only pure Virgin after childbearing as Mother of God the Word, saying: Glory to Thee!

*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Thou wast born ineffably of the Virgin, O Christ, and hast enlightened those who were in darkness, who cry: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Friday Evening, Theotokion:*
Thou who alone didst receive the Uncontainable, and didst bear God the Word incarnate *(Woodcut headpiece vignette)*
...pray that our souls may be saved.

*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Pray, O Virgin, with the apostles and martyrs, that those who stand before the judgment seat may find great mercy.

*(Decorative divider band)*

*Tone 8.*
*On Sunday Evening, Theotokion:*
Having taken up the voice of the Archangel Gabriel, let us say: Rejoice, Mother of God, who didst bear Christ the Giver of life unto the world!

*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
Heaven sings of thee, O full of grace, unwedded Mother, and we glorify thine unsearchable childbearing, O Birthgiver of God: pray that our souls may be saved.

*On Monday Evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, praise of the universe! Rejoice, temple of the Lord! Rejoice, overshadowing mountain! Rejoice, refuge of all! Rejoice, golden candlestick! Rejoice, honorable glory of the Orthodox! Rejoice, Mary, Mother of Christ God! Rejoice, paradise! Rejoice, divine table! Rejoice, tabernacle! Rejoice, all-golden jar! Rejoice, hope of all!

*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Thy protection, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin, is spiritual medicine; for fleeing therein, we are delivered from spiritual infirmities.

*On Wednesday Evening, Theotokion:*
I, O holy Virgin Birthgiver of God, flee unto thy protection: I know that through thee I shall find salvation, for thou art able, O pure one, to help me.

*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Snatch me, O Sovereign Lady, from the hand of the man-slaying serpent, who wishes to swallow me up utterly by cunning; shatter his jaws, I pray thee, and destroy his plots, that being delivered from his claws, I may magnify thy defense.

*On Friday Evening, Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, gate of the Word, Mother of our God: pray that we may be saved.

*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Accept the prayers of thy servants, O Sovereign Lady, and deliver us from every necessity and sorrow.

*(Decorative headpiece band)*

*Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokia of the Eight Tones:*

*Tone 1. Theotokion:*
When Gabriel announced unto thee: "Rejoice!", O Virgin, at the sound the Master of all was becoming incarnate in thee, the holy ark, even as righteous David said: Thou wast shown forth more spacious than the heavens, having borne thy Creator. Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who hath set us free through thy childbearing!

*Tone 2. Theotokion:*
All thy mysteries are beyond comprehension, all most glorious, O Birthgiver of God! With purity sealed and virginity preserved, thou wast known to be a true Mother, having given birth to the true God; entreat Him that our souls may be saved.

*(Woodcut headpiece vignette)*

*Tone 3. Theotokion:*
Thee, who didst mediate the salvation of our race, do we hymn, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin; for having assumed flesh from thee, Thy Son and our God, having accepted the Passion by the Cross, delivered us from corruption, as He is the Lover of mankind.

*Tone 4. Theotokion:*
The mystery hidden from all ages and unknown to Angels was through thee, O Birthgiver of God, made manifest unto those upon earth: God becoming incarnate in unconfused union, and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby having resurrected the first-formed, He saved our souls from death.

*Tone 5. Theotokion:*
Rejoice, impassable gate of the Lord! Rejoice, wall and protection of those who flee unto thee! Rejoice, storm-free haven and unwedded one, who didst bear in the flesh thy Creator and God: cease not to pray for those who hymn and worship thy childbearing!

*Tone 6. Theotokion:*
Calling Thy Mother blessed, Thou camest unto the Passion by voluntary choice, shining forth upon the Cross, desiring to seek out Adam, saying unto the Angels: Rejoice with Me, for the lost coin is found![^531] O our God, Who didst order all things wisely: glory to Thee!

*Tone 7. Theotokion:*
As a treasury of our resurrection, lead up from the pit and the depth of transgressions those who trust in thee, O all-hymned one; for thou didst save those liable to sin, having given birth to our Salvation; who before childbearing wast a Virgin, and in childbearing a Virgin, and after childbearing remainest still a Virgin.

*Tone 8. Theotokion:*
O Thou Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, O Good One, Who by death didst overthrow death, and didst manifest the Resurrection as God: despise not what Thou hast fashioned with Thy hand; show forth Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; accept the Birthgiver of God who bore Thee, who prays for us, and save, O our Savior, a desperate people!

*(Decorative headpiece band)*

*Dismissal Theotokia,*
*After the troparia of the saints, sung throughout the whole year, both at Vespers, and at "God is the Lord" at Matins, and again at the end of Matins:*

*Tone 1.*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Beholding the wonder of wonders in thee, O full of grace, creation rejoices; for thou didst conceive without seed, and ineffably didst bear Him Whom the ranks of the Angels cannot behold; pray unto Him for our souls.

*At the end of Matins:*
O most pure Birthgiver of God, blessed in heaven, and glorified on earth: Rejoice, unwedded Bride!

*(Woodcut headpiece vignette)*

*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Thou who without being consumed didst conceive the Divine Fire, and without seed didst bear the Lord, the Fountain of life, O Birthgiver of God full of grace: save those who magnify thee.

*At the end of Matins:*
Him Who is without mother in heaven beyond understanding and hearing, thou didst bear on earth without father; pray unto Him, O Birthgiver of God, for our souls.

*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Having thy protection, O most pure one, and being delivered from terrible things by thy prayers, preserved everywhere by the Cross of thy Son, as is meet we all piously magnify thee.

*At the end of Matins:*
O most pure Birthgiver of God, in heaven: *(see fol. 228r)*

*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
Thou who without being consumed didst conceive the Divine Fire: *(see fol. 228v)*

*At the end of Matins:*
Him Who is without mother in heaven: *(see fol. 228v)*

*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
Having thy protection: *(see fol. 228v)*

*At the end of Matins:*
O most pure Birthgiver of God: *(see fol. 228r)*

*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
When Gabriel announced unto thee: "Rejoice!", O Virgin, at the sound the Master of all was becoming incarnate in thee, the holy ark, even as righteous David said: Thou wast shown forth more spacious than the heavens, having borne thy Creator. Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who hath set us free through thy childbearing!

*At the end of Matins:*
Him Who is without mother in heaven: *(see fol. 228v)*

*(Decorative divider band)*

*Tone 2.*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Being a fountain of tenderheartedness, vouchsafe us mercy, O Birthgiver of God; look upon a people that hath sinned; manifest thy power as always: for hoping in thee, we cry "Rejoice!" unto thee, as of old Gabriel did, supreme commander of the bodiless hosts.

*At the end of Matins:*
Holy Mother of the ineffable Light, honoring thee with angelic hymns, we piously magnify thee.

*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Partakers of the divine nature have we become through thee, O Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin; for thou didst bear for us God incarnate; therefore as is meet we piously magnify thee.

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying: Rejoice, cloud of the unwaning Light, having borne the Lord in the bosom of glory!

*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Most glorious art thou, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin; we sing of thee: for by the Cross of thy Son, hades was overthrown, and death was put to death; we that were dead arose, and were accounted worthy of life; we received paradise, the ancient enjoyment. Therefore, giving thanks, we glorify Christ our God as mighty and alone plenteous in mercy.

*At the end of Matins:*

*(Woodcut headpiece vignette)*
Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying: Rejoice, rod from which without seed God budded forth, destroying death upon the tree!

*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
Partakers of the divine nature have we become: *(see fol. 228v)*

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying: *(see fol. 228v)*

*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
Most glorious art thou, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin: *(see fol. 228v)*

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying: Rejoice, rod: *(see fol. 229r)*

*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
All thy mysteries are beyond comprehension, all most glorious, O Birthgiver of God! With purity sealed and virginity preserved, thou wast known to be a true Mother, having given birth to the true God; entreat Him that our souls may be saved.

*At the end of Matins:*
Holy Mother of the ineffable Light: *(see fol. 228v)*

*(Rubrical direction)*
*If "Alleluia" occur, in whatever tone, these are said:*[^532]

*Troparion:*
Apostles, martyrs, and prophets, hierarchs, venerable and righteous ones, who have fought the good fight and kept the faith, having boldness toward the Savior: entreat Him as good on our behalf, that our souls, we pray, may be saved.

*Troparion:*
Remember, O Lord, as Thou art good, the souls of Thy servants, and pardon whatsoever they have sinned in this life; for no one is sinless, except Thee, Who art able to grant rest even unto the departed.

*Theotokion:*
Holy Mother of the ineffable Light: *(see fol. 228v)*

*(Decorative divider band)*

*Tone 3.*
*On Sunday Evening and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
At the beauty of thy virginity and the most radiant purity of thee, Gabriel marveled, and cried unto thee, O Birthgiver of God: What worthy praise shall I bring unto thee? What shall I name thee? I am at a loss and astounded. Therefore, as I was commanded, I cry unto thee: Rejoice, O full of grace!

*At the end of Matins:*
Whithersoever one finds salvation, thither also he rightly flees; and what other such refuge is there as thou, O Birthgiver of God, sheltering our souls?

*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Our refuge and strength, O Birthgiver of God, mighty help of the world: by thy prayers shelter thy servants from every necessity, O sole blessed one.

*At the end of Matins:*
Whithersoever one finds salvation: *(see fol. 229r)*

*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Having acquired the Cross of thy Son, O Birthgiver of God, as a rod of strength: thereby we cast down the insolence of the enemy, unceasingly magnifying thee with love.

*At the end of Matins:*
Our refuge and strength: *(see fol. 229r)*

*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
The prophets proclaimed, the apostles taught, the martyrs confessed, and we have believed that thou art truly the Birthgiver of God: therefore do we also magnify thine ineffable childbearing.

*At the end of Matins:*
Whithersoever one finds salvation: *(see fol. 229r) (Woodcut headpiece vignette)*

*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
Having acquired the rod of strength: *(see fol. 229r)*

*At the end of Matins:*
Our refuge and strength, O Birthgiver of God: *(see fol. 229r)*

*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
Thee, who didst mediate the salvation of our race, do we hymn, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin; for having assumed flesh from thee, Thy Son and our God, having accepted the Passion by the Cross, delivered us from corruption, as He is the Lover of mankind.

*At the end of Matins:*
The prophets proclaimed: *(see fol. 229r)*

*(Decorative divider band)*

*Tone 4.*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Unto thee who wast nurtured in the temple in the Holy of Holies, clothed with faith and wisdom and boundless virginity, the supreme commander Gabriel brought from heaven the salutation and "Rejoice!": Rejoice, blessed one! Rejoice, most glorious one: the Lord is with thee!

*At the end of Matins:*
As thou art higher than all creation, being at a loss to hymn thee worthily, O Birthgiver of God: therefore we pray thee, have mercy on us.

*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Let us sinners and humble ones now run earnestly unto the Birthgiver of God, and let us fall down in repentance, crying from the depth of our soul: O Sovereign Lady, help, having tenderheartedness toward us; make haste, for we perish from the multitude of our transgressions; turn not thy servants away empty, for thee alone do we have as our hope.

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying: Thou art the bush wherein Moses unconsumed beheld the fire of the Godhead as a flame.
